### [1] [The Gribov problem on the horizon: A short guide to the Gribov-Zwanziger framework and its refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10303)
*Antonio D. Pereira*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This mini-review examines the Gribov problem in Euclidean Yang-Mills theories—arising from gauge-fixing ambiguities (Gribov copies)—and outlines the development and implications of the Gribov-Zwanziger action, including condensate refinements, infrared propagator predictions, lattice comparisons, BRST symmetry considerations, and open questions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The non-perturbative obstruction to global gauge fixing—the existence of Gribov copies—undermines the Faddeev-Popov prescription and necessitates a geometric understanding of gauge-field space to correctly describe infrared dynamics.

Method: Analytical construction and refinement of the Gribov-Zwanziger effective action, incorporation of dimension-two condensates, analysis of infrared gluon and ghost propagators, comparison with lattice QCD data, and examination of BRST symmetry realization.

Result: The Gribov-Zwanziger framework yields infrared-suppressed gluon propagators and enhanced ghost propagators, qualitatively consistent with lattice simulations; condensate extensions improve quantitative agreement and restore certain BRST-related identities in the infrared.

Conclusion: The Gribov problem is essential for a consistent non-perturbative formulation of Yang-Mills theory; the Gribov-Zwanziger approach provides a viable infrared completion of gauge-fixed quantization, though conceptual issues—especially regarding BRST invariance and physical observables—remain open.

Abstract: Gauge fixing in Yang-Mills theories encounters a non-perturbative obstruction: distinct gauge-equivalent configurations can satisfy the same gauge condition. These are the so-called Gribov copies and their existence challenges the global validity of the Faddeev-Popov prescription. Addressing this ambiguity motivates a framework that connects the geometry of gauge-field space to infrared dynamics. This mini-review introduces the Gribov problem in Euclidean Yang-Mills theories and traces the construction of the Gribov-Zwanziger action and its refinement through the introduction of dimension-two condensates. We discuss the resulting infrared propagators, their comparison to lattice results, and the role of BRST symmetry, before surveying selected applications and open questions.

</details>


### [2] [Matrix-vector models for the 3D Ising CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12071)
*Kaichi Mizohata,Tatsuya Seko,Asato Tsuchiya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT using interacting fermions in the lowest Landau level on a sphere, reformulating deformed Hamiltonians into basis-independent matrix–vector and then pure matrix models; with a Gaussian deformation, the bosonic matrices acquire a heat-kernel propagator, and exact diagonalization (up to 16 fermions) shows low-energy spectra match the 3D Ising CFT over a range of the width parameter ξ, without UV/IR mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct UV-complete, non-perturbative realizations of 3D CFTs—specifically the 3D Ising model—using fermionic quantum mechanics on a sphere, avoiding pathologies like UV/IR mixing present in conventional fuzzy-sphere field theories.

Method: Starts from interacting fermions in the lowest Landau level on S²; introduces a class of angular-momentum-damped deformed Hamiltonians; reformulates each as a basis-independent matrix–vector quantum mechanical model, then integrates out fermionic vectors to obtain a bosonic matrix model; selects a Gaussian damping function leading to a heat-kernel propagator for bosonic matrices with width parameter ξ; performs exact diagonalization of the deformed Hamiltonians at criticality for up to N = 16 fermions.

Result: The low-energy spectra of the deformed Hamiltonians at criticality agree quantitatively with known 3D Ising CFT scaling dimensions over a finite range of ξ; the resulting matrix models exhibit no UV/IR mixing in the low-lying spectrum—unlike standard fuzzy-sphere constructions.

Conclusion: Gaussian-deformed lowest-Landau-level fermion models yield robust, UV-regular matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT, establishing a new route to non-perturbative CFT construction free from UV/IR mixing artifacts.

Abstract: The construction of three-dimensional conformal field theories (3D CFTs) using interacting fermions in the lowest Landau level on a sphere has recently proven remarkably efficient, while it differs from that of conventional field theories on the fuzzy sphere. Focusing on the 3D Ising CFT, we consider a class of models that are defined by deformed Hamiltonians specified by a function which controls how the interaction is damped at high angular momenta, and this class includes the original model. We show that each model in the class can be reformulated in a basis-independent manner as a matrix--vector quantum-mechanical model, and, upon integrating out the fermionic vectors, as a matrix model. We then focus on a Gaussian choice, for which the bosonic matrices acquire a heat-kernel propagator whose width is controlled by a parameter $\xi$. Through exact diagonalization with the number of fermions up to $16$, we find that the low-energy spectra of the deformed Hamiltonians at criticality agree with those in the 3D Ising CFT for a range of $\xi$. We thus obtain matrix-model realizations of the 3D Ising CFT that, in contrast to conventional fuzzy-sphere field theories, show no sign of UV/IR mixing in the low-lying spectrum.

</details>


### [3] [Fast, training-free simplification of spinor-helicity amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10115)
*Yifan Fei,Zhen Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: A training-free, greedy best-first search method simplifies spinor-helicity amplitude expressions using on-shell identities; it recovers Parke-Taylor from 1024 Feynman forms, solves all benchmark cases faster and more reliably than prior ML methods, and provides verified, derivation-aware simplifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable physics-driven simplification of scattering amplitudes without reliance on training data or human ansätze—particularly for extracting structural insights (e.g., preferred poles, factorizations) from messy, identity-scrambled or diagram-generated expressions.

Method: Greedy best-first search over equivalence-preserving on-shell identities (e.g., Schouten, momentum conservation, Dirac equations), guided by heuristic expression ranking; no machine learning or parameter tuning; derivations are explicitly recorded and verified over finite fields at on-shell kinematics.

Result: Solves 100% of public ML test sets (4–6 point); derives Parke-Taylor formula from all 1024 distinct Feynman-diagram forms of the 5-gluon MHV amplitude; handles 21,600 larger test cases with extended search/ranking; mean runtime 0.02–0.3 s/core; all results independently verified.

Conclusion: Training-free symbolic search is effective for amplitude simplification, enabling derivation-aware compression, structural analysis, and paving the way for automated discovery of physically meaningful representations.

Abstract: We present a training-free method that simplifies massless spinor-helicity expressions by a greedy best-first search over on-shell identities, and test it at four, five, and six points. Applied to the color-ordered five-gluon MHV tree-level amplitude, the search derives the Parke-Taylor formula from all 1024 Feynman-diagram expressions with different polarization reference spinors and up to 228 numerator terms. On public test sets used by previous machine-learning methods, the search solves every case and generally outperforms these methods in solve rate. Its mean wall-clock time per case is 0.02-0.3 s on one CPU core. With longer searches and an alternative ranking of candidate expressions, it also solves all 21,600 cases of a new suite of larger test expressions. Each result comes with an explicit derivation and is independently checked over finite fields at on-shell kinematics. For the classes studied here, the method effectively compresses long expressions produced by identity scrambling or Feynman-diagram calculations into compact forms. This is a first step toward extracting physics from amplitudes by relating distinct compact expressions, selecting representations with preferred factors and poles, and guiding the discovery of new amplitude structures.

</details>


### [4] [Large-D approach to nearly critical holographic matter and Yang-Mills deconfinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10203)
*Matti Järvinen,Leonardus B. Putra,Dorin Weissman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic large-D-inspired expansion for 5D Einstein-dilaton black holes dual to nearly critical 4D strongly coupled field theories; it derives effective non-relativistic hydrodynamics, thermodynamics, and domain wall solutions—including confinement-deconfinement walls in improved holographic QCD—and matches surface tension to lattice data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a controllable holographic framework for nearly critical, strongly coupled 4D field theories by leveraging the large-D limit of gravity via dimensional reduction, enabling systematic access to non-equilibrium dynamics and phase structure.

Method: Systematic dimensional reduction of 5D Einstein-dilaton gravity inspired by the large-D expansion; derivation of effective near-horizon and near-boundary equations; use of holographic renormalization and black hole thermodynamics to extract boundary energy-momentum tensor and hydrodynamic constitutive relations; construction of static and steady-state domain wall solutions with planar, cylindrical, and spherical symmetry; completion of near-horizon geometry via holographic RG flow to AdS5.

Result: A closed set of non-relativistic hydrodynamic equations emerges at leading nontrivial order; energy-momentum conservation is shown to be equivalent to satisfaction of these equations; analytic domain wall solutions (including momentum-carrying steady states) are obtained; near-horizon geometry is consistently matched to an asymptotically AdS5 region; surface tension of the confinement-deconfinement wall in improved holographic QCD is computed and agrees quantitatively with lattice QCD.

Conclusion: The large-D-motivated expansion provides a robust, thermodynamically consistent holographic effective theory for strongly coupled near-critical systems, successfully describing equilibrium and non-equilibrium phase interfaces—particularly validating its predictive power for QCD-like confinement transitions.

Abstract: We study a systematic expansion of dynamical black hole solutions in a class of five-dimensional Einstein-dilaton theories that are holographically dual to nearly critical, strongly coupled four-dimensional field theories. The expansion is motivated, through dimensional reduction, by the large-D limit of pure gravity, where D is the number of spacetime dimensions. At leading nontrivial order, the Einstein equations reduce to a closed set of non-relativistic hydrodynamic equations in four dimensions, as first shown in our previous letter; these equations generalize the large-D effective theory of black branes. We derive the thermodynamics and hydrodynamics of the system from black hole thermodynamics and the holographic dictionary, and show that the energy-momentum tensor extracted from the near-boundary geometry is conserved precisely when the effective equations hold. We analyze planar, cylindrical, and spherical domain walls between coexisting phases, as well as non-equilibrium steady-state walls with nonzero momentum flux. We also show how the near-horizon geometry can be completed by a holographic flow to an asymptotically AdS5 region. Finally, as an application to the confinement-deconfinement transition of Yang-Mills theory, we construct the domain wall between the confined and deconfined phases in improved holographic QCD, compute its surface tension, and compare it with recent lattice results.

</details>


### [5] [Deep Inelastic Scattering and Proton Structure Functions in AdS/QCD models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12693)
*Rafael A. Costa-Silva,Henrique Boschi-Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies holographic fermionic deep inelastic scattering (DIS) in a deformed AdS model using SUSY-inspired truncation of fermionic potentials; it shows that quadratic approximation reproduces soft-wall model results for structure functions F₁,₂, and higher-order approximations improve agreement with experimental data at large Bjorken-x.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study fermionic DIS in a deformed AdS background while preserving isospectrality between chiral modes via supersymmetric (SUSY) partner potentials, bridging bottom-up holography with QCD phenomenology.

Method: Imposes SUSY partner condition on Schrödinger-like equations for left/right fermionic chiral modes; applies systematic truncation of the resulting effective potentials; computes DIS structure functions F₁(q²,x) and F₂(q²,x) in quadratic and higher-order approximations of the deformed AdS metric; compares against full numerical solution and experimental data.

Result: Quadratic truncation yields F₁,₂ identical to soft-wall model predictions; higher-order approximations progressively improve agreement with experimental F₂ data at x = 0.65, 0.75, 0.85; confirms convergence toward full deformed AdS solution as expansion order increases.

Conclusion: SUSY-motivated potential truncation provides a controlled, computationally efficient framework for holographic fermionic DIS in deformed AdS; high-x structure function description benefits significantly from beyond-quadratic corrections, validating the approach for precision QCD-holography correspondence.

Abstract: In this work we study the holographical fermionic deep inelastic scattering in a deformed AdS model. Imposing that the potentials that emerge from the Schrödinger-like equations for the fermionic chiral left and right modes are SUSY partners to preserve isospectrality, we implement a systematical truncation of such potentials. Then, we show that in the quadratic approximation of the deformed AdS model, the fermionic DIS and the proton structure functions $F_{1,2}(q^2,x)$ are identical with the results obtained from the soft wall model. Further, we obtain and compare high-order approximations for the fermionic potentials with the full solution of the deformed AdS model and show, as expected, that as greater the order of approximation, the better are the results for $F_{1,2}(q^2,x)$ when compared with experimental data explicitly for the Bjorken parameter values $x=0.85, 0.75$ and $0.65$.

</details>


### [6] [A Free-Field Realization of Boundary Symmetries in Discrete Jackiw--Teitelboim Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10074)
*H.T. Özer,Aytül Filiz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a finite-periodic, free-field Miura realization of the Virasoro–dilaton algebra underlying sl(2,R) Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity, using a single logarithmic Cartan field and discrete Miura map; it yields an induced Poisson structure for both stress tensor and dilaton (weight −1), matches continuous transformation laws, but does not equate the Miura bracket with the canonical BF boundary-charge algebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a lattice-compatible, free-field realization of the classical Virasoro–dilaton sector in JT gravity—specifically, to unify the stress tensor and dilaton within a single Cartan field framework while preserving correct conformal weights and Poisson geometry without ad hoc brackets.

Method: Applies the discrete Miura map and its associated free-field Poisson bracket to a single logarithmic Cartan field to build a lattice spin-two (Virasoro) current; constructs the dilaton as a periodic composite satisfying an inhomogeneous difference equation and monodromy condition; derives the induced mixed Poisson bracket; analyzes transfer dynamics, quadratic adjoint invariant, and finite Fourier algebra versus continuous Virasoro–dilaton transformations.

Result: An explicit finite-periodic free-field realization where both the Virasoro generator and dilaton (conformal weight −1) emerge from the same Cartan variables; the Poisson bracket for the dilaton is fully induced—not postulated—and consistent with boundary transformation laws of JT gravity.

Conclusion: The construction successfully provides a Miura-based free-field model of the Virasoro–dilaton system compatible with JT gravity’s classical structure and boundary symmetries, but leaves open the identification of the resulting Poisson algebra with the canonical BF boundary-charge algebra.

Abstract: We investigate a finite-periodic Miura free-field realization of the classical Virasoro--dilaton sector relevant to sl(2,R) Jackiw--Teitelboim (JT) gravity. Starting from a single logarithmic Cartan field, we use the established discrete Miura map and its free-field Poisson bracket to construct the lattice spin-two current. The dilaton is then realized as a periodic composite of the same Cartan variables, selected by an inhomogeneous difference equation and its monodromy condition. This construction yields an explicit induced mixed Poisson bracket without postulating an independent dilaton bracket. We examine the associated transfer problem and quadratic adjoint invariant, and test the finite Fourier algebra against the continuous Virasoro--dilaton transformation, in which the dilaton has conformal weight minus one. The construction supplies a free-field counterpart of the JT boundary transformation laws; it does not by itself identify the Miura Poisson structure with the canonical BF boundary-charge algebra.

</details>


### [7] [Baby Universes Nucleate by Hiding Observers from the Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10112)
*Sergio E. Aguilar-Gutierrez,Takato Mori*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a double-scaled SYK model with two coupled systems and an observer modeled as a heavy particle; it shows that wormhole growth is constrained by the observer, leading to a first-order phase transition where a baby universe carries away the observer and its entropy, causing a drop in Krylov complexity and loss of Holevo information at the boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the role of observers in closed universes within holography, particularly how an observer (as a localized physical system) affects spacetime geometry (e.g., wormhole dynamics) and quantum information flow across the boundary.

Method: Analyzes a double-scaled Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK) model with two copies coupled via a four-parameter interaction generalizing Maldacena-Qi; introduces an observer as a heavy particle state; computes wormhole length via Krylov complexity; identifies rare Hamiltonian string cancellations enabling baby universe nucleation; evaluates thermodynamic and information-theoretic consequences (entropy budget, latent heat, Holevo information loss).

Result: The presence of the observer forces wormhole growth to halt and triggers a first-order transition: rare string cancellations produce a closed baby universe carrying the observer and its microstate entropy; the now-empty wormhole shrinks discretely, releasing energy matching T × S_observer as latent heat; Krylov complexity drops sharply, signaling baby universe emergence; boundary loses Holevo information about observer microstates.

Conclusion: Observers in holographic models induce observable, thermodynamically sharp transitions in bulk geometry and boundary complexity, providing a concrete mechanism for observer-dependent spacetime emergence and information localization—supporting a dynamical, information-theoretic resolution to the black hole and baby universe information puzzles.

Abstract: Observers are expected to be essential to describe closed universes, but their description within a conventional holographic theory remains challenging. We study two double-scaled Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev models coupled by a four-parameter interaction, which includes a generalized Maldacena-Qi coupling, in a state that contains an observer, represented as a particle. With a heavy particle inside, the wormhole connecting the two systems can only grow. Rare products of Hamiltonian strings cancel the string of the particle: a closed baby universe then carries the observer, and the wormhole, now empty, recovers discrete states of finite length. The energy gained by the empty wormhole pays for the entropy of the observer, in a first-order transition whose latent heat is the transition temperature times this entropy. Krylov complexity, which measures the wormhole length, drops at the transition and thus detects from the boundary the emergence of the baby universe, whose boundary description is actively debated. The boundary also loses the Holevo information about the microstates of the observer, which the baby universe carries.

</details>


### [8] [Small Defects at Large Charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10113)
*Jesse Woods*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes conformal data for cusped line defects with impurities in the abelian Higgs model in the semiclassical large-charge limit, obtaining scaling dimensions to O(q³) and resumming singular behavior near critical coupling; it also evaluates the Bremsstrahlung function, fusion-limit operator coefficient, and Casimir energy, comparing results with perturbative, numerical, and bootstrap bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute conformal data of impurity-deformed cusped line defects in a controlled semiclassical regime—specifically the large-charge limit—where exact non-perturbative methods are intractable but physical observables (e.g., scaling dimensions, Bremsstrahlung) encode rich dynamics of defect CFTs and phase structure.

Method: Semiclassical analysis in the large-charge limit combined with perturbation theory in the impurity charge q; all-order resummation of Feynman diagrams via effective action techniques; analytic continuation near critical gauge coupling; matching to known perturbative expansions and numerical solutions; comparison with conformal bootstrap bounds on cusps.

Result: Scaling dimension of impurity computed to O(q³) uniformly in 't Hooft couplings; leading singular behavior near loss of conformality resummed to all orders in q; Bremsstrahlung function and fusion-limit irrelevant operator coefficient determined at leading order; Casimir energy shown to be semiclassically uncorrected; agreement verified with perturbative and numerical results and recent cusp bounds.

Conclusion: The semiclassical large-charge framework provides a powerful, resurgent tool for extracting high-precision conformal data of line defects with impurities; the interplay between rapidity scaling and semiclassics reveals structural constraints on defect RG flows and universality classes.

Abstract: We compute conformal data of cusped line defects with impurities for the abelian Higgs model in the semiclassical large-charge limit. By working perturbatively in the impurity charge $q$, we obtain the scaling dimension of the impurity to $\mathcal{O}(q^3)$ to all orders in the 't Hooft quartic and gauge couplings. Each term represents a resummation of infinitely many Feynman diagrams. Near the critical gauge coupling where conformality is lost, we resum the leading singular behaviour to all orders in $q$. We also comment on the spectrum of excited cusp states at $\mathcal{O}(q)$. Additionally, we compute the leading contributions to the Bremsstrahlung function, the coefficient of the leading irrelevant operator in the fusion limit, and argue that the Casimir energy receives no corrections semiclassically. Our results agree with known perturbative data and numerical solutions. We compare them with recent bounds on cusps. We comment on the interplay between the large rapidity limit and semiclassics.

</details>


### [9] [Scale-separated quasi-dS and the Swampland](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10116)
*Rafael Álvarez-García,Ivano Basile,Shi Chen,Cumrun Vafa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests the Swampland dS conjecture and TCC in M-theory compactifications, showing that quasi-de Sitter vacua with weak coupling, positive potential, and controlled EFT are excluded for hyperbolic and rigid flat internal manifolds—even with Casimir corrections—except possibly where EFT breaks down.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether string theory/M-theory can accommodate metastable de Sitter-like vacua consistent with observational cosmology, while respecting Swampland constraints (dS conjecture and Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture).

Method: Analyzes scalar potentials in M-theory compactifications on hyperbolic and rigid Riemann-flat manifolds; incorporates one-loop quantum corrections via scalar-field Casimir energy; evaluates gradient bound ||∇V||/V against the TCC threshold 2/√(d−2) under effective field theory (EFT) control.

Result: De Sitter critical points satisfying V > 0, ||∇V||/V < 2/√(d−2), and V ≪ m² are excluded under EFT control; the slope cannot fall below the TCC bound except possibly in an O(1) region of moduli space where EFT validity fails; numerical bounds on Casimir contributions are derived.

Conclusion: The results support the Swampland dS conjecture and TCC: no parametrically controlled, scale-separated quasi-de Sitter solutions exist in these M-theory settings, reinforcing tension between standard inflationary cosmology and quantum gravity consistency conditions.

Abstract: Ideas from the Swampland program (dS conjecture and TCC) suggest that it is impossible to obtain solutions of string theory/M-theory in the weak-coupling regions describing our current universe, with $V>0$, $\|\nabla V\|/V < 2/\sqrt{d-2}$ and $V \ll m^2$ (scale-separated "quasi-dS") where $m$ is the mass of the light tower. We test this expectation in M-theory compactifications and find that even after including the quantum effect of Casimir energy, for hyperbolic and (rigid) Riemann-flat manifolds, de Sitter critical points under EFT control are excluded: the slope of the potential cannot drop below the asymptotic value predicted by the TCC $\|\nabla V\|/V\gtrsim 2/\sqrt{d-2}$ except perhaps in an order-one region in the bulk of moduli space, where EFT control is lost. At the parametric level, our results hold at one-loop level in supergravity; using the scalar-field Casimir energy as a proxy, we also obtain explicit numerical bounds to provide a ballpark estimate of how much control one can expect to achieve in such settings.

</details>


### [10] [Irregularization of punctures: From $T_N$ to Argyres--Douglas via $\mathcal{N}=1$ flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10118)
*Ki-Hong Lee,Shlomo S. Razamat,Jaewon Song,Thomas Waddleton*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a geometric connection between two classes of 4d N=2 SCFTs—Argyres-Douglas D_p(g) theories (from irregular 6d (2,0) compactifications) and regular-puncture compactifications—by showing that D_p(g) theories arise from N=1 compactifications on a two-punctured sphere with flux, followed by gauging a maximal puncture symmetry; it yields new Lagrangian constructions for D_p(su_3) and links their Lagrangian existence to that of T_g theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically explain disparate constructions of 4d strongly coupled SCFTs—specifically, bridging irregular-puncture (Argyres–Douglas) and regular-puncture compactifications of 6d (2,0) theories—and to address the longstanding problem of finding Lagrangian descriptions for non-Lagrangian Argyres–Douglas theories.

Method: The authors use 6d (2,0) theory compactifications on Riemann surfaces with punctures and background fluxes, analyze symmetry enhancement and R-symmetry mixing (including geometric u(1) isometry), perform symmetry gauging (often of emergent symmetries), and employ Higgs branch RG flows to relate theories; they explicitly construct D_p(su_3) via gauging and verify consistency with known central charges and operator spectra.

Result: A unified geometric derivation of D_p(g) Argyres–Douglas theories from N=1 two-punctured sphere compactifications with linear flux; explicit Lagrangian constructions for D_p(su_3); identification of the origin of the N=2 R-symmetry from both 2d R-symmetry and geometric isometry; reduction of the Lagrangian existence problem for D_p(g) to that of T_g theories.

Conclusion: The D_p(g) theories are not fundamentally distinct from regular-puncture constructions but emerge via controlled symmetry gauging and flux tuning in N=1 setups; this provides both conceptual unification and practical tools—especially new Lagrangians—for previously non-Lagrangian SCFTs, and suggests broader applicability to other non-Lagrangian theories connected via Higgs flows.

Abstract: In this paper, we directly relate constructions of two large classes of $4d$ strongly coupled SCFTs: 1) compactifications of type $\mathfrak{g}$ $6d$ $(2,0)$ theories on spheres with irregular punctures leading to the $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ Argyres--Douglas theories, and 2) the compactifications of the same $6d$ $(2,0)$ theories on spheres with regular punctures. In particular, we show that the $\mathcal{N}=2$ $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ Argyres--Douglas theories can be obtained starting from $\mathcal{N}=1$ compactifications of 6d $(2,0)$ theories of type $\mathfrak{g}$ on a sphere with two maximal punctures with flux linearly dependent on $p$ by gauging one of the maximal puncture symmetries, possibly with additional matter sectors. Importantly, part of the $\mathcal{N}=2$ R-symmetry of $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ theories arises from the R-symmetry of the two-punctured sphere and the $\mathfrak{u}_1$ geometric isometry symmetry. This construction gives in particular a geometric explanation of some of the $\mathcal{N}=1$ constructions of Argyres--Douglas theories, and also provides explicit new Lagrangian constructions (involving gauging of emergent symmetries in general) for $D_p({\mathfrak{su}_3})$ theories. More generally, we relate the problem of finding a Lagrangian for $D_p(\mathfrak{g})$ Argyres--Douglas theories (and thereby a large class of theories that are connected by Higgs branch RG flow) to the problem of finding Lagrangians for the $T_{\mathfrak{g}}$ theories.

</details>


### [11] [Integrable AdS$_2$ branes in mixed flux AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10120)
*Davide Bonomi,Volker Schomerus*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes classical integrability of open Green–Schwarz superstrings ending on half-BPS D3-branes wrapping AdS₂×S² in AdS₃×S³×T⁴ with mixed NSNS and RR flux, by constructing field-dependent reflection matrices, a double-row monodromy matrix, and verifying cancellation of fermionic boundary terms via a closed two-form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend known integrable D-brane boundary conditions—previously established for pure NSNS (WZW) or bosonic mixed-flux backgrounds—to the full type IIB superstring with mixed flux, thereby enabling integrability-based studies of AdS₃/CFT₂ interfaces.

Method: Uses the D3-brane DBI+WZ action to derive worldvolume electric/magnetic fields stabilized by SL(2,ℝ) rotation; imposes fermionic boundary condition cancellation by adding a closed two-form to the boundary action; constructs field-dependent reflection matrices acting on the Cagnazzo–Zarembo Lax connection; builds a double-row monodromy matrix encoding infinitely many conserved charges.

Result: Demonstrates classical integrability of the open superstring for arbitrary mixtures of NSNS and RR flux; shows that induced bosonic boundary conditions match the derived worldvolume fields; identifies the dual CFT interpretation as conformal interfaces in a deformation of the symmetric product orbifold of T⁴.

Conclusion: Half-BPS AdS₂×S² D3-branes in AdS₃×S³×T⁴ with mixed flux define classically integrable boundary conditions for the full Green–Schwarz superstring, unifying and generalizing prior bosonic and supersymmetric integrability results, and providing a concrete holographic setup for integrable defects in AdS₃/CFT₂.

Abstract: We show that half-BPS D3-branes wrapping AdS$_2\times$S$^2$ in AdS$_3\times$S$^3\times$T$^4$ with mixed NSNS and RR flux define integrable boundary conditions for the supercoset sigma model of the Green--Schwarz superstring. This extends the integrable D-branes of Dekel and Oz to backgrounds with a Wess--Zumino term, and previous bosonic results for mixed flux to the full superstring. Using the D3-brane action, we find that the electric and magnetic worldvolume fields that stabilise the branes at generic positions are obtained from those of the AdS$_2\times$S$^2$ branes of the WZW model by the same $SL(2,\mathbb{R})$ rotation that maps the pure NSNS background to the mixed flux one, acting on the D3-brane as electric--magnetic duality. On the worldsheet, cancelling the fermionic boundary terms requires introducing a specific closed two-form in the boundary action, and its contribution to the bosonic boundary conditions reproduces precisely these worldvolume fields. We then construct field-dependent reflection matrices that lift the boundary conditions to gluing conditions for the Cagnazzo--Zarembo Lax connection and describe displacements along geodesics normal to the brane. The resulting double-row monodromy matrix generates infinitely many conserved charges, which establishes the classical integrability of the open superstring for any mixture of fluxes. In AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$, these branes are expected to be dual to conformal interfaces in a deformation of the symmetric product orbifold of T$^4$.

</details>


### [12] [Emergent de Sitter Universes in Non-Hermitian Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10121)
*Daniel Areán,Suman Das,David Garcia-Fariña,Juan F. Pedraza*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs regular, asymptotically AdS black holes whose interiors dynamically evolve into nonsingular, expanding de Sitter cosmologies via a smooth bounce enabled by non-Hermitian holography and NEC violation; it unifies AdS/CFT, dS/CFT, and static-patch holography within a single geometric framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge AdS/CFT with competing dS holographic frameworks (dS/CFT and static-patch) by realizing a concrete, regular spacetime geometry that embeds all three perspectives without ad hoc constructions like branes or gluing.

Method: Constructs asymptotically AdS black hole solutions with regular interiors using non-Hermitian holography—specifically, engineering NEC-violating matter sectors that enable a smooth interior bounce and asymptotic approach to a de Sitter attractor; the geometry features a single event horizon and terminates at a spacelike future boundary I⁺, fully embedded in Einstein gravity with controlled quantum/semi-classical sources.

Result: A class of regular, asymptotically AdS black holes whose interiors evolve into expanding, nonsingular dS cosmologies ending at a spacelike I⁺; observables computed at AdS boundaries encode data consistent with both Euclidean dS/CFT and observer-centered static-patch holography.

Conclusion: Provides the first explicit, self-contained gravitational solution realizing a unified holographic setting where AdS/CFT naturally encodes dS quantum gravity descriptions—supporting the idea that dS holography may emerge from AdS via interior dynamics governed by non-Hermitian CFTs.

Abstract: We construct a class of asymptotically anti-de Sitter (AdS), regular black holes whose interiors evolve into nonsingular, expanding de Sitter (dS) cosmologies. Our construction relies on non-Hermitian holography, where violations of the null energy condition allow the interior geometry to undergo a smooth bounce and approach a late-time dS attractor. The cosmology emerges dynamically behind a single event horizon, extending to a spacelike future boundary, $\mathcal I^+$, without introducing auxiliary branes or gluing distinct geometries. This provides a novel setting to explore the connections between three complementary holographic perspectives: a non-Hermitian conformal field theory (CFT) at the AdS boundaries, a putative Euclidean CFT at $\mathcal I^+$ (dS/CFT), and observer-centered static-patch holography. Remarkably, observables anchored at the AdS boundaries reveal concrete connections with these complementary notions of dS holography. Our construction thus offers a new holographic laboratory for systematically exploring how future-boundary and static-patch descriptions of dS quantum gravity may emerge within the established setting of AdS/CFT.

</details>


### [13] [Holographic warped CFTs on AdS$_3$, parity violation and unitarity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10126)
*Kristiansen Lara,Ricardo Troncoso*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes boundary conditions for General Relativity in AdS₃ with parity-odd extensions (e.g., gravitational Chern–Simons), showing that canonical generators form a warped conformal algebra — specifically, a Virasoro ⊕ u(1) Kac–Moody algebra with shifted central charges. Unitarity requires nonnegative Kac–Moody level k_KM ≥ 0, while black hole entropy nonnegativity forces k_KM ≤ 0; thus, consistency selects k_KM = 0. At this value, the theory degenerates unless torsion is set to zero, yielding Topologically Massive Gravity at the chiral point — hinting at a need to extend boundary conditions to include logarithmic modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile unitarity and thermodynamic consistency (nonnegative black hole entropy) in holographic warped CFTs dual to AdS₃ gravity with parity-odd terms, and to clarify the role of boundary conditions in selecting physically viable theories.

Method: Canonical analysis of the Einstein–Hilbert action augmented by parity-odd terms (e.g., gravitational Chern–Simons) in AdS₃; computation of asymptotic symmetry generators and their Dirac brackets; evaluation of central extensions via Barnich–Compère prescription; thermodynamic analysis of black hole entropy using Wald formalism; examination of field equation degeneracy and torsion constraints.

Result: The asymptotic symmetry algebra remains Virasoro ⊕ u(1) Kac–Moody with shifted central charges; unitarity and entropy bounds jointly fix the Kac–Moody level to k_KM = 0; imposing vanishing torsion at k_KM = 0 recovers Topologically Massive Gravity at the chiral point; this suggests boundary conditions must be generalized to allow logarithmic solutions.

Conclusion: The only consistent, unitary, and thermodynamically sound realization of these warped boundary conditions occurs at the chiral point of TMG, where the theory supports a well-defined holographic dual — but requires extending the boundary conditions beyond standard asymptotics to incorporate logarithmic branches.

Abstract: We consider recently introduced boundary conditions for General Relativity on AdS$_3$, whose canonical generators span the warped conformal algebra, in the case of the Einstein-Hilbert action supplemented by parity-odd terms that do not modify the field equations in vacuum. The canonical generators are shown to acquire nonlinear contributions, and their algebra remains being the semidirect sum of Virasoro and $u(1)$ Kac-Moody, but with shifted central extensions. Thus, for suitable values of the parity-odd coupling, the Kac-Moody level can be nonnegative ($k_\text{KM}\geq 0$), admitting unitary representations. Nevertheless, requiring the black hole entropy to be nonnegative, implies $k_\text{KM}\leq 0$, in agreement with the established understanding of holographic warped CFTs. Compatibility of both requirements (entropy and unitarity) then singles out the case $k_\text{KM}=0$. However, the field equations degenerate in this case, which can be amended by imposing vanishing torsion as a constraint. The theory then precisely becomes Topologically Massive Gravity (TMG) at the chiral point, suggesting the extension of our boundary conditions to accommodate nontrivial logarithmic branches.

</details>


### [14] [The Ghost and Its Secret Partner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10128)
*Luca Buoninfante*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper identifies a hidden asymptotic composite field ('ghost's secret partner') coupled to the ghost field in four-derivative theories (e.g., quadratic gravity), causing persistent interference between negative-norm ghost states and positive-norm composite states—challenging isolated ghost asymptotics and suggesting dynamical origins for amplitude prescriptions or superselection rules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual issues arising from indefinite-norm vector spaces in higher-derivative quantum field theories, particularly the physical interpretation of ghost fields and their asymptotic behavior.

Method: Analysis of the analytic structure of the ghost propagator—specifically, first-sheet complex conjugate poles above multi-particle threshold—in four-derivative theories, with focus on quadratic gravity; examination of asymptotic state composition and interference in indefinite-metric Hilbert spaces.

Result: Ghost propagators exhibit complex conjugate poles signaling a hidden asymptotic composite field; this induces persistent interference between the negative-norm one-particle ghost state and a positive-norm composite state, preventing the ghost from appearing as an isolated asymptotic excitation.

Conclusion: The ghost’s asymptotic dynamics are intrinsically entangled with a composite partner, offering a dynamical mechanism—rather than ad hoc rules—for suppressing unphysical negative probabilities in indefinite-norm theories.

Abstract: The propagator of a ghost field acquires a pair of first-sheet complex conjugate poles above the multi-particle threshold. We show that this pole structure reveals the existence of a hidden asymptotic composite field -- the ghost's secret partner -- that remains coupled to the ghost asymptotically. This coupling induces persistent interference between the negative-norm one-particle state and the positive-norm composite-particle state. We analyze this phenomenon in four-derivative theories, in particular quadratic gravity, where the quantum dynamics prevents the spin-two ghost from appearing as an isolated asymptotic excitation. Although our results do not yet rule out observable negative probabilities, they may provide a dynamical basis for alternative amplitude prescriptions or superselection rules usually chosen or imposed ad hoc in theories with indefinite-norm vector spaces.

</details>


### [15] [An RG perspective on the (un)naturalness of Starobinsky inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10326)
*Francesca Campani,Francesco Ferrarin,Serena Giardino,Alessia Platania*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the large R^2 coupling in Starobinsky inflation conflicts with effective field theory expectations, using asymptotically safe gravity and renormalization group analysis; it finds that higher-curvature couplings are comparable at Planck scale but hierarchically suppressed at inflationary scales, validating Starobinsky as a natural low-energy limit while predicting small, testable deviations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent tension between the large R^2 coupling required in Starobinsky inflation and the EFT expectation that Wilson coefficients should be of order unity, by testing this via first-principles RG flow in asymptotically safe gravity.

Method: Performs a first-principles renormalization group calculation within asymptotically safe quantum gravity, including higher-curvature operators up to fourth order (R, R^2, R□R, R_{μν}R^{μν}, etc.), and analyzes UV-to-IR flow of dimensionless couplings, linking irrelevant to relevant ones via linear relations along RG trajectories.

Result: At the Planck scale, dimensionless higher-curvature couplings are generated at comparable size (O(1)), satisfying UV consistency; at sub-Planckian (inflationary) energies, canonical scaling suppresses irrelevant operators by powers of (H/M_Pl), recovering Starobinsky dynamics as a natural low-energy approximation; small calculable corrections to scalar/tensor spectra arise from residual higher-order terms.

Conclusion: Starobinsky inflation is not fine-tuned but emerges naturally from asymptotically safe gravity; its large R^2 coupling reflects IR suppression rather than UV pathology, and higher-order terms—though negligible for current CMB constraints—yield potentially observable deviations in next-generation surveys (DESI, ACT).

Abstract: Starobinsky inflation is often regarded as a successful minimal model of the early universe, yet it relies on a large $R^2$-coupling, in apparent tension with the expectation that Wilson coefficients in a gravitational effective field theory are of the same order. We test this expectation with a first-principles renormalization group calculation. We use asymptotically safe gravity as a testing ground to define ultraviolet-complete renormalization group trajectories and to uniquely predict higher-order couplings in terms of the free parameters of the theory---the cosmological constant and the $R^2$-coupling in Planck units. Including higher-curvature operators up to the fourth order, we find that indeed the flow generates dimensionless couplings of comparable size in the ultraviolet, with the irrelevant couplings tied to the relevant ones by linear relations. However, at sub-Planckian energies a canonical-like scaling takes over, and the irrelevant couplings get suppressed by powers of the ratio between the inflationary and Planck scales. This hierarchy yields the suppression of higher-order operators needed for consistency with cosmological data, so that the $R^2$-dominated dynamics emerges as a natural low-energy approximation. At the same time, the higher-order terms required by fundamental physics induce small but calculable deviations from the Starobinsky predictions, which may be worth considering in light of the latest and future DESI and ACT results.

</details>


### [16] [Non-Relativistic Supersymmetric Gauge Theories and Soliton Solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10479)
*Yoonbai Kim,O-Kab Kwon,D. D. Tolla,Hanwool Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs non-relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories in 2+1D via null reduction of 4D N=1 theories, identifies the Bargmann-extended super-Galilean algebra as the symmetry, and finds BPS vortices only in the massless multi-field supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model—whose vacuum moduli space is a weighted projective space enabling integer/fractional vortices—and connects it to Ginzburg-Landau superconductivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct consistent non-relativistic limits of supersymmetric gauge theories that preserve both gauge symmetry and supersymmetry, and to explore whether physically relevant solitons (e.g., vortices) survive in such Galilean-invariant settings—particularly with implications for condensed matter analogs like superconductivity.

Method: Null reduction from 4D N=1 relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories to 2+1D non-relativistic ones; explicit construction of supersymmetric Abelian Higgs models (massive and massless); derivation and analysis of BPS equations under Galilean supersymmetry; identification of vacuum moduli space via scalar field constraints; radial ansatz for vortex equations; analytical and numerical solution of vortex profiles; comparison with Ginzburg-Landau theory.

Result: The massive supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model admits no nontrivial BPS vortices due to mass obstruction; the massless model with multiple charged scalars supports BPS vortices when charge/winding conditions are satisfied; its vacuum moduli space is a weighted projective space, yielding both integer and fractional vortices; radial vortex solutions are obtained analytically (asymptotics) and numerically (profiles); the model reproduces key features of Ginzburg-Landau superconductivity while preserving full non-relativistic supersymmetry and gauge invariance.

Conclusion: Non-relativistic supersymmetry and gauge symmetry can coexist in 2+1D via null reduction, and rich topological soliton physics—including vortex structures relevant to superconductivity—emerges naturally in the massless multi-scalar setting, with moduli space geometry directly controlling vortex quantization.

Abstract: This paper investigates non-relativistic supersymmetric gauge theories obtained through null reduction, with particular focus on supersymmetric Abelian Higgs models and their soliton solutions. Starting from four-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = 1$ relativistic supersymmetric theories, we apply null reduction to derive Galilean-invariant non-relativistic theories in $2 + 1$ dimensions while preserving gauge symmetry and supersymmetry. The resulting symmetry structure is shown to correspond to the Bargmann extension of the super-Galilean algebra. We first analyze the massive supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model and show that, although it possesses nontrivial vacuum configurations, the mass term obstructs the existence of nontrivial BPS vortex solutions. We then study the massless supersymmetric Abelian Higgs model with multiple charged scalar fields, for which the BPS equations admit vortex-type soliton solutions under suitable charge and winding number conditions. The vacuum moduli space is identified as a weighted projective space, leading naturally to both integer and fractional vortex configurations. By imposing radial symmetry, we derive the corresponding vortex equations and examine their solutions analytically and numerically. Finally, we relate the vacuum structure of the model to the Ginzburg-Landau theory of superconductivity, highlighting how familiar superconducting behavior can emerge from a symmetry-preserving non-relativistic supersymmetric framework.

</details>


### [17] [Configurational Information and Hadron Spectroscopy in Holographic QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10741)
*P. H. O. Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis applies Configurational Information Measures (CIMs) — grounded in entropy and complexity — within AdS/QCD frameworks to model hadron spectra, derive Regge-like mass trajectories for mesons and baryons, and predict masses of unassigned resonances for experimental cross-validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and characterize experimentally observed but unassigned hadronic resonances by leveraging information-theoretic tools (CIMs) that encode stability and phase behavior, combined with holographic QCD models.

Result: Successful interpolation of cataloged light mesons, strange vector kaons (with quark-mass effects), and light-flavor baryons (at finite temperature) into CIM-parameterized Regge trajectories; physically consistent mass predictions for higher excitations, with candidate matches proposed for several unassigned resonances.

Conclusion: CIMs provide a robust, information-theoretic lens to extract spectral structure from holographic hadron models; the CIM–AdS/QCD framework yields predictive, interpretable mass relations beyond standard Regge phenomenology, supporting resonance identification and thermal hadron physics.

Abstract: Based on the concepts of entropy and complexity, Configurational Information Measures (CIMs) are tools capable of extracting information regarding physical systems from their spatially localized energy density, determining properties such as stability limits and phase transitions. In this thesis, CIMs are employed to investigate hadron spectroscopy. By utilizing phenomenological AdS/QCD models in conjunction with CIMs, cataloged meson and baryon resonances are interpolated into structures analogous to Regge trajectories, from which analytical expressions are derived to estimate the masses of higher-order excited states. These estimates are compared with experimental data to search for possible correspondences that may elucidate the properties of particles that have been experimentally detected but remain unidentified. Using this CIM-based AdS/QCD hybrid method, this work analyzes light mesons within a dynamical model, strange vector kaons in a framework that accounts for constituent quark masses, and light-flavor baryons at finite temperature.

</details>


### [18] [Monodromy Defects and Bulk Renormalization Group Flows on the Fuzzy Sphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10815)

Main category: hep-th

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological defect properties (e.g., symmetry realization, entropy, stability) depend not only on monodromy but also on bulk couplings, RG flow, and spectator fields—particularly in non-perturbative, large-N and fuzzy-geometry settings where standard CFT tools fail.

Method: Analytic RG analysis along anisotropy flows; large-N saddle-point evaluation; conformal weight–defect entropy relation; dimensional analysis and Ward identity constraints for domain walls; two-loop perturbative expansion; explicit construction of half-twist defects on fuzzy S² and fuzzy S⁴ using noncommutative geometry and matrix model techniques.

Result: O(3) half-twist flows to O(2) half-twist when twisted components remain light; untwisted spectators increase defect entropy; charged relevant perturbation pins the O(2) line; pinning timing (before/after easy-plane flow) yields matching IR junction data; only opposite (easy-axis) flow preserves untwisted-component pinning; domain walls are relevant on pinned O(2) lines and marginally relevant on O(2)/O(3)/O(4) lines; wall dimension decomposes into tilt stiffness and angular shape, consistent across Ward identity, two-loop, and large-N; winding suppresses condensate and lowers wall dimension/entropy release in fully twisted large-N model; entropy comparison enables disordering criterion; regular half-twist is constructed on fuzzy S⁴.

Conclusion: Defect infrared physics is governed by interplay between monodromy, bulk anisotropy, spectator scaling dimensions, and large-N dynamics—not monodromy alone. Pinning, wall relevance, and entropy are quantitatively linked and flow-dependent. The fuzzy sphere provides a controllable noncommutative framework to probe defect universality beyond standard CFT, with generalization to higher-dimensional fuzzy spheres feasible.

Abstract: A monodromy around a defect specifies a symmetry transformation, but the low energy properties of the defect depend on its interaction with bulk degrees of freedom and also itself. We study this dependence on the fuzzy sphere. Along an anisotropy flow, an $O(3)$ half-twist approaches the $O(2)$ half-twist when its twisted components stay light and disappears when they become massive. At large $N$, untwisted spectators give a positive correction to the defect entropy, fixed by the conformal weight of the free defect. The surviving $O(2)$ monodromy admits a relevant charged perturbation that pins the line. Pinning before or after the easy-plane bulk flow gives matching infrared junction data. A pin of the untwisted component survives only the opposite, easy-axis flow. Walls between opposite pinnings test the stability of the candidate ordered line. They are relevant on the pinned $O(2)$ monodromy lines studied and favoured to be relevant, with a smaller margin, on the ordinary $O(2)$ line, while the ordinary $O(3)$ and $O(4)$ walls lie close to marginality. We separate the wall dimension into a tilt stiffness and an angular shape, comparing them with a Ward identity, a two-loop expansion and a large-$N$ saddle. In the fully twisted large-$N$ model, winding weakens the condensate and lowers the wall dimension and the entropy released by pinning. The resulting entropy comparison permits disordering into the unpinned line wherever the leading wall estimate is relevant. Finally, we construct the regular half-twist on the fuzzy four-sphere as a starting point for analogous questions in five dimensions.

</details>


### [19] [eAdS- embedded AdS, tensionless strings and contracted Carroll-Weyl symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10907)
*Ulf Lindström,Bo Sundborg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'eAdS null string' by embedding a null string in AdS_{d+1} via a Dirac conformal d+2 dimensional construction, augmented with a Stückelberg scalar field φ that enables local scaling invariance commuting with the AdS isometry algebra so(d,2); it arises as the tensionless limit of a tensile string in both embedding space and intrinsic AdS descriptions, and its symmetry and constraint algebras are explicitly analyzed.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Dirac conformal embedding into d+2-dimensional ambient space; introduction of a Stückelberg scalar field φ transforming as a shift under local scaling; gauging of the scaling symmetry; analysis of symmetry algebras (so(d,2) and local scaling) and first-class constraint algebras in both embedding space and intrinsic AdS coordinates.

Result: Construction of the eAdS null string action with manifest local scaling invariance commuting with so(d,2); demonstration that it is the tensionless limit of an embedded tensile string and its intrinsic AdS reduction; full derivation of constraint algebras and symmetry structure in both formulations.

Conclusion: The eAdS null string provides a novel, algebraically consistent gauged formulation of a null string in AdS, resolving the non-commutativity issue of previous models and offering a geometrically transparent tensionless limit linked to both embedding-space and intrinsic AdS dynamics.

Abstract: We adapt the Dirac conformal $d+2$ dimensional embedding construction to define a null string in $AdS_{d+1}$. Introducing an extra Stückelberg field $\varphi$, which is a scalar under $AdS$ and shifts under local scaling of embedding space, the model acquires a local scaling invariance. We refer to this string as the embedded anti-De Sitter, $eAdS$, null string. A very useful property of this new gauged formulation is that, unlike previous gauged actions in $AdS$ background, the local scaling symmetry commutes with the $so(d,2)$ symmetry of $AdS$. Interestingly, the $eAdS$ null string is the tensionless limit of a tensile string in embedding space and the same holds for its reduction to an intrinsic description of AdS. We display the symmetries and constraint algebras both in embedding space and directly in AdS.

</details>


### [20] [Spectral function, OPE, and QNMs detect different singular points of black branes with unequal R-charges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10998)
*Hyunwoo Oh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies black brane singularities in the STU model with three R-charges; it identifies three curvature singularities at $R = -q_i$, only one physical ($R = -q_3$), and shows via spectral function, OPE radius, and QNM structure that different observables probe distinct singular points—governed by their optical times $T_i$ in the complex plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how holographic observables encode information about multiple (including unphysical) spacetime singularities in charged black brane geometries, particularly in the STU model with unequal R-charges.

Method: Analytical WKB analysis of the retarded correlator of $T_{xy}$ at zero spatial momentum, complemented by numerical checks; examination of spectral function asymptotics, OPE convergence radius, and quasinormal mode (QNM) distribution in the complex frequency plane.

Result: Three optical times $T_i$ associated with singularities $R = -q_i$ form a right triangle in the complex $T$-plane; spectral function large-frequency tail is dominated by $T_1,T_2$ (smallest Im $T_i$); OPE radius set by $|T_1|$ (smallest modulus); QNMs align along $1/T_2$ (smallest arg $T_i$) and the negative imaginary axis (encoding $T_3$, the physical singularity).

Conclusion: Different holographic observables—spectral function, OPE convergence, and QNM spectrum—probe distinct curvature singularities in the bulk geometry, revealing a geometric origin for their analytic structure in the complex time/frequency plane.

Abstract: We consider black brane solutions in the STU model, with three R-charges labeled by the parameters $q_1,q_2,q_3$. For equal R-charges $q_1=q_2=q_3$, the solution becomes the Reissner-Nordström black brane. For unequal R-charges $q_1>q_2>q_3\geq0$, there are three curvature singular points at $R\equiv r^2=-q_i$. Only $R=-q_3$ is the physical singularity, and the other two lie beyond it. Their optical times $T_i$, the complex times of null rays from the boundary to these singular points and back, form a right triangle in the complex $T$-plane. We probe these singular points using three observables, all built from the retarded correlator of $T_{xy}$ at zero spatial momentum: spectral function, OPE coefficients, and QNMs. The exponentially small correction to the spectral function at large real frequency is dominated by $T_1$ and $T_2$, which have the smallest ${\rm Im}\,T_i$. The OPE convergence radius is determined by $T_1$, which has the smallest $|T_i|$. A line of QNMs lies along the direction of $1/T_2$, since $T_2$ has the smallest phase $\arg T_i$. Furthermore, there is a second line of QNMs along the negative imaginary axis, which carries information about $T_3$ corresponding to the physical singularity. We derive these results from a WKB analysis and check them numerically. The three observables are therefore sensitive to different singular points of the geometry.

</details>


### [21] [Kinematically Unrestricted Flat Space Scattering from AdS$_{d+1} \times$ S$^p$/CFT$_d$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11028)
*Bhanu Narra*

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge flat-space scattering amplitudes with AdS/CFT by systematically constructing physical scattering states—including compact-direction momentum—from CFT data in the flat-space limit, addressing limitations of prior constructions that ignored internal momentum.

Conclusion: Scattering in flat space—including internal compact dimensions—can be fully encoded in the large-R-charge sector of boundary CFTs; this strengthens the conceptual and computational foundations of flat-space holography and provides a concrete framework for extracting S-matrices beyond the standard massless limit.

Abstract: We construct scattering states for scalar fields with generic kinematics from CFT operators in the flat space limit of AdS/CFT. These states allow for the construction of an unrestricted S-matrix from the flat space limit of CFT correlation functions, which we check for various tree level interactions. We also describe a generalization of the Mellin space formalism which incorporates the compact momentum, and we discuss some possible implications for flat space holography.

</details>


### [22] [Stanley Deser 19th March 1931 - 21st April 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11323)

TL;DR: This is not a research paper but an obituary or biographical tribute to Stanley Deser, highlighting his foundational contributions to theoretical physics—especially the ADM formalism in general relativity, supergravity, and gravitational wave physics—and his pivotal role in establishing LIGO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To honor and document the scientific legacy and impact of Stanley Deser, a leading figure in gravitation and quantum field theory.

Method: Biographical narrative summarizing key career milestones, major theoretical contributions, institutional affiliations, and recognition through awards and memberships.

Result: A concise synthesis of Deser’s life achievements, emphasizing his role in shaping modern gravitational physics and experimental initiatives like LIGO.

Conclusion: Stanley Deser’s work fundamentally advanced classical and quantum gravity, with enduring influence on both theoretical frameworks (e.g., ADM, supergravity) and large-scale experimental endeavors (e.g., LIGO).

Abstract: Stanley Deser was Ancell Professor Emeritus at Brandeis University and Visiting Professor at the California Institute of Technology. He was a world-renowned theoretical physicist whose accomplishments include the Arnowitt-Deser-Misner (ADM) formulation of general relativity, supergravity with Zumino, the dynamics of flat space with Jackiw and 't Hooft and many other contributions to the quantum theory of gravity. In the 1980s he was instrumental in securing National Science Foundation approval of the LIGO gravitational wave experiment against much opposition. He was awarded the Einstein Medal in 2015 for important contributions to general relativity, in particular the development of the ADM formalism. Other awards include: The Dannie Heineman Prize 1994; Fellow of the National Academy of Sciences 1994; Honorary Member of the Torino Academy of Sciences 2001; Member of the American Academy of Arts and Sciences 1979, Fellow of the American Institute of Physics.

</details>


### [23] [Liberation in the Heavy Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11594)
*Sridip Pal,Gordon Rogelberg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the asymptotic growth of CFT four-point functions of identical scalars at large operator dimension Δ, introducing the notion of 'liberation'—exponential growth matching that of generalized free fields—and links it to sparsity conditions on the light spectrum and twist gap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand when heavy scalar four-point correlators in CFTs exhibit universal, geodesic-like (GFF-mimicking) exponential growth in the large-Δ limit—a signature of semiclassical bulk physics in AdS/CFT—and to characterize spectral conditions ensuring such behavior.

Method: Analytic bootstrap techniques, crossing symmetry analysis, and asymptotics of conformal blocks in the large-Δ regime; uses twist and dimension sparsity constraints, modular invariance (in 2D), and comparison with generalized free field correlators.

Result: Liberation occurs at the crossing-symmetric point iff the light spectrum is sparse; extends to z, z̄ on same side of 1/2; full liberation holds if primary twist gap > √2 Δ; fails near z+z̄=1 for twist cutoffs < Δ; 2D Virasoro primaries cannot be liberated unless c ≫ Δ; sharpens universality of large-c torus partition functions.

Conclusion: Spectral sparsity—especially in twist—is both necessary and sufficient for liberation, providing a precise diagnostic for semi-classical bulk behavior in CFTs, with sharp dimensional distinctions (e.g., 2D vs. higher-D) and quantitative bounds on gaps.

Abstract: We study CFT four-point functions of identical scalar primaries as their dimension $\Delta$ grows for fixed $0<z, \bar z<1$, and call them liberated if they grow at the same exponential rate as those of a generalized free field (GFF), as is the case for heavy free particles propagating along geodesics in AdS. We show that the correlator is liberated at the crossing symmetric point if and only if the light spectrum is sparse, and that liberation then holds wherever $z$ and $\bar z$ lie on the same side of $1/2$. If the low twist states are also sparse, liberation holds everywhere except in an explicit band around $z+\bar z=1$. In particular, if the primary twist gap lies above $\sqrt{2}\Delta$, then the correlator is liberated everywhere. For twist cutoffs below $\Delta$, composites of two spinning generalized free fields fail to be liberated in a region that fills the band near the double lightcone limit. In two dimensions, Virasoro primaries cannot be liberated unless the central charge grows faster than $\Delta$. The same framework sharpens the universality of large-$c$ torus partition functions of two-dimensional CFTs.

</details>


### [24] [Decoupling Across Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11687)
*Param Gogia,Caius Gordon-Bruno,Emily Nardoni,Peter Weck*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a method to identify decoupled sectors in 4d N=1 SCFTs arising from M5-branes on T² × C²/Z₂, using anomaly polynomial matching between field-theoretic reduction and anomaly inflow; it leverages equivariant localization in singular/resolved M-theory geometries and cross-validates results via dual Y^{p,q} and class S₂ quiver descriptions, mapping chiral ring generators to M2-brane states on 2-cycles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically isolate and characterize decoupled (non-interacting) sectors in 4d N=1 SCFTs obtained from compactifications of 6d (1,0) theories on tori with flux — a problem obscured by flip fields and geometry dependence.

Method: Anomaly polynomial comparison: (i) field-theoretic reduction of the 6d anomaly polynomial plus flip-field contributions vs. (ii) anomaly inflow computed via equivariant localization in singular and resolved M-theory backgrounds; supplemented by analysis of dual quiver descriptions (Y^{p,q} and class S₂ necklace quivers) and chiral ring isomorphisms mapped to M2-brane zero modes on resolved 2-cycles.

Result: A precise prescription for identifying decoupled sectors via the difference of inflow anomalies in singular vs. resolved geometries; explicit chiral ring isomorphism between dual quivers; parameter regimes where dual quivers flow to seemingly distinct interacting fixed points; and one-to-one correspondence between chiral ring generators and M2-branes wrapping holomorphic 2-cycles in the resolved geometry.

Conclusion: Decoupled sectors in such 4d SCFTs are unambiguously captured by inflow anomaly differences, validating geometric resolution as a physical tool; duality maps preserve chiral data, and the full spectrum admits a direct M-theory interpretation via wrapped M2-branes.

Abstract: We present a prescription for identifying decoupled sectors of 4d SCFTs obtained from compactifications of branes. Central to our analysis is the comparison of anomaly polynomials obtained via field-theoretic reduction versus anomaly inflow. Our flagship examples are the 4d $\mathcal{N}=1$ SCFTs obtained by compactifying the 6d $(1,0)$ theory from M5-branes probing a $\mathbb{C}^2/\mathbb{Z}_2$ orbifold singularity on a torus with flux. The anomaly polynomial of the interacting fixed point is known to decompose into the $T^2$ reduction of the 6d anomaly polynomial, plus a contribution from flip fields introduced to remove operators from the spectrum. Adopting the inflow perspective, the contribution from the flipped sector is obtained as the difference between inflow anomaly polynomials in the singular and resolved geometries, which we efficiently compute using equivariant localization. Helpfully, the 4d SCFTs admit two dual quiver gauge theory descriptions: $Y^{p,q}$ quiver gauge theories, and class $\mathcal{S}_2$ necklace quivers obtained from trinions. We study the interacting and decoupled sectors on both sides of the duality and construct an explicit map between their chiral rings, identifying a range of parameter space for which the quivers flow to apparently different interacting fixed points. Each chiral ring generator is furthermore matched to an M2-brane wrapping a 2-cycle in the resolved M-theory geometry.

</details>


### [25] [Landau-Ginzburg Theory of a Holographic Superconductor: A Horizon Perspective on the Zero-Temperature Ground State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11706)

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how a quartic scalar self-interaction (λ|ϕ|⁴) modifies the phase structure of a holographic superconductor with non-linear electrodynamics, revealing three distinct regimes in the superconducting dome as λ increases: (i) a Lifshitz-scaling region that shrinks and vanishes at λ*, (ii) a no-AdS region where flows terminate before reaching asymptotics, and (iii) a regime (λ > λ_c) of fully asymptotically AdS hairy black holes; crucially, the critical point — where horizon scalar magnitude is maximal — corresponds to the deepest minimum of a Landau–Ginzburg–like effective potential evaluated *at the black hole horizon*, not the boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how bulk scalar self-interactions affect the emergent quantum critical structure — particularly Lifshitz scaling and superconducting dome topology — in strongly coupled holographic superconductors beyond mean-field boundary descriptions.

Method: Holographic renormalization and numerical integration of Einstein–Maxwell–scalar equations with non-linear electrodynamics and quartic scalar potential; analysis of near-horizon and asymptotic scaling behavior, effective horizon potential construction, and classification of solution families across parameter space (λ, temperature, chemical potential).

Result: Three distinct dynamical regimes emerge with increasing λ: (1) shrinking Lifshitz region vanishing at λ*; (2) emergence of a 'no-AdS' region bounded by a flow termination edge; (3) dominance of asymptotically AdS hairy black holes for λ > λ_c. The critical point — maximal horizon scalar value — coincides with the global minimum of the horizon Landau–Ginzburg effective potential, establishing horizon-localized symmetry breaking.

Conclusion: The quartic coupling λ controls the topology of the superconducting phase diagram and the locus of symmetry breaking: while conventional Landau–Ginzburg theory identifies order from boundary data, here the critical physics is encoded in the horizon effective potential, demonstrating that the black hole horizon can serve as the physical site of spontaneous symmetry breaking in holography.

Abstract: We investigate the effect of a quartic scalar self-interaction, $\lambda|\phi|^4$, on a holographic superconductor in which non-linear electrodynamic couplings generate a superconducting dome containing an extended Lifshitz regime. The quartic interaction leaves the linearized scalar dynamics around the normal phase unchanged while modifying the non-linear solutions where the scalar field is large. As $\lambda$ increases, the solutions within the dome pass through three stages. The Lifshitz region shrinks until its scaling solution disappears at $\lambda^*$, leaving a no-AdS region, beyond whose edge the flows end before reaching any asymptotic region, and above $\lambda_c$ the dome consists entirely of asymptotically AdS hairy black holes. At the bottom of the dome we identify the point where the horizon value of the scalar field is largest, which we refer to as the critical point. There this value lies at the deepest symmetry-breaking minimum that the horizon effective potential, of Landau-Ginzburg form, can have. Unlike a conventional Landau-Ginzburg description, this interpretation rests on the scalar field at the horizon rather than on the boundary order parameter. Below $\lambda^*$, the constant scalar of the Lifshitz fixed point sits instead at the minimum of the effective potential of its asymptotic scaling region.

</details>


### [26] [The tertiary invariant and discrete Green-Schwarz couplings in heterotic string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11731)
*Hiroki Wada,Kazuya Yonekura*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the tertiary invariant—a pairing between 2D (0,1) supersymmetric QFTs—for sigma models on Sp(2) and SU(3) with H-flux, coupled to non-supersymmetric heterotic theories; it reduces the invariant to a Dirac index on base manifolds and obtains Z₃ and Z₂ values that match known Green–Schwarz couplings and confirm anomaly inflow from worldsheet data.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To directly evaluate the recently introduced tertiary invariant—motivated by topological modular forms—for concrete non-supersymmetric heterotic backgrounds, and to test its consistency with established anomaly inflow predictions (e.g., to NS5-branes).

Method: Interprets Sp(2) and SU(3) as S³-bundles over spheres, fills each S³-fiber with a 4-disk carrying a gauge instanton, and reduces the tertiary invariant to the index of a Dirac operator on the base manifold; computes this index for pairings with the so(16)⊕so(16) heterotic string and the near-horizon heterotic 6-brane internal theories.

Result: Nonzero tertiary invariant values: Z₃ for the Sp(2)–so(16)⊕so(16) pairing and Z₂ for the SU(3)–6-brane pairing; these determine the respective Green–Schwarz discrete topological couplings.

Conclusion: The worldsheet-based computation of the tertiary invariant reproduces the Z₃ discrete term previously derived via anomaly inflow to NS5-branes, thereby confirming the consistency of the invariant and validating its physical interpretation in heterotic string theory.

Abstract: The tertiary invariant is a recently introduced pairing between two-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=(0,1)$ supersymmetric quantum field theories, motivated by topological modular forms. We evaluate it directly from its definition for the sigma models on $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$ and $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ with unit $H$-flux, paired with the internal theories of the non-supersymmetric $\mathfrak{so}(16)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(16)$ heterotic string and of the near-horizon region of the non-supersymmetric heterotic 6-brane, respectively. Regarding the target spaces as $S^3$ bundles over spheres, we fill in every fiber with a four-disk carrying a gauge instanton and reduce the invariant to the index of a Dirac operator on the base. We find nonzero values of order three and two, which determine the Green-Schwarz couplings of the two theories on $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$ and $\mathrm{SU}(3)$, respectively. The former, a $\mathbb{Z}_{3}$-valued discrete topological term of the $\mathfrak{so}(16)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(16)$ theory, was previously studied through anomaly inflow to NS5-branes. Our computation, which uses only the worldsheet theories, agrees exactly with that result and thus confirms the anomaly inflow.
