### [27] [Proof of the Rényi QNEC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11760)
*Thomas Faulkner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a general proof of the Rényi quantum null energy condition (QNEC) using Araki-Masuda weighted $L_p$ norms for all $p > 2$, extending prior algebraic proofs limited to $p = 2$; it introduces and analyzes a weighted conditional $L_p$ norm linked to quantum expansion/anti-quantity, and studies associated weighted conditional entropies in half-sided modular inclusions and general von Neumann subalgebras with a cyclic/separating vector, recovering a conditional dynamical entropy at $p = 2$ relevant to black hole physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the quantum null energy condition (QNEC) beyond the $p=2$ case—where the algebraic proof is known—to all $p > 2$ using Araki-Masuda $L_p$ norms, thereby deepening the connection between quantum information-theoretic quantities (e.g., weighted conditional entropies) and gravitational/quantum field theoretic constraints like energy conditions in curved spacetime and black hole dynamics.

Method: Employs the Araki-Masuda theory of non-commutative $L_p$ spaces for von Neumann algebras; constructs and analyzes a weighted conditional $L_p$ norm as an extrapolation of quantum expansion/anti-quantity; derives properties of the associated weighted conditional entropy in two settings: (i) half-sided modular inclusions and (ii) general von Neumann subalgebras sharing a cyclic and separating vector; takes the $p \to 2$ limit to recover known dynamical entropy structures.

Result: A rigorous, general proof of the Rényi QNEC for all $p > 2$; characterization of key properties (e.g., monotonicity, convexity, behavior under modular flow) of the weighted conditional $L_p$ norm and its associated entropy; demonstration that the $p=2$ limit reproduces a conditional dynamical entropy previously defined in black hole thermodynamics and quantum gravity contexts.

Conclusion: The Rényi QNEC holds universally across the $p > 2$ regime within the Araki-Masuda framework, confirming robustness of the QNEC as a quantum-informational constraint tied to modular structure; the weighted conditional $L_p$ norm serves as a natural quantum-informational proxy for gravitational expansion, unifying concepts from quantum field theory, operator algebras, and black hole physics.

Abstract: We give a general proof of the Rényi QNEC formulated using the Araki-Masuda weighted $L_p$ norms for all $p > 2$. This generalizes the algebraic proof of the QNEC applicable at $p=2$. Our approach utilizes a weighted version of a conditional $L_p$ norm which extrapolates the quantum expansion/ant-quantity. We elucidate some properties of the corresponding weighted conditional entropy both for half-sided modular inclusions and for general von Neumann subalgebras with a common cyclic and separating vector. The $p=2$ limit computes a conditional dynamical entropy originally defined in the context of black hole dynamics.

</details>


### [28] [Discretising 2D Fermionic CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11807)
*Dazhuo Cheng,Hexuan Li,Gong Cheng,Ling-Yan Hung*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends exact fixed-point tensor constructions to fermionic minimal models, analytically building fermionic fixed-point tensors for rational CFTs using boundary-changing operators on triangles, incorporating fermionic parity and spin structures; it recovers NS/R sectors, yields accurate scaling dimensions for the Majorana fermion, and links discretised fermionic CFTs to spin-TQFT state-sums via modular tensor categories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an analytic, exact fixed-point tensor formalism for fermionic rational conformal field theories—extending prior bosonic constructions—and enable efficient, principled tensor network simulations of fermionic critical phenomena.

Method: Extends Cheng et al.'s fixed-point tensor construction to fermions by defining the fermionic fixed-point tensor as a correlation function of boundary-changing operators on a triangle, with explicit treatment of fermionic parity and spin structure; constructs boundary tensors and strips; incorporates modified weights for shrinkable boundaries; uses fermionisation of bosonic RCFTs and modular tensor category–guided discretisation; recovers spin structures combinatorially on triangulated spin surfaces.

Result: Analytic construction of fermionic fixed-point tensors recovering both Neveu-Schwarz and Ramond sectors; numerical convergence to exact bulk scaling dimensions (e.g., for c=1/2 Majorana fermion) with minimal descendant truncation; systematic mapping of discretised fermionic CFT data (OPE coefficients) to spin-TQFT state-sums; consistent recovery of fermionic signs from spin surface discretisation.

Conclusion: The framework provides analytically derived, physically faithful fixed-point tensors for fermionic RCFTs, bridging boundary CFT, modular tensor categories, and spin-TQFTs—enabling rigorous tensor network studies of fermionic criticality.

Abstract: We extend the exact fixed-point tensor construction of Cheng et al. to fermionic minimal models, providing an analytic construction of fixed-point tensors for fermionic rational conformal field theories. The fermionic fixed-point tensor is defined as a correlation function of boundary-changing operators on a triangle, with careful treatment of fermionic parity and spin structure. Boundary tensors are also constructed and combined with triangle tensors to form strips, enabling the extraction of scaling dimensions for boundary-changing operators. The construction incorporates modified weights for shrinkable boundary conditions, systematic depiction of spin structures, recovering both the Neveu-Schwarz and Ramond sectors. As a benchmark, our tensor with a minimal truncation of descendant levels yields convergence to the exact bulk scaling dimensions of many low energy states for the free Majorana fermion ($c=1/2$), demonstrating the efficiency of approximate fixed point tensors from boundary changing operators. We also make use of a more general description of fermionic CFTs based on fermionisation of rational bosonic CFTs, and make a generic prescription for discretisation making use of their connections to modular tensor categories. We show that the discretised spin structures on a spin surface naturally recover the signs needed to recover the R/NS sectors. We propose that the collection of OPE coefficients of the discretised 2d CFT can be naturally interpreted as a state-sum of spin-TQFTs, analogous to the bosonic case. This work provides analytically computed starting points for tensor network simulations of fermionic critical phenomena.

</details>


### [29] [Critical Slowing Down and the Crossover to Quantum Scrambling in Holographic CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11889)
*Mozib Bin Awal,Prabwal Phukon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies critical relaxation dynamics and quantum information scrambling in the CFT dual to a RN-AdS black hole using holography; it finds universal critical slowing down with exponent Δ ≈ 2/3, extends scaling analysis to include central charge, and identifies a crossover scale between thermodynamic relaxation and fast scrambling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how critical thermodynamic relaxation in boundary CFTs—holographically dual to near-extremal black holes—relates to quantum information scrambling, especially beyond single-parameter (e.g., temperature) scaling and incorporating finite-central-charge effects.

Method: Holographic mapping from RN-AdS black hole thermodynamics to boundary CFT; stochastic modeling of horizon radius via Langevin and Fokker–Planck equations; spectral analysis of Fokker–Planck operator; scaling analysis with combined variable involving temperature and central charge C; analytical and numerical comparison of relaxation time τ and scrambling time t_scram.

Result: Critical slowing down with autocorrelation time τ ∼ |ε|^(−2/3); suppression of smallest nonzero Fokker–Planck eigenvalue; universal scaling persists under joint (T, C) variations; crossover scale between relaxation and scrambling identified as ε̂ ∼ (C ln C)^(−3/2), confirmed analytically and numerically.

Conclusion: Critical dynamical scaling in holographic CFTs is robust under central-charge variation, and the interplay between thermodynamic relaxation and quantum scrambling is quantitatively governed by a universal crossover scale that depends nontrivially on C—linking finite-N (or finite-C) effects, critical dynamics, and quantum chaos in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We investigate critical relaxation dynamics and their connection to quantum information scrambling in the conformal field theory dual to a Reissner-Nordström anti-de Sitter (RN-AdS) black hole. Employing the holographic dictionary, we formulate the thermodynamics in terms of boundary CFT variables and study the stochastic evolution of the reduced horizon radius using Langevin and Fokker-Planck formalisms. We demonstrate pronounced critical slowing down in the dual CFT, characterized by an enhancement of the autocorrelation time and stochastic fluctuations, accompanied by a suppression of the lowest nonzero Fokker-Planck eigenvalue. The autocorrelation time exhibits a universal power-law divergence, $\tau\sim|\epsilon|^{-\Delta}$, with $\Delta\simeq 2/3$, consistent with the analytical prediction and previous bulk gravitational results. Going beyond the conventional single-parameter analysis, we incorporate variations of the CFT central charge and identify a combined scaling variable involving temperature and central charge, demonstrating that the leading relaxation dynamics retain the same critical exponent along different trajectories in the two-dimensional parameter space. Finally, we explore the interplay between thermodynamic relaxation and quantum information scrambling by comparing the relaxation timescale with the fast-scrambling timescale. The comparison reveals a crossover between thermodynamic relaxation and quantum scrambling for which we derive the corresponding crossover scale analytically and find that, in the large-central-charge limit, it behaves as $\hat{\epsilon}\sim(C\ln C)^{-3/2}$. We also verify this numerically using the full nonlinear free-energy landscape. Our results connect critical dynamical scaling, finite-central-charge effects, and quantum scrambling within the holographic CFT framework.

</details>


### [30] [Proof of the QNEC, without assumptions and under a weight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11920)
*Thomas Faulkner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper completes a rigorous proof of the Quantum Null Energy Condition (QNEC) within the Half-Sided Translations/Modular Inclusions framework, removing prior assumptions tied to finite-null-energy purifications; it establishes convexity of relative entropy along horizon cuts for black hole exterior states vs. Hartle-Hawking state, and extends QNEC to a weight-based Hilbert space relevant for evaporating black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To remove the restrictive assumption of finite-null-energy purifications required in prior QNEC proofs and extend applicability to physically realistic scenarios like evaporating black holes.

Method: Rigorous operator-algebraic methods within the Half-Sided Translations and Modular Inclusions (HST/HSMI) framework; derivation of convexity properties of relative entropy under modular flow; generalization via weights on von Neumann algebras.

Result: A fully general proof of QNEC without purification constraints; demonstration that relative entropy between exterior field states and Hartle-Hawking state is convex in horizon cut parameter; a generalized QNEC using a weight-based Hilbert space suitable for flat-space evaporating black holes.

Conclusion: The QNEC holds universally in the HST/HSMI setting, and its extension to weight-based states strengthens its relevance for dynamical black hole evaporation beyond stationary settings.

Abstract: We complete a proof of the quantum null energy condition (QNEC) in the mathematical framework of Half-Sided Translations and Modular Inclusions (HST/HSMI) removing an assumption that applied to previous proofs. Here we establish a theorem, which when translated to a physical setting implies that the relative entropy comparing the state of fields outside a stationary black hole with a Hartle-Hawking (HH) state is always a convex function of the horizon cut. Previous proofs only applied when a purification with finite null energy was available. We also obtain a QNEC generalization in which the HH Hilbert space is replaced with one based on a weight which is plausibly of relevance to evaporating black holes in flat space.

</details>


### [31] [Semiclassical Asymptotics of Entanglement in Torus-Link States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11961)
*Vikash Ganwariya,Simran Sain,Siddharth Dwivedi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the semiclassical (k→∞) entanglement entropy of torus link-complement states in SU(2)_k Chern–Simons theory, finding it governed by edge contributions only, expressed via Hurwitz zeta functions and dependent on k mod n; it further analyzes scaling with d and n, and confirms commutativity of k→∞ and d→∞ limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study multipartite entanglement in topological quantum field theories using link-complement states—specifically, T_{d,dn} torus links—as natural probes of quantum correlations in (2+1)D SU(2) Chern–Simons theory.

Method: Derives asymptotics of moments of the reduced density matrix in the semiclassical limit k→∞ using stationary-phase analysis; decomposes the integrable representation space into left-edge, right-edge, and bulk regions; isolates dominant edge contributions while showing bulk is exponentially suppressed.

Result: Leading-order entanglement entropy is fully determined by two edge regions and expressed in terms of Hurwitz zeta functions; exhibits periodic dependence on k modulo n; for large d, reduces to a binary entropy weighted by normalized edge amplitudes; k→∞ and d→∞ limits commute.

Conclusion: Edge-dominated structure of entanglement in torus link complements reveals universal features of multipartite topological entanglement; the periodicity in k mod n signals discrete topological interference effects; commutativity of limits supports robustness of the large-party semiclassical regime.

Abstract: We investigate the entanglement entropy of topological quantum states associated with $T_{d,\,dn}$ torus links in (2+1)-dimensional SU(2) Chern--Simons theory at level $k$. These links consist of $d$ components with pairwise linking number $n$, and the corresponding link-complement states provide a natural setting to study multipartite entanglement in topological quantum field theories. We analyze the semiclassical limit $k\to\infty$ of the entanglement entropy by deriving the asymptotic behavior of the moments of the reduced density matrix. To achieve this, we decompose the integrable-representation space into left-edge, right-edge, and bulk regions and employ the stationary-phase method to evaluate their respective contributions. We find that the leading asymptotic behavior is governed entirely by the two edge regions, while the bulk contribution is asymptotically suppressed. The resulting expression for the limiting entanglement entropy is given in terms of Hurwitz zeta functions and exhibits a dependence on the residue class of $k$ modulo $n$. We further investigate the dependence of the limiting entanglement entropy on the winding parameter $n$ and the number of link components $d$. In the large-$d$ limit, the entropy reduces to a binary entropy determined by two normalized weights associated with the left and right edges. Finally, we show that the two iterated limits, $k\to\infty$ and $d\to\infty$, commute, reproducing the result obtained previously by taking the large-party limit first.

</details>


### [32] [Two-Loop $n$-Point Identical Helicity Amplitude for Gravity Minimally Coupled to a Chiral Fermion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12096)
*Zvi Bern,Calvin,Chen,Enrico Herrmann,Anthony Morales,Radu Roiban,Michael S. Ruf*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the two-loop identical-helicity n-point amplitude in gravity coupled to a chiral fermion, showing it is rational and proportional to a tree-level gravity amplitude with an R^3 insertion; the result is obtained via KLT relations from a gauge-theory amplitude with F^3 insertion, constructed using a chiral bootstrap, and verified up to 13 points for crossing symmetry and soft limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent progress in the two-loop identical-helicity sector of gauge theory—specifically when the one-loop self-dual integrability anomaly (Bardeen–Cangemi or Costello–Paquette) vanishes—motivates extending the analysis to gravity coupled to a chiral fermion, where the analogous self-dual anomaly also vanishes.

Method: The authors compute the infrared-subtracted two-loop four-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity + chiral fermion; observe its rational structure and proportionality to the pure-gravity tree amplitude with an R^3 operator insertion; construct the n-point gauge-theory tree amplitude with a single F^3 insertion via chiral bootstrap; then apply Kawai-Lewellen-Tye (KLT) relations to map it to the corresponding gravity amplitude with R^3 insertion, which matches the two-loop amplitude; finally verify crossing symmetry and soft limits up to n = 13.

Result: A closed-form expression for the two-loop n-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity minimally coupled to a chiral fermion, expressed as a rational function proportional to the tree-level n-point gravity amplitude with a single R^3 insertion; explicit verification confirms crossing symmetry and correct soft behavior through 13 points.

Conclusion: The two-loop identical-helicity amplitudes in gravity + chiral fermion are fully determined by tree-level data with higher-dimension operators (R^3), revealing a deep connection between loop-level quantum effects and classical effective operators—enabled by anomaly cancellation, chiral bootstrap, and KLT factorization.

Abstract: Motivated by recent developments in the two-loop identical-helicity sector of gauge theory when the one-loop self-dual integrability anomaly of Bardeen and Cangemi, or the equivalent twistor-space version of Costello and Paquette, vanishes, we compute the two-loop four-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity coupled to a single chiral fermion, for which the corresponding self-dual anomaly also vanishes. We show that the infrared-subtracted amplitude is purely rational and proportional to the identical-helicity tree amplitude of pure gravity with a single insertion of the operator $R^3$. Using this connection, we obtain a closed-form expression for the two-loop $n$-point identical-helicity amplitude in gravity minimally coupled to a fermion. We do so by first obtaining the corresponding identical-helicity gauge-theory tree amplitude with a single $F^3$ insertion using a chiral bootstrap. We then convert these $n$-point gauge theory amplitudes using Kawai-Lewellen-Tye to obtain gravity amplitudes with an $R^3$ insertion, which are necessarily equal to the desired two-loop identical helicity amplitudes of gravity coupled to a chiral fermion. As a nontrivial check, we explicitly verify crossing symmetry and the expected soft limits of our expressions through 13 points.

</details>


### [33] [Rotating the Replica: Twisted Rényi Coefficients from CFT Operator Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12133)
*Jyotirmoy Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Twisting vacuum Rényi entropies by spatial rotation encodes CFT data for asymptotic spinning operator counting; angular-momentum-twisted Rényi entropy logarithmic coefficients in 4D are fully determined by rotating-sphere thermal partition function (including Weyl anomaly), verified in free CFTs and holographic N=4 SYM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extract universal conformal field theory data—particularly related to the spectrum of high-spin operators—from entanglement measures via symmetry twisting, bridging entanglement entropy, thermal physics on curved backgrounds, and anomaly structure.

Method: Analytic continuation of Rényi entropies with spatial rotation (angular momentum twist) applied to a spherical region in 4D CFT; systematic local derivative expansion of the rotating-sphere thermal partition function; matching logarithmic terms in the twisted Rényi entropy to thermal data, including full Weyl anomaly contribution.

Result: Exact all-order relation between logarithmic coefficients of angular-momentum-twisted vacuum Rényi entropies for a 4D ball and the rotating-sphere thermal free energy; confirmed in free scalar/fermion CFTs and via AdS₅×S⁵ holography for large-N N=4 SYM.

Conclusion: Twisted Rényi entropies provide a precise, anomaly-inclusive probe of spinning operator asymptotics in 4D CFTs, unifying entanglement, thermal geometry, and conformal anomalies.

Abstract: We show that twisting vacuum Rényi entropies by a spatial rotation reveals conformal field theory data governing the asymptotic counting of spinning operators. For a ball in four dimensions, the logarithmic coefficients of these angular-momentum-twisted Rényi entropies are determined by rotating-sphere thermal data to all orders in the local derivative expansion, including the Weyl-anomaly contribution. We test this relation in free theories and in strongly coupled, large-$N$ $\mathcal N=4$ Yang--Mills theory using holography.

</details>


### [34] [Einstein--Yang--Mills Plane Waves: Uniqueness and an Exact Curvature Double Copy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12204)
*Metin Gurses,Tahsin Cagri Sisman,Bayram Tekin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes Coleman-Güven plane waves of Yang-Mills theory coupled to gravity to arbitrary D dimensions, identifying uniqueness conditions for Coleman's linear transverse profile, constructing explicit solutions in AdS, and analyzing regularity and curvature constraints in higher-curvature gravities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dimensional dependence, uniqueness, and robustness of exact non-abelian plane wave solutions in Yang-Mills–gravity systems—particularly whether Coleman's special linear profile and its geometric properties (e.g., Riemann = F²) persist beyond four dimensions and standard Einstein gravity.

Method: Analytical construction and classification of plane-wave ansätze in D-dimensional spacetimes; energy density boundedness analysis; algebraic comparison of Riemann tensor and color-contracted Yang-Mills field strength squared; explicit integration in anti-de Sitter background; singularity analysis on spherical transverse spaces; extension to quadratic and Gauss–Bonnet gravity via field equations.

Result: Coleman's linear transverse profile is uniquely selected (up to gauge) by both bounded energy density and the identity R_{μνρσ} = Tr(F_{μν}F_{ρσ}); AdS solutions exist in closed form for all D ≥ 4 but violate R = F²; spherical transverse waves are always singular; hyperbolic transverse waves in higher-curvature gravity avoid singularities only under defocusing or strong higher-curvature dominance; all results extend to generic diffeomorphism- and gauge-invariant theories.

Conclusion: Coleman's profile is structurally privileged in D dimensions—not just as a solution but as the unique one satisfying physically and geometrically meaningful constraints; its persistence across gravity extensions underscores its fundamental role in exact non-abelian wave dynamics.

Abstract: Coleman found plane waves of Yang--Mills theory that are genuinely non-abelian in four dimensions and yet obey linear field equations, and Güven showed that they remain exact when gravity is included. We study these waves in $D$ dimensions. Coleman's profile, which is linear in the transverse coordinates, is singled out twice: up to gauge, it is the only profile with bounded energy density, and the only one for which the full Riemann tensor equals the color-contracted square of the Yang--Mills field strength that sources it. In anti-de Sitter space, we construct a closed-form family for every $D\geq4$; there, the full Riemann tensor cannot equal this color square. Waves whose transverse space is a sphere are always singular. In quadratic and Gauss--Bonnet gravity, a hyperbolic transverse space avoids this only if the waves defocus light or the higher-curvature terms become comparable to the Einstein term. All solutions extend, with changed coefficients, to generic gravity and gauge theories.

</details>


### [35] [Zeta function in Coulomb background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12294)
*M.Bordag,I.G. Pirozhenko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the renormalized one-loop vacuum energy of a massive scalar field inside a spherical cavity with Dirichlet boundary conditions and a Coulomb potential α/r, using zeta-function regularization and heat-kernel subtraction; it determines new Coulomb-dependent heat kernel coefficients, establishes finiteness of the renormalized energy in the infinite-volume limit as α e(α/m), and shows the result breaks down for massless fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define a finite, physically meaningful one-loop vacuum energy for a massive scalar field in a Coulomb background — a problem complicated by boundary effects, infrared sensitivity, and lack of known heat kernel coefficients for Coulomb-modified Bessel spectra.

Method: Zeta-function regularization: constructs spectral zeta function from zeros of confluent hypergeometric (Kummer) function governing eigenvalues; analytically continues to s = −1/2; subtracts divergent part via heat kernel expansion; determines unknown α- and α²-dependent heat kernel coefficients by matching analytic continuation; retains part of mode generating function unexpanded and uses integral representation of digamma function for continuation.

Result: Finite renormalized vacuum energy is obtained; new heat kernel coefficients proportional to α and α² are derived; in infinite-volume limit, E₀^ren,∞(α,m) = α e(α/m) with dimensionless e depending only on α/m; energy vanishes as m→∞ (consistent with normalization) and diverges as m→0, indicating breakdown for massless case.

Conclusion: A physically normalized, finite one-loop vacuum energy exists for massive scalar fields in a Coulomb background with spherical confinement, but requires nontrivial extension of heat kernel methods and fails in the massless limit—suggesting need for alternative vacuum definition there.

Abstract: We calculate the one-loop vacuum energy of a massive scalar field confined to the interior of a sphere with Dirichlet boundary conditions and subject to a Coulomb background potential $\alpha/r$. The spectrum is determined by the zeros of a confluent hypergeometric (Kummer) function, and the corresponding zeta function is constructed and analytically continued to $s=-1/2$. The renormalization is performed by subtracting the divergent part of the zeta function, defined through the heat kernel expansion, which removes the non-decreasing powers of the mass and leaves a finite, physically normalized vacuum energy. While the heat kernel coefficients for the pure Bessel (Coulomb-free) case are well known, the Coulomb contributions to the coefficients are not; we determine them by matching the analytic continuation of the zeta function to the general form of the divergent part, obtaining terms proportional to $\alpha$ and $\alpha^2$. A key technical point is that the uniform asymptotic expansion of the mode generating function cannot be used in full; a part must be retained unexpanded, and an integral representation of the digamma function is used to carry out the analytic continuation. In the infinite-volume limit the renormalized vacuum energy tends to a constant $E_0^{ren,\infty}(\alpha,m)=\alpha e(\alpha/m)$, with a dimensionless function $e$ that depends only on the ratio $\al/m$. This limit shows that a finite, physically meaningful renormalized vacuum energy can be defined for a massive scalar field in the background of a Coulomb source. For $m\to\infty$ it vanishes in accordance with the normalization condition, while for $m\to0$ it diverges, so that the present calculation does not apply to a massless field, pointing to the need of another vacuum state in that case.

</details>


### [36] [On a soliton with a non-normalisable zero mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12387)
*Guido Franchetti,Stefano Bolognesi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes the holomorphic baby Skyrme model as a tractable soliton system with a non-normalisable zero mode; analyzes the massless, non-normalisable phase modulus via a radial (1+1)D effective action with boundary conditions and WKB/numerical evaluation; suggests this as a prototype for systems like monopoles with similar zero-mode structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a simple, analytically tractable model exhibiting a non-normalisable zero mode — a feature present in more complex topological solitons (e.g., certain monopoles) but difficult to study directly.

Method: Analyzes the holomorphic baby Skyrme model; identifies the 1-soliton structure analogous to a fixed-size lump; distinguishes massive size modulus from massless phase modulus; treats the latter via radial (1+1)D effective action on the half-plane with soliton-core–imposed boundary conditions; employs WKB approximation and numerical evaluation; formulates an effective moduli space approximation incorporating a boundary term at a cutoff scale.

Result: Demonstrates that the phase zero mode is massless and non-normalisable due to the soliton tail; constructs and solves the radial effective action with appropriate boundary conditions; validates the WKB approximation numerically; establishes a consistent effective description involving a boundary term.

Conclusion: The holomorphic baby Skyrme model serves as a controlled prototype for studying non-normalisable zero modes; its effective (1+1)D description with boundary conditions provides a framework generalizable to higher-dimensional solitonic systems such as monopoles in 3+1 dimensions.

Abstract: We consider the holomorphic baby Skyrme model, which we propose as a simple tractable example of a soliton with a non-normalisable zero mode. The $1$-soliton has the same structure as a lump of fixed size. The size modulus of the sigma model becomes massive, while the phase modulus remains massless. The latter is non-normalisable in the $(0+1)$-dimensional sense because of the soliton tail. A suitable way to treat it is to consider the radial $(1+1)$-dimensional effective action on the half-plane. The soliton core sets the boundary condition for the effective model, which we evaluate with the WKB approximation and also numerically. We discuss the analogue of the effective moduli space approximation with a $1+1$ model and a boundary term at a certain cutoff scale. This may serve as a prototype for more complex systems featuring non-normalisable zero modes such as certain kinds of monopoles in $3+1$ dimensions.

</details>


### [37] [First-order origins of second-order supersymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12418)
*Stevan Cebrian,Mikhail S. Plyushchay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs one-dimensional second-order supersymmetry from conserved first-order operators on surfaces with cyclic coordinates, using Witten’s mechanics with two fermionic modes and a centrally extended N=4 algebra; it relates quantum corrections to Schwarzian derivatives via isothermal coordinates, classifies factorizations by the sign of the quadratic invariant C (real for C ≤ 0, complex for C > 0), and identifies geometric and curvature-dependent realizations—including on the hyperbolic plane and flat cylinder—with consistent inner products.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and geometrically interpret second-order supersymmetric quantum mechanics in one dimension, linking it to conserved charges, cyclic reductions, curvature effects, and consistent Hilbert-space structures (e.g., positive inner products) across different signatures of the central invariant C.

Method: Uses Witten's supersymmetric quantum mechanics with two fermionic degrees of freedom and a centrally extended N=4 superalgebra; applies cyclic reduction and isothermal coordinate transformations to relate the auxiliary Hill equation to Laplace/wave equations; employs gauge transformation of the charge kernel and Schwarzian derivative analysis; performs Pauli reduction and diagonalization of the one-fermion sector; examines Riemannian vs. indefinite metric constructions and curvature-dependent invariants on constant-curvature surfaces (e.g., hyperbolic plane, flat cylinder).

Result: Derives explicit second-order supercharges and partner Hamiltonians with correlated quantum corrections expressed as Schwarzian derivatives; classifies scalar factorizations—real for C ≤ 0 (Riemannian case) and complex for C > 0 (indefinite case); reveals curvature-dependent invariants and additional conserved charges for Pöschl–Teller/free-particle systems on the hyperbolic plane; constructs local first-order chains in one dimension with positive-definite inner products for arbitrary C; provides a flat-cylinder realization of C > 0 with real transverse deformation and Hermitian two-component intermediate.

Conclusion: Second-order supersymmetry in 1D admits unified geometric construction via cyclic and Pauli reductions, where the sign of the central quadratic invariant C governs the nature (real/complex) of factorizations and dictates admissible geometries (Riemannian, indefinite, curved); consistency with unitarity (positive inner product) is achievable for all C, including C > 0, through appropriate surface realizations and intermediate structures.

Abstract: One-dimensional second-order supersymmetry is constructed from conserved first-order operators on a surface with a cyclic coordinate. In Witten's mechanics with two fermionic modes, a centrally extended $N=4$ algebra permits a conserved product connecting the zero- and two-fermion sectors. Cyclic reduction gives the second-order supercharge and the correlated quantum corrections in the charge and partner Hamiltonians. Isothermal coordinates identify the auxiliary Hill equation with the separated Laplace or wave equation for the gauge-transformed charge kernel, and express the correction as a Schwarzian derivative. The central momentum fixes the quadratic invariant $C$: the Riemannian construction gives $C\leq0$, and its indefinite counterpart gives $C\geq0$, with a separately chosen positive adjoint after reduction. Diagonalizing the one-fermion intermediate recovers real scalar factorizations for $C\leq0$ and complex ones for $C>0$. Pauli reductions provide a second first-order mechanism; on the hyperbolic plane they reveal a curvature-dependent invariant and additional charges of a regular free-particle--Pöschl--Teller pair in a selected momentum sector. In one dimension, a formally Hermitian two-component intermediate with a positive inner product yields local first-order chains for arbitrary $C$. For a constant superpotential, a real transverse deformation on a flat cylinder also gives a first-order surface realization of $C>0$ with a positive inner product.

</details>


### [38] [Cubic Curvature Corrections to Taub-NUT-AdS Black Holes and Their Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12482)
*Yu-Qi Chen,Hai-Shan Liu,Hong Lu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how cubic curvature corrections affect the thermodynamics of four-dimensional Taub-NUT-AdS black holes, deriving first-order analytic solutions and consistently determining corrected thermodynamic quantities—including mass, NUT charge, Hawking temperature, Wald entropy, and Gibbs free energy—using two independent Reall-Santos methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the robustness of Taub-NUT-AdS black hole thermodynamics under higher-derivative (cubic curvature) corrections in four dimensions.

Method: Constructs the first-order analytic solution in higher-derivative couplings; computes corrected Hawking temperature and Wald entropy; determines NUT charge and Gibbs free energy via Maxwell relations and two improved Reall-Santos methods (field redefinition and background subtraction); matches thermodynamic variables to fix ambiguities.

Result: The NUT potential is uncorrected; the Gibbs free energy is fully fixed by matching both Reall-Santos methods, yielding consistent mass and NUT charge; all thermodynamic quantities remain well-defined and mutually compatible under cubic curvature corrections.

Conclusion: The thermodynamic framework of Taub-NUT-AdS black holes remains self-consistent and stable under leading-order cubic curvature corrections.

Abstract: We investigate cubic curvature corrections to four-dimensional Taub-NUT-AdS black holes and their thermodynamics. We construct the complete analytic solution to the first order in the higher-derivative couplings, and derive the corrected Hawking temperature and Wald entropy. The NUT potential remains unchanged, while reconstructing the NUT charge and Gibbs free energy through the Maxwell relation leaves just one undetermined function of the NUT parameter. We then independently evaluate the Gibbs free energy using two improved Reall-Santos methods, based on either the field redefinition or the background subtraction. After matching the thermodynamic variables, both methods yield identical results and fix the undetermined function, providing a consistent determination of the mass and NUT charge. Our work therefore establishes that the thermodynamics of Taub-NUT-AdS black hole withstands the higher-derivative corrections.

</details>


### [39] [On Thermodynamic Role of the Gauss-Bonnet Topological Term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12698)
*Marek Liška,David Kubiznak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the thermodynamic role of the Gauss-Bonnet (GB) topological term in 4D gravity, showing that its purported entropy contribution depends on horizon boundary action choice; it identifies a boundary prescription where Wald entropy matches Jacobson-Myers entropy even off-shell; confirms no GB-induced corrections to conserved charges from smooth outer boundaries; and reveals that the GB term modifies the Hawking-Page phase transition—inducing a zeroth-order transition for positive coupling—offering a potential observational signature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in the literature regarding whether the topologically trivial 4D Gauss-Bonnet term contributes to black hole entropy and thermodynamics, despite vanishing field equations, and to clarify its physical observability via phase structure.

Method: Analytical gravitational thermodynamics: Wald formalism for entropy and conserved charges; careful treatment of boundary terms—including horizon and outer boundaries—in the variational principle; comparison with Jacobson-Myers entropy for stationary and non-stationary horizons; thermodynamic analysis of AdS black holes including free energy and phase transitions.

Result: 1) The horizon entropy shift attributed to the 4D Gauss-Bonnet term is not intrinsic but boundary-action–dependent; 2) A specific horizon boundary term restores agreement between Wald and Jacobson-Myers entropies universally (including dynamical horizons); 3) No corrections arise in ADM-like conserved charges from outer boundaries, irrespective of asymptotics or boundary conditions; 4) The GB term alters the Hawking-Page critical temperature and induces a zeroth-order phase transition for α > 0; 5) Results extend qualitatively to other Lovelock terms in their critical dimensions.

Conclusion: The 4D Gauss-Bonnet term, while topological and kinematically inert, carries nontrivial thermodynamic consequences—contingent on boundary prescriptions—and can leave detectable imprints in black hole phase structure, challenging the notion that topological terms are physically inert in four dimensions.

Abstract: We revisit the thermodynamic implications of the Gauss-Bonnet topological term in four spacetime dimensions. It has been previously claimed in the literature that this term, although it does not modify the equations of motion, shifts the black hole entropy by a contribution proportional to the Euler characteristic of the horizon. We argue that this conclusion is sensitive to the choice of the boundary action on the horizon. We then identify a choice for which the Wald entropy expression naturally reproduces the Jacobson-Myers entropy, even for non-stationary black holes. We also show that there are no further contributions to conserved quantities from smooth outer boundaries, regardless of the choice of boundary conditions there and of the asymptotic behaviour of the spacetime. Moreover, we demonstrate that the topological Gauss-Bonnet term changes the critical temperature of the Hawking-Page phase transition, opening, in principle, a way for detecting its presence in the gravitational action. In fact, for a positive Gauss-Bonnet coupling constant, we observe a zeroth-order Hawking phase transition at high temperatures and pressures. While our discussion focuses on the case of the Gauss-Bonnet term in 4D, the results are more general and qualitatively apply to other Lovelock Lagrangians in their respective critical dimensions.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how bulk geometric quantities—specifically the vacuum volume of an entanglement wedge polygon—respond to matter perturbations in AdS/CFT, and to clarify the distinction between entropic and volumetric probes of bulk physics.

Method: Semiclassical gravity analysis in AdS₃ with time-symmetric coherent scalar field perturbations; comparison of quadratic responses of entanglement entropies (via six-interval combinations) and entanglement wedge volume; continuum reconstruction using narrowing quartets; independent boundary CFT computation for a dimension-3/2 primary operator.

Conclusion: Entanglement wedge volume is a qualitatively distinct bulk observable from entanglement entropy—it carries complementary, non-redundant information about bulk matter distributions, and its topological vacuum value acquires a nontrivial, signature-sensitive semiclassical correction.

Abstract: The vacuum volume of an entanglement wedge polygon is topological. We determine its semiclassical quadratic response to time-symmetric coherent scalar matter in AdS$_3$. A contrast of six interval entropies measures a nonnegatively weighted energy integral, whereas volume depends on the unweighted integral. For negative mass squared above the stability bound, perturbations with the same quadratic coefficient of global energy can match all six vacuum entropies through second order yet have opposite volume responses. A continuum of narrowing quartets reconstructs the volume response with controlled noise. An independent CFT calculation for a dimension-$3/2$ primary reproduces an exact polygon edge formula.

</details>


### [41] [M-theory anomaly localization and holographic $c$-extremization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12802)
*Carolina Matté Gregory*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes characteristic class contributions (cubic and C₃∧X₈) to the trial anomaly polynomial for supersymmetric AdS₃×M₈ backgrounds with M₈ a projective bundle over a 6-manifold; uses c-extremization to identify the projective-invariant denominator J and derive central charge corrections linear in unshifted flux; verifies results via fibre integration and localization, and matches known field-theory anomalies for wrapped M5-branes on S⁴ bundles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine the full central charges (including flux-dependent corrections) of 2D SCFTs dual to supersymmetric AdS₃×M₈ solutions in M-theory, particularly when the internal space is a projective bundle, and to reconcile geometric anomaly inflow with field-theoretic c-extremization and known holographic results.

Method: Computes contributions of cubic and C₃∧X₈ terms to the trial anomaly polynomial; applies c-extremization to eliminate Abelian currents from C₃, identifying the projective-invariant J as the denominator; evaluates X₈ push-forward to extract pure R-symmetry, mixed R–Betti, and gravitational anomalies; performs direct projective fibre integration and compares with localization; tests on three flux-quantized backgrounds, including non-polynomial ones; cross-checks against wrapped M5-brane anomaly on S⁴ bundle.

Result: Derives central charge formula c⁽⁰⁾ = −I₃ + 3I₊I₋/J; shows X₈ contribution yields central-charge corrections linear in unshifted flux; identifies mixed R–Betti anomaly as universally required and equally contributing to both central charges; confirms mixed coefficient matches both fibre integration and localization; reproduces known N³ and N terms of field-theoretic anomaly for M5-branes on S⁴; establishes consistency conditions for interpreting results as bona fide CFT central charges.

Conclusion: The geometric anomaly polynomial framework, combined with c-extremization and careful treatment of projective invariance and flux quantization, consistently yields central charges for AdS₃/CFT₂ dualities involving projective-bundle geometries; however, full interpretation as unitary CFT central charges requires additional physical inputs—namely, unitarity, normalizable vacuum, appropriate boundary conditions, complete current mixing, and control over omitted anomaly sectors.

Abstract: We compute the cubic and $C_3\wedge X_8$ caracteristic class contributions to the trial anomaly polynomial of supersymmetric $AdS_3\times M_8$ backgrounds with $M_8=P(\mathcal O \oplus \mathcal L)\to B_6$. Including the Abelian currents descending from $C_3$, we identify the projective invariant $J$ as the denominator arising from their elimination by $c$-extremization and recover the central charge $c^{(0)}=-I_3+3I_+I_-/J$. The $X_8$ push-forward determines the pure R-symmetry, mixed R--Betti and gravitational anomalies. Evaluating it at the classical extremum gives the associated central-charge corrections, linear in the overall unshifted flux. The mixed term contributes equally to both central charges and is required in general. We evaluate these formulae for three flux-quantized backgrounds, including non-polynomial solutions. Direct projective fibre integration reproduces the mixed coefficient obtained by localization. On the wrapped-$M5$ $S^4$ bundle, the same couplings reproduce the independently known field-theory anomaly through orders $N^3$ and $N$. Their interpretation as full CFT central charges requires a unitary SCFT with a normalizable vacuum, suitable supersymmetric boundary conditions, a complete mixing sector of global currents and control of omitted anomaly sectors. A conditional Euclidean relative-equivariant interpretation is discussed separately.

</details>


### [42] [Global Green-Schwarz response on quaternionic sphere bundles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12810)
*Yuqi Li,Hao-Yu Sun*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the normalized Green-Schwarz phase for 10D three-sphere bundles of quaternionic lines over closed spin 7-manifolds, showing it depends on global topological data—specifically integral String structures—via a combination of a local anomaly polynomial integral and a real eta invariant from a 12D APS filling; the result is an integral relative characteristic number (mod ℤ), equal to 8× Crowley-Goette invariant under cohomological vanishing conditions, and distinguishes all three possible third-root phases via distinct integral String structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how the normalized Green-Schwarz phase—though locally determined by anomaly polynomials—depends on global background data (e.g., integral String structures), beyond local curvature expressions, especially in heterotic/F-theory compactifications involving quaternionic geometry and spin 7-manifolds.

Method: Computes the Green-Schwarz phase as sum of: (i) a local term—the integral of the anomaly polynomial over the 10-manifold—and (ii) a global term—the real eta invariant of a Dirac operator on a 12-dimensional Atiyah-Patodi-Singer filling bounding the 10-manifold; uses differential trivializations of a characteristic class (½p₁(T) + c₂(E_ℂ)) and constraints from Spin(16) gauge bundles and integral cohomology.

Result: The additive phase response equals one-third of an integral relative characteristic number modulo integers; under H³(B;ℚ)=H⁴(B;ℚ)=0, it equals eight times the Crowley-Goette invariant; for Sp(2), calibration yields exactly 1/3, matching the known third-root phase; distinct integral String structures on a fixed 10-manifold realize all three ℤ₃ phase values.

Conclusion: The Green-Schwarz phase is not purely local—it detects global integral topological data (e.g., String structures), encoded via eta invariants and relative characteristic classes; this provides a sharp topological classification of anomaly cancellation beyond differential-form cohomology, with implications for topological modular forms and vertex operator superalgebras, but no microscopic theory construction.

Abstract: Local anomaly polynomials describe local variations, but a normalized Green-Schwarz phase also depends on global background data. We explain this dependence for ten-dimensional three-sphere bundles of quaternionic lines over closed spin seven-manifolds. The backgrounds retain two specified $\mathrm{Spin}(16)$ gauge bundles and an integral differential trivialization on the base, including its boundary identifications. The trivialized class is the sum of half the first Pontryagin class of the tangent bundle and the second Chern class of the quaternionic line's underlying complex bundle. For these backgrounds, the calculation combines a generally nonzero local Green-Schwarz integral with a real eta invariant evaluated by a twelve-dimensional Atiyah-Patodi-Singer filling. Together, these terms determine an integral relative characteristic number; the additive response is one third of that number, modulo integers. When the base's third and fourth rational cohomology groups vanish, the response equals eight times the Crowley-Goette invariant, without assuming that the base is simply connected. A calibration using the actual quaternionic line bundle gives additive value one third on $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$, recovering the known third-root phase. Distinct integral String data on one fixed ten-manifold give all three phase values, showing which global background information the response can detect. The physical identifications retain their prescribed fermion data, while the separate application to integral topological modular forms and vertex operator superalgebras requires compatible integral lifts. The response calculation constructs neither a microscopic realization nor an equivalence of complete theories.

</details>


### [43] [Kasner Exponents from the Bouncing Singularity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31699)
*Hyunwoo Oh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a formula for the additional exponent $b_t$ in the singularity of thermal two-point functions of holographic CFTs, linking it to Kasner exponents near black hole singularities; it validates the formula against numerical data and extends predictions to new cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermal correlators in holographic CFTs encode information about black hole singularities, particularly the nature of the singularity via subleading exponents in geodesic approximations.

Method: Analyzes bouncing null geodesics near timelike singularities in AdS black brane geometries; computes the contribution of waves reflected off the singularity to the large-frequency retarded correlator; extracts the exponent $b_t$ from the singularity structure of the two-point function using Kasner parametrization of near-singularity geometry.

Result: Derives exact analytical formula $b_t = (d-1) \max\left( \frac{p_i - p_t}{1 - p_t}, \frac{1}{2} \right)$ for planar AdS$_{d+1}$ black branes; shows strong agreement with Čeplak–Valach numerical results, outperforming prior empirical relations; provides new predictions for $b_t$ in Kasner-known but uncomputed cases.

Conclusion: The exponent $b_t$ is universally governed by Kasner exponents, confirming that thermal correlators probe the local geometry near spacelike/timelike singularities in a quantifiable, calculable way—supporting the idea that holography resolves singularity physics through boundary correlators.

Abstract: Thermal two-point functions of holographic CFTs can probe the black hole singularity. When a bouncing geodesic exists, the correlator at leading order in $1/N$ develops a singularity $G(t, \vec{x}=0) \propto (t_c-t)^{-2\Delta+b_t}$, where $\Delta$ is the scaling dimension of the operator. We show that the additional exponent $b_t$ is determined by the Kasner exponents near the singularity. From the wave reflected off the singularity, which gives an exponentially small term in the large-frequency retarded correlator, we derive the formula $b_t=(d-1) \max \left( \frac{p_i-p_t}{1-p_t}, \frac{1}{2} \right)$ for planar black branes in AdS$_{d+1}$, where $p_t$ and $p_i$ are the Kasner exponents of the time and spatial directions. We compare our formula with the numerical values of Čeplak and Valach. It reproduces the relation that they proposed for a special class of black branes. For more general black branes, where no formula had been found, our formula agrees with most of their numerical data and fits them much better than the extension of their relation. We also give predictions for $b_t$ in theories where the Kasner exponents are known, but $b_t$ has not been computed.

</details>

### [44] [Sensitivity to Sterile Neutrinos with Elastic Neutrino-Electron and Highly Elastic Neutrino-Nucleus Scattering in the DUNE Near Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10271)
*DUNE Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates DUNE Near Detector's sensitivity to eV-scale sterile neutrinos in a 3+1 oscillation framework using three exclusive event samples—highly elastic νμ CC, νe CC, and ν+e scattering—to mitigate flux and interaction uncertainties; it achieves ~0.01–0.015 90% CL sensitivity on |Uμ4|² and |Ue4|² for Δm² ≈ 4–10 eV².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe eV-scale sterile neutrinos via short-baseline oscillations at the DUNE Near Detector, leveraging its capability beyond primary long-baseline oscillation goals and addressing anomalies (e.g., LSND, MiniBooNE) that suggest light sterile neutrinos.

Method: Performs a sensitivity study using GEANT4-simulated DUNE ND events in three low-systematics channels: highly elastic νμ CC, νe CC, and ν+e elastic scattering; employs spectral shape analysis of reconstructed lepton/electron energy; treats |Ue4|², |Uμ4|², |Uτ4|², and Δm² as free parameters, fixing |Uτ4|² = 0; uses frequentist statistical inference for 90% CL limits.

Result: Strongest sensitivity in 4 ≲ Δm² ≲ 10 eV² range, where oscillatory distortions are resolvable in energy spectra; with one year of on-axis FHC beam, 90% CL upper limits reach |Uμ4|² ≃ 0.01 and |Ue4|² ≃ 0.015, depending on degeneracies between mixing parameters.

Conclusion: The DUNE Near Detector offers competitive sensitivity to eV-scale sterile neutrinos in the 3+1 model, especially in the Δm² ∼ O(1–10) eV² region, and its multi-channel strategy—combining high-correlation CC selections and theoretically clean ν+e scattering—effectively constrains systematic uncertainties critical for sterile-neutrino searches.

Abstract: The Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE) is a next-generation neutrino experiment designed for precision measurements of long-baseline three-flavor neutrino oscillations. The DUNE Near Detector (ND) is also sensitive to short-baseline oscillations arising from mixing with an eV-scale sterile neutrino. In this paper, we present a sensitivity study for sterile-neutrino oscillations in the $3+1$ framework using three complementary event samples in the simulated DUNE ND: highly elastic $\nu_\mu$ charged-current (CC), highly elastic $\nu_e$ CC, and neutrino-electron elastic-scattering ($\nu+e$) events. These highly exclusive samples are chosen to reduce neutrino-interaction and flux uncertainties: the low-hadronic-energy CC selections enhance the correlation between reconstructed lepton energy and neutrino energy, while the precisely calculable $\nu+e$ cross section provides an independent constraint on the neutrino flux. Residual interaction-model dependence remains in the CC samples. The statistical analysis considers $|U_{e4}|^2$, $|U_{\mu4}|^2$, $|U_{\tau4}|^2$, and $\Delta m^2$, with sensitivity results presented as fixed-$\Delta m^2$ slices for $|U_{\tau4}|^2=0$. The strongest sensitivity occurs for $4 \lesssim \Delta m^2 \lesssim 10~\mathrm{eV}^2$, where oscillations produce resolvable distortions of the reconstructed-energy spectra. For one year of on-axis forward-horn-current running, the 90\% confidence-level sensitivity reaches approximately $|U_{\mu4}|^2 \simeq 0.01$ and $|U_{e4}|^2 \simeq 0.015$, depending on the value of the other mixing parameter.

</details>


### [45] [NuSTEC Review: Current Experimental and Theoretical Challenges of Neutrino-Nucleus Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10465)
*A. P. Furmanski,J. G. Morfín,L. Alvarez-Ruso,A. M. Ankowski,A. Ashkenazi,M. Sajjad Athar,S. Dolan,S. Dytman,S. Gardiner,R. González-Jiménez,R. J. Hill,M. Hostert,J. Isaacson,N. Jachowicz,M. Kabirnezhad,T. Katori,S. R. Klein,F. Kling,M. Martini,G. D. Megias,B. A. Messerly,K. Niewczas,A. Nikolakopoulos,J. Nowak,J. M. Paley,V. Pandey,A. Papadopoulou,L. Pickering,N. Rocco,J. T. Sobczyk,J. Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This review examines challenges in modeling neutrino-nucleus interactions at GeV energies, emphasizing their impact on precision neutrino oscillation measurements and calling for interdisciplinary collaboration to reduce theoretical uncertainties in event generators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise neutrino property measurements—especially oscillation parameters—require accurate modeling of neutrino-nucleus interactions; however, current theoretical uncertainties in hadronic and nuclear physics limit experimental sensitivity.

Method: Review and critical analysis of open questions in neutrino-nucleus scattering processes (quasi-elastic, resonance, deep-inelastic) across GeV energies; assessment of how event generators handle these; cross-comparison with electron-nucleus data as a constraint; evaluation of impacts on oscillation analyses.

Result: Identifies key unresolved issues—including final-state interactions, nuclear effects, resonance modeling, and partonic structure—in neutrino-nucleus interactions; quantifies their influence on oscillation parameter extraction; highlights electron-scattering as a vital calibration tool; underscores necessity of joint nuclear/high-energy theory-experiment efforts.

Conclusion: Advancing neutrino physics demands coordinated progress in nuclear theory, electroweak modeling, and experimental validation—particularly via improved neutrino event generators informed by electron-nucleus data and ab initio nuclear frameworks.

Abstract: The precise measurement of neutrino properties is among the highest priorities in fundamental particle physics, involving many experimental and theoretical efforts worldwide. Since the experiments rely on the interactions of neutrinos in the nuclear environment, the planned advances in the scope and precision of these experiments require a commensurate effort in understanding and modeling the hadronic and nuclear physics of these interactions, as incorporated in neutrino event generators. This modeling is essential to every phase of the experimental analyses, and the theoretical uncertainties play an important role in interpreting experimental results. In this Review of current challenges, we summarize the open questions in neutrino-nucleus interactions at GeV-scale energies, and discuss in detail the potential impacts of these unknowns on the measurement of neutrino properties, using the determination of oscillation parameters as a central example. After an Executive Summary and a concise Overview of the issues, we address how the neutrino event generators treat these challenges, how electron-nucleus interactions can help understanding these challenges and the important unanswered questions for each underlying physics process---from quasi-elastic to deep-inelastic scattering. Across every topic, we find that current challenges can be met only through active support and collaboration among specialists in strong interactions and electroweak physics, including theorists and experimentalists from both the nuclear and high-energy physics communities.

</details>

In the $K\eta^{\prime}$ system, a resonant contribution with quantum numbers $J^{P}=1^{-}$ is observed for the first time and is denoted as the $K_1^*(1870)$ meson. Its statistical significance is $11.0\sigma$, and its mass and width are measured to be $M=(1869.0 \pm 14.6\pm 18.6$) MeV/$c^{2}$ and $\Gamma=(167.3 \pm 34.0\pm 24.2$) MeV, respectively. The branching fraction of the $\chi_{c1}\to K^{\mp}K_1^*(1870)^{\pm}\to K^{+}K^{-}\eta^{\prime}$ decay chain is determined to be $(1.48 \pm 0.30\pm 0.33)\times10^{-5}$. The branching fraction of the $\chi_{c1}\to K^{+}K^{-}\eta^{\prime}$ decay is measured to be $(7.90 \pm 0.48)\times10^{-4}$. Several intermediate scalar, vector, and tensor states are required to provide a good description of the data. These results provide new input for understanding strange-meson spectroscopy and investigating possible hybrid-state candidates.

### [47] [Machine learning assisted $ν/\barν$ discrimination in atmospheric neutrino events for CPV sensitivity in a 20-kton liquid scintillator detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11474)
*Dek Hao Liew,Mohammad Nizam,Shu Tian Eu,Siew Yan Hoh*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper explores using machine learning (XGBoost) to statistically separate neutrinos from antineutrinos in a magnetic-field-free liquid scintillator detector (e.g., JUNO), thereby improving sensitivity to leptonic CP violation using atmospheric neutrinos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: JUNO-like detectors lack magnetic fields and thus cannot distinguish neutrino/antineutrino charge signs, degrading CP violation sensitivity; atmospheric neutrinos offer complementary CP probes but require statistical separation methods.

Method: Generated atmospheric νμ and νe CC events with GENIE (Honda flux) in JUNO geometry; applied 3-flavor oscillation reweighting (OscProb, Earth matter, NuFIT); modeled detector response via energy/direction smearing and neutron multiplicity; trained logistic regression, XGBoost, and MLP on inelasticity, neutron multiplicity, and lepton energy; selected XGBoost; evaluated CP sensitivity via Poisson likelihood with systematic nuisance parameters and marginalization over 5 oscillation parameters within 3σ ranges.

Result: XGBoost achieves AUC ≈ 0.83 for both flavors; CP sensitivity improves from 1.49σ to 1.96σ (NO) and from 1.14σ to 1.61σ (IO) under √N approximation for 200 kt·yr exposure.

Conclusion: ML-based statistical ν/ν̄ separation partially recovers CP violation sensitivity lost in charge-blind detectors; results are preliminary and warrant further studies with realistic detector response and full systematics.

Abstract: Atmospheric neutrinos span a broad range of energies and baselines, offering a complementary probe of leptonic CP violation. However, a JUNO-like liquid scintillator detector without a magnetic field cannot directly separate neutrino and antineutrino interactions, reducing CP violation sensitivity. This study investigates whether machine learning based statistical separation can improve CP violation sensitivity in a 20-kton detector. $\nu_{\mu}$ and $\nu_e$ charged current events are generated with GENIE (using Honda flux) in a JUNO-like detector geometry and are reweighted using OscProb three flavor oscillation probabilities incorporating Earth matter effects and NuFIT parameters. Detector response is approximated by smearing lepton and hadronic energies, neutrino direction, and neutron multiplicity. Logistic regression, XGBoost, and multilayer perceptron (MLP) classifiers are trained on inelasticity, neutron multiplicity, and charged lepton energy, with XGBoost selected for the analysis. CP violation sensitivity is evaluated for a 200 kton-year exposure using a Poisson likelihood with systematic nuisance parameters and marginalization over five oscillation parameters within their respective $3\sigma$ ranges. XGBoost achieves an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) of approximately 0.83 for both flavor samples. Under the square root approximation, peak CP violation sensitivity improves from 1.49 $\sigma$ to 1.96 $\sigma$ for normal mass ordering and from 1.14 $\sigma$ to 1.61 $\sigma$ for inverted mass ordering. Machine learning based statistical neutrino-antineutrino separation can recover part of the CP violation sensitivity lost in charge blind detectors. These preliminary results motivate further studies incorporating validated detector response and systematic uncertainties.

</details>

Abstract: Using a sample of $(2712.4 \pm 14.3) \times 10^{6}$ $\psi(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we report the first observation and measurement of the branching fractions for the decays $\chi_{cJ}\to \phi \omega \eta$ and $\chi_{cJ} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}$ ($J = 0,1,2$) with statistical significance greater than $5\sigma$. The branching fractions are determined to be $\mathcal B(\chi_{c0} \to \phi \omega \eta) = (5.43 \pm 0.23 \pm 0.52) \times 10^{-4}$, $\mathcal B(\chi_{c1} \to \phi \omega \eta) = (2.51 \pm 0.14 \pm 0.24) \times 10^{-4}$ and $\mathcal B(\chi_{c2} \to \phi \omega \eta) = (3.03 \pm 0.15 \pm 0.28) \times 10^{-4}$ for the $\eta$ mode, and $\mathcal B(\chi_{c0} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}) = (1.23 \pm 0.23 \pm 0.15) \times 10^{-4}$, $\mathcal B(\chi_{c1} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}) = (1.09 \pm 0.17 \pm 0.13) \times 10^{-4}$ and $\mathcal B(\chi_{c2} \to \phi \omega \eta^{\prime}) = (0.98 \pm 0.17 \pm 0.11) \times 10^{-4}$ for the $\eta^{\prime}$ mode, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic.

</details>


### [49] [Measurements of $C\!P$ violation with charm hadrons at Belle and Belle II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11957)
*Jaeyoung Kim*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports recent Belle and Belle II searches for CP violation in various charm-hadron decays—including mesonic (e.g., D→ηh⁺, ππ, Kₛ⁰Kₛ⁰, π⁺π⁻π⁰) and baryonic (Ξc⁺, Λc⁺ three-body) modes—using up to 1.4 ab⁻¹ of integrated luminosity. All measured CP asymmetries are consistent with zero within uncertainties, yielding no evidence for CP violation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe CP violation beyond the Standard Model in charm decays—a theoretically suppressed but experimentally accessible sector—by testing CP asymmetries across diverse final states, including novel baryonic channels and isospin/U-spin relations.

Method: Analysis of Belle (980 fb⁻¹) and Belle II (428 fb⁻¹) datasets; measurements of direct CP asymmetries (A_CP) in multiple decay channels; use of inclusive charm-flavor tagging; tests of isospin structure in D→ππ and U-spin symmetry in charmed-baryon decays.

Result: All reported direct CP asymmetries—including first measurements for Ξc⁺→Σ⁺h⁺h⁻ and Λc⁺→ph⁺h⁻—are statistically consistent with zero; no significant CP violation observed.

Conclusion: Current precision confirms CP conservation in these charm-hadron decays; results constrain new physics models affecting charm-sector CP violation and validate theoretical expectations of SM-suppressed effects.

Abstract: Recent searches for $C\!P$ violation in charm-hadron decays at Belle and Belle II are presented. The measurements cover $D^+_{(s)}\to\eta h^+$, $D\to\pi\pi$, $D^0\to K_S^0K_S^0$, and $D^0\to\pi^+\pi^-\pi^0$, together with the singly Cabibbo-suppressed baryon decays $\Xi_c^+\to\Sigma^+h^+h^-$ and $\Lambda_c^+\to ph^+h^-$, where $h=\pi,K$. The Belle II analyses use the Run~1 data sample of $428\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, supplemented by $980\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of Belle data for the $K_S^0K_S^0$ measurements. We report preliminary results for the $\eta h^+$ modes, complementary tests of the isospin structure of $D\to\pi\pi$, and an application of inclusive charm-flavor tagging. The first individual decay-rate asymmetries in these three-body charmed-baryon modes also provide tests of $U$-spin symmetry. All reported asymmetries are consistent with $C\!P$ symmetry within the present precision.

</details>


### [50] [Beam-beam effects on measurements at electron-positron colliders: LEP neutrino counting and Higgs factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12265)
*Brendon Madison*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces FeeBL, a new beam-beam simulation framework, to analyze electromagnetic beam-beam effects at e±e± colliders. It corrects prior simulation biases (~2% in beam deflection), revises LEP neutrino count uncertainty (Nν = 2.9965 ± 0.0040), fixes errors in pair production modeling (Breit-Wheeler, Bethe-Heitler, Landau-Lifshitz), validates depolarization and luminosity spectra against ILC/HALHF, and assesses collision energy monochromatization—achieving sub-MeV widths in linear collider setups (at luminosity cost) but only weak narrowing in FCC-ee unless ~10 m horizontal dispersion is added.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve accuracy of beam-beam interaction modeling—critical for luminosity, polarization, collision energy, and background predictions—at current (LEP) and future (ILC, HALHF, FCC-ee, Higgs factories) e±e± colliders, where systematic biases and implementation errors in prior simulations limit precision physics reach.

Method: Developed Fast e±e± Beam and Luminosity (FeeBL), a new beam-beam simulation framework; validated against analytical field-integration (LEP Bhabha), cross-checked with published LEP, ILC, HALHF, and FCC-ee results; performed bias quantification, uncertainty re-evaluation, error diagnosis/correction in pair production modules, and parametric studies of monochromatization via dispersion tuning and Gaussian beam overlap modeling.

Result: Identified and corrected ~2% bias in prior beam-deflection simulations; revised LEP Nν to 2.9965 ± 0.0040 (reduced uncertainty); fixed implementation errors in Breit-Wheeler, Bethe-Heitler, and Landau-Lifshitz pair production models; reproduced beam depolarization and luminosity spectra consistent with ILC/HALHF; achieved sub-MeV collision energy peak widths in exploratory linear collider configurations (with luminosity penalty); found FCC-ee requires ~10 m horizontal-only dispersion to reach ~1 MeV width.

Conclusion: FeeBL enables higher-fidelity beam-beam simulation, revealing and correcting systematic errors that impact precision electroweak and Higgs physics. The revised uncertainty for Nν and corrected background models strengthen confidence in LEP legacy and future collider designs; monochromatization analysis provides concrete design guidance—especially the dispersion requirement for FCC-ee—to achieve MeV-scale energy resolution for direct Higgs production.

Abstract: Beam-beam electromagnetic interactions affect the luminosity, collision energy, polarization, and particle backgrounds relevant to precision measurements at electron-positron colliders. We investigate these effects using Fast $e^{\pm}e^{\pm}$ Beam and Luminosity (FeeBL), a new beam-beam simulation framework, and compare its predictions with published results from LEP and proposed future colliders. We reproduce the analytical field-integration calculations of LEP Bhabha scattering and identify an approximately 2% bias in previously published beam-deflection simulations. Applying the corresponding luminosity correction and a revised uncertainty assessment motivated by the observed agreement among the four LEP experiments yields ($N_\nu = 2.9965 \pm 0.0040$), compared with the previously reported ($2.9963 \pm 0.0074$). The revised uncertainty is conditional on an assumed common rescaling of experiment specific uncertainties and motivates further investigation of the original uncertainty budgets. We identify and correct implementation errors in the modeling of Breit-Wheeler, Bethe-Heitler, and Landau-Lifshitz pair production, with implications for detector backgrounds at Higgs factories. Simulated beam depolarization and luminosity spectra are compared with existing ILC and HALHF predictions. Finally, we investigate collision energy monochromatization for direct Higgs production, obtaining sub-MeV collision energy peak widths in exploratory linear collider configurations at substantial luminosity cost, while finding significantly weaker narrowing for the FCC-ee configurations studied. We derive a rigid Gaussian beam overlap model and estimate that FCC-ee needs roughly 10 m of horizontal only dispersion to reach widths of $\approx1~\mathrm{MeV}$.

</details>


### [51] [Search for Dark Photon Dark Matter at 74--159 $μ$eV/$c^2$ Using Broadband Cryogenic Receivers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12774)
*Hiroki Takeuchi,Shunsuke Adachi,Shunki Endo,Keigo Hanimura,Michinori Hirose,Shunsuke Honda,Yuki Sakurai,Junya Suzuki,Osamu Tajima*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: A direct search for dark photon dark matter (DPDM) using cryogenic receivers and broadband spectroscopy found no significant signal, setting improved 95% CL upper limits on kinetic mixing χ of (3.1–17)×10⁻¹² over 74–159 μeV/c².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To directly detect dark photon dark matter via its conversion to photons at a metal surface—leveraging the mass-dependent resonant frequency—and to improve upon existing experimental constraints, especially in the 74–159 μeV/c² range where prior direct limits were weak or absent.

Method: Used cryogenic K- and Ka-band receivers coupled with a broadband spectrometer to search for DPDM-to-photon conversion signals from a metal surface; scanned 74–159 μeV/c² (18–38.5 GHz) with integration time 4.8×10⁵ s per frequency bin; assumed DPDM constitutes all local dark matter density.

Result: No statistically significant signal detected; derived 95% CL upper limits on kinetic mixing χ = (3.1–17)×10⁻¹² across 99.8% of the search band; improvements exceed one order of magnitude over most of 74–110 μeV/c² and represent first direct limits over most of 110–159 μeV/c².

Conclusion: This experiment sets the strongest direct laboratory constraints to date on DPDM in the sub-160 μeV/c² mass range, probing well below cosmological bounds and narrowing viable parameter space for ultralight vector dark matter.

Abstract: Dark photons are hypothetical massive vector bosons that couple to ordinary photons through kinetic mixing $\chi$. Dark photon dark matter (DPDM) converts into photons at a metal surface, with a frequency set by its mass. Using cryogenic K- and Ka-band receivers with a broadband spectrometer, we searched for this signal over 74--159 $\mu$eV/$c^2$ (18--38.5 GHz) with an integration time of $4.8\times10^5$ s per frequency bin. We found no statistically significant signal and set upper limits of $\chi<(3.1$--$17)\times10^{-12}$ at the 95% confidence level over 99.8% of the search range, assuming DPDM constitutes all local dark matter. These results improve on previous limits by more than an order of magnitude over most of 74--110 $\mu$eV/$c^2$ and provide the first direct limits over most of 110--159 $\mu$eV/$c^2$, approximately two orders of magnitude below cosmological bounds.

</details>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [52] [Cylinder spectral curve and 1-cut Matrix model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11015)
*Bertrand Eynard*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bidirectional correspondence between the geometry of spectral curves (specifically 2-branchpoint, cylinder-type curves) and formal random matrix models, providing an explicit method to reconstruct the matrix model's potential V(x) and two-body interaction R(x,x') from the spectral curve data using boundary integral operators and generalized cycles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the geometric description of spectral curves (arising in large-N random matrix theory) and the underlying algebraic data (potential V and interaction R) of the matrix model, enabling reconstruction of the model from its spectral curve.

Method: Uses boundary integral operators and generalized cycles defined on a two-sheeted Riemann surface (cylinder with two simple ramification points) associated with the 1-cut spectral curve; constructs inverse mapping from spectral curve geometry to formal matrix model parameters.

Result: Provides an explicit constructive method to recover the one-body potential V(x) and two-body interaction R(x,x') of a formal random matrix model solely from the geometric data of its 2-branchpoint spectral curve embedded in a cylinder.

Conclusion: There exists a well-defined, constructive equivalence between 2-branchpoint spectral curves on a cylinder and formal random matrix models; the spectral curve geometry fully encodes and determines the model's defining potentials.

Abstract: For a random matrix, with eigenvalues in a one-body potential $V(x)$ and a 2-body interaction $R(x,x')$, it is known that the resolvent converges in the large $N$ limit to an analytic function in the complex plane minus cuts, called the spectral curve. In the 1-cut case, a two-sheeted neighborhood of the cut is a cylinder with two simple ramification points. We describe these spectral curve data by boundary integral operators and generalized cycles. Vice-versa, given a 2-branchpoints spectral curve in a cylinder, there exists a formal random matrix model that corresponds to it. The goal of this article is to show the method to recover a formal random matrix model, with its potential and 2-body interaction, from the geometry of the spectral curve.

</details>


### [53] [Projective symmetries and differential recursions for the large-$N$ Gross--Witten--Wadia and one-dimensional QCD matrix models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11670)
*S. Voloshyn*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a deep algebraic and projective correspondence between the fixed-charge planar Gross–Witten–Wadia (GWW) model and large-N, fixed-N_f/N one-dimensional QCD, linking their resolvent equations (quartic vs. sextic), recursion structures, boundary conditions (Narayana triangle), and symmetry (S_4 via signed permutations); it shows how QCD reduces to GWW in the many-flavor limit while preserving key projective data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
