Motivation: To uncover structural parallels and universal features between two distinct large-N gauge theories—the fixed-charge GWW model and source-free 1D QCD—by analyzing their free energy equations, resolvent constraints, and combinatorial boundaries.

Method: Analytical derivation of algebraic equations for the free energy derivative from the resolvent and one-cut condition; analysis of finite-N Toeplitz determinants for branch selection; use of signed permutations of parameter triples to realize S_4 symmetry and unify projective descriptions; derivation of differential recurrences (PDEs and discrete recursions) from the sextic equation; identification and analysis of boundary data including the Narayana triangle and symmetric product formulas for collapsed slices.

Result: A quartic (GWW) and sextic (QCD) algebraic equation govern the derivative of the free energy; QCD’s sextic yields a second-order PDE enabling both row-wise (two-apart) and column-wise (finite-width) reconstruction of the coefficient pyramid from GWW boundary data; the Narayana triangle emerges as an exact combinatorial boundary; a collapsed slice counts decorated bipartite plane trees with an exact symmetric product formula; many-flavor scaling collapses the sextic to the quartic while preserving transverse projective structure and coalescing the divided-difference triple.

Conclusion: The GWW model and 1D QCD share a common projective algebraic framework governed by S_4 symmetry and resolvent-derived algebraic equations; their apparent differences stem from parameter scaling and boundary conditions, not fundamental structure—suggesting universality in large-N gauge theory recursion and combinatorics.

Abstract: We compare the fixed-charge planar Gross--Witten--Wadia (GWW) model with source-free one-dimensional QCD at large $N$ and $N_f/N$ fixed. In both models the resolvent and one-cut condition give an algebraic equation for a derivative of the free energy: a quartic for GWW and a sextic for QCD. Finite-$N$ Toeplitz determinants independently check the selected QCD branch. Signed permutations of homogeneous parameter triples realize $S_4$; the resulting action on the algebraic branches and operator labels gives a common projective description of the two models. For QCD, the sextic yields a differential recurrence linking small-charge rows two apart. The same second-order PDE gives a finite-width column recursion that reconstructs the coefficient pyramid from its GWW boundary. A distinct boundary, obtained from the first resummed coefficient, is the Narayana triangle. A formally collapsed slice counts decorated bipartite plane trees and admits an exact symmetric product formula. The many-flavor scaling reduces the sextic to the quartic and retains transverse projective data: the QCD divided-difference triple coalesces at this boundary, whereas the limiting quartic satisfies the full standard GWW third-order system. We distinguish algebraic degree, mixed differential order, and fixed-parameter ordinary differential order throughout.

</details>


### [54] [Real-Time Path Integral Resurgence: Which Classical Path Contributes?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12510)
*Job Feldbrugge*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes resurgence theory as a practical alternative to Picard-Lefschetz theory for identifying relevant complex classical paths in real-time Feynman path integrals; it computes high-order asymptotic series using Feynman diagrams and cumulant recursions, extracts path adjacency from divergent Borel-Padé tails, applies the South-East rule to determine relevance, validates predictions against the exact propagator for a Rosen-Morse barrier (including tunnelling), achieves exponential accuracy via resummation, and outlines extension to relativistic Hamiltonian-constrained systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Picard-Lefschetz theory—while theoretically sound for selecting contributing complex paths in real-time path integrals—relies on computationally intractable intersection numbers of Lefschetz thimbles in infinite dimensions; a more practical, computable criterion for path relevance is needed.

Method: High-order asymptotic expansion of real and complex classical actions using Feynman diagrams and a transport recursion for cumulants; divergence analysis via Borel-Padé resummation to infer adjacency among saddle points; application of the 'South-East rule' to map adjacency to relevance; numerical verification against exact quantum propagator for Rosen-Morse potential; extension framework sketched for relativistic systems with Hamiltonian constraints.

Result: Identification of relevant complex classical paths—including the tunnelling trajectory—in front of and behind a Rosen-Morse barrier; tracking of ordinary and higher-order Stokes phenomena as endpoint varies; exponential-accuracy reproduction of the exact propagator via resummed series; demonstration that resurgence bypasses explicit thimble construction while preserving physical correctness.

Conclusion: Resurgence theory provides a robust, computationally feasible, and physically transparent framework for evaluating real-time path integrals—replacing intractable thimble intersection calculations with asymptotic analysis and Stokes phenomena, and naturally generalizing to constrained relativistic settings.

Abstract: Which complex classical paths contribute to a real-time Feynman path integral? Picard-Lefschetz theory answers this question with the intersection numbers of the thimbles, which are notoriously hard to compute in infinite dimensions. We show that resurgence provides a practical alternative. We compute the asymptotic series of the real and complex classical paths to high order, with Feynman diagrams and with a transport recursion for the cumulants, and read off the adjacency of the classical paths from the divergent tails of the series with Borel-Padé methods. The South-East rule then turns adjacency into relevance. For a particle meeting a Rosen-Morse barrier, we identify the relevant complex classical paths in front of and behind the barrier, including the complex path that carries quantum tunnelling, follow ordinary and higher-order Stokes phenomena as the final point moves, and verify the predictions with the exact propagator. The resummed series reproduce the propagator with exponential accuracy. Finally, we outline how the expansion extends to relativistic path integrals governed by a Hamiltonian constraint.

</details>


### [55] [On the Seven-particle Clusters of Lennard-Jones and Thomson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09925)
*Li Shen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the pentagonal bipyramid (PBP) is the unique global minimizer for both the 7-particle Lennard-Jones energy in ℝ³ and the 7-charge Thomson energy on S², using a novel graph-geometric framework with weak-interaction graphs, combinatorial reduction, geometric certification, and coercivity arguments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish exact, rigorous global optimality and uniqueness for the seven-particle Lennard-Jones and Thomson problems—two fundamental models of particle aggregation and electrostatics—and to uncover a shared geometric organizing principle independent of interaction type.

Method: Develops a graph-geometric framework based on weak-interaction graphs (thresholded by pair geometry); applies pair-energy counting, graph classification, subset bounds, and force balance to reduce candidate minimizers; uses exact geometric certificates and uniform restoring-energy estimates (LJ) or whole-domain coercivity and a global localisation theorem (Thomson) to prove uniqueness.

Result: The pentagonal bipyramid is the unique global minimizer (up to rigid symmetries and relabelling) for both the 7-particle Lennard-Jones energy in ℝ³ and the 7-charge Thomson energy on S²; the Thomson result upgrades prior numerical certificates to exact, deterministic uniqueness.

Conclusion: Fivefold symmetry in the PBP arises universally for seven particles across distinct physical regimes—short-range attractive/repulsive potentials and pure repulsion on a sphere—revealing an underlying geometric organizing principle decoupled from interaction specifics.

Abstract: In this Letter, we develop a graph-geometric framework and prove that the pentagonal bipyramid (PBP) is the unique global minimiser of both the seven-particle Lennard-Jones energy in $\mathbb{R}^3$ and the seven-charge Thomson energy on $S^2$, up to rigid symmetries and particle relabelling. For the Thomson problem, this strengthens a recent deterministic global-optimality certificate to numerical tolerance into an exact global-optimality and uniqueness theorem. A weak-interaction graph, defined by thresholding pair geometry, organises configuration space without imposing symmetry or discarding any pair interaction. For Lennard-Jones, pair-energy counting, graph classification, subset bounds, and force balance reduce all possible minimisers to 29 bounded domains; exact geometric certificates exclude 28, localise the survivor near the PBP, and a uniform restoring-energy estimate proves uniqueness. The Thomson proof follows the same progression: graph reduction, geometric localisation, and rigidity, but finalises through whole-domain coercivity and a global localisation theorem. The same fivefold order therefore emerges in two very different physical settings: an unconstrained cluster with a preferred bond length and purely repelling charges confined to a sphere, showing a common geometric organising principle for seven particles that is not tied to either interaction.

</details>


### [56] [On transportation of constrictions on phase-lock areas of overdamped Josephson junction by means of isomonodromic deformation of the associated special double confluent Heun equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09931)
*S.I. Tertychniy*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes constriction locations in phase-lock areas for an overdamped Josephson junction model using isomonodromic deformations of a double confluent Heun equation, linked to a novel representation of the Third Painlevé equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To locate constrictions (parameter values where phase-lock regions shrink to points) in the parameter space of the overdamped Josephson junction model — a problem with implications for nonlinear dynamics and superconducting electronics.

Method: Applies isomonodromic deformation theory to the associated special double confluent Heun equation; introduces a new representation of the Third Painlevé equation coupled with an auxiliary nonlinear second-order ODE for efficient numerical/analytical treatment.

Result: Numerical computation of constriction locations; derivation of several qualitative conjectures about the structure of phase-lock areas based on heuristic data.

Conclusion: The isomonodromic approach combined with the new Painlevé representation provides an effective framework for analyzing constrictions in the phase-lock geometry of the Josephson junction model.

Abstract: An example of computation of locations of constrictions on the phase-lock area in the parameter space for the equation modeling dynamics of overdamped Josephson junction is discussed. Several conjectures of qualitative character are inferred from the heuristic data thus obtained. The method applying isomonodromic deformations of the special double confluent Heun equation associated with the noted model is used. Such a deformation is controlled by the data provided by solution to a Third Painlevè equation for which a new representation in conjunction with the corresponding non-linear second order differential equation fairly efficient in the treatment of the problem in question is proposed.

</details>


### [57] [Positivity Improving Quantum Markov Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10042)
*Luigi Accardi,Abdessatar Souissi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies positivity-improving Markov operators (unital completely positive maps) on matrix algebras, establishing their self-duality, equivalence to a quantum Markov–Dobrushin–Doeblin minorization condition, and a Kraus-space characterization: such maps have no rank-one operator in the orthogonal complement of their Kraus space. It derives a sharp lower bound (2d−1) on Kraus rank for d×d matrices, proves NP-hardness of general verification, provides full classifications for qubits and qutrits, and shows failure of norm contraction in infinite dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the classical notion of strictly positive stochastic matrices to the quantum setting via unital completely positive maps, and to characterize when such quantum channels 'regularize' positivity — i.e., map any nonzero positive semidefinite input to a strictly positive (invertible) output — with implications for ergodicity, mixing, and convergence in quantum Markov processes.

Method: Uses operator algebra, quantum information theory, and functional analysis: establishes equivalences via dual maps and minorization conditions; employs Hilbert–Schmidt duality to link positivity improvement to Kraus-space geometry; derives rank bounds using linear algebra and constructs extremal examples (e.g., Hankel-type channels); classifies low-dimensional cases via explicit parameterization and semidefinite programming; proves complexity hardness via reduction; analyzes infinite-dimensional counterexamples using spectral and compactness arguments.

Result: 1) Positivity improvement is self-dual and equivalent to faithful quantum Markov–Dobrushin–Doeblin minorization. 2) A Markov operator is positivity improving iff its Kraus space’s HS-orthogonal complement contains no rank-one operator. 3) Minimal Kraus rank is 2d−1 for d×d matrices; attained by explicit Hankel channels. 4) Full effective classifications for d=2 (qubits) and d=3 (qutrits). 5) NP-hardness of general decision problem. 6) In infinite dimensions, positivity improvement does not imply norm contraction.

Conclusion: Positivity improvement is a robust, geometrically characterized property of quantum channels with sharp finite-dimensional bounds and structural rigidity, but exhibits qualitative failure of classical implications (e.g., contraction) in infinite dimensions — highlighting key distinctions between finite- and infinite-dimensional quantum Markov dynamics.

Abstract: A Markov operator on a matrix algebra, that is, a unital completely positive map, is called positivity improving if it maps every nonzero positive element to an invertible one. This is the noncommutative counterpart of a stochastic matrix with strictly positive entries. We show that positivity improvement is self-dual and equivalent to a faithful quantum Markov--Dobrushin--Doeblin minorization. We also compare the scalar trace minorization with the optimal common-lower-bound construction of the quantum Markov--Dobrushin coefficient. We then characterize positivity improvement through the Kraus space of the map: a Markov operator is positivity improving if and only if the Hilbert--Schmidt orthogonal complement of its Kraus space contains no operator of rank one. As a consequence the Kraus rank of a positivity improving Markov operator on the algebra of $d\times d$ matrices is at least $2d-1$, and an explicit Hankel-type channel shows that this bound is attained in every dimension. Although deciding positivity improvement is NP-hard in general, we obtain complete and effectively checkable classifications for qubits and qutrits, together with families of examples (Pauli, Weyl and Werner--Holevo channels) showing that all our estimates are optimal. Finally we show that, in infinite dimensions, positivity improvement no longer implies any contraction of the Markov operator.

</details>


### [58] [Geometric classification of Noether invariants of the Time-Dependent damped Duffing Oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10522)
*Michael Tsamparlis,and Aniekan Magnus Ukpong*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a general geometric classification of Noether point symmetries for time-dependent conformal Lagrangians modeling damped dynamical systems, yielding explicit Noether generators, gauge functions, and invariants; it recovers the Bateman invariant for the damped harmonic oscillator and discovers a new quadratic invariant for a specific parameter regime of the damped Duffing oscillator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify Noether point symmetries for time-dependent conformal Lagrangians describing dissipative systems with linear damping, enabling derivation of exact invariants without restricting the conformal factor A(t), metric g_ij, or potential V(x).

Method: Geometric classification via coupling temporal parameters of the conformal factor A(t) with the homothetic algebra of the kinetic metric g_ij using compatibility and integrability conditions; derivation of a main theorem characterizing Noether generators, gauge functions, and invariants, split by the sign and gradient nature of homothetic vectors.

Result: A fully general, covariant main theorem determining Noether symmetries and invariants; application yields the classical Bateman invariant for the damped harmonic oscillator and a new exact time-dependent quadratic invariant for the damped Duffing oscillator under the condition a = 2γ²/9.

Conclusion: The framework unifies and generalizes existing results on Noether symmetries for damped systems; the newly discovered invariant for the anharmonic case constitutes a rare global conservation law for a nonlinear dissipative system, with implications for integration and quantization.

Abstract: We present a systematic geometric classification of the Noether point symmetries of time-dependent conformal Lagrangians of the form $L = A(t)\left(\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}\dot{x}^{i}\dot{x}^{j} - V(x)\right)$, whose Euler--Lagrange equations describe dynamical systems with time-dependent linear damping. The classification is carried out by coupling the temporal parameters of the conformal factor $A(t)$ with the homothetic algebra of the kinetic metric $g_{ij}$ through a set of compatibility and integrability conditions. The central result is a main theorem which determines the Noether generators, the associated gauge functions and the resulting invariants. The theorem splits naturally into two cases according to whether the quantity $D - 2\psi$ is non-zero or zero, with further subcases depending on whether the homothetic vectors are gradient or non-gradient. The theorem imposes no restrictions on $A(t)$, $g_{ij}$ or $V(x)$, and is therefore fully general and covariant. We apply the theorem to the time-dependent damped Duffing oscillator (DDO) in Euclidean space $E^{3}$ with $A(t) = e^{\gamma t}$ and potential $V = \frac{1}{2}a\,r^{2} + \frac{% \lambda}{4}r^{4}$. For the damped harmonic oscillator ($\lambda = 0$) the theorem reproduces the classical Bateman invariant as a special case. For the anharmonic case we obtain a new exact time-dependent quadratic invariant, valid when the linear stiffness satisfies $a = 2\gamma^{2}/9$. Each invariant is verified explicitly by direct differentiation along the equations of motion. The new invariant provides a rare example of a global conservation law for a fully nonlinear dissipative system, and can be used for the integration and quantization of the DDO.

</details>


### [59] [The random matrix hard edge --- applications, exact PDFs and large $N$ expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10990)
*Peter J. Forrester,Anas A. Rahman,Bo-Jian Shen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews hard edge phenomena in Hermitian random matrices—especially where eigenvalue density vanishes on one side—and presents new asymptotic results for the Jacobi ensemble: correlation functions and conditioned gap probabilities admit large-N expansions in inverse even powers of a shifted parameter $N_{\rm hJ}'$, with differential operator characterizations of corrections at $\beta = 1,2,4$ and a conjecture for general $\beta > 0$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and extend understanding of hard edge scaling in random matrix ensembles—particularly the Jacobi ensemble—with emphasis on universal asymptotic structure beyond leading order, motivated by both theoretical interest in spectral edges and applications in mathematical physics and statistics.

Method: Analytic asymptotic analysis of exact eigenvalue probability density functions; derivation of large $N$ expansions for correlation functions and conditioned gap probability generating functions under hard edge scaling; use of differential operators to characterize subleading corrections; extension from known cases ($\beta = 1,2,4$) to conjecture for general $\beta > 0$.

Result: For all even $\beta$, correlation functions and conditioned gap probability generating functions of the Jacobi ensemble admit $1/N_{\rm hJ}'^2$-type asymptotic expansions. At $\beta = 2$, the first two corrections are expressed via second- and fourth-order differential operators acting on the limiting law; analogous second-order operators are identified for $\beta = 1,4$, enabling a conjectured universal form for all $\beta > 0$.

Conclusion: The hard edge scaling limit exhibits a robust hierarchical structure governed by differential operators, with universality in the form of the expansion parameter $N_{\rm hJ}'$ and operator order across symmetry classes; this provides a framework for higher-order corrections in edge universality beyond the classical Tracy–Widom regime.

Abstract: A hard edge of a Hermitian random matrix occurs when the eigenvalue density is strictly zero on one side of the edge, and nonzero on the other. Another possibility is that the eigenvalues occur in $\pm$ pairs, which creates a hard edge at the origin for the positive eigenvalues. Both review material and new results are presented for random matrix ensembles exhibiting a hard edge. The review material has two focuses: one is on applications of the random matrix hard edge, while the other is on exact eigenvalue probability density functions. The new results relate to the large $N$ expansion of correlation functions and conditioned gap probability generating functions with hard edge scaling for the Jacobi ensemble. For all even $\beta$ we are able to show that these quantities admit expansions in inverse even powers of the variable $N_{\rm hJ}':= N - 1/2 + (a + b + 1)/\beta$. For the $\beta =2$ Jacobi unitary ensemble, we can relate the leading (next leading) correction to the limit law to a particular second order (fourth order) differential operator applied to the latter. The analogous second order differential operator is found for $\beta=1,4$, leading then to its conjectured form for all $\beta>0$.

</details>


### [60] [Exactly integrable field theory in $(2+1)$-dimensional space-time: the spectral equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11182)
*Sergey Sergeev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a complete system of nested Bethe Ansatz equations for the spectrum of a relativistic evolution operator on a Kagomé lattice, built from the q-oscillator R-matrix satisfying the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define and solve the spectrum of a one-step relativistic evolution operator in quantum field theory using integrable structures on a two-dimensional auxiliary lattice.

Method: Uses the q-oscillator algebra on a Kagomé lattice; employs the tetrahedral R-matrix as local evolution kernel; derives a full set of spectral equations analogous to nested Bethe Ansatz equations, valid for arbitrary occupation-number initial data.

Result: Explicit derivation of a complete, consistent system of spectral equations for the evolution operator; proof that the system is well-defined for arbitrary initial states and admits a thermodynamic limit.

Conclusion: The evolution operator’s spectrum is fully characterized by a nested Bethe Ansatz–type system rooted in 3D integrability (tetrahedron equation), enabling rigorous analysis in both finite and thermodynamic limits.

Abstract: The primary object in quantum field theories is the one-step relativistic evolution operator. In this paper we consider the evolution operator defined on the auxiliary Kagomé lattice with the $q$-oscillator algebra of observables, the local ingredient of the evolution operator is the $q$-oscillator $R$-matrix of the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation. We construct explicitly the complete system of equations (which can be seen as a specific form of nested Bethe Ansatz equations) defining the spectrum of the evolution operator. This system of equations is valid for an arbitrary initial data (occupation numbers), and the thermodynamical limit is well defined for this system.

</details>


### [61] [The Lee-Yang property of the Blume-Emery-Griffiths model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11484)
*Yuri Kozitsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Lieb-Sokal theorem to show that a spin-1 model with quadratic and quartic interactions and a single-site field retains the Lee-Yang property under explicit low-temperature conditions on coupling strengths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Lee-Yang property beyond standard ferromagnetic pairwise interactions, specifically to spin-1 models with $S_i^2 S_j^2$ terms and on-site anisotropy, where classical criteria (e.g., Lieb-Sokal) do not directly apply.

Method: Analytic continuation and zero-free region analysis of the partition function in the complex magnetic field plane; derivation of explicit temperature- and parameter-dependent lower bounds $C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})$ on $J_{ij}$ ensuring absence of zeros on the imaginary axis.

Result: The Lee-Yang property holds for the spin-1 Hamiltonian $-\sum (J_{ij} S_iS_j + K_{ij}S_i^2S_j^2) + \sum \Delta S_i^2$ for all real $K_{ij}$ and $\Delta > 0$, provided $J_{ij} > C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})$, where $C \to +\infty$ as $\beta \to 0$ (i.e., high-temperature limit).

Conclusion: The Lee-Yang property persists for this extended spin-1 model, but only under sufficiently strong ferromagnetic couplings and at low enough temperatures—indicating a temperature-dependent stability of the property beyond standard pairwise models.

Abstract: The Lieb-Sokal theorem provides a tool for establishing the Lee-Yang property of the partition function of a spin model if the corresponding single-spin partition function has this property and the interaction term in its Hamiltonian is of the form $-\sum J_{ij} S_iS_j$ with $J_{ij}\geq 0$. According to the result of this paper, the spin $S=1$ model with the Hamiltonian $-\sum (J_{ij} S_iS_j + K_{ij}S_i^2S_j^2) + \sum \Delta S_i^2$ has the Lee-Yang property for all $K_{ij}\in \mathds{R}$ and $\Delta >0$, whenever each $J_{ij}$ exceeds an explicitly computed bound $C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})>0$, such that $C(\beta, \Delta, K_{ij})\to + \infty$ as $\beta \to 0$. The latter suggests that the Lee-Yang property of this model may hold only for sufficiently low temperatures.

</details>


### [62] [SU(2) Yang-Mills: Spectral Bounds, Vacuum Concentration, and Criteria for the Continuum Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11534)
*Aleksandar Mihajlovic*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops quantitative methods to analyze the continuum limit of gauge theories, focusing on preserving nonzero renormalized states and a positive spectral gap as spatial cutoffs are removed. It establishes physical-time dynamics in infinite volume via local correlations, derives entropy and fidelity bounds tied to vacuum concentration, and provides certified benchmarks for glueball and winding-channel observables—though it does not prove a 4D spatial-cutoff construction or an exact continuum mass gap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously separate and quantify the distinct requirements—nonzero renormalized states and positive spectral estimate—in continuum mass-gap arguments for lattice gauge theories, especially under removal of spatial cutoff and infinite-volume limits.

Method: Combines gauge reduction, reflection positivity, strong-coupling estimates, local history correlation analysis, Schur decomposition, collective projection, exact blocking, and corrected effective-potential theorems; employs finite-matrix criteria, entropy/fidelity analysis, and representation-wise 2D benchmarking.

Result: Constructs subsequential infinite-volume physical-time dynamics; derives extensive entropy bounds, fidelity obstructions for electric cutoffs, and representation-space growth constraints; certifies a three-link conditioning functional up to Wilson coupling 0.354; validates published glueball measurements and reproduces winding-channel/replica studies with explicit statistical scope; provides reproducible benchmarks and continuum transfer criteria.

Conclusion: The work delivers rigorous, quantitative separation of mass-gap prerequisites and supplies certified tools for continuum transfer, but stops short of establishing a nontrivial 4D spatial-cutoff construction or proving a uniform positive spectral bound in the full continuum limit.

Abstract: A continuum mass-gap argument must preserve nonzero renormalized states and a positive spectral estimate while the spatial cutoff is removed and physical volume grows. We develop quantitative results that separate these requirements. Gauge reduction, reflection positivity and attributed strong-coupling estimates give physical-transfer bounds with nontrivial local sectors. At fixed spatial cutoff and positive coupling, we construct subsequential infinite-volume physical-time dynamics from local history correlations, with nonconstant gauge-invariant observables. For finite spatial boxes, explicit bounds apply to the entire physical vacuum complement; finite-matrix criteria include all omitted representations. Vacuum concentration gives extensive entropy bounds, quantitative fidelity obstructions for hard electric cutoffs, and necessary growth of retained representation spaces. A sharper local comparison yields complete bounds on fixed discarded-link sectors, while collective projection and Schur estimates isolate the additional input needed for a volume-uniform gap. The work also certifies a finite three-link conditioning functional through Wilson coupling 0.354, with its physical interpretation conditional on the stated block-comparison hypotheses. Exact blocking, corrected effective-potential theorems and a representation-by-representation two-dimensional benchmark clarify which approximations preserve the relevant observables. Published glueball measurements retain their original attribution; their reanalysis and the reproduced winding-channel and replica studies have explicitly statistical scope. Complete derivations and reproducible calculations support a set of certified benchmarks and continuum transfer criteria. A nontrivial four-dimensional spatial-cutoff construction and a positive spectral bound along it are not established; no exact continuum mass is asserted.

</details>


### [63] [Universal Critical Relations and Nonuniversal Scaling in Inverse-Square Ising Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11546)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes connected correlations in ferromagnetic Ising chains with long-range interactions decaying as $J_n \sim B/n^2$, showing that critical excess magnetization under a field scaling inversely with spin separation governs correlation structure; it identifies logarithmic integrability conditions for finite thermal slope, derives exact inverse-logarithmic decay and square-root cusps for finite cores, and demonstrates that a $c/\log(n+e)$ correction with $c>1$ yields continuously varying logarithmic exponents and linear cusps—while a lower bound on disordered susceptibility rules out standard Kosterlitz–Thouless scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range interactions ($n^2 J_n \to B > 0$) affect critical behavior, correlation structure, and universality class of one-dimensional ferromagnetic Ising chains—particularly whether Kosterlitz–Thouless physics applies or if novel logarithmic scaling emerges.

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis of the critical excess magnetization under a field scaled as $h_n \propto 1/n$; exact tail computation for correlation functions; rigorous estimation of disordered susceptibility; classification of cusp singularities (square-root vs. linear) based on correction terms to the logarithmic decay.

Result: Connected correlations exhibit inverse-logarithmic decay $\sim 1/\log n$ for finite-core interactions, with square-root cusps; a relative correction $c/\log(n+e), c>1$, induces continuously varying logarithmic powers and linear cusps; crucially, a proven lower bound on disordered susceptibility contradicts the Kosterlitz–Thouless $\chi \sim \exp(c/\sqrt{T_c - T})$ law.

Conclusion: The system belongs to a distinct universality class governed by logarithmic scaling laws, not Kosterlitz–Thouless; correlation structure is determined by critical magnetization response to $1/n$-scaled fields, and cusp type and exponent are tunable via subleading corrections—establishing a new paradigm for marginal long-range Ising systems.

Abstract: For ferromagnetic Ising chains with $n^2J_n\to B>0$, the critical excess magnetization in a field inversely proportional to spin separation determines the connected correlation. Their slowly varying profile has finite logarithmic integral exactly when the ordered thermal slope is finite. For any finite nonnegative core, an exact tail gives inverse-logarithmic correlations and a square-root cusp. A relative correction $c/\log(n+e)$ with $c>1$ gives continuously varying logarithmic powers and a linear cusp. A lower bound on its disordered susceptibility excludes the usual Kosterlitz--Thouless law.

</details>


### [64] [Lieb-Robinson bound for one-dimensional continuum fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11655)
*Marius Lemm,Hailun Lu,Harman Preet Singh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes the first Lieb-Robinson bound (LRB) for a realistic one-dimensional continuum fermion system, enabling application of locality-based methods to such systems under thermodynamically stable conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The exponential growth of Hilbert space dimension in quantum many-body systems necessitates exploiting locality; while Lieb-Robinson bounds (LRBs) have been established for spin and some lattice boson systems, none existed for realistic continuum systems—constituting a major barrier to extending locality-based techniques.

Method: The authors develop a novel analytical framework to derive an LRB for 1D continuum fermions, imposing constraints on initial states via controlled local particle number and energy moments to ensure thermodynamic stability.

Result: First rigorous Lieb-Robinson bound proven for a standard 1D continuum fermionic system, valid for initial states with bounded local particle number and energy moments.

Conclusion: This result removes the principal technical obstacle preventing use of the locality toolbox—including light-cone dynamics, area laws, and efficient simulation methods—on physically relevant continuum quantum systems.

Abstract: The central difficulty in quantum many-body physics is that the Hilbert space dimension grows exponentially with the total degrees of freedom. A key feature to circumvent this problem is locality, which manifests dynamically through an approximate causal structure in the form of a Lieb-Robinson bound (LRB). LRBs have been proved for quantum spin systems and certain lattice boson systems, but so far never for realistic continuum systems. We present the first LRB that applies to a standard system of one-dimensional continuum fermions. It applies to initial states with controlled moments of local particle number and energy and is thus thermodynamically stable. Our result breaks the main technical barrier that has existed for using the vast locality toolbox on continuum systems.

</details>


### [65] [Transformations of Hilbert Module Reference Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11683)
*Lucus Brady*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper gives a functorial characterization of Hilbert module classical and quantum reference frames, constructs a category for equivariant couplings and such frames, proves the frame-system construction is functorial, and identifies a unitary groupoid governing frame transformations—illustrated in examples including the two-state switch and Hilbert spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously formalize classical and quantum reference frames within the framework of Hilbert modules using category theory, enabling systematic treatment of symmetry, equivariance, and frame transformations in quantum dynamics.

Method: Functorial categorical methods: defining functors between categories of equivariant couplings and Hilbert module reference frames; constructing a category whose objects are equivariant couplings and morphisms encode compatibility with frame structure; deriving frame transformation groupoids via unitarity constraints on intertwiners.

Result: A functorial correspondence between equivariant coupling data and Hilbert module reference frames; existence of a 'unitary' groupoid encoding admissible frame transformations for a fixed Hilbert module dynamical system; explicit examples demonstrating the groupoid in the indistinguishable two-state classical switch and standard Hilbert space settings.

Conclusion: Hilbert module reference frames admit a natural, symmetry-respecting categorical formulation via equivariant couplings, and their transformations are governed by a unitary groupoid—providing a unified, structural foundation for both classical and quantum reference frame theory.

Abstract: In this paper, we provide a functorial characterization of Hilbert module classical and quantum reference frames. Using the functorial characterization, we construct a category for equivariant couplings and Hilbert module reference frames. We prove the construction of the reference frame system from the equivariant coupling data yields a functor. We also deduce a ``unitary" groupoid as frame transformations for Hilbert module reference frames of a fixed Hilbert module dynamical system. We provide some examples of these frame transformations in the case of the indistinguishable classical two state switch and in the case of Hilbert spaces.

</details>


### [66] [Certified finite-precision readout for $p$-adic kernel aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12254)
*Su Hu,Min-Soo Kim*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops finite-precision $p$-adic certificates for norm-based readouts, aggregate exponentials, and multiclass classifiers, enabling exact decisions (e.g., maximizer identification, classification) before full numerical resolution—leveraging non-archimedean valuation theory and yielding sharp precision thresholds, rational arithmetic algorithms, and perturbation bounds independent of summand count.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable mathematically exact downstream decisions (e.g., classification, ranking) in $p$-adic computation using only finite-precision approximations—avoiding costly full resolution while guaranteeing correctness via valuation-theoretic structure.

Method: Leverages $p$-adic valuation theory and non-archimedean analysis to derive rigorous finite-precision certificates: (1) a resolved-set condition fixing exact maximizers at precision $v_*+1$; (2) a centered-moment certificate for aggregates of $p$-adic exponentials with rational-arithmetic evaluation and explicit remainder bound; (3) a necessary and sufficient certification condition for multiclass norm-minimizing classifiers, with sharp threshold $v^{(2)}+1$; applied to discrete $p$-adic path integrals on affine chains.

Result: Exact total-variation identity separating unresolved $p$-adic mass from real rounding error; rational-arithmetic algorithm for exponential aggregates avoiding individual evaluations; aggregate perturbation bound free of summand-count factors; sharp precision threshold $v^{(2)}+1$ for multiclass classification ensuring correct decoding without resolving the winning discriminant; exact output-independence at zero effective quadratic coupling in path-integral actions.

Conclusion: Finite-precision $p$-adic approximations, when certified via valuation-aware conditions, suffice for mathematically exact norm-based inference—enabling efficient, provably correct decision-making in $p$-adic machine learning, statistical ranking, and quantum-inspired discrete path integrals.

Abstract: In non-archimedean numerical analysis, a verified $p$-adic approximation can determine an absolute value, and hence downstream probabilistic rankings and classification decisions, with mathematical exactness long before the underlying number is fully resolved. We turn this observation into rigorous finite-precision certificates for norm-based readouts of amplitude families. For a common error radius, a nonempty resolved set already fixes every exact maximizer, ties included, at the sharp integer absolute precision $v_*+1$, and conditioning on that set yields an exact total-variation identity separating unresolved $p$-adic mass from real rounding error. For aggregates of $p$-adic exponentials, we give a centered-moment certificate with an explicit remainder bound: on rational data it runs in exact rational arithmetic without individual exponential evaluations, attains its minimal bound at an active phase, and resolves every nonzero aggregate at high order; we also obtain an aggregate perturbation bound free of summand-count factors. For multiclass $p$-adic classifiers that decode by minimizing the norm of a class discriminant, we prove a necessary and sufficient certification condition and show that the sharp common precision threshold is $v^{(2)}+1$, governed by the second-largest discriminant valuation, so the winning discriminant need not itself be resolved. We apply these certificates to discrete $p$-adic path-integral actions on uniform affine chains, deriving explicit action centers and proving exact output-independence at zero effective quadratic coupling. Pseudocode and a reproducible reference implementation accompany the theory.

</details>


### [67] [On transition probabilities for the ASEP on the half-line](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12258)
*Alexandr Garbali,Cengiz Gazi,Jan de Gier,William Mead,Michael Wheeler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives and verifies a new multiple contour integral formula for the transition probability in the asymmetric simple exclusion process (ASEP) on the half-line with boundary particle insertion, using coordinate Bethe ansatz and symmetric rational functions; it also computes the conservation probability of particle number up to a given time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a second, novel multiple contour integral representation for the transition probability in the boundary-driven half-line ASEP—complementing an existing one—and to compute the probability of particle number conservation over time.

Method: Coordinate Bethe ansatz to verify that the proposed contour integral satisfies the ASEP evolution equation and initial condition; manipulation of symmetric rational functions and contour deformations for proofs.

Result: A new, rigorously verified multiple contour integral formula for the transition probability; an exact integral expression for the probability that the particle number remains unchanged up to a given time.

Conclusion: Two distinct contour integral representations exist for the transition probability in this boundary ASEP model; the new formula is analytically validated and enables exact computation of particle-number conservation probability.

Abstract: We study the asymmetric simple exclusion process on the half-line with particle insertion and no particle removal at the boundary. We point out that there exist two distinct multiple contour integral formulas for the transition probability between two finite particle configurations containing the same number of particles. We recall one of these formulas from the literature and prove a second, new formula by showing that it satisfies the evolution equation using a coordinate Bethe ansatz calculation and verify that the initial condition is implemented by the contour integrals. As an application we sum this formula over all final configurations and obtain an integral formula for the probability that the number of particles is conserved up to a given time. The proofs use symmetric rational functions and their properties together with contour deformations.

</details>


### [68] [An Algebraic Foundation of Rate-Product Style Quantity Calculus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12428)
*Dan Jonsson*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Extends quantity calculus using scalable monoid-heaps to handle non-multiplicable quantities, weak rates, and products, with applications in metrology and physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize quantity calculus beyond the limitations of traditional frameworks—specifically, to accommodate quantities that cannot be multiplied and to formally incorporate weak rates and products.

Method: Introduces and employs the novel algebraic structure of a scalable (right) monoid-heap to reformulate and extend the existing quantity calculus framework.

Result: A generalized quantity calculus capable of representing non-multiplicable quantities, weak rates, and products; validated through concrete examples in metrology and physics.

Conclusion: The scalable monoid-heap provides a robust and flexible foundation for modern quantity calculus, enhancing its applicability to diverse physical and metrological contexts.

Abstract: Using the new notion of a scalable (right) monoid-heap, the quantity calculus in [1] is generalized to include quantities that may not be possible to multiply as well as both (weak) rates and products of quantities. Several examples are given of applications in metrology and physics

</details>


### [69] [The finite projective classification of complex spinors via momentum maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12549)
*Bartłomiej Sikorski,Adam Sawicki*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies projective orbits of complex spinors using momentum map geometry, constructing critical-point representatives in the Clifford–Fock model and leveraging Vinberg’s graded Lie algebra theory to establish orbit completeness and closure order—especially in dimension 14.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, geometric classification of projective spinor orbits via symplectic/momentum map methods, bypassing classical case-by-case approaches and enabling computation of orbit dimensions and closure relations.

Method: Uses the momentum map for the spin group action on spinor modules; constructs orbit representatives as critical points of its squared norm in the Clifford–Fock model; applies Vinberg's theory of graded Lie algebras and stabilizer analysis to prove completeness and determine orbit closures.

Result: Recovers classical orbit counts (e.g., 1,1,1,2,3,5 in odd dimensions; 1,1,1,2,2,4,9 in even dimensions), computes projective dimensions of all orbits, and—newly—determines the full orbit closure order in dimension 14 via monotonicity of the squared momentum norm.

Conclusion: Momentum map geometry provides a powerful, uniform framework for spinor orbit classification, yielding structural insights (dimensions, closures) beyond classical enumerations and validating Vinberg-theoretic methods as independent foundations.

Abstract: We describe the projective orbits of complex spinors from the point of view of the momentum map. We treat the irreducible spin modules in odd dimensions 1,3,5,7,9,11 and the half-spin modules in even dimensions 2,4,6,8,10,12,14, which are the only cases with finitely many orbits. In the Clifford--Fock model, we construct representatives as sums of pure basis spinors, and the coefficients are chosen so that the representative is a critical point of the squared norm of the momentum map. The resulting momentum values in a fixed Weyl chamber distinguish the orbits. Completeness follows from Vinberg's theory of graded Lie algebras and stabilizer actions, independently of the classical spinor classifications. We recover the classical counts of 1,1,1,2,3,5 orbits in the odd cases and 1,1,1,2,2,4,9 in the even cases, and compute the projective dimension of every orbit. In dimension fourteen we also determine the closure order of the orbits, using the fact that the minimum of the squared momentum norm increases strictly under degeneration.

</details>


### [70] [One-Particle Resonances and Eigenstate Thermalization in Periodic Free-Fermion Lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12701)
*Tristan Benoist,Vojkan Jakšić,Claude-Alain Pillet,Anna Szczepanek,Cornelia Vogel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal bounds on particle-number fluctuations in eigenstates of a free-fermion model on a d-dimensional periodic lattice, showing variance scales as NL^{d−1} and relative fluctuations decay as L^{d−1}/(Nη²), with optimality proven via resonance analysis and Hilbert–Schmidt estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify eigenstate thermalization for particle number in free fermionic systems, specifically how strongly individual many-body eigenstates deviate from macroscopic expectations—addressing whether and how ETH holds for extensive observables in non-interacting settings.

Method: Decomposes the centered particle-number operator into zero-transfer and nonzero-transfer components w.r.t. one-particle energy differences; controls the former via Hilbert–Schmidt norm estimation, the latter using a sharp resonance bound derived from Mann’s theorem on linear relations among roots of unity.

Result: Proves an eigenstate particle-number variance bound of order NL^{d−1}; corresponding relative fluctuation bound is L^{d−1}/(Nη²); both are shown to be optimal—achieved by explicit many-body eigenstates—and the maximal nonzero-gap multiplicity is Θ(L^{d−1}).

Conclusion: The free-fermion cubic lattice exhibits weak but quantifiable eigenstate thermalization for particle number: fluctuations vanish in the thermodynamic limit at fixed density, yet slower than volume scaling—scaling with surface area—and the bounds are tight across all dimensions.

Abstract: We study eigenstate fluctuations of the particle number in the nearest-neighbour free-fermion model on the periodic cubic lattice $(\mathbb{Z}/L\mathbb{Z})^d$. For every fixed dimension, every particle number, every subvolume, and every normalized energy eigenstate, we prove a bound of order $NL^{d-1}$ for the particle-number variance. The corresponding eigenstate-thermalization estimate is of order $L^{d-1}/(N\eta^2)$ for fluctuations of relative size $\eta$. In particular, at positive particle density the bound decays as the inverse linear size. The proof decomposes the centered particle-number operator according to one-particle energy transfer and treats the zero- and nonzero-transfer components separately. The nonzero-transfer part is controlled by a sharp resonance bound, proved from Mann's theorem on relations among roots of unity, while the zero-transfer part is estimated by a Hilbert-Schmidt argument. These bounds are optimal in every dimension: the maximal nonzero-gap multiplicity has order $L^{d-1}$, and suitable many-body eigenstates realize matching variance lower bounds and show that the concentration bound cannot be improved in estimates uniform in the tolerance parameter.

</details>


### [71] [Global Asymptotics of a Toda Soliton Condensate with Random Scattering Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12748)
*Guido Mazzuca,Mateusz Piorkowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a dense N-soliton condensate for the infinite Toda lattice in the N→∞ limit, using random scattering data (eigenvalues and norming constants as independent random variables), and establishes deterministic macroscopic behavior via probabilistic Riemann–Hilbert analysis and Deift–Zhou steepest descent, revealing five finite-gap regimes with nonlinear regime boundaries and deriving nonlinear dispersion relations linked to the density of states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend soliton gas theory beyond deterministic or infinitesimal norming constant assumptions by treating both eigenvalues and norming constants as independent random variables — allowing arbitrarily large norming constants under a mild moment condition — and to characterize the emergent macroscopic dynamics of the Toda lattice in this dense, stochastic regime.

Method: Develops a probabilistic Riemann–Hilbert problem framework to prove self-averaging; applies Deift–Zhou nonlinear steepest descent to the continuum limit; derives Nonlinear Dispersion Relations; uses ensemble averaging and spectral asymptotics tied to the soliton gas density of states.

Result: 1) Random Toda variables converge deterministically to an effective continuum model at rate O(N^{−δ}), 0 < δ < 1/2; 2) Full leading-order macroscopic solution decomposes into five explicit finite-gap regimes (elliptic and genus-2 modulated waves) with O(1/ln N) error; 3) Regime interfaces are nonlinear curves (not straight characteristics); 4) Nonlinear Dispersion Relations are established; 5) Macroscopic averages are determined by the soliton gas density of states.

Conclusion: The dense random Toda soliton condensate exhibits universal deterministic macroscopic structure governed by finite-gap solutions and nonlinear dispersion, robust against large initial randomness — demonstrating self-averaging and providing a rigorous stochastic foundation for soliton gas hydrodynamics.

Abstract: We study a dense $N$-soliton condensate for the infinite Toda lattice in the limit $N \to \infty$. In contrast to previous mathematical studies of soliton gases where the norming constants are assumed to be deterministic or infinitesimal, we consider initial scattering data where both the discrete eigenvalues and the norming constants are independent random variables. The norming constants $\gamma_j$ are allowed to be arbitrarily large under a mild moment condition $\mathbb{E}[\gamma_j^\nu] < \infty$ ($\nu > 1$). Our main result is divided into two parts. First, we develop a probabilistic Riemann--Hilbert framework to show that the system self-averages: despite large, independent fluctuations in the initial data, the random Toda lattice variables converge deterministically to an effective continuum model at a polynomial rate $\mathcal{O}(N^{-\delta})$ for $0 < \delta < 1/2$. Second, by performing the Deift--Zhou nonlinear steepest descent analysis of the continuum problem, we obtain the complete leading-order macroscopic description of the lattice across the entire space-time plane. The solution decomposes into five distinct finite-gap regimes, characterized explicitly by modulated elliptic and genus-2 waves with an error of order $\mathcal{O}(1/\ln N)$. In contrast to the straight characteristic rays typical of standard soliton gas limits, the interfaces separating these five macroscopic regimes in the space-time plane are genuinely non-linear curves; in particular, the boundary of the modulated genus-2 region is completely non-linear. Finally, under our assumptions, we establish the Nonlinear Dispersion Relations for the Toda soliton condensate and prove that the macroscopic ensemble averages are determined by the soliton gas density of states.

</details>


### [72] [Eigenvector Occupation Statistics and Extremes in the Rosenzweig-Porter Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12858)
*Yan V. Fyodorov,Mariya Shcherbina,Tatyana Shcherbina*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes eigenvector delocalization and occupation statistics in the GUE Rosenzweig-Porter model at intermediate disorder (1 < γ < 2), deriving exact finite-N projective density, proving Lorentzian self-consistent profile with resonant size K_N ~ N^{2−γ}, exponential fluctuations, a q = 1/2 transition in inverse participation ratios, two-branch amplitude spectrum, Poisson limits for extremal components (Gumbel for largest, exponential for smallest), and absence of freezing for fixed-q IPRs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize eigenvector spatial structure—especially occupation probabilities, inverse participation ratios, and extremal statistics—in the critical regime of the Rosenzweig-Porter model where delocalization coexists with strong fluctuations (1 < γ < 2), bridging ergodic and localized phases.

Method: Conditional spectral analysis using normalized one-eigenpair Kac-Rice formula; derivation of exact finite-N projective density; reduction to Hubbard-Stratonovich integral over 2×2 Hermitian matrices; saddle-point asymptotics; quenched analysis on a high-probability regularity event for diagonal disorder.

Result: Exact asymptotic distribution of occupation probabilities p_i = |u_i|²: self-consistent Lorentzian profile with effective resonant size K_N(E) ≍ N^{2−γ}; local exponential fluctuations; precise asymptotics of all generalized inverse participation sums ∑p_i^q with phase transition at q = 1/2; two-branch thickened amplitude-counting spectrum; Poisson point process limits for both largest (Gumbel) and smallest (exponential, scale N^{−1−γ}) components; no freezing for any fixed q > 0.

Conclusion: In the intermediate disorder regime (1 < γ < 2), bulk eigenvectors exhibit nontrivial multifractal-like statistics with scale-dependent resonance, yet remain fully delocalized without freezing—demonstrating a robust, analytically tractable example of non-ergodic extended states.

Abstract: We study the occupation probabilities $p_i=|u_i|^2$ of a normalized bulk eigenvector in the GUE Rosenzweig-Porter model $A_N=B_N+\tau_NG_N$, with $\tau_N^2=N^{1-\gamma}$ and $1<\gamma<2$. Conditioning on an eigenvalue at a regular bulk energy $E$ through the normalized one-eigenpair Kac-Rice law, we derive an exact finite-$N$ projective density and reduce its asymptotic analysis to a Hubbard-Stratonovich integral over $2\times2$ Hermitian matrices, which we evaluate by the saddle-point method. The resulting occupation field is governed by a self-consistent Lorentzian profile with effective resonant size $K_N(E)\asymp N^{2-\gamma}$ and local exponential fluctuations. We prove the asymptotics of all generalized inverse participation sums and identify a transition at $q=1/2$. We also obtain a two-branch thickened amplitude-counting spectrum. At the two ends of the occupation field we prove marked Poisson point-process limits: the largest components have Gumbel fluctuations, whereas the smallest component has an exponential limit on the scale $N^{-1-\gamma}$. Finally, for every fixed $q>0$ the largest component contributes an asymptotically vanishing fraction of the inverse participation sum, showing that these fixed-$q$ statistics do not freeze. The statements are quenched, uniformly on a high-probability regularity event for the diagonal disorder, and hence also hold in the annealed setting.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [73] [Black-hole ringdown at an exceptional point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10661)
*Kyriakos Destounis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies damped oscillations (ringdown) of rotating, charged black holes in de Sitter space, focusing on exceptional points where photon-sphere and near-horizon modes coalesce; it computes the amplitude of a linear-in-time response term arising at such points, demonstrates high-accuracy mode-fitting (0.00383% error for mode pair), and shows that tuning the initial perturbation—without altering geometry—can suppress the linear term and shift the inferred frequency from short ringdown fits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how black hole ringdown behavior changes near exceptional points—where quasinormal modes coalesce—and to quantify the impact of the resulting algebraic (linear time) growth on gravitational waveform modeling and parameter inference.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of constrained Einstein-Maxwell solutions for Kerr-Newman-de Sitter black holes; computation of quasinormal mode spectra including photon-sphere and near-horizon families; high-precision fitting of late-time curvature signals with damped oscillatory + linear-time ansätze; controlled variation of initial data to isolate and suppress the linear term.

Result: The linear-in-time term’s amplitude is explicitly computed; with seven modes included, the fit residual for the coalescing mode pair is 0.00383%, versus 0.01401% for a pure exponential fit; the linear term can be suppressed by tuning initial data, causing measurable shifts in fitted frequency over short ringdown durations.

Conclusion: Near exceptional points, black hole ringdown exhibits non-exponential (algebraic) transients governed by initial-data-dependent coefficients; this implies that short-duration ringdown analyses may misestimate frequencies unless mode coalescence and associated algebraic terms are properly accounted for—highlighting limitations of standard exponential QNM models in critical parameter regimes.

Abstract: Black holes ring in damped oscillations. At an exceptional point, photon-sphere and near-horizon modes merge, and their response can acquire a linear time factor. We compute its amplitude from constrained Einstein-Maxwell data for a rotating, charged black hole in de Sitter space. With seven competing modes fixed, the late-time curvature error is 0.00383$\%$ for the pair and 0.01401$\%$ for a refitted exponential. We suppress the linear term by tuning the initial disturbance at fixed geometry. Hence, near coalescence, changing only the initial disturbance can shift the frequency inferred from a short ringdown fit.

</details>


### [74] [Quasinormal Modes of Black holes in the generalized Schwarzschild-de Sitter solution of $f(R)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11895)
*Dhruba Jyoti Gogoi,Himanshu Chaudhary,Tiberiu Harko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of scalar, vector, and axial tensor perturbations in a static spherically symmetric black hole solution of f(R) gravity — a modification of general relativity featuring linear and logarithmic metric corrections. Using high-order WKB and analytical methods, the authors find that QNMs can closely resemble Schwarzschild ones for suitable parameter choices; parameters A and η induce nonlinear and sometimes opposing effects on oscillation frequency and damping rate, with scalar perturbations showing distinct behavior at large A. Contour plots and time-domain waveforms corroborate the analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how deviations from general relativity — specifically in f(R) gravity with a Schwarzschild-type black hole solution containing linear and logarithmic corrections — affect gravitational-wave ringdown signatures via quasinormal modes of different perturbation types.

Method: Higher-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approximation combined with an analytical approach to compute quasinormal modes for scalar, vector, and axial tensor perturbations; contour plotting in parameter space; time-domain integration to generate gravitational wave profiles.

Result: QNMs can closely match Schwarzschild values for appropriate parameter choices; parameter A induces a nonlinear increase in both real (oscillation) and imaginary (damping) parts of QNMs; parameter η has A-dependent effects — similar to A when A is small, but opposite when A is large, especially for scalar perturbations; scalar perturbations show qualitatively distinct behavior relative to vector and axial tensor cases at large A.

Conclusion: The f(R) black hole model under study produces observable, parameter-sensitive deviations in gravitational-wave ringdown spectra, particularly for scalar perturbations, offering potential avenues for testing modified gravity with future gravitational-wave observations.

Abstract: We analyze scalar, vector, and {axial} tensor perturbations in a Schwarzschild-type static spherically symmetric solution of modified $f(R)$ gravity. This solution differs from the Schwarzschild metric of general relativity through the presence of a linearly growing term and a logarithmic correction, and it depends on four integration constants. Using the higher-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approximation and an analytical method, we calculate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) for all three types of perturbations. Our results show that, for appropriately chosen parameter values, the QNMs closely match those of the Schwarzschild black hole. In particular, the model parameter $A$ produces a nonlinear increase in both the oscillation frequency and damping rate of ringdown gravitational waves. A similar effect arises for the parameter $\eta$ when $A$ is small, while for larger $A$ values the effects are opposite, where the scalar perturbations display a distinct deviation compared to the vector and {axial} tensor cases. Contour plots of QNMs in the parameter space support these findings. Finally, time-domain profiles are obtained to illustrate the dynamical evolution of the gravitational wave signal.

</details>


### [75] [Analogue Gravity: the Subcritical Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10006)
*F.Belgiorno,S.L. Cacciatori,A.Viganò*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically investigates quantum pair creation in subcritical analogue white holes with subluminal dispersion, using the Corley model and overbarrier scattering; it reveals that complex turning points enable mode conversion and non-Planckian pair creation without Hawking partners, and identifies a critical regime where pair creation vanishes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether quantum pair creation can occur in analogue gravity systems without horizons—specifically in the subcritical white hole configuration—and to resolve inconsistencies between naive WKB predictions and numerical/phenomenological results in the literature.

Method: Analytical treatment of the wave equation in weakly dispersive media using the Corley dispersion model; application of quantum-mechanical overbarrier scattering formalism; careful analysis of complex turning points and their role in mode conversion beyond standard WKB.

Result: Demonstration of non-Planckian pair creation in the subcritical white hole case, enabled by complex turning points inducing mode conversion absent in naive WKB; identification of a critical regime between subcritical and transcritical cases where pair creation is suppressed.

Conclusion: Pair creation in subcritical analogue white holes is analytically viable and physically meaningful despite absence of horizons or Hawking partners; complex turning point analysis is essential for capturing mode conversion; the critical regime acts as a transition boundary with zero particle production.

Abstract: We focus on the subcritical case in Analogue Gravity for a subluminal dispersion relation, and investigate the problem of pair creation in the absence of an horizon analytically, for the subcritical white hole case. We limit our attention to weakly dispersive media. In particular, we take into account for simplicity the Corley model and adopt an approach inspired by quantum mechanics in the so-called overbarrier scattering. The presence of complex turning points is shown to play a fundamental role in allowing a mode conversion which would be absent in a naive WKB approach. We show that there is still a pair creation phenomenon, which, in the subcritical case, is non-Planckian and without a Hawking partner. Our fully analytical results are qualitatively in agreement with the literature at hand. Furthermore we confirm the existence of a critical regime betwen the subcritical and the transcritical ones where no pair creation occurs.

</details>


### [76] [Shallow-water black holes and lattice superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10539)
*Antonino Flachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates superradiance in a rotating shallow-water vortex analogue black hole with a topographic sill defining the horizon, experimentally separating horizon-driven energy extraction from viscous dissipation and confirming theoretical predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate the role of the event horizon in superradiance from dissipative effects (e.g., viscosity) in fluid analogues of black holes, which has been experimentally challenging.

Method: Realisation of a rotating lattice fluid analogue black hole using a shallow-water vortex with a topographic sill setting a drain-anchored, supercritical horizon; measurement of wave energy flux across the horizon under varying lattice viscosities and wave frequencies; comparison with non-rotating and counter-rotating cases.

Result: Superradiant energy extraction (outward flux) is observed only for co-rotating waves with pattern frequency below the horizon’s angular velocity; flux direction reverses otherwise; horizon-driven extraction dominates at low frequencies, while viscous dissipation inside the corotation radius contributes significantly near the band edge—halving the extraction at operational viscosity and vanishing at lowest viscosity.

Conclusion: The topographic-sill-defined horizon acts as an inviscid absorber enabling clean observation of superradiance; this setup disentangles horizon physics from bulk dissipation and provides a scalable, low-cost platform for analogue gravity studies.

Abstract: Rotating black holes can amplify waves that scatter off them, a process known as superradiance that fluid analogues bring into the laboratory. Separating the horizon's role from dissipation in the surrounding fluid remains difficult. Here we realise a rotating analogue black hole in a lattice fluid: a shallow-water vortex whose horizon is set by a topographic sill, so that the only sink lies behind a supercritical layer that lets nothing out. We measure superradiance as energy extraction: the flux at the horizon is outward for co-rotating waves whose pattern rotates more slowly than the horizon, and inward for faster waves, counter-rotating waves and a non-rotating hole. A scan over three lattice viscosities separates the extraction at the horizon, which is inviscid, from that of the viscous fluid inside the corotation radius: well below the band edge the horizon is the absorber; near the edge the viscous share is half at the operating viscosity and vanishes at the lowest. A drain-anchored horizon, as in water-tank experiments, gives the same signs. Our analysis gives insight into the mechanism of superradiance and offers a cheap, flexible platform for designing analogue-gravity experiments.

</details>


### [77] [Engineering a digital acoustic black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10540)
*Antonino Flachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates the realization of an analogue black hole using a synchronous digital circuit implemented on an FPGA, defining and measuring key gravitational analogues—including sound speed, flow, horizon, causal structure, ringdown, and superradiance—while showing exponential information leakage from within the digital horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish digital circuits as a reconfigurable, experimentally accessible platform for simulating gravitational phenomena—particularly black hole physics—by mapping spacetime geometry onto discrete, causal logic structures.

Method: Design and implementation of a synchronous digital circuit on an FPGA that emulates a 1+1D acoustic black hole; mapping continuum concepts (sound speed, flow velocity, horizon) to logic-level parameters (clock rates, signal propagation delays, bit-flip dynamics); experimental probing via signal generator and oscilloscope to measure horizon stability, causal cone, quasinormal ringdown, and rotational superradiance.

Result: Experimental confirmation of horizon stability, causal structure, exponentially damped ringdown modes, and rotational superradiance; observation that a single bit flip inside the digital horizon leaks out with amplitude decaying exponentially with logical depth—mirroring quantum tunneling or Hawking-like emission in analogue gravity.

Conclusion: Digital circuits constitute a novel, scalable, and programmable class of analogue gravitational systems; this work realizes a black hole as a cellular automaton, bridging digital logic, condensed-matter analogue gravity, and fundamental spacetime concepts.

Abstract: We show how to design and build an analogue black hole as a synchronous digital circuit. We define the sound speed, the flow and the horizon of such a circuit in terms of its own logic, and realise one on a field-programmable gate array, where the black hole is probed electrically with a signal generator and an oscilloscope. We measure the stability of the horizon, its causal cone, the ringdown and rotational superradiance. A single flipped bit inside the horizon leaks out by an amount that falls exponentially with depth. Digital circuits thus form a reconfigurable class of gravitational analogues and realise an analogue black hole as a cellular automaton.

</details>


### [78] [ERG, axions, and a PT-symmetric accelerating Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10584)
*Nick E. Mavromatos,Sarben Sarkar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper proposes that a repulsive gravitational phase—potentially explaining cosmic acceleration without dark energy—may arise from a PT-symmetric gravitational theory with anomalous Chern-Simons-axion couplings, where an infrared phase transition (signaled by ERG beta-function singularities) shifts the theory from Hermitian to PT-symmetric gravity with negative effective Newton's constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain late-time cosmic acceleration without invoking dark energy, by exploring whether non-Hermitian but PT-symmetric gravity—motivated by string-inspired axion–Chern–Simons couplings—can yield repulsive gravity.

Method: Uses the Exact Renormalisation Group (ERG) in modified local potential approximation to analyze beta functions of chiral anomalous axion–photon couplings; identifies singularities as indicators of infrared phase transitions from Hermitian to PT-symmetric gravitational dynamics.

Result: ERG analysis suggests singularities in beta functions may trigger a transition to a PT-symmetric gravitational phase with an effectively repulsive (negative) Newton's constant, offering a geometric origin for cosmic acceleration.

Conclusion: A PT-symmetric extension of gravity, emergent via ERG-driven phase transition in axionic systems with chiral anomalies, provides a viable alternative to dark energy for explaining observed cosmic acceleration.

Abstract: In conventional quantum mechanics, the Hamiltonian is required to be Hermitian, ensuring real energy eigenvalues. Certain non-Hermitian Hamiltonians can also possess entirely real spectra when they are invariant under the combined action of parity ($\mathcal P$) and time reversal ($\mathcal T$). Such Hamiltonians are called unbroken parity--time ($\mathcal{PT}$)-symmetric. We conjecture that, in a string-inspired scenario, a repulsive phase of gravity may be due to a $\mathcal{PT}$-symmetric gravitational theory, characterised by anomalous Chern-Simons terms, coupled to axion fields. The conjecture is based on the behaviour of the chiral anomalous couplings of these axions to electromagnetic potentials. Within the Exact Renormalisation Group (ERG) framework in the modified local potential approximation, we argue that singularities in the ERG beta functions for such couplings may indicate an infrared phase transition. At the energy scales of these singularities, we conjecture that the field theory transitions from the Hermitian phase to a ${\mathcal P}{\mathcal T}$-- symmetric theory, of gravity, characterised by a repulsive Netwon's ``constant''. This may induce the acceleration observed in the Universe at late eras, without the need for a dark--energy component.

</details>


### [79] [Scalar-tensor reconstruction of coherent quantum black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10616)
*R. Casadio,A. Giusti,R. da Rocha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs scalar-tensor and Horndeski realisations of spherically symmetric black hole geometries emerging from quantum coherent states with finite core and extension; it identifies domain restrictions on kinetic positivity and shows semiclassical gravity cannot fully capture the quantum-originated interior/exterior geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge quantum-inspired black hole geometries (with finite core and extension) with classical effective field theory descriptions, and assess the limits of semiclassical gravity in capturing quantum-originated structure.

Method: Reconstruction of scalar-tensor theories (non-minimal coupling, kinetic coupling, scalar potential) and Horndeski theory from a given spherically symmetric metric; analysis of kinetic term sign, domain of validity, and horizon/interior-exterior coverage.

Result: A consistent scalar-tensor realisation is found where the kinetic term is positive only in finite radial domains, linking core size and extension to domain boundaries; an equivalent Horndeski reconstruction with derivative coupling is also obtained, but both fail to include the event horizon or simultaneously reproduce arbitrarily large interior and exterior regions.

Conclusion: The quantum-coherent origin of the black hole geometry lies partially beyond the reach of semiclassical scalar-tensor or Horndeski descriptions — indicating intrinsic limitations of such effective theories in recovering full quantum gravitational structure.

Abstract: We construct a scalar-tensor realisation of spherically symmetric black hole geometries that effectively arise from quantum coherent states characterised by a finite core and finite extension. We determine the non-minimal coupling to the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian, kinetic coupling, and scalar potential and show that the kinetic term is positive only in finite domains. For fixed black hole mass, the boundaries of these domains provide a natural mechanism that relates the core size and spatial extension to the dynamics of the scalar-tensor realisation. We further show that the same geometry can also be obtained from a Horndeski action with derivative coupling and determine its domain of validity. Neither reconstruction includes the event horizon nor can it simultaneously reproduce arbitrarily large portions of the interior and exterior of the black hole, whose quantum origin therefore appears to remain partially outside the regime of applicability of semiclassical theories of gravity.

</details>


### [80] [The Energy-Momentum Tensor of a Compact Object in General Relativity to all Multipole Orders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10783)
*Trevor Scheopner,Felix Lichtner*
