Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a natural basis of independent tensors conjugate to individual multipole moments of compact objects in general relativity, enabling a novel exact distributional expression for the energy-momentum tensor in terms of reduced multipole moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve degeneracy among spin and tidal operators in effective field theory descriptions of compact object motion, where multiple operators affect the same observable or multipole moment.

Method: Uses the Mathisson–Papapetrou–Dixon formalism to construct a canonical basis of independent tensors, each conjugate to exactly one multipole moment, and derives an exact distributional representation of the energy-momentum tensor.

Result: A natural, independent tensor basis is constructed; a novel exact distributional formula for the energy-momentum tensor is obtained in terms of reduced multipole moments and these tensors.

Conclusion: The proposed basis eliminates operator degeneracy and provides a clean, canonical mapping between multipole structure and spacetime geometry, improving the theoretical foundation for modeling compact object dynamics in gravitational wave physics.

Abstract: In effective field theory approaches to compact object motion in general relativity, spin and tidal effects are encoded by operators formed from the Riemann tensor and its derivatives. Many such operators contribute degenerately to observables, with a single one often affecting several multipole moments of the body. Using the Mathisson--Papapetrou--Dixon formalism, we construct a natural basis of independent tensors which are each canonically conjugate to precisely one multipole. We find the novel exact distributional formula for the energy-momentum tensor of a compact object in terms of its reduced multipole moments and these tensors.

</details>


### [81] [Variational Approach to the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon Equations of Motion for Extended Objects in General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10794)
*Trevor Scheopner,Felix Lichtner*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reviews the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (MPD) equations for finite-size objects in curved spacetime, constructs them from a manifestly gauge-invariant variational principle suitable for effective descriptions of compact objects, analyzes enforcement conditions for the covariant spin supplementary condition, and identifies the natural curvature-tensor basis conjugate to multipole moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous, gauge-invariant foundation for the MPD formalism—essential for modeling relativistic compact objects (e.g., black holes, neutron stars) as extended bodies with arbitrary multipole structure—in contexts like gravitational-wave modeling and effective field theory.

Method: Systematic derivation of the MPD equations from a manifestly diffeomorphism- and worldline-reparametrization-invariant action principle; analysis of constraints via the covariant spin supplementary condition (SSC); identification of curvature-multipole conjugacy using symmetry-adapted tensor bases and multipole expansion techniques.

Result: A fully gauge-invariant variational formulation of the MPD theory is established; necessary and sufficient conditions for consistent SSC enforcement are clarified; the natural curvature-tensor basis (e.g., electric/magnetic parts of Riemann and its derivatives) conjugate to mass-type and current-type multipole moments is explicitly identified.

Conclusion: The MPD framework can be robustly derived from first principles via a gauge-invariant action, enabling systematic effective descriptions of compact objects with controlled multipolar structure; this strengthens theoretical foundations for gravitational self-force, tidal response, and waveform modeling.

Abstract: The exact motion of a finite size object in curved spacetime is captured by the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon equations. They associate a unique worldline, momentum, and spin to that object and determine the evolution of such in terms of its fully independent multipole moments. We provide a thorough review of this theory and show how to build it directly via a manifestly gauge invariant variational principle well-suited for effective descriptions of relativistic compact objects. We discuss conditions under which the covariant spin supplementary condition can be enforced and identify the natural basis of curvature tensors conjugate to the object's multipole moments.

</details>


### [82] [Thermodynamic, Optical, and Wave Signatures of Charged Letelier AdS Black Holes with PFDM and Quintessence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10942)
*B. Eslam Panah,S. Gurtas Dogan,A. Guvendi,O. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a 4D charged Letelier–AdS black hole with string cloud, PFDM, and Kiselev quintessence; it computes thermodynamic quantities, horizon properties, photon sphere, shadow, and scalar wave scattering, but cautions that key physical interpretations (e.g., mass, quasinormal modes) require additional asymptotic or spectral analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the combined gravitational effects of exotic matter components—string cloud, PFDM, and quintessence—on black-hole thermodynamics, geometry, and wave propagation in AdS spacetime, while carefully delineating what can be rigorously inferred from the phenomenological metric alone.

Method: Analytic derivation of curvature invariants, horizon equation, temperature (via surface gravity), entropy (Bekenstein–Hawking), electric potential, horizon-based mass function; thermodynamic cycle analysis (rectangular benchmark); photon-sphere and shadow formalism for null geodesics; separation and radial Helmholtz equation for massless scalar field; identification of effective potentials, evanescent zones, and trapping regions.

Result: Explicit expressions for temperature, entropy, potential, and mass function; $W=0.095$ and $\eta\simeq15.39\%$ for a specific geometric-unit rectangular cycle; photon-sphere radius and shadow angle derived; scalar field yields frequency-dependent barriers whose barrier-top scale matches photon-sphere capture scale; however, PFDM density is negative for $\beta>0$, and horizon-derived mass lacks asymptotic charge/boundary justification.

Conclusion: The analysis provides a self-consistent, parameter-specific characterization of the adopted phenomenological black-hole geometry, yielding robust algebraic thermodynamic identities and geometric optics results—but physical interpretations requiring asymptotic mass definition, conserved charges, or quasinormal mode spectra remain undetermined without further analysis.

Abstract: We study a four-dimensional charged Letelier--anti-de Sitter (AdS) black-hole metric containing a cloud of strings, a phenomenological perfect-fluid-dark-matter (PFDM) source, and a Kiselev-type quintessence term. We derive the curvature invariants, horizon temperature, entropy, electric potential, and horizon-based mass function, and analyze local thermal response and the associated extended-phase-space identities. A rectangular benchmark cycle gives $W=0.095000$ and $\eta\simeq15.39\%$ for the explicitly stated geometric-unit parameters; these values are model- and cycle-specific and are not presented as a universal efficiency enhancement. For geometric optics, we derive the photon-sphere condition, critical impact parameter, and finite-radius shadow angle. For a massless, minimally coupled scalar field, we obtain a radial-coordinate Helmholtz equation and identify frequency-dependent barriers, evanescent intervals, and candidate trapping regions; the eikonal barrier-top scale approaches the photon-sphere capture scale. Two qualifications delimit the interpretation. First, with the stated PFDM stress-tensor convention, $\rho_{\rm PFDM}=-\beta/(8\pi r^3)$, so positive $\beta$ describes a negative-density effective source. Second, the mass function follows from the horizon equation, but identifying it with a conserved asymptotic AdS mass requires a separate charge and boundary-normalization analysis. The potential-well analysis likewise does not determine complex quasinormal frequencies. We therefore present the results as a consistent, parameter-specific analysis of the adopted phenomenological geometry, while distinguishing algebraic thermodynamic identities from conclusions requiring asymptotic-charge or spectral calculations.

</details>


### [83] [Nonlinear Scalarization of Reissner-Nordström Black Holes in Scalar-Torsion-Maxwell Teleparallel Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11686)
*Joaquín Robledo,Yerko Vásquez,P. A. González*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies nonlinear scalarization of Reissner-Nordström black holes in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity with polynomial nonminimal coupling to the Weitzenböck invariant; it demonstrates that a sixth-order coupling term enables dynamical formation of stationary scalar-hairy black holes via effective-potential minima, constructs asymptotically flat solutions classified by scalar field node number, and quantifies backreaction on metric and torsion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore scalarization mechanisms beyond linear instabilities—specifically, whether nonlinear effects alone can generate stable scalar hair on charged black holes in teleparallel gravity with nonminimal scalar–torsion coupling.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis: (i) derivation of field equations in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity with polynomial coupling functions; (ii) linear stability analysis to exclude tachyonic modes; (iii) nonlinear evolution of finite-amplitude scalar perturbations in the decoupling limit; (iv) full numerical integration of coupled Einstein–scalar–Maxwell–torsion equations to construct hairy black hole solutions; (v) classification by node number and parameter-space mapping of solution branches.

Result: A sixth-order coupling term induces nonlinear saturation via emergent local minima in the effective potential; asymptotically flat, charged black hole solutions with scalar hair are numerically constructed; multiple solution branches exist, labeled by node number; higher-order coupling suppresses higher-node branches; scalar backreaction measurably modifies metric functions and torsion scalar relative to Reissner-Nordström.

Conclusion: Nonlinear scalarization is viable in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity without linear tachyonic instabilities; higher-order coupling terms enable robust, stationary scalar hair on charged black holes, enriching the space of black hole solutions and offering new phenomenological signatures distinct from GR or standard scalar-tensor theories.

Abstract: We investigate the nonlinear scalarization of Reissner-Nordström black holes in scalar-torsion-Maxwell teleparallel gravity, in which the scalar field is nonminimally coupled to the Weitzenböck invariant. We consider polynomial coupling functions that avoid tachyonic instabilities at the linear level, thereby exploring a scalarization mechanism driven by nonlinear effects. In the decoupling limit, we study the nonlinear evolution of finite-amplitude scalar perturbations and show that the inclusion of a sixth-order coupling term allows the scalar field to dynamically evolve toward nontrivial stationary configurations. This behavior can be understood in terms of the effective potential, which develops nontrivial local minima that provide a mechanism for nonlinear saturation. By numerically solving the fully coupled field equations, we construct asymptotically flat charged black hole solutions endowed with scalar hair. We classify these solutions according to the number of nodes in the scalar field profile, identifying multiple branches whose structure and multiplicity depend on the coupling parameters. We further show that the higher-order coupling progressively suppresses higher-node branches and characterize the scalar backreaction through deviations of the metric functions and the torsion scalar from the Reissner-Nordström background.

</details>


### [84] [Direct product geometries in $n$-dimensional linear, quadratic, and conformal gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12483)
*Petr Jizba,Tereza Lehečková*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper classifies exact direct-product spacetime solutions in various higher-derivative gravity theories—including linear, pure quadratic-curvature, and higher-dimensional conformal gravity—focusing on dimensional splits like (2+m) and (2+...+2), allowing nonconstant curvature in 2D subspaces; it reveals algebraic vs. differential constraints on geometries, absence of standard static black holes in conformal gravity for n > 4, and provides explicit analytic solutions in 4D and 6D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify exact solutions of direct-product spacetimes in beyond-GR gravity theories—especially where conventional methods fail—and to understand how solution structure depends on theory-specific parameters (e.g., quadratic curvature couplings) and geometric assumptions (e.g., constant vs. nonconstant 2D curvature).

Method: Analytic classification of n-dimensional direct-product metrics M^{m1} × M^{m2} (and generalizations) under field equations of linear gravity, general pure quadratic-curvature gravity (aR² + bR_{μν}R^{μν} + cR_{μνρσ}R^{μνρσ}), and a higher-dimensional conformal gravity model; derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions (algebraic or ODE-based) for admissible geometries; analysis of conformal equivalence, Petrov classification, and electromagnetic compatibility.

Result: Complete classification of solution spaces: (i) metrics with only constant-curvature subspaces satisfy purely algebraic relations; (ii) those with nonconstant-curvature 2D parts obey either algebraic curvature-profile constraints or, in a special quadratic-gravity subclass, a second-order ODE; (iii) no standard static black-hole metrics (e.g., Schwarzschild–Tangherlini) exist in the direct-product sector of the considered conformal gravity for n > 4; (iv) explicit analytic solutions constructed in 4D and 6D.

Conclusion: Direct-product ansätze yield rich, theory-sensitive exact solution families in higher-derivative gravities; the presence/absence of nonconstant 2D curvature fundamentally alters constraint nature (algebraic ↔ differential); conformal gravity’s direct-product sector is highly restrictive for black-hole physics in D > 4, highlighting structural distinctions from GR and quadratic theories.

Abstract: Direct-product spacetimes offer a tractable route to exact solutions in gravity theories beyond general relativity. We study $n$-dimensional direct-product spacetimes of $(m_1+m_2)$, $(2+m)$, and $(2+\ldots+2)$ type in linear gravity, general pure quadratic-curvature gravity, and one of the recently proposed higher-dimensional extensions of conformal gravity, allowing the two-dimensional subspaces to have nonconstant curvature. We classify the resulting solution spaces and determine how the allowed geometries depend on the gravitational theory and, in quadratic gravity, on the curvature couplings. Metrics containing two-dimensional part(s) of nonconstant curvature are governed either by algebraic conditions on a curvature profile or, in one special class of quadratic gravity, by a differential equation. Relations governing metrics with only constant-curvature $m$-dimensional subspaces are purely algebraic. We further examine conformal relations to linear-gravity solutions, generalized Petrov types, and compatibility with electromagnetic sources. Particularly noteworthy is that standard static black-hole geometries are absent from the direct-product sector of the considered form of conformal gravity for $n>4$. Four- and six-dimensional cases are analyzed in detail, and explicit analytic solutions illustrating the resulting structure are presented whenever possible.

</details>


### [85] [Thermodynamics of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson black holes from background subtraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12683)
*Chang-Xu Yan,Zili Mou,Yu Tian,Hongbao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves gauge ambiguity in non-asymptotically flat charged rotating black holes in a Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field by uniquely fixing the time generator and horizon electric potential using an integrability condition and asymptotically flat initial conditions; it then computes mass and angular momentum via background subtraction against Minkowski spacetime, ensuring consistency with the first law, CR relation, and Smarr formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the gauge ambiguity in defining the time generator and horizon electric potential for non-asymptotically flat charged rotating black holes immersed in a Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field.

Method: Applies a newly established integrability condition with asymptotically flat charged rotating black holes as initial condition to uniquely fix the gauge; computes conserved quantities (mass and angular momentum) via background subtraction using Minkowski spacetime as reference.

Result: Gauge is uniquely fixed; mass and angular momentum are consistently derived; first law of black hole thermodynamics, Christodoulou-Ruffini (CR) relation, and Smarr formula are all satisfied.

Conclusion: The gauge ambiguity is resolved, and thermodynamic consistency is restored for such black holes, demonstrating that standard black hole mechanics extends robustly even in non-asymptotically flat settings with external magnetic fields.

Abstract: The non-asymptotic flatness of charged rotating black holes immersed in the Bertotti-Robinson magnetic field causes the ambiguity in the gauge choice for the time generator and the horizon electric potential. By using the newly established form of the integrability condition together with the gauge choice for the asymptotically flat charged rotating black holes as the initial condition, we have found that the gauge can be uniquely fixed. With this fixed gauge, we further obtain the mass and angular momentum by the background subtraction method with the Minkowski spacetime as the reference spacetime. As a result, not only is the first law of black hole thermodynamics automatically satisfied, but also both the CR relation and the Smarr formula are obeyed.

</details>


### [86] [Scalar quasinormal modes of the exact spinning Morris--Thorne wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12838)
*Mark Sukaiti,Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a massless scalar field on a rotating Morris-Thorne wormhole using high-precision spectral methods; it finds all converged modes are damped and lie outside the superradiant window, with asymptotic clustering near its upper edge governed by a limiting radial equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess linear stability of rotating traversable wormholes by analyzing scalar field QNMs on an exact spinning Morris-Thorne background—a two-ended, asymptotically rotating geometry where standard black hole techniques do not directly apply.

Method: Formulates the wave equation on the full two-ended spacetime, extracts axis behavior and outgoing boundary conditions at both infinities, compactifies the radial coordinate, and solves a quadratic eigenvalue problem via 2D Chebyshev collocation; uses analytic constraints from the exact background to validate spectra and employs independent numerical integration and Kerr QNM benchmarks for verification.

Result: All converged QNMs for m = 1,2,3 and j ≤ 19 are damped (Im ω < 0) and lie outside the superradiant window 0 < ω/(mΩ₋) < 1; frequencies exhibit mirror symmetry in Re ω̂ about 3πjm/2; multipole dependence reduces to mj only; low-lying modes are numerically verified; large-mj asymptotics follow ω̂ ∼ 3πjm/2 + O((mj)⁻¹ᐟ²).

Conclusion: No unstable (exponentially growing) scalar QNMs are found within the computed parameter range, suggesting modal stability for these sectors; however, full stability requires analysis of unresolved parts of the scalar spectrum and coupled metric-matter perturbations.

Abstract: We study the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a minimally coupled massless scalar field on the exact spinning Morris-Thorne wormhole. The problem is posed on the complete two-ended geometry, whose asymptotic rest frames rotate relative to each other. After extracting the axis behaviour and the outgoing phases at both infinities, we compactify the radial coordinate and construct a quadratic eigenvalue problem by two-dimensional Chebyshev collocation. The analytic background yields exact constraints on the spectrum. At fixed azimuthal number $m$, the frequencies $\widehat{\omega}=r_0\omega$ are mirror symmetric about $\operatorname{Re}\widehat{\omega}=3\pi jm/2$, where $r_0$ is the throat radius and $j=J/r_0^2$ is the dimensionless angular momentum. Each multipole family depends on $m$ and $j$ only through $mj$ and three angular Chebyshev polynomials represent exactly the multipoles $L=|m|,|m|+1,|m|+2$. A conserved flux confines nonthreshold real quasinormal frequencies to the superradiant window $0<\omega/(m\Omega_-)<1$, where $\Omega_-=3\pi J/r_0^3$. For the sampled sectors $m=1,2,3$ and spins up to $j=19$, every converged frequency is damped and lies outside this window. Independent integration verifies the low-lying modes, while Kerr spectra provide a separate benchmark for the two-dimensional solver. A limiting radial equation explains why the computed mode frequencies approach the upper edge of the superradiant window with frequency offsets proportional to $(mj)^{-1/2}$ at large $mj$. A complete stability assessment requires further analysis of the unresolved scalar spectrum and of perturbations of the coupled metric-matter system.

</details>


### [87] [Surface Enhanced Anisotropy and Its Impacts on Massive Neutron Star Configurations in Einstein Gravity : Stability, Structure, and Multimessenger Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09974)
*Ritabrata Biswas,Arpan Bhattacharya,Subhajit Pal*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs physically viable anisotropic neutron star models in General Relativity using a third-order polynomial EOS and Buchdahl class-I metric, satisfying all key physical and stability criteria, and achieving consistency with multimessenger astrophysical constraints (GW170817, NICER) and Bayesian parameter estimation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a self-consistent, physically realistic model of anisotropic neutron stars within General Relativity that satisfies fundamental energy conditions, stability criteria, causality, and modern multimessenger observational constraints.

Method: Uses a third-order polynomial equation of state (p_r = Aρ + Bρ² + Cρ³), Buchdahl class-I metric ansatz (e^{q(r)} = L(1+τr²)/(L−τr²)), Darmois-Israel junction conditions for interior–exterior matching, generalized TOV equation for hydrostatic equilibrium, adiabatic index and sound-speed analysis for stability and causality, and Bayesian MCMC for statistical inference of tidal Love number, compactness, and anisotropy parameter.

Result: All stellar solutions satisfy finiteness, positivity, monotonicity of energy density and pressures; vanishing central anisotropy; causal sound speeds (v_r², v_t² < 1) obeying Herrera cracking condition; adiabatic index > 4/3 ensuring dynamical stability; compactness < 0.384 (Buchdahl limit); surface redshifts within physical bounds; maximum masses exceeding observed pulsar masses and extending into the lower mass-gap; tidal deformabilities consistent with GW170817 and NICER; mass–compactness relation in agreement with observations; Bayesian MCMC yields robust posterior constraints on k₂, C, and τ.

Conclusion: The framework provides a physically viable, mathematically consistent, and observationally compatible description of anisotropic neutron stars in GR—validating anisotropy as a plausible microphysical feature reconciling theory with multimessenger data.

Abstract: Anisotropic neutron-star configurations are investigated within Einstein's General Relativity using a third-order polynomial equation of state, (p_r=A\rho+B\rho^2+C\rho^3), and the Buchdahl class-I metric ansatz, (e^{q(r)}=\frac{L(1+\tau r^2)}{L-\tau r^2}). Physically viable interior solutions are constructed and smoothly matched to the exterior Schwarzschild spacetime using the Darmois-Israel junction conditions. The stellar properties are examined through the matter distribution, pressure anisotropy, hydrostatic equilibrium, compactness, surface redshift, tidal deformability, causality, and dynamical stability. The energy density and radial and tangential pressures remain finite, positive, and monotonically decreasing throughout the interior, while the anisotropy vanishes at the centre and increases smoothly toward the surface. Generalized Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equation confirms equilibrium through the balance of gravitational, hydrostatic, and anisotropic forces. Furthermore, adiabatic index satisfies the Chandrasekhar stability criterion, and both sound speeds obey the causality and Herrera cracking conditions. The compactness remains below the Buchdahl limit, with physically acceptable surface redshifts. The predicted maximum masses exceed the corresponding observed masses, with some configurations extending into the lower mass-gap region. The tidal deformabilities are consistent with multimessenger constraints from GW170817 and NICER. In addition, the theoretical mass-compactness relation and its prediction intervals show good agreement with observational estimates. Finally, Bayesian Markov Chain Monte Carlo analysis provides statistically reliable constraints on the tidal Love number, compactness, and perturbation parameter. These results establish a physically viable and observationally compatible framework for describing anisotropic neutron stars within General Relativity.

</details>


### [88] [Periodic orbits and gravitational wave radiation of a phantom Dirac--Born--Infeld regular black hole Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09975)
*N. Heidari,A. A. Araújo Filho,Iarley P. Lobo,V. B. Bezerra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies periodic orbits and gravitational wave emissions in a regular black hole spacetime supported by a phantom Dirac–Born–Infeld scalar field, analyzing geodesics, zoom-whirl trajectories, periastron advance of the S2 star, and GW waveforms via a quadrupole-based numerical kludge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe observational signatures—especially orbital dynamics and gravitational waveforms—that could distinguish phantom Dirac–Born–Infeld regular black holes from standard Schwarzschild/Kerr black holes, using astrophysical constraints (e.g., S2 star orbit) and waveform morphology.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of timelike geodesics; construction of effective potential and circular/zoom-whirl orbits; perturbative study of orbital closure sensitivity; comparison with S2 periastron advance data; quadrupole-based numerical kludge computation of gravitational waveforms (both h+ and h× polarizations).

Result: The regular core parameter significantly modifies effective potential, circular orbit stability, zoom-whirl behavior, periastron precession rates, and gravitational waveform phase/amplitude structure—particularly in high-eccentricity or strong-field regimes.

Conclusion: Periodic orbital features and associated gravitational waveforms provide viable discriminators for phantom DBI regular black holes; current S2 observations already impose nontrivial bounds on the regular core parameter, and future LISA-like missions could further constrain or detect deviations.

Abstract: We investigate periodic orbits and gravitational wave radiation in the spacetime of an asymptotically flat regular black hole supported by a phantom Dirac--Born--Infeld scalar field. Starting from the timelike geodesic equations, we examine the effective potential and the characteristic circular orbits and construct periodic zoom--whirl trajectories. We further explore the sensitivity of orbital closure to small energy perturbations and examine the periastron advance of the $S2$ star around Sgr A$^*$ to assess possible constraints on the regular core parameter. The gravitational wave signals associated with representative periodic trajectories are then calculated within a quadrupole based numerical kludge approximation, considering both polarization modes. Particular attention is given to the influence of the regular core on orbital configurations and waveform characteristics, providing a complementary perspective on gravitational aspects of phantom Dirac--Born--Infeld regular black holes.

</details>


### [89] [Observational signatures of Bardeen black holes under nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09979)
*Haiyuan Feng,Dan-Dan Cui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the shadow and optical appearance of the Bardeen black hole in nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED), deriving its effective optical geometry, analyzing photon dynamics, and comparing thin-disk vs. spherical accretion models to identify NLED-specific observational signatures in the strong-field regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify distinguishable observational signatures of nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED) in the strong-field regime via the shadow and optical appearance of the NLED-Bardeen black hole, contrasting it with the standard Bardeen BH.

Method: Derivation of the effective metric for photon propagation in NLED; analysis of horizon-photon sphere-shadow inequality; ray-tracing simulations for thin-disk and spherical accretion models; classification of photon trajectories by impact parameter; computation of intensity profiles and Doppler effects.

Result: The NLED-Bardeen BH violates the full horizon-photon sphere-shadow inequality; increasing magnetic charge g/M shrinks the shadow and shifts emission peaks inward; thin-disk models show 1–3 intensity peaks depending on model; spherical accretion yields intensity peaks near critical impact parameter, suppressed by Doppler redshift in infalling case.

Conclusion: Shadow size, ring substructure, and intensity distribution—especially their systematic dependence on magnetic charge—are potential probes of NLED effects, enabling observational discrimination between NLED-Bardeen and standard Bardeen black holes.

Abstract: We investigate the shadow and optical appearance of the Bardeen black hole (BH) within nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED). Since photon propagation in nonlinear electromagnetic field can be equivalently described as null-geodesic motion in the effective metric induced by NLED, we derive the corresponding effective optical geometry of the NLED-Bardeen BH. Furthermore, by examining the horizon-photon sphere-shadow inequality, we demonstrate that the NLED-Bardeen BH does not satisfy the complete inequality chain. In addition, we investigate photon dynamics and the corresponding optical images generated by both thin disk and spherical accretion models. Increasing $g/M$ reduces the photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter, resulting in a more compact shadow and an inward shift of the emission peak. For the thin disk accretion model, photon trajectories are classified into direct emission, lensing ring, and photon ring components according to the corresponding impact parameter. We find that Model 1 exhibits three distinct intensity peaks, whereas Models 2 and 3 display two and one peaks. The systematic dependence of these features on the magnetic charge indicates that the NLED-Bardeen BH may exhibit observational signatures distinguishable from those of the Bardeen BH. In static spherical accretion, the intensity peaks near the critical impact parameter because of the enhanced optical path length, while infalling spherical accretion introduces an additional Doppler redshift that suppresses the observed intensity. These results indicate that the shadow size, ring structure, and intensity distribution can serve as potential observational signatures of NLED effects in the strong-field regime.

</details>


### [90] [Scalable early warning for binary neutron-star mergers in third-generation gravitational-wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10133)
*Martin Gerini,Rodrigo Tenorio,Davide Gerosa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a STFT-CNN method for low-latency detection of binary neutron-star coalescences in 3G GW observatories, achieving matched-filter sensitivity with >10⁴× speedup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard search methods are computationally prohibitive for long-duration, Earth-motion-affected signals from third-generation gravitational-wave observatories like the Einstein Telescope, hindering low-latency, early-warning multi-messenger astronomy.

Method: A convolutional neural network operating on short-time Fourier transform (STFT) spectrograms, implicitly marginalizing over extrinsic parameters (e.g., sky location, orientation, time) to reduce the search space to chirp mass only; trained to approximate the optimal Bayesian detection statistic by learning nonlinear time-frequency signal morphology.

Result: On simulated injections across ETΔ and ET2L configurations and signal durations (5 Hz → 25 min; 2 Hz → 6 hrs), the network matches GPU-optimized matched-filter detection sensitivity while reducing computational cost by >4 orders of magnitude—processing time drops from minutes to milliseconds.

Conclusion: The STFT-CNN approach enables real-time, low-latency detection of pre-merger binary neutron-star signals in third-generation detectors, making rapid electromagnetic follow-up and multi-messenger alerts feasible.

Abstract: Third-generation gravitational-wave observatories will observe binary neutron-star coalescences for hours before merger. Over these extended time windows, Earth's motion and the long signal durations make standard search methods computationally demanding for low-latency detection. We propose an approach based on short-time Fourier transforms and convolutional neural networks that implicitly marginalizes over extrinsic parameters, effectively reducing the search to the chirp mass. The network learns the signals' nonlinear time-frequency morphology, approximating the optimal Bayesian detection statistic. Using a large-scale campaign of simulated signal injections, we compare the network's performance with a GPU-optimized matched-filtering baseline for both the ET$\Delta$ and ET2L configurations of the Einstein Telescope and signal durations from approximately 25 minutes ($f_{\rm min} = 5$ Hz) to approximately 6 hours ($f_{\rm min} = 2$ Hz). The network achieves comparable detection sensitivity while reducing computational costs by over four orders of magnitude, bringing processing times from minutes to milliseconds. This efficiency paves the way for rapid early-warning alerts for multi-messenger astronomy in the third-generation detector era.

</details>


### [91] [Stealth scalars on prescribed dark-energy backgrounds: reconstruction, branch regularity, and perturbative bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10231)
*José Blanco,Cuauhtemoc Campuzano,Víctor H. Cárdenas,Giovany Cruz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs homogeneous stealth scalar fields on FLRW cosmologies with constant or evolving dark-energy equations of state; the scalar contributes no stress-energy, so cosmic expansion is driven by an independent background sector; eliminating the potential leads to a Riccati equation for the scalar profile, enabling distinctions among exact stealthness, global regularity, and sector-preserving identification—shown to be dynamically non-invariant in mixed flat constant-w cosmologies; for wCDM (radiation-free), solutions split into regular/singular branches via a background-dependent hypergeometric threshold; at conformal coupling, reconstruction reduces to a comoving-distance quadrature valid for arbitrary regular flat histories including radiation; CPL phantom-divide crossing does not singularize the stealth equation, though perturbations require separate treatment; the scalar–tensor formulation yields luminal high-frequency propagation under positive effective Planck mass; local tracking without screening implies strong suppression of late-time gravitational responses by Cassini constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and characterize homogeneous stealth scalar fields—i.e., scalar fields whose stress-energy tensor vanishes identically—on physically relevant cosmological backgrounds, thereby isolating the role of an independent dark-energy sector while probing consistency conditions (stealthness, regularity, dynamical invariance) and observational viability (e.g., solar-system tests).

Method: Analytic reconstruction of homogeneous scalar field profiles on prescribed FLRW backgrounds via elimination of the self-interaction potential, yielding a Riccati equation; classification of solutions using hypergeometric functions for wCDM; exact quadrature derivation at conformal coupling; analysis of dynamical invariance under background evolution; examination of high-frequency propagation speed and effective Planck mass in scalar–tensor frame; application of Cassini bound to constrain gravitational response from local field tracking.

Result: Stealth scalar profiles are fully reconstructible via Riccati equation; exact stealthness is strictly weaker than global regularity and distinct from sector-preserving identification, which is proven dynamically non-invariant in mixed constant-w cosmologies; for radiation-free wCDM, regular and singular branches are separated by a background-dependent hypergeometric threshold; at conformal coupling, reconstruction simplifies to a comoving-distance integral, valid even with radiation; CPL phantom crossing leaves the background stealth equation nonsingular; scalar–tensor description ensures luminal propagation and positive effective Planck mass; Cassini constraint suppresses late-time gravitational responses if the field tracks its cosmological value locally without screening.

Conclusion: Stealth scalar fields can be consistently reconstructed on diverse FLRW backgrounds, but their physical viability hinges critically on regularity, dynamical consistency of sector identification, and tight experimental bounds—particularly Cassini—which severely limit observable gravitational effects unless screening mechanisms are operative.

Abstract: We reconstruct homogeneous stealth scalar fields on prescribed Friedmann--Lemaître--Robertson--Walker backgrounds with constant or evolving dark-energy equations of state. The scalar stress tensor vanishes, so the expansion history is supported by an independent background sector. Eliminating the self-interaction potential yields a Riccati equation for the scalar profile. We use it to distinguish exact stealthness from global regularity and from the stronger identification of a term in the scalar stress tensor with the prescribed dark-energy density. We prove that this sector-preserving identification is not dynamically invariant in a mixed flat constant-$w$ cosmology. For radiation-free $w$CDM backgrounds, a hypergeometric representation allows the regular and singular branches to be separated by a background-dependent threshold. At conformal coupling the reconstruction instead reduces to an exact comoving-distance quadrature, valid for arbitrary regular flat histories, including radiation. A CPL phantom-divide crossing does not singularize the background stealth equation, although the perturbations of the prescribed dark-energy sector require an independent treatment. The scalar--tensor description gives luminal high-frequency propagation under a positive effective Planck mass. If the field tracks its cosmological value locally without screening, Cassini constraints strongly suppress the corresponding late-time gravitational responses.

</details>


### [92] [Relativistic Simulations of Self-Gravitating Radiation Fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10320)
*Bing-Jyun Tsao,Pablo Laguna,Octavio Guerrero*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a numerical method to simulate pure radiation fluids in general relativistic hydrodynamics by treating radiation as a massless perfect fluid, implemented and validated within the Whisky/Einstein Toolkit framework, with applications to black hole accretion and photon star stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate numerical simulations of radiation-dominated systems (e.g., early universe, gamma-ray bursts, accretion flows) in strong-gravity regimes where standard radiative transfer is computationally prohibitive and radiation pressure dominates dynamics.

Method: Treats radiation as a self-gravitating, zero-rest-mass perfect fluid; modifies the Whisky code (within Einstein Toolkit) by updating source terms in the Einstein and hydrodynamic equations, adapting the primitive variable recovery scheme for ultra-relativistic equations of state (P = ρ/3), and adjusting Riemann solvers (e.g., HLLE or Roe) for stiff radiation fluid dynamics; validates via 1D relativistic shock tube tests.

Result: Successful reproduction of analytical shock tube solutions; stable simulation of stationary radiation accretion onto a Schwarzschild black hole; computation of unstable photon star equilibria exhibiting either gravitational collapse to a black hole or dispersion under small perturbations.

Conclusion: Modeling radiation as a massless perfect fluid is a viable and efficient approach for studying strongly gravitating radiation-dominated systems in full general relativity, enabling new explorations of photon hydrodynamics in astrophysical and cosmological contexts.

Abstract: We present an approach for numerical simulations of pure radiation fluids within the general relativistic hydrodynamics framework. The approach models radiation as a self-gravitating perfect fluid with vanishing rest mass, enabling simulations of radiation fluids in strong-gravity spacetimes. Building on the existing relativistic hydrodynamics code Whisky, within the Einstein Toolkit framework, we introduce modifications to incorporate radiation fluids, including updates to the source terms, recovery scheme, and Riemann solvers. Modifications are validated using standard shock tube tests. We present results for radiation accretion onto a single black hole, as well as a photon star solution for radiation in unstable hydrostatic equilibrium, demonstrating both collapse and dispersion under perturbations.

</details>


### [93] [Generalized Second Law of Thermodynamics in $f(Q)$ Symmetric Teleparallel Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10604)
*Nadeem Azhar,Kayoomars Karami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper verifies the generalized second law (GSL) of thermodynamics on the cosmological apparent horizon in f(Q) symmetric teleparallel gravity, deriving a compact master formula for total entropy production and showing GSL holds for three viable f(Q) models—power-law, exponential, and logarithmic—under ordinary matter; GSL validity is tied to the sign of Ḣ (i.e., no phantom crossing), while energy condition violations differ across models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether the generalized second law of thermodynamics remains valid in modified gravity frameworks beyond general relativity—specifically in f(Q) symmetric teleparallel gravity—where spacetime geometry is described by non-metricity rather than curvature, and to clarify the role of horizon thermodynamics in constraining viable f(Q) cosmologies.

Method: The authors adopt Wald-type entropy for the apparent horizon with prefactor Af_Q/4G plus a correction term derived from the modified Friedmann equations in f(Q) gravity. They perform symbolic computation to obtain a closed-form expression for apparent horizon entropy and its time derivative, then combine it with matter entropy flux to derive a master formula for T_A Ṡ_tot. They apply this to three observationally consistent f(Q) models and analyze energy conditions via effective energy–momentum components.

Result: A compact, exact master formula T_A Ṡ_tot = 4πḢ(Ḣ f_Q + H ḟ_Q)/H⁴ (with 8πG = 1) is derived and verified; GSL holds throughout cosmic history for all three f(Q) models. GSL validity reduces generically to Ḣ ≥ 0 (no phantom phase) for pressureless matter satisfying the null energy condition. Energy condition violations are model-specific: power-law and exponential models violate only the strong energy condition, whereas the logarithmic model violates null, weak, and dominant conditions for redshift z > −1, and strong condition for z < 3.84.

Conclusion: The GSL is robust in f(Q) gravity for standard matter content, not due to fine-tuned model parameters but because of a fundamental structural feature—the monotonicity of the Hubble parameter. This suggests GSL serves as a powerful consistency condition for modified gravity theories, independent of specific f(Q) functional form, provided the effective expansion history avoids phantom behavior.

Abstract: We investigate the validity of the generalized second law (GSL) of thermodynamics on the cosmological apparent horizon in $f(Q)$ symmetric teleparallel gravity, using the Wald-type entropy prefactor $Af_Q/4G$ together with a correction term derived directly from the modified Friedmann equations. We derive, and verify by explicit symbolic computation, a closed-form expression for the apparent horizon entropy in $f(Q)$ gravity and show that its time derivative combines with the matter entropy rate into a remarkably compact master formula, $T_A\dot S_{\rm tot} = 4\pi\dot H(\dot H f_Q + H\dot f_Q)/H^4$ (in units $8\pi G=1$), which correctly reduces to the known general-relativistic limit. We apply this formalism to three observationally viable $f(Q)$ models --- power-law, exponential, and logarithmic. We find that the GSL is satisfied throughout cosmic history for all three models, and relate this robustness to a general, theory-independent structural condition: for ordinary pressureless matter obeying the null energy condition, GSL validity reduces to the sign of $\dot H$, i.e., to the absence of a phantom crossing in the total effective expansion history. The geometric sector exhibits model-dependent energy conditions, power-law and exponential models violate only the strong condition, while the logarithmic model violates the null, weak, and dominant conditions for \(z>-1\), and the strong condition for \(z<3.84\).

</details>


### [94] [A note on Positivity and Rigidity for an Expanding Quasi-Local Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10880)
*Eric Ling,Annachiara Piubello*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper strengthens endpoint rigidity for the expanding quasi-local energy $E_\lambda$ on constantly umbilic initial data, proving that $E_\lambda = 0$ at the threshold $\lambda = \alpha$ implies $(\Omega,g)$ is isometric to a closed Euclidean 3-ball of radius $\leq 1/\lambda$; it also provides a counterexample disproving positivity and rigidity beyond $\alpha$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve open questions from [ALP26] regarding the sharpness of the positivity threshold $\alpha$ and the geometric implications of vanishing $E_\lambda$, particularly whether endpoint vanishing implies full rigidity (i.e., Euclidean ball structure).

Method: Combines the Shi-Tam rigidity theorem for Brown-York energy with a strict concavity argument applied to $E_\lambda$ as a function of $\lambda$; constructs an explicit geometric counterexample violating positivity and rigidity beyond $\alpha$.

Result: Proof that $E_\lambda = 0$ at $\lambda = \alpha$ implies $(\Omega,g)$ is isometric to a closed Euclidean 3-ball of radius $\leq 1/\lambda$; explicit construction showing $E_\lambda$ can be negative for $\lambda > \alpha$, and vanishing $E_\lambda$ need not imply rigidity in that regime.

Conclusion: The endpoint rigidity for $E_\lambda$ is sharp and geometrically precise at $\lambda = \alpha$, but the conjectured global positivity and rigidity fail beyond this threshold—establishing $\alpha$ as a strict critical value separating qualitatively different behavior.

Abstract: We study positivity and rigidity for an expanding quasi-local energy $E_\lambda$ associated with constantly umbilic initial data $(\Omega,g,k=\lambda g)$, introduced in earlier work [ALP26] as an analogue of the Brown-York energy adapted to an expanding flat de Sitter background. Under nonnegative scalar curvature and suitable boundary assumptions, our previous results established positivity for $\lambda$ below a geometrically determined threshold $\alpha$, while vanishing $E_\lambda$ at $\lambda=\alpha$ forced the boundary to be round. In this paper, we strengthen this endpoint rigidity statement by showing that, if $E_\lambda=0$ when $\lambda=\alpha$, then $(\Omega,g)$ is isometric to a closed Euclidean 3-ball of radius at most $1/\lambda$. The proof combines the rigidity theorem of Shi-Tam for the Brown-York energy with a strict concavity argument. We also construct an explicit counterexample to the positivity and rigidity conjecture formulated in [ALP26], showing that the energy can become negative beyond this threshold and that vanishing energy need not imply rigidity.

</details>


### [95] [Qubit representation of the mass observable in quantized Schwarzschild interior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10933)
*Babak Vakili*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a quantum-computational framework to represent and measure the mass observable of the Schwarzschild black-hole interior in Wheeler–DeWitt quantum gravity, using truncated two-mode oscillator states, Pauli decompositions, resource scaling analysis, and a two-qubit VQE implementation with constraint verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge canonical quantum gravity (specifically Wheeler–DeWitt quantization of the Schwarzschild interior) with near-term quantum computation by enabling quantum simulation and measurement of gravitational observables—starting with black-hole mass.

Method: 1. Work in the physical Hilbert space of correlated two-mode oscillator states satisfying the Wheeler–DeWitt constraint; 2. Construct finite-dimensional representations of the mass operator; 3. Map them to qubit operators via Pauli decomposition; 4. Analyze scaling of Pauli term count and independent measurement settings with truncation size; 5. Implement a two-qubit variational quantum eigensolver (VQE) for the smallest nontrivial truncation; 6. Design a separate quantum-circuit protocol to verify the Wheeler–DeWitt constraint.

Result: Analytical scaling laws for Pauli term count (~N²) and measurement settings (~N); numerical validation of the mass spectrum via VQE with high fidelity; successful circuit-level verification protocol for the constraint; quantitative resource characterization for encoding/measuring mass.

Conclusion: The work establishes a concrete, resource-quantified pathway from canonical quantum gravity to quantum simulation, demonstrating that gravitational observables like black-hole mass can be faithfully encoded, measured, and constrained on quantum hardware.

Abstract: We develop a quantum-computational framework for the mass observable of the Wheeler--DeWitt quantized Schwarzschild black-hole interior. Working in the physical sector spanned by correlated two-mode oscillator states, we construct finite-dimensional representations of the mass operator and map them onto qubit operators through Pauli decomposition. We analyze the resulting representations for increasing truncation sizes and establish analytically the scaling of the number of nonvanishing Pauli terms and independent measurement settings. These results provide a quantitative characterization of the quantum resources required to encode and measure the black-hole mass observable. As a representative benchmark, we implement a two-qubit variational quantum eigensolver for the smallest nontrivial truncation and find excellent agreement with the exact finite-dimensional spectrum. We further describe a quantum-circuit procedure for verifying the Wheeler--DeWitt constraint independently of the mass estimation. Our results provide a direct connection between Wheeler--DeWitt quantum gravity and quantum computation and establish a structured route toward the quantum simulation of gravitational observables.

</details>


### [96] [Dynamical entropy on local causal horizons: the Einstein equation of state and canonical-energy flux](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11006)
*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs two local geometric functionals—S_loc and S_L—for null hypersurfaces, linking them to the Einstein equation via thermodynamic reasoning: S_loc yields Einstein’s equation (up to cosmological constant) from equilibrium heat balance; S_L provides a monotonic, caustic-avoiding entropy-like functional whose positivity and balance condition are equivalent to Einstein’s equation given conservation and normalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive the Einstein equation from local thermodynamic principles on null hypersurfaces without assuming global equilibrium or final equilibrium cuts, while ensuring geometric locality, monotonicity, and physical interpretability of gravitational entropy.

Method: Uses twist-free null hypersurfaces and constructs two entropy functionals: (i) Hollands-Wald-Zhang (HWZ) functional S_loc, satisfying an exact off-shell Raychaudhuri identity; (ii) logarithmic functional S_L = (4G)⁻¹∫(1 + ln J − Vθ)dA_ref, defined on a caustic-free generator bundle with Jacobian J. Applies local equilibrium arguments, Unruh temperature, stress-tensor conservation, and affine-parameter-based balance conditions; derives shift laws under affine origin changes and identifies anchoring-invariant content.

Result: S_loc recovers R_kk = 8πG T_kk (hence Einstein equation with undetermined Λ) from boost heat balance; S_L satisfies an exact positive-definite balance law under null convergence and is nondecreasing until caustics; equivalence between this balance and Einstein’s equation is proven given conservation and normalization; density 1 + ln J − Vθ is uniquely selected by cut-locality, positivity, and first-order HWZ normalization; both functionals depend on affine origin, but anchoring-invariant content is identified; near equilibrium, S_L matches HWZ dynamical entropy to first order and its temperature-weighted Hessian equals HWZ modified canonical-energy flux.

Conclusion: The Einstein equation emerges as a necessary and sufficient condition for the thermodynamic consistency of local gravitational entropy functionals on null hypersurfaces—specifically, for the monotonicity and balance of S_L and the heat-equilibrium derivation of S_loc—without invoking holography or global stationarity, reinforcing the thermodynamic origin of gravity at the level of null geometry.

Abstract: For a twist-free null hypersurface, the Hollands-Wald-Zhang (HWZ) representative $S_{\rm loc}=(4G)^{-1}\int(1-V\theta)dA$, local on each cross-section (cut), obeys an exact off-shell Raychaudhuri identity with no term linear in the expansion, so no final equilibrium cut is needed. Given an entropy density $1/4G$, the Unruh temperature $\kappa/2\pi$ of an approximate local boost, local equilibrium cuts, and stress-tensor conservation, equating the leading equilibrium change of $S_{\rm loc}$ to matter boost heat yields $R_{kk}=8\pi GT_{kk}$ for every null direction, hence the Einstein equation with undetermined cosmological constant, an equation of state in Jacobson's conditional sense. On a fixed caustic-free generator bundle with $dA=J\,dA_{\rm ref}$, the functional $S_{\rm L}=(4G)^{-1}\int(1+\ln J-V\theta)dA_{\rm ref}$ obeys an exact balance per reference cell with curvature, shear, and expansion terms all positive, so under null convergence $S_{\rm L}$ is nondecreasing for $V\geq0$ until the first caustic. Conversely, requiring this balance for positive affine parameter on every small bundle and null direction is equivalent to the Einstein equation, given conservation and the stated normalization. Cut-locality, positivity, and first-order HWZ normalization select the density $1+\ln J-V\theta$ uniquely within a two-function class. Both functionals depend on the affine origin; we give their shift laws, identify the anchoring-invariant content, and show the origin is physical data fixed by the equilibrium cut. Near equilibrium, $S_{\rm L}$ agrees with the HWZ dynamical entropy at first order, and the temperature-weighted change of its Hessian equals the HWZ modified canonical-energy flux for stationary-vacuum perturbations. At finite amplitude, $S_{\rm L}$ is only a monotone geometric functional; reading it as entropy requires a coarse-graining postulate, stated but not derived.

</details>


### [97] [Covariant Hamiltonian Gravity in Spherical Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11099)
*Cong Zhang,Zhoujian Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs an effective Einstein equation within covariant Hamiltonian gravity in spherical symmetry, showing that prescribed static spherically symmetric metrics (e.g., generalized Vaidya) solve it as vacuum solutions; it demonstrates that standard null dust (Vaidya) can source dynamical black holes—including regular ones—beyond GR, and argues that quantum gravity corrections alone cannot explain Hawking evaporation without violating the null energy condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether standard matter sources (e.g., null dust) can consistently drive dynamical black-hole geometries in effective gravity theories beyond general relativity, and to assess the role of quantum gravity corrections in black-hole mass loss and evaporation.

Method: Covariant Hamiltonian formulation of gravity in spherical symmetry; derivation of an effective Einstein equation whose vacuum solutions include a chosen one-parameter family of static, spherically symmetric metrics; explicit construction and analysis of generalized Vaidya solutions and their associated energy-momentum tensor and energy conditions.

Result: Generalized Vaidya spacetimes are exact solutions of the effective Einstein equation with standard Vaidya energy-momentum tensor; such solutions support dynamical black holes—including regular black holes—and require violation of the null energy condition for ingoing mass loss, implying quantum gravity corrections alone cannot reproduce Hawking-like evaporation.

Conclusion: Standard null dust is sufficient to generate physically meaningful dynamical black-hole geometries in effective gravity frameworks beyond GR; however, Hawking-like evaporation necessitates exotic matter (null energy condition violation), suggesting that quantum gravity corrections must be supplemented with additional physical mechanisms to recover semi-classical black-hole thermodynamics.

Abstract: Building upon the covariant Hamiltonian gravity in spherical symmetry, we construct the effective Einstein equation governing gravitational dynamics. For any prescribed one-parameter family of static, spherically symmetric metrics, this equation can be constructed such that the prescribed family constitutes their vacuum solutions. As an application, we construct generalized Vaidya solutions to the effective Einstein equation and show that the corresponding energy-momentum tensor takes precisely the standard Vaidya form. The results demonstrate that standard null dust sources can serve as matter source for dynamical black-hole geometries beyond general relativity, including regular black holes. Furthermore, this work suggests that quantum gravity corrections alone may not account for Hawking-like evaporation, as mass loss in the ingoing Vaidya solution necessarily requires a violation of the null energy condition within our effective framework.

</details>


### [98] [Exceptional Points in Scalar Quasinormal Modes of Rastall String-Fluid Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11366)
*Ming Zhang,Xiao-Mei Wang,Ya-Nan Wang,Miao Wang,Lei Zhang,Xufen Zhang,De-Cheng Zou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates exceptional points (EPs) in scalar quasinormal modes of Rastall string-fluid black holes using a localized Pöschl–Teller perturbation; it identifies a resolution-stable second-order EP on the regular branch via high-precision spectral numerics and multiple defectiveness diagnostics, but finds no convergent EP on the logarithmic branch.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously identify and characterize exceptional points—degenerate eigenvalues with defective eigenvectors—in the quasinormal mode spectrum of a modified black hole spacetime, testing robustness of EPs under numerical discretization and physical parameter variation.

Method: Introduces a localized Pöschl–Teller perturbation to the scalar wave potential with tunable bump position $a$ and amplitude $\varepsilon$; solves the hyperboloidal QNM eigenvalue problem using two-domain Chebyshev collocation and arbitrary-precision linear algebra; applies five complementary EP diagnostics: complex discriminant, eigenvector coalescence, Jordan-chain residuals, square-root splitting of eigenvalues, and closed-loop mode exchange.

Result: A resolution-stable second-order EP is found on the regular ($A=1$, $\mathcal{R}=-1$) branch across spectral resolutions $N=24$ to $N=60$; semidiscrete time evolution confirms the characteristic non-exponential Jordan response $(C+Dt)e^{i\omega_{\rm EP}t}$; on the logarithmic ($A=2$) branch, apparent defective points appear at finite $N$ but fail to converge, precluding continuum EP claims.

Conclusion: Exceptional points can be robustly realized in black hole QNM spectra under controlled perturbations, provided the background branch supports spectral convergence; the regular Rastall string-fluid black hole admits a physically meaningful EP, whereas the logarithmic branch does not support a well-defined EP in the continuum limit.

Abstract: In this paper, we study exceptional points (EPs) in scalar quasinormal modes(QNMs) of Rastall string-fluid black holes by adding a localized Pöschl--Teller perturbation to the scalar potential, with the bump position $a$ and amplitude $\eb$ serving as independent controls. We solve the hyperboloidal problem with two-domain Chebyshev collocation and arbitrary-precision linear algebra, and use the complex discriminant, eigenvector coalescence, Jordan-chain residuals, square-root splitting and closed-loop mode exchange as complementary tests of defectiveness. For the regular $A=1$, $\eR=-1$ branch, the two-parameter search locates a resolution-stable second-order EP from $N=24$ to $N=60$, while constrained semidiscrete evolution gives the Jordan response $(C+Dt)e^{\ii\omega_{\rm EP}t}$. For the logarithmic $A=2$ branch, defective points occur in each finite truncation but their coordinates do not converge, so we make no continuum claim for this branch.

</details>


### [99] [Negative Dynamical Friction in Dark Matter Spikes around Rotating Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11380)
*Wei Xiong,Peng-Cheng Li,Bin Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Dark matter spikes around rotating massive black holes can cause negative dynamical friction—supplying orbital energy to secondary black holes in EMRIs—leading to significant phase shifts (~100 rad) and environmental redshift corrections comparable to (but opposite sign of) first-order conservative self-force; both effects must be included for precision EMRI modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how dark matter environments affect extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs), particularly whether dark matter spikes induce measurable deviations from vacuum GR predictions via dynamical friction and conservative metric perturbations.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical modeling of orbital-energy exchange and conservative metric response due to dark matter spikes around Kerr black holes; computation of phase accumulation and frequency-domain redshift corrections for prograde circular orbits, including spin-dependent stability analysis beyond the ISCO.

Result: Negative dynamical friction occurs for stable prograde orbits in sufficiently spinning black hole–dark matter spike systems; total phase shift ~10^2 rad over four years; fixed-frequency environmental redshift correction is comparable in magnitude and opposite in sign to the first-order conservative self-force.

Conclusion: Both environmental energy exchange (via dynamical friction) and conservative metric response from dark matter are non-negligible and must be incorporated into high-precision EMRI waveform models for future space-based gravitational-wave detectors like LISA.

Abstract: Dark matter may affect extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) through orbital-energy exchange and a conservative metric response. Here we show that dark matter spikes around rotating massive black holes can drive negative dynamical friction, supplying orbital energy to secondary black holes on stable prograde circular orbits. The energy-gain region extends beyond the innermost stable circular orbit once the black-hole spin exceeds a model-dependent threshold. For an illustrative four-year inspiral with Milky Way--like halo parameters and mass ratio $q=10^{-5}$, the total dynamical-friction contribution produces a phase difference of order $10^2$ radians relative to vacuum. For the same system, the fixed-frequency environmental redshift correction is comparable in magnitude and opposite in sign to the first-order conservative self-force correction. Both environmental energy exchange and the conservative metric response are therefore essential to precision EMRI modeling.

</details>


### [100] [Pushing the accuracy of the quasi-circular baseline for the SEOBNR family of models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11449)
*Cheng Foo,Alessandra Buonanno,Héctor Estellés,Lorenzo Pompili*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: SEOBNRv5iHM is an improved quasi-circular, aligned-spin gravitational-waveform model in the effective-one-body framework, featuring a new phenomenological intermediate region (replacing non-quasi-circular corrections) for flexible ringdown attachment per mode; it achieves significantly lower unfaithfulness vs. numerical relativity—e.g., median 4.25×10⁻⁵ for (2,2) mode—outperforming SEOBNRv5HM and other semi-analytical models, especially at high masses and with higher-order modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate modeling of binary black hole gravitational-wave signals is critical for source characterization and astrophysical inference as detector sensitivities improve; existing models suffer from inaccuracies in late inspiral and merger-ringdown due to rigid ringdown attachment and non-quasi-circular corrections.

Method: Developed SEOBNRv5iHM by modifying SEOBNRv5HM: removed non-quasi-circular corrections and introduced a phenomenological intermediate region enabling independent, amplitude-peak-aligned ringdown attachment for each higher-order mode; calibrated and validated against 591 SXS catalog waveforms.

Result: For (2,2) mode: median max-unfaithfulness = 4.25×10⁻⁵ (4.5× better than SEOBNRv5HM); at M ≥ 150 M⊙, drops to 9.7×10⁻⁶ — comparable to NRHybSur3dq8; with ℓ_max = 5 and inclination π/3, median max-unfaithfulness = 4.2×10⁻⁴ (2.6× better than SEOBNRv5HM, 1.9× better than IMRPhenomTHM).

Conclusion: SEOBNRv5iHM sets a new standard for accuracy in quasi-circular, aligned-spin EOB waveforms; its improved fidelity reduces waveform systematics and provides a more robust foundation for future spin-precessing and eccentric extensions.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave observations have opened a new era of astronomy, with accurate modeling of binary black hole signals becoming increasingly important for source characterization and astrophysical inference as detector sensitivities improve. We present SEOBNRv5iHM, an updated quasi-circular, aligned-spin inspiral-merger-ringdown gravitational-waveform model with higher-order modes, constructed within the effective-one-body formalism and building upon SEOBNRv5HM. The improvements mainly concern the late inspiral and the merger-ringdown: we remove the non-quasi-circular corrections used in previous models and instead introduce a phenomenological intermediate region between the inspiral and the ringdown, which allows the ringdown attachment time to be chosen independently for each mode, including at the peak of the mode's amplitude. The model is calibrated and validated against 591 quasi-circular aligned-spin waveforms from the third Simulating eXtreme Spacetimes catalog. For the $(2,2)$ mode, when considering the maximum unfaithfulness against numerical relativity over a total mass range of 10-300$M_\odot$, SEOBNRv5iHM achieves a median of $4.25\times10^{-5}$, a factor of 4.5 below SEOBNRv5HM, and a factor of 3-8 below other semi-analytical models, with $84\%$ of cases below $10^{-4}$. For $M\ge150 M_\odot$, the median drops to $9.7\times10^{-6}$, comparable to the numerical-relativity surrogate NRHybSur3dq8. For polarization mismatches at inclination $\pi/3$, with modes up to $\ell_\mathrm{max} = 5$, the median maximum unfaithfulness is $4.2\times10^{-4}$, a factor of 2.6 below SEOBNRv5HM, 1.9 below IMRPhenomTHM, and 6-11 below the other semi-analytical models. Since spin-precessing and eccentric models are built on top of the quasi-circular, aligned-spin baseline, these improvements will make future SEOBNR models more accurate, reducing waveform systematics in upcoming analyses.

</details>


### [101] [Smooth Rigidity Near Extremal Horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11495)
*Ye Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves ambiguity in smooth Einstein spacetime extensions near degenerate Killing horizons by introducing a spacelike graph condition on the orbit space metric; it establishes local isometry of vacuum germs with matching transverse jets in d ≥ 4, provides spectral and weighted matching criteria for classification, extends to Einstein–Maxwell and rotating Kerr–de Sitter/Nariai settings, and yields rigidity results without assuming axial symmetry for small rotation parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the non-uniqueness problem in smooth extensions of vacuum Einstein metrics across degenerate Killing horizons—where identical transverse Taylor series do not guarantee local isometry—and to establish rigidity under physically relevant geometric conditions.

Method: Combines a terminal wave-map gauge with energy estimates on the orbit cylinder; employs weighted jet-matching criteria, spectral analysis of the Lichnerowicz Laplacian, and all-order tensor estimates; constructs spacelike graphs over compact horizon sections and leverages compactness and curvature sign conditions.

Result: Smooth vacuum Einstein germs with identical marked transverse jets are locally isometric near compact degenerate horizons in d ≥ 4 when the orbit-space metric admits a spacelike graph; for positive-Einstein sections, rigidity reduces to parallel first transverse data; multiwarped realizations converge even with negative-curvature directions; finite-regularity version holds for round sections; rigidity extends to 4D Einstein–Maxwell (charged-Nariai) and rotating Kerr–de Sitter/Nariai with a²/r₀² ≤ 1/8 without axial symmetry assumption.

Conclusion: The spacelike graph condition eliminates jet-based ambiguity in horizon extensions; smooth classification becomes finite-data–driven (e.g., first transverse derivative or weighted jet order), enabling robust rigidity theorems across multiple gravitational theories and horizon geometries.

Abstract: The Einstein equations constrain the transverse Taylor series at a degenerate Killing horizon, but even complete knowledge of that series need not determine a smooth spacetime: a nonzero difference can vanish to every order at the horizon. We show that this ambiguity disappears when the limiting metric on the space of Killing orbits admits a spacelike graph over a compact horizon section. In dimensions $d\geq4$, smooth vacuum Einstein germs with the same marked transverse jets are then locally isometric on a two-sided neighborhood of the whole section. The proof combines a terminal wave-map gauge with energy estimates on the orbit cylinder and yields a finite weighted matching criterion. For unwarped horizons with positive-Einstein sections, a condition on the full Lichnerowicz spectrum reduces the smooth classification to parallel first transverse data. All such data have convergent multiwarped Einstein realizations, including examples whose sections have negative sectional-curvature directions. We also obtain a finite-regularity version for round sections. The continuation argument extends to four-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell theory and gives smooth charged-Nariai rigidity. Finally, rotating Nariai data admit the required spacelike graph throughout the regular branch; an all-order tensor estimate yields smooth Kerr--de Sitter or rotating-Nariai rigidity for $a^2/r_0^2\leq1/8$, without assuming ambient axial symmetry.

</details>


### [102] [Quasinormal modes of a scale-dependent black hole in 5D spacetime with cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11584)
*Ángel Rincón,Zdeněk Stuchlík*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a 5D scale-dependent black hole with positive cosmological constant under scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations; it derives analytical effective potentials, applies WKB approximation for QNM frequencies, and confirms stability (negative imaginary parts) across all perturbations for small quantum corrections parameterized by ε.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quantum-gravity corrections to black hole dynamics via scale-dependent gravity, specifically how small deviations from General Relativity (parameterized by ε) affect quasinormal mode spectra and stability in five dimensions with positive cosmological constant.

Method: Derives exact analytical solutions for the 5D scale-dependent black hole metric and corresponding effective potentials (from the lapse function) for scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations; employs first-order ε-approximation of the lapse function; computes QNMs using the WKB semi-analytical method (up to 6th order); compares results against the classical (ε = 0) case.

Result: Computed QNM frequencies for all three perturbation types show negative imaginary parts across the explored low-ε regime, confirming mode stability; quantitative deviations from classical frequencies are tabulated and plotted; WKB method is validated as accurate for this scale-dependent background.

Conclusion: The 5D scale-dependent black hole with positive Λ remains linearly stable under scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations for small quantum corrections (low ε); QNM spectra encode distinguishable imprints of scale dependence, offering potential observational signatures for quantum-gravity effects.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a scale-dependent black hole in five dimensions with a positive cosmological constant, considering i) scalar, ii) electromagnetic, and iii) Dirac perturbations. After deriving exact analytical solutions within the framework of scale-dependent gravity in five dimensions and selecting a different family of solutions (cf. \cite{Contreras:2019fwu}), we analyze the corresponding effective potentials (given by the lapse function) for each perturbation type. We employ a first-order approximation for the lapse function, as it closely matches the exact case for small $\varepsilon$. Since quantum corrections are expected to be small, our focus is on a relatively low range of the scale-dependent parameter $\varepsilon$. We confirm that the WKB semi-analytical approach can be used to compute the QNMs for these perturbations to the background geometry. We calculate the QNM frequencies for the classical case (for comparison) and for cases where the scale-dependent parameter $\varepsilon$, which quantifies deviations from General Relativity, is non-zero. The results are summarized in a series of tables and figures. Finally, we find that, regardless of the perturbation type, the solutions are stable, as the imaginary part of each QNM frequency is negative.

</details>


### [103] [Horizon-penetrating rotating black-hole configurations in VCDM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11775)
*Antonio De Felice*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs stationary, rotating, and possibly charged black-hole solutions in VCDM (Vector-Tensor theory with a preferred foliation), showing that regular black-hole metrics do not guarantee regularity of the preferred foliation; it analyzes constant-mean-curvature (CMC) foliations crossing horizons, introduces horizon-regular flux formulations, performs high-resolution numerical scans and comparisons, establishes geometric and analytic bounds (e.g., on lapse and inner endpoints), and clarifies limitations regarding cosmological interpretation and interior dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether regular black-hole metrics (e.g., Kerr-Newman, Kerr-Newman-de Sitter) automatically yield regular configurations in Vector-Scalar-Tensor theories with a preferred foliation (VCDM), and to understand how the foliation behaves across horizons and at asymptotic/infrared boundaries.

Method: Analytical construction of stationary rotating/charged solutions on the constant-auxiliary Einstein-Maxwell branch of VCDM; derivation of nonzero constant-mean-curvature (CMC) foliations; formulation of horizon-regular flux conditions; high-resolution numerical scanning (17 survey configs + mean-flux scan at fixed geometry); flat/de Sitter paired comparisons; residual analysis, geometric reconstruction, grid convergence tests; analytical bounding (e.g., exact lapse bound for Δ_r > 0); classification of inner endpoint types (singular finite-proper-distance vs. asymptotic trumpet).

Result: Kerr-Newman and Kerr-Newman-de Sitter geometries admit CMC foliations crossing both future black-hole and (in de Sitter case) future cosmological horizons; horizon-regular flux uniquely specifies inner data and distinguishes hyperboloidal/expanding asymptotics; varying flux alters preferred-foliation shear without affecting standard test-matter background; local lapse/shear differences scale approximately as ∼(κM)²; an exact lapse bound excludes zeros in the regular exterior block; singular vs. trumpet inner endpoints are distinguished; no dynamical interior selection is implied by stationarity alone.

Conclusion: Regularity of the metric does not ensure regularity of the VCDM preferred foliation; the foliation structure is independently constrained by flux and boundary conditions, with horizon-crossing CMC slices being admissible but requiring careful specification of inner data and asymptotics; the stationary framework is insufficient to determine the rotating interior endpoint or enable local cosmological interpretation without additional matching assumptions.
