Abstract: A regular black-hole metric need not define a regular configuration of VCDM, whose preferred foliation is part of the gravitational solution. We construct stationary rotating, possibly charged configurations on the constant-auxiliary Einstein-Maxwell branch. Their Kerr-Newman and Kerr-Newman-de Sitter geometries carry nonzero constant-mean-curvature foliations that cross the future black-hole horizon and, in the de Sitter case, the future cosmological horizon. A horizon-regular flux formulation specifies the inner data and the distinct hyperboloidal and expanding asymptotic ends. Seventeen survey configurations are supplemented by a mean-flux scan at fixed four-geometry and paired flat/de Sitter comparisons down to $\kappa M = 0.01$, where $K_0 = 3\kappa$. Varying the flux changes the preferred-foliation shear without changing the background seen by standard test matter. The paired local lapse and shear differences have effective powers close to two over the computed range, with the lapse comparison requiring a fixed time normalization. Continuous residuals, geometric reconstructions, and selected grid comparisons test the numerical solutions. An exact lapse bound excludes zeros in the regular stationary exterior block with $\Delta_r > 0$. We distinguish singular finite-proper-distance endpoints from asymptotic trumpet ends and give conditional bounds on a stationary spacelike inner limit. A local cosmological interpretation requires additional matching assumptions; dynamical selection and the rotating interior endpoint are not determined by the stationary construction.

</details>


### [104] [Efficiency enhancement in the Unruh quantum Otto engine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11917)
*Yongjie Pan,Baocheng Zhang,Qingyu Cai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates efficiency enhancement in an Unruh quantum Otto engine (UQOE) composed of two unentangled, differently accelerated Unruh-DeWitt detectors interacting with a Minkowski vacuum scalar field. Efficiency gains arise not from entanglement but from proper-time asymmetry affecting population weighting; separable post-heat-absorption states suffice. Cyclic operation is ensured under passivity, closure, and energy conservation, and detector separation modulates entanglement generation and feasibility of enhanced efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether entanglement generation is necessary for efficiency enhancement in relativistic quantum thermal machines, specifically in Unruh quantum Otto engines.

Method: Analyzes a UQOE built from two initially unentangled Unruh-DeWitt detectors with distinct accelerations coupled to a massless scalar field in Minkowski vacuum; uses a diagonal free Hamiltonian and defines thermodynamic quantities (work/heat) relative to one detector's proper time; identifies cyclic operating points satisfying initial-state passivity, cycle closure, and energy conservation; examines impact of inter-detector separation on entanglement, coherence, and efficiency.

Result: Demonstrates efficiency enhancement beyond conventional UQOE without requiring entanglement—achievable even when the state after heat absorption is separable; identifies valid cyclic regimes; shows detector separation suppresses entanglement but introduces constraints on nonlocal coherence, limiting parameter space for cycle closure and maximal efficiency gain.

Conclusion: Entanglement generation is not a necessary condition for efficiency enhancement in Unruh quantum Otto engines; proper-time asymmetry and careful selection of cyclic parameters enable enhanced performance with separable intermediate states.

Abstract: We investigate the efficiency enhancement of an Unruh quantum Otto engine (UQOE) composed of two initially unentangled Unruh-DeWitt (UDW) detectors. The two detectors have different accelerations and interact with a massless scalar field in the Minkowski vacuum. We choose a diagonal free Hamiltonian for the UQOE and define its work and heat with respect to the proper time of one of the detectors. Consequently, the coherence terms generated through the interaction with the scalar field do not directly contribute to the work and heat of the UQOE. Instead, the efficiency enhancement arises from the relative proper-time factor, which assigns different weights to the population changes of the two detectors. We then identify cyclic operating points that satisfy the conditions of initial-state passivity, cycle closure, and energy conservation. Their efficiency is higher than that of a conventional UQOE, and the state of the UQOE after the heat-absorption stage is separable. Finally, we study the effect of the separation between the two detectors on the engine efficiency. During the interaction of the two detectors with the field, increasing their separation reduces the generated entanglement; however, this separation enters the nonlocal coherence terms, thereby restricting the range of parameter settings compatible with cycle closure and influencing the achievable efficiency enhancement. Our results demonstrate that the UQOE can achieve enhanced efficiency even when its state after the heat-absorption stroke is separable, showing that entanglement generation is not a necessary condition for efficiency enhancement.

</details>


### [105] [Deep-Learning-Assisted Bayesian Inference for Extreme-Mass-Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11922)
*Jian-Jun Song,Zhong-Wu Xia,Wen-Hong Ruan,Zu-Cheng Chen,Hongwei Yu,Puxun Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a deep-learning-assisted Bayesian inference framework for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs), combining neural-network-guided localization, particle swarm optimization, and MCMC to efficiently recover posteriors without truth-known initialization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: EMRIs exhibit highly multimodal, narrow likelihood surfaces, making standard Bayesian inference—especially initialization-free parameter estimation—extremely challenging in realistic observational scenarios.

Method: The method integrates three components: (1) a trained neural network to localize high-likelihood regions in parameter space; (2) particle swarm optimization (PSO) to refine the initial guess; and (3) Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) sampling for full posterior inference—all applied to simulated 6-month EMRI waveforms.

Result: The framework recovers posteriors consistent with reference MCMC runs initialized at true parameters—even when no such knowledge is available—and completes inference in ~10 hours per signal post-training.

Conclusion: Neural-network-guided hybrid optimization enables efficient, initialization-robust Bayesian inference for EMRIs, significantly advancing feasibility of parameter estimation for future space-based gravitational-wave observatories like LISA.

Abstract: Extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) pose a major challenge to Bayesian parameter inference because of their complex, highly multimodal likelihood surfaces. Locating narrow high-likelihood regions is particularly difficult without knowledge of the true source parameters, which is unavailable in real observations. We develop a deep-learning-assisted Bayesian inference framework that combines neural-network-guided parameter-space localization, particle swarm optimization, and Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) sampling. Applied to simulated six-month EMRI signals, the framework recovers posterior distributions in close agreement with reference MCMC analyses initialized near the true parameter values, without requiring such knowledge for initialization. Once the neural network has been trained, the complete inference pipeline takes approximately 10 hours per signal.

</details>


### [106] [Single and repetitive Penrose processes in genuine uncharged Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11943)
*Ruo-Cen Wu,Hanjun Zou,Meng Xu,Yun-Long Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an analytical framework for the Penrose process in the uncharged Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR0) black-hole spacetime immersed in a Bertotti-Robinson electromagnetic vacuum, rigorously distinguishing thermodynamic and geometric energies, solving particle decay dynamics in the ergoregion, and quantifying energy extraction efficiency under magnetic field influence—showing decreasing efficiency and eventual termination of repetitive extraction due to orbital constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously model energy extraction via the Penrose process in a magnetized, non-asymptotically flat black-hole spacetime (KBR0), where standard assumptions (e.g., asymptotic flatness, vanishing background fields) fail, necessitating a consistent thermodynamic–geometric framework that reconciles Killing energy, thermodynamic mass generation, and electromagnetic coupling.

Method: Analytical derivation of the Penrose process in KBR0 spacetime using multibranch parameter mapping from thermodynamic states to metric parameters; distinction between coordinate Killing energy and thermodynamic mass-generator energy; exact solution for equatorial neutral massive particle decay at radial turning points inside the ergoregion; derivation of orbital accessibility criteria and six physical constraints for viable extraction; numerical evaluation of three efficiency metrics (η_EROI, η_EUE, η_thermo) across magnetic field strengths and spin parameters.

Result: 1) Exact decay solution and six necessary constraints for complete Penrose extraction in KBR0; 2) Monotonic decrease of η_EROI and η_EUE with increasing external magnetic field, while η_thermo increases; 3) Two boundary mechanisms limit feasible magnetic field strength for single extraction; 4) All repetitive extraction sequences terminate due to orbital accessibility failure—either local-slope violation or far-field branch closure; 5) Final black-hole state evolves along a universal integral curve, independent of parent-particle specific energy (which only sets sequence endpoint).

Conclusion: The Penrose process in magnetized KBR0 spacetime is fundamentally constrained by electromagnetic coupling and ergoregion geometry: magnetic fields suppress extraction efficiency and restrict feasibility, while repetitive extraction inevitably halts due to dynamical orbital obstructions—not energy depletion—leaving most rotational energy intact; thermodynamic–geometric decoupling is essential for accurate modeling in non-asymptotically flat, strongly magnetized regimes.

Abstract: We establish an analytical framework for the Penrose process in the genuine uncharged Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR0) black-hole spacetime within a Bertotti-Robinson (BR) electromagnetic vacuum, consistently linking particle decay, radial transport, thermodynamic states, and geometric reconstruction by distinguishing coordinate Killing energy from thermodynamic mass-generator energy and performing multibranch parameter mapping from thermodynamic states to background metric parameters. Furthermore, we present the analytical solution for the decay of equatorial massive neutral particles at a common radial turning point inside the ergoregion, the orbital accessibility criteria, and the six physical constraints required for a complete extraction event. Numerical calculations demonstrate that for both near-extremal and lower-spin inputs, in both single and repetitive Penrose processes, the net energy return on investment ($\eta_{\text{EROI}}$) and the rotational energy utilization efficiency ($\eta_{\text{EUE}}$) decrease with increasing external magnetic field, whereas the rotational energy reservoir consumption rate per unit incident energy ($\eta_{\text{thermo}}$) increases. For the inputs considered, the feasible domain and the upper bound of the initial magnetic field for single extraction are constrained by two boundary mechanisms: the obstruction of parent-particle infall and the loss of negative energy in the captured fragment. Finally, for repetitive extraction processes within the surveyed regime, all sequences terminate due to orbital accessibility obstructions, explicitly local-slope failure and far-field incident-branch closure, with the final black-hole state retaining most of its initial rotational energy reservoir. Moreover, the black-hole state evolves along a common integral curve independent of the parent particle's specific energy, which solely alters the sequence endpoint.

</details>


### [107] [Deviations from Kerr: Critical curves and photon-ring critical parameters for general observer inclinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12050)
*Hao-Peng Yan,Xiang-Qian Li,Xiao-Jun Yue*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an analytic first-order perturbation framework to compute deviations of photon-ring observables (critical curve, demagnification, time delay, azimuthal advance) in stationary, axisymmetric, separable spacetimes relative to Kerr geometry, using a Kerr-relative decomposition of radial metric functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify how deviations from the Kerr metric affect observable photon-ring features—crucial for testing the Kerr hypothesis and interpreting EHT-like observations—by isolating the role of radial metric deformations while preserving Kerr's angular structure and separability.

Method: Employs a Kerr-relative decomposition of the radial part of the optical metric; derives analytic first-order corrections to critical curve normal displacement, demagnification exponent, time delay, and azimuthal advance for generic observer inclination; leverages intrinsic relations among exact observables to disentangle shared optical information from radial instability effects.

Result: Obtains factorized expression $\tilde u_n=\tilde F_{\mathrm K}\mathscr C$ for signed normal displacement of the critical curve; shows demagnification depends additionally on radial Lyapunov exponent, while time delay and azimuthal advance share intrinsic relations with the curve; validates first-order predictions against exact finite-deformation profiles and numerical ray-tracing on regular exterior photon-shell branches.

Conclusion: First-order analytic deviations of photon-ring observables are robust and physically interpretable in separable, Kerr-like spacetimes; the formalism provides a diagnostic tool to map specific radial metric deformations to distinct observational signatures near the critical curve, enhancing tests of strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We study critical curves and photon-ring critical parameters in stationary, axisymmetric spacetimes with separable null geodesics and Kerr angular structure. A Kerr-relative decomposition of the radial metric functions gives analytic first-order departures of these observables for observers at general inclination. The critical curve's signed normal displacement factorizes as $\tilde u_n=\tilde F_{\mathrm K}\mathscr C$, where $\tilde F_{\mathrm K}$ depends only on the Kerr reference geometry and $\mathscr C$ is an explicit combination of optical metric deviations. The demagnification exponent, time delay, and azimuthal advance are evaluated at a common position angle on the observer screen. Intrinsic relations among the exact observables express the optical information shared by the curve, time delay, and azimuthal advance, while demagnification additionally depends on radial instability. Applications to representative metrics illustrate how different radial deformations produce distinct departures from Kerr around the critical curve. Comparisons with exact finite-deformation profiles and independent numerical calculations verify the first-order predictions on regular, accessible exterior photon-shell branches.

</details>


### [108] [Next-to-next-to-leading gravitational radiation-reaction corrections to quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic compact binary orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12093)
*Donato Bini,Luc Blanchet,Guillaume Faye,Andrea Geralico,Quentin Henry,Sara Rufrano Aliberti,Emeric Seraille*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes radiation-reaction corrections at 4.5PN order for both quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic orbits of compact binaries using the Lagrange variation-of-constants method, confirming analytic equivalence of local effects between the two orbital regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate next-to-next-to-leading-order (4.5PN) radiation-reaction effects—including non-local tail and recoil contributions—into the orbital dynamics of compact binaries, bridging gaps between elliptic and hyperbolic treatments.

Method: Lagrange method of variation of constants applied to equations of motion with 4.5PN RR acceleration (in harmonic coordinates, both general and center-of-mass frames); separate perturbation setups for quasi-elliptic vs. quasi-hyperbolic orbits; careful treatment of non-local integrals (4PN tails, 4.5PN recoil); analytic continuation used to compare results.

Result: Complete 4.5PN RR corrections to orbital elements for both quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic motion; explicit verification that local RR effects are analytically equivalent across orbital types via continuation; full characterization of non-local contributions.

Conclusion: The 4.5PN radiation-reaction dynamics admit a unified description for bound and unbound orbits at the level of local effects, supporting robust gravitational-wave modeling across the full parameter space of compact binary coalescences.

Abstract: We apply the Lagrange method of variation of constants to compute the radiation-reaction (RR) corrections to the quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic motion of compact binary systems at 4.5PN order, i.e., at next-to-next-to-leading order in the RR. We rely on the recent determination of the 4.5PN RR acceleration, in a general frame and in the center-of-mass frame, in harmonic coordinates. The non-local effects, due to the tails at 4PN order and to the gravitational-wave recoil at 4.5PN order, are carefully evaluated. Although the calculation is implemented differently in the quasi-elliptic and quasi-hyperbolic cases (different perturbation equations and choices of orbital parameters), we verify by analytic continuation that the results for the local effects are equivalent in the two cases.

</details>


### [109] [Gravity Without Absolutes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12489)
*Julian Barbour,Maria I. R. Lourenço*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper argues that reductionism, long dominant in physics since Newton, may need replacement by holism due to stagnation and unresolved foundational issues; using Newtonian gravity as a testbed, it shows that applying Newton’s second law in absolute space/time leads to infinite arbitrary solutions for N-body universes, and that Leibniz’s Principle of Sufficient Reason (PSR) eliminates this arbitrariness—implying necessarily infinite particle number and suggesting correlation (not causation) as the fundamental explanatory structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the lack of major advances in physics over the last half-century and unresolved problems in dominant paradigms by re-examining foundational assumptions—specifically, whether holism, rather than reductionism, should guide future progress.

Method: Analytical reconstruction of Newtonian mechanics: treating Newton’s second law as central, examining its consequences in absolute space and time for closed N-body universes (not subsystems), then applying Leibniz’s Principle of Sufficient Reason to constrain solutions and infer ontological implications—including the necessity of an infinite particle count.

Result: Newton’s second law in absolute space/time yields infinitely many physically indistinguishable solutions for N-body universes; PSR removes this arbitrariness and implies that all such universes are dynamically nontrivial and that the number of particles must be infinite; in such a universe, correlation—not causal evolution—may underlie apparent temporal dynamics.

Conclusion: Reductionist formulations of classical physics harbor hidden arbitrariness that holist principles like PSR resolve; this supports a paradigm shift toward holism, where global constraints (e.g., sufficient reason) and present-state correlations—not local causal laws in absolute background structures—provide the foundation for physical theory.

Abstract: By formulating his theory of universal gravitation in absolute space and time, Newton demonstrated the power of reductionism; it has been behind every major advance in physics since then. However, the lack of significant advances over the last half-century, together with unresolved problems in the dominant paradigms of physics and cosmology, suggest that holism may need to replace reductionism as the way forward. The ontological simplicity and historical significance of Newtonian gravity makes it the ideal theory for examining this possibility. Taking Newton's second law of motion to be the core of his theory, we show that its use within the reductionist framework of absolute space and time introduces an infinity of arbitrary solutions if used to describe $N$-body universes rather than subsystems within them. We show that Leibniz's principle of sufficient reason (PSR) eliminates the infinite arbitrariness and transforms the predicted nature of $N$-body universes; they are all shown to be unexpectedly interesting. An even greater change is enforced if one asks what principle could determine the number of particles in the universe. The PSR suggests it can only be infinite. We suggest that, in such a universe, correlation could replace causation while still giving the appearance that each state has been created by dynamical evolution from the past, whereas its actual explanation may lie entirely within the present state. While much work remains to develop these ideas into a viable theory, we are confident that it advances the case for holism.

</details>


### [110] [Numerical-Relativity Particle-in-Cell Simulations: Self-Gravitating Collisionless Matter in Dynamical Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12831)
*Jiaxi Wu,Elias R. Most*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new numerical-relativity particle-in-cell (NRPIC) framework for solving the Einstein–Vlasov system in 3D, enabling fully kinetic, collisionless relativistic simulations coupled to dynamical spacetime evolution via Z4c formalism and adaptive mesh refinement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable full nonlinear studies of gravitational collapse, black-hole dynamics in multi-scale systems, and extremal black hole formation—problems requiring accurate, kinetic, and relativistic treatments beyond fluid approximations.

Method: Develops an NRPIC algorithm built on AthenaK; couples relativistic particle dynamics (via a Boris-type geodesic integrator reformulated as Lorentz-force-like evolution) to Z4c spacetime evolution in moving-puncture gauge; employs conservative stress-energy deposition on AMR grids; leverages electrodynamic analogies from plasma PIC methods for geometric integration.

Result: Validated against spherical dust collapse; successfully simulates prolate collapse through caustics to BH formation; demonstrates stability/nonlinear migration of relativistic clusters; performs first 3D simulation of dual overdensity collapse → BH binary inspiral, merger, and post-merger accretion in a collisionless envelope.

Conclusion: The NRPIC framework provides a robust, scalable, and physically faithful tool for studying gravitational instability, structure formation, and strong-field dynamics in general relativity with collisionless matter—opening avenues for modeling dark-matter-dominated or neutrino-rich astrophysical scenarios.

Abstract: Gravitational collapse and instability, black-hole dynamics in systems with large scale separations, and questions surrounding extremal black hole formation require full nonlinear studies using accurate and adapted numerical tools. To this end, we present a new algorithm to study collisionless relativistic systems using a fully kinetic description of matter coupled to the evolving spacetime. In detail, we present a numerical-relativity particle-in-cell (NRPIC) framework for solving the Einstein--Vlasov system in three spatial dimensions. Implemented on top of the performance-portable \texttt{AthenaK} code, the method couples particle dynamics to Z4c spacetime evolution in moving puncture gauge through conservative stress-energy deposition on adaptively refined meshes. Our implementation makes use of advances in the computational modeling of relativistic plasmas using modern PIC models, now made available to general-relativistic gravity via an equivalent electrodynamic formulation. We introduce a Boris-type particle pusher that exploits the electrodynamic (Lorentz force) form of the geodesic force. We validate the coupled evolution against spherical dust collapse, follow prolate collapse through collisionless caustics into black-hole formation, and investigate the stability and nonlinear migration of relativistic clusters. Highlighting its potential to study gravitational instability in NR, we simulate the collapse of two overdensities within a common collisionless envelope, following black-hole formation, binary inspiral and merger, and subsequent mass accretion onto the remnant.

</details>


### [111] [Beyond $Λ$CDM: Probing Structure Growth and Observational Viability of Generalized ${f\,(Q , \mathcal{L}_{m})}$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12839)
*Raj Kumar Das,Sivasish Paul*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies an extended f(Q, L_m) gravity model with additive minimal coupling, deriving cosmological observables and testing it against background (H(z), BAO, SNe Ia) and growth (fσ₈) data; non-conservation of the matter continuity equation (for ν≠1) is interpreted as particle creation, but growth data strongly prefer ν→1, disfavouring extra freedom beyond ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether generalized additive minimal coupling in f(Q, L_m) gravity—introducing non-conservation of the matter sector (ν ≠ 1)—can alleviate tensions or improve fits relative to ΛCDM, while assessing the role of structure growth data in constraining such extensions.

Method: Derives Hubble function H(z) from a novel parametrization of f(Q, L_m) = −Q^μ + α L_m^ν + β; formulates perturbed field, Euler, and continuity equations under non-conserved matter; performs Bayesian parameter inference using OHD, DESI DR2 BAO, Pantheon+, Union3, DES-SN5YR, and fσ₈ data; computes Bayesian evidence relative to ΛCDM.

Result: Background data moderately favour certain ν ≠ 1 realisations; however, inclusion of fσ₈ forces ν → 1 and yields decisively lower Bayesian evidence than ΛCDM, indicating growth data strongly constrain and disfavour the non-conserved extension.

Conclusion: While additive minimal coupling with ν ≠ 1 offers theoretical flexibility (e.g., effective particle creation), current growth observations (fσ₈) tightly restrict ν to unity, rendering the extension indistinguishable from standard f(Q) gravity coupled minimally to matter—and thus not empirically preferred over ΛCDM.

Abstract: $f(Q, \mathcal{L}_m)$ gravity is a well-known extension of $f(Q)$ gravity, obtained through non-minimal or additive minimal coupling in the action's Lagrangian density. Background-level cosmography within this framework, along with comparisons to $\Lambda$CDM, has been the focus of prior studies. The present work considers a generalized power-law form of the additive minimal coupling, $f(Q,\mathcal{L}_m) = -Q^\mu + \alpha \mathcal{L}_m^{\,\nu} + \beta$, from which the Hubble function $H(z)$ is derived through a novel parametrization. When $\nu \neq 1$, the matter continuity equation is rendered non-conserved, a feature that can be interpreted as an effective particle-creation mechanism. Constraints on the model parameters are obtained from Observational Hubble Data, DESI DR2 BAO measurements, and three SNe Ia compilations (Pantheon+, Union3, DES-SN5YR), allowing their cosmographic effects to be examined. Beyond the background level, the perturbed field, Euler, and continuity equations are derived under non-conservation, and their impact on structure growth is studied through a joint analysis incorporating the $f\sigma_8$ probe. Bayesian evidence, computed relative to $\Lambda$CDM, shows that the model is favoured by background data in several cases, with moderate support for one realisation, whereas the inclusion of $f\sigma_8$ drives $\nu\to1$ and decisively disfavours the additional freedom, highlighting the importance of growth data in testing such extensions.

</details>


### [112] [Mapping of Spacetimes with Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12857)
*Estuti Shukla,David Radice,Rossella Gamba,Sebastiano Bernuzzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a neural network method to learn coordinate transformations from numerical relativity simulations to analytical gauge-fixed spacetimes (e.g., Schwarzschild or Kerr), enabling gauge-invariant comparison and perturbative waveform modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gauge freedom in numerical relativity hinders physical interpretation and cross-simulation comparison; a data-driven, functional-form-agnostic method is needed to map numerical metrics to canonical gauges.

Method: A neural network is trained end-to-end to learn a smooth coordinate map by minimizing the difference between the pullback-transformed numerical metric and a target analytical metric—no parametric ansatz for the transformation is assumed.

Result: The method recovers known analytical coordinate maps for Schwarzschild/Kerr; successfully maps moving-puncture black hole simulations to ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein Schwarzschild and approximates Kerr for spinning cases; enables Teukolsky-based waveform extraction matching full nonlinear results.

Conclusion: Learned coordinate maps provide a robust, gauge-agnostic bridge between numerical spacetimes and analytical backgrounds, supporting perturbation theory, cross-code validation, and physical interpretation.

Abstract: Gauge freedom in numerical relativity complicates the interpretation of simulation coordinates and the comparison of different descriptions of the same spacetime. We present a data-driven neural network method for learning coordinate maps from numerical metric data to a prescribed target gauge. The network is trained by matching the transformed source metric to the target metric, without assuming a functional form for the coordinate transformation. We first validate the method on analytical Schwarzschild and Kerr geometries, recovering known transformations up to the isometries of the target spacetime. We then apply it to numerical black hole evolutions in moving-puncture coordinates. For a non-spinning black hole, the learned map relates the time-dependent numerical metric to a stationary Schwarzschild metric in ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates. For a spinning puncture, the method provides an approximate identification with a Kerr background despite departures from Kerr in the numerical spacetime. We use this identification to construct curvature perturbations and evolve them with the Teukolsky equation, obtaining waveforms that closely match those extracted from the full nonlinear simulation. These results demonstrate that learned coordinate maps can relate numerical spacetimes to analytical backgrounds, with potential applications to cross-code validation and black hole perturbation theory.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [113] [An Information-Theoretic Perspective on Spectral Reconstruction in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10671)
*Nan Wang,Long-cheng Gui,Jian Liang,Jun Shi,Han-yang Xing*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces an information-theoretic framework based on Kullback–Leibler divergence to quantify how much finite, noisy lattice correlator data constrain spectral reconstructions—accounting for both pointwise spectral strength and inter-energy correlations—and demonstrates its utility in evaluating reliability and prior dependence in Bayesian spectral reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spectral reconstruction from finite, noisy lattice QCD correlators is ill-posed; distinguishing data-driven features from prior-induced artifacts requires a rigorous, quantitative measure of information gain from data.

Method: The authors formulate KL divergence between prior and posterior spectral distributions to measure information gain, decomposing it into diagonal (per-energy) and off-diagonal (spectral correlation) components; they smooth the measures via Gaussian smearing over energy scales and implement within a hierarchical Bayesian framework with posterior sampling.

Result: Applied to synthetic three-peak spectra and physical proton correlator data, the information measures successfully identify energy regions where reconstruction is strongly data-constrained versus dominated by priors; they correlate with stability and resolution of reconstructed peaks.

Conclusion: KL-based information measures provide a semiquantitative, interpretable metric for assessing reliability and prior sensitivity in spectral reconstruction—enabling principled validation beyond point estimates or goodness-of-fit alone.

Abstract: Assessing the reliability of spectral reconstruction from finite, noisy lattice correlators requires understanding the constraints supplied by the data relative to prior assumptions. We develop an information-theoretic framework using Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergences to quantify the information supplied by the data through changes from prior to posterior probability distributions. Our analysis accounts for both changes in the probability distributions of spectral strength at individual energies and constraints encoded in correlations across the spectrum. We smear these information measures over neighboring energies using Gaussian weights to obtain a smoothed assessment at a specified energy scale. We demonstrate this framework using a hierarchical Bayesian reconstruction method with posterior sampling, applied to mock correlator data generated from a three-peak spectrum and a near-physical lattice proton correlator. These calculations show that the information measures help explain the properties of reconstructed spectra. They also provide a semiquantitative criterion for assessing how strongly reconstructed spectral structures are constrained by the data and how reliable they are in light of prior effects.

</details>


### [114] [Constrained field-space estimators for background-field lattice QCD observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10773)
*Scott Moerschbacher,Frank X. Lee*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper compares fixed-order stencils and covariance-optimized estimators for extracting weak-field response coefficients (e.g., magnetic moments) from lattice QCD correlators under background magnetic fields. Using nucleon and decuplet (Δ) data, it finds fixed O(B⁴)–O(B⁸) stencils are statistically near-optimal, robust to model misspecification, and satisfy exact symmetry constraints (e.g., μ_Δ⁺/μ_Δ⁺⁺ = 1/2), whereas covariance-optimized fits—though marginally more precise (1–2%) on some ensembles—exhibit large sensitivity to omitted higher-order response terms and violate exact relations by up to 25σ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether covariance-optimized generalized least-squares (GLS) estimators provide meaningful gains over simple fixed cancellation stencils in background-field lattice calculations, particularly regarding statistical precision versus robustness to model misspecification (e.g., truncation of field-series expansion).

Method: Treats weak-field coefficient extraction as linear estimation in field space; compares fixed O(B⁴)–O(B⁸) finite-difference stencils against covariance-optimized GLS estimators; uses archival quenched nucleon double ratios (~1000 configs, six quark masses) and exact decuplet correlator results (Δ baryons) as controlled benchmarks; applies null-space tests to assess model validity; quantifies leverage of estimator weights on omitted response modes.

Result: Fixed stencils achieve nearly identical precision to GLS estimators (within 1–2%), satisfy exact SU(3)-symmetry-derived magnetic moment relations (e.g., μ_Δ⁰ = 0, μ_Δ⁺/μ_Δ⁺⁺ = 1/2) within errors, and pass null-space tests more reliably; GLS estimators gain marginal precision but introduce up to 1.8σ shifts in moments and exhibit 10³–10⁴× greater weight leverage on omitted modes—causing >10σ violations of exact relations in decuplet controls.

Conclusion: Statistical precision gains from covariance optimization are negligible compared to the loss in robustness under model misspecification; fixed stencils are preferred for reliability, interpretability, and adherence to physical symmetries—highlighting a fundamental trade-off between efficiency and model insensitivity in lattice background-field analyses.

Abstract: Background-field lattice calculations extract weak-field response coefficients from correlators computed at several field strengths. We treat this extraction as linear estimation in field space and compare fixed cancellation stencils with covariance-optimized, generalized-least-squares estimators of the same coefficient, using archival nucleon double ratios on about 1000 quenched configurations at six quark masses and decuplet correlators for which exact answers follow from the lattice contractions. The fixed $O(B^4)$-$O(B^8)$ stencils are nearly coincident and cost almost nothing in precision. Weight-free null-space tests reject the two-term response model in most nucleon ensembles; whether they reject the three-term model depends on the temporal fits and on the noise of the estimated temporal covariance, which raises the null-contrast error by a factor of 1.7-2.4. With that noise included, covariance optimization gains 1-2% in precision at the five lighter masses (3-30% with two-state time fits, which also lower moments by 3-10%) and nothing on independent data halves, while its weights carry three to four orders of magnitude more leverage on omitted response modes than the fixed stencils and move the moments by up to 1.8 marginal standard errors. The decuplet controls show what such leverage does under misspecification: the first-order responses obey $\mu_{\Delta^+}/\mu_{\Delta^{++}}=1/2$ and $\mu_{\Delta^0}=0$ exactly. The lowest-order estimate misses the first by 10-25 standard errors. The fixed $O(B^4)$-$O(B^8)$ stencils satisfy it within errors in the production windows, whereas the correlated linear fit in $B^2$, with the same constraints as the $O(B^4)$ stencil, misses it by 3-17%; the null-space test rejects that fit's model. The results distinguish statistical precision from robustness to omitted response modes; they are not physical-point magnetic moments.

</details>


### [115] [Flavor decomposition of the nucleon axial and tensor charges from lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10816)
*C. Alexandrou,S. Bacchio,M. Di Carlo,A. Evangelista,C. Iona,F. Pittler,G. Spanoudes*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes nucleon axial and tensor charges using twisted-mass clover-improved fermions on four lattice spacings, confirming agreement with experiment for the isovector axial charge and providing precise values for isoscalar axial and tensor charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely determine nucleon axial and tensor charges—key quantities for testing the Standard Model and probing new physics—using controlled lattice QCD simulations with physical quark masses and multiple lattice spacings to enable robust continuum extrapolation.

Method: Lattice QCD simulations with twisted-mass clover-improved fermions on four ensembles at lattice spacings a = 0.05–0.08 fm, fixed physical volume (~5.5 fm), and near-physical u/d, s, and c quark masses; both connected and disconnected diagrams computed to extract isovector and isoscalar axial (g_A) and tensor (g_T) charges.

Result: g_A^{u−d} = 1.263(34)(26), consistent with experiment; g_A^{u+d} = 0.458(16)(1); g_T^{u−d} = 0.951(21)(5); g_T^{u+d} = 0.556(16)(5).

Conclusion: The high-precision results validate lattice methodology for nucleon matrix elements, confirm the standard model value of g_A^{u−d}, and provide benchmark values for isoscalar axial and tensor charges—crucial for dark matter direct detection and neutron electric dipole moment studies.

Abstract: We determine the nucleon axial and tensor charges using four ensembles of twisted-mass clover-improved fermions with approximately equal physical volumes of about 5.5~fm and four lattice spacings, $ a=0.05$~fm, 0.06~fm, 0.07~fm, and 0.08~fm. The mass of the degenerate up and down, strange and charm quarks are tuned to approximately their physical values. We compute both connected and disconnected contributions to determine the isovector and isoscalar charges and their flavour decomposition. For the nucleon isovector axial charge we find $g_A^{u-d}=1.263(34)(26)$, in agreement with the experimental value. For the isoscalar axial charge we find $g_A^{u+d}=0.458(16)(1)$, while for the corresponding tensor charges we find $g_T^{u-d}=0.951(21)(5)$ and $g_T^{u+d}=0.556(16)(5)$.

</details>


### [116] [Evaluating generative samplers for lattice field theory by scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11108)
*Satoshi Iso,Lingxiao Wang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper critiques the use of global effective sample size (ESS) as a sole diagnostic for generative samplers in lattice field theory, showing it can mask long-distance observable inaccuracies. It introduces a scale-resolved diagnostic based on coarse-grained distribution overlap to isolate infrared (long-wavelength) errors, demonstrates its utility in free and φ⁴ theories, and explores mitigation strategies including marginal Metropolis tests and local updates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global ESS is insufficient for assessing accuracy of long-distance observables in lattice field theory sampling, as it conflates short- and long-wavelength errors; a more scale-aware diagnostic is needed to diagnose and correct infrared biases.

Method: Introduces a scale-resolved diagnostic quantifying overlap between coarse-grained model and target distributions across length scales; applies it to exactly solvable free field and 2D φ⁴ theory near criticality; evaluates diffusion samplers, marginal Metropolis tests with exact local updates, and analyzes variance bounds under bounded proposal errors.

Result: In free theory, two samplers with comparable global ESS exhibit starkly different susceptibility biases (32% vs. 0%); in 2D φ⁴, diffusion samplers show rapid decay of path-weight ESS fraction but stable low-magnetization moments; marginal Metropolis maintains O(1) acceptance and reduces—but does not eliminate—infrared bias; bounded relative errors in proposal variances yield volume-independent bounds on stationary mode variance accuracy.

Conclusion: Global ESS is misleading for infrared physics; scale-resolved overlap diagnostics are necessary and informative; marginal Metropolis with local updates improves but does not fully resolve long-wavelength bias; accurate infrared observables require either improved proposals or external estimation of target marginals.

Abstract: Generative samplers for lattice field theory are commonly evaluated by their effective sample size (ESS). We show that a small global ESS need not imply inaccurate long-distance observables: errors spread over many short-distance modes can dominate this diagnostic. We introduce a scale-resolved diagnostic based on the overlap between coarse-grained model and target distributions. At the lattice cutoff, this overlap determines the asymptotic ESS fraction; lower cutoffs isolate long-wavelength errors. In the exactly solvable free theory, two samplers with comparable ESS yield a $32\%$ bias and no bias in the susceptibility, respectively. In two-dimensional $\phi^4$ theory near criticality, trained diffusion samplers show the same separation of scales: their path-weight ESS fraction falls rapidly over the volumes studied, while low moments of the magnetization remain close to their target values. Varying the reference parameters substantially changes infrared observables, with little effect on the log-weight variance. Given the target magnetization distribution, a marginal Metropolis test maintains an acceptance rate of order one in our tests. Combined with exact local updates, it reduces the remaining mismatch, although residual bias remains at finite relaxation length. In the free field, uniformly bounded relative errors in the proposal mode variances give a volume-independent bound on the number of updates needed for a prescribed accuracy of the chain's stationary mode variances at fixed correlation length. The target marginal must be supplied or computed separately; estimating it from the samplers' own weights can inherit the poor global overlap.

</details>


### [117] [Renormalized symmetry probes for emergent symmetries and non-chiral lattice fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12142)
*Ting-Wai Chiu*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces renormalized, RG-invariant probes (κ_AB) to detect symmetry restoration—especially chiral and emergent chiral-spin SU(2)_CS—in finite-temperature QCD using lattice meson correlators; it handles regularization-induced symmetry breaking via ratio-based renormalization (R_AB), validates the framework across fermion discretizations, and confirms hierarchical chiral-symmetry restoration in N_f=2+1+1 QCD with domain-wall quarks, identifying charm-containing spin-1 mesons as optimal chiral-spin probes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct robust, renormalization-group-invariant observables for detecting symmetry restoration (e.g., chiral and emergent chiral-spin SU(2)_CS) in high-temperature QCD, especially when lattice regularizations break chiral symmetry or symmetries are only emergent (not exact in the massless action).

Method: Introduces symmetry ratios κ_AB defined by renormalizing thermal/screening meson correlators in each flavor channel relative to the \bar ud reference channel at a common scale; separates scheme dependence into R_AB, which equals 1 for exact symmetries (making κ_AB RG-invariant) and carries physical cutoff dependence for emergent symmetries (canceling in κ_AB); applies to Wilson, twisted-mass, and staggered fermions; uses tensor channel pairs (T_k, X_k) to enforce R_{T_kX_k}=1 on isotropic lattices; includes analytic free-quark benchmarks (κ_{fade}=2/3, κ_{CS}≤1/3).

Result: Lattice results for N_f=2+1+1 QCD with physical-domain-wall quarks (a=0.064 fm, T=192–1539 MeV) show hierarchical chiral-symmetry restoration from light to charm sectors, invariant under renormalization; \bar uc and \bar sc channels exhibit the widest chiral-spin windows—even at tighter thresholds—identifying spin-1 mesons with one charm quark as optimal probes of the chiral-spin regime; lattice artifacts are small for chiral-spin probes but comparable to signal for exact-symmetry probes.

Conclusion: The proposed renormalized symmetry ratios κ_AB provide a rigorous, scheme-independent (for exact symmetries) or physically interpretable (for emergent symmetries) framework to quantify symmetry degeneracy in thermal QCD; chiral-spin symmetry restoration is most cleanly observed in charm-strange and charm-up mesonic channels, supporting their use as precision probes of emergent symmetries near the QCD crossover.

Abstract: The degeneracy of hadron correlators in symmetry-related channels signals symmetry restoration in high-temperature QCD. RG-invariant probes of this degeneracy need care when the regularization breaks chiral symmetry, or when the symmetry is not exact in the massless action, as for the emergent chiral-spin symmetry $SU(2)_{CS}$. We renormalize the thermal and screening meson correlators of every flavor sector by the thermal correlator of the same channel in the $\bar ud$ sector at a common reference point. The symmetry ratio $\kappa_{AB}$ then depends on the renormalization only through the ratio $R_{AB}$. For exact symmetries, $R_{AB}=1$ and $\kappa_{AB}$ is RG invariant and scheme independent in mass-independent schemes. For emergent symmetries, $R_{AB}\neq1$ carries the relative logarithmic cutoff dependence of the two channels, which cancels in $\kappa_{AB}$, and the residual dependence on the reference scale is physical. The framework applies to Wilson, twisted-mass and staggered fermions, where $R_{AB}$ absorbs the channel-dependent renormalization, and the tensor pair $(T_k,X_k)$ keeps $R_{T_kX_k}=1$ on isotropic Wilson-type lattices. Lattice artifacts are a small correction to the chiral-spin probes $\kappa_{CS}$ and $\kappa_{fade}$, but as large as the signal of the exact-symmetry probes. For free quarks, we derive $\kappa_{fade}=2/3$ for every mass and separation, and $\kappa_{CS}\le1/3$. We apply the renormalized probes to $N_f=2+1+1$ QCD with optimal domain-wall quarks at the physical point, with $a=0.064$ fm and $T=192$-$1539$ MeV. The hierarchical restoration of the chiral symmetries from the light to the charm sectors is confirmed, and is invariant under renormalization. The $\bar uc$ and $\bar sc$ sectors have the widest chiral-spin windows, also at a tighter threshold. Thus spin-1 mesons with one charm quark are the most attractive channels for the chiral-spin regime.

</details>


### [118] [Optimised Distillation for Nucleon Correlation Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12474)
*J. A. Crawford,J. Finkenrath,L. Barca*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper addresses challenges in extracting the nucleon ground state energy from correlation functions by developing optimized distillation profiles to enhance ground-state overlap and suppress excited-state contamination in two- and three-point functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the dual challenges of excited-state dominance at early Euclidean times and exponential signal-to-noise degradation at late times when extracting nucleon ground-state energies from correlation functions.

Method: Constructs optimized distillation profiles that modulate Laplacian eigenmode contributions to nucleon correlation functions, thereby improving ground-state overlap and suppressing excited-state contamination in both two- and three-point functions.

Result: Demonstrates progress in building optimized distillation profiles for nucleons that enhance ground-state isolation and reduce excited-state effects in correlation function analysis.

Conclusion: Optimized distillation profiles offer a promising strategy to extend the reliable Euclidean time window for nucleon ground-state energy extraction, improving precision in lattice QCD spectroscopy.

Abstract: The calculation and analysis of nucleon correlation functions is essential for understanding the observed spectra and structure of these ubiquitous hadrons. Two challenges arise in their analysis: at early Euclidean times, they are dominated by excited state contributions and at later Euclidean times, the signal-to-noise ratio degrades exponentially. This leads to a common challenge in identifying a reasonable window on which to extract the ground state energy. By modulating the contribution of each Laplacian eigenmode to the correlator via distillation profiles, we build optimised operators which improve the overlap with the nucleon ground state. In these proceedings, we present our progress on constructing optimised distillation profiles for nucleons and their excited state suppression in two- and three-point correlation functions.

</details>


### [119] [Symanzik Effective Hamiltonian for Lattice Quantum Field Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12520)
*Fernanda Steffens,Carsten Urbach*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Proposes a symmetry- and dimensional-analysis-based framework to construct the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian for lattice QFTs, enabling systematic operator classification by lattice spacing order and supporting future non-perturbative Hamiltonian improvement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable systematic, non-perturbative improvement of lattice quantum field theories in the Hamiltonian formulation by constructing the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian rigorously from symmetries and dimensional analysis.

Method: Uses dimensional analysis and symmetries intrinsic to the Hamiltonian formulation to classify all possible local operators contributing at each order in the spatial lattice spacing; applies equations of motion and identities to reduce the operator basis; validates on φ⁴ theory and pure gauge theory.

Result: Derives consistent Symanzik effective Hamiltonians for φ⁴ and pure gauge theories matching prior classical improvement results; establishes a general, symmetry-constrained operator classification scheme applicable to arbitrary lattice Hamiltonians.

Conclusion: The framework provides a foundation for non-perturbative improvement programs in Hamiltonian lattice QFT, bridging formal effective-theory construction with practical numerical simulation needs.

Abstract: We propose a framework to construct the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian for lattice quantum field theories directly based on dimensional analysis and symmetries in the Hamiltonian formulation. This allows to classify all operators which can contribute at a given order in the spatial lattice spacing. This list can be reduced using the equations of motion and other identities. We exemplify the framework for $\phi^4$ and pure gauge theory, obtaining results consistent with earlier publications for the case of classical improvement. It provides the means to develop a non-perturbative improvement programme in the Hamiltonian formulation of lattice quantum field theories in the future.

</details>


### [120] [Unpolarized proton PDF with physical quark masses and large momenta](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12730)
*Xiang Gao,Andrew Hanlon,Jinchen He,Xiangyu Jiang,Swagato Mukherjee,Peter Petreczky,Qi Shi,Yong Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-precision lattice QCD calculation of the proton’s isovector unpolarized parton distribution function (PDF) using the large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) framework, with physical quark masses, Coulomb-gauge-fixed operators, and NLO+NNLL matching to the MS-bar scheme; results show improved signal quality, reduced uncertainties, and strong agreement with phenomenological PDFs in both momentum and coordinate space—especially in the imaginary part—marking progress toward precision PDF benchmarking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform a first-principles, ab initio determination of the proton's isovector unpolarized PDF at physical quark masses, overcoming challenges in signal-to-noise ratio at large hadron momenta and operator separations, and enabling rigorous comparison with phenomenological PDFs for validation of lattice QCD methodologies.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation on a (2+1)-flavor ensemble (a = 0.076 fm), employing Coulomb-gauge-fixed quark bilinears within LaMET; use of kinematically enhanced nucleon interpolators, high-statistics measurements, static RI/MOM and hybrid renormalization schemes; extraction of quasi-PDF followed by NLO perturbative matching + NLL resummation to obtain the light-cone PDF in the MS-bar scheme.

Result: A high-precision isovector PDF with significantly reduced statistical and systematic uncertainties; consistency with prior lattice results (a = 0.06 fm, m_π = 300 MeV); quantitative agreement with phenomenological PDFs over 0.25 ≲ |x| ≲ 0.75; novel agreement in coordinate space, including the imaginary part of short-distance matrix elements after NLO matching.

Conclusion: The calculation demonstrates robustness and reliability of LaMET-based lattice PDF extractions at physical parameters, with cross-validation against phenomenology in both x- and coordinate-space representations—constituting a significant step toward precision benchmarking and eventual sub-5% uncertainty goals for nucleon structure calculations.

Abstract: We present a lattice quantum chromodynamics (QCD) calculation of the proton isovector ($u-d$) unpolarized parton distribution function (PDF) at physical sea and valence quark masses, using Coulomb-gauge-fixed quark bilinears within the large-momentum effective theory framework. The calculation is performed on a (2+1)-flavor ensemble with a lattice spacing of $a=0.076$ fm and proton momenta up to $2.3$ GeV. We combine kinematically enhanced nucleon interpolating operators, the Coulomb-gauge method, and high-statistics measurements to improve the signal quality at large momenta and quark separations. We use the static regularization-independent momentum subtraction and hybrid schemes to renormalize the bare matrix elements. The light-cone PDF in the $\overline{\rm MS}$ scheme is obtained from the quasi-PDF through next-to-leading-order (NLO) perturbative matching supplemented by next-to-leading-logarithmic renormalization-group resummation. With reduced uncertainties, the resulting PDF is consistent with our previous calculation on a similar lattice ensemble with $a=0.06$~fm and a valence pion mass of 300 MeV. Notably, despite the reduced uncertainties, the agreement with the phenomenological PDF persists over the longitudinal momentum fraction range $0.25\lesssim|x|\lesssim0.75$, where the lattice calculation is expected to be reliable. This agreement is further supported by a comparison in coordinate space, where our short-distance lattice matrix elements are consistent with those obtained from the phenomenological PDF through NLO matching. In particular, we observe close agreement in the imaginary part for the first time. Together, these results represent an encouraging step toward precision benchmarking of lattice QCD calculations of PDFs.

</details>


### [121] [The structure of the pion at large $N_c$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12771)
*Isaac Zhu,Fernando Romero-López,Phiala E. Shanahan*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the pion's quark momentum fraction ⟨x⟩_q in the large-N_c limit using the TEK reduced model with twisted-mass fermions, finding ⟨x⟩_q = 0.267(105) at 2 GeV in MS-bar, significantly smaller than N_c=3 results — suggesting gluons dominate light-cone momentum in the large-N_c limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large-N_c behavior of the pion's partonic structure, specifically how the light-cone momentum is distributed between quarks and gluons, by isolating the quark momentum fraction ⟨x⟩_q in the N_c → ∞ limit where gluon contributions are expected to become dominant.

Method: Uses the Twisted Eguchi-Kawai (TEK) reduced model to simulate large-N_c QCD; generates pure-gauge TEK configurations at multiple lattice spacings; computes pion two-point correlators with maximally twisted mass fermions at various quark masses; extracts the bare ⟨x⟩_q matrix element via the Feynman-Hellmann theorem; applies one-loop perturbative renormalization to convert to the MS-bar scheme at 2 GeV; performs global continuum, chiral, and large-N_c extrapolations.

Result: ⟨x⟩_q^MS-bar(2 GeV) = 0.267(105) at physical pion mass in the large-N_c limit — a value ~40–50% smaller than typical N_c=3 lattice or phenomenological determinations (~0.4–0.5).

Conclusion: The small ⟨x⟩_q in the large-N_c limit implies that gluons carry the majority of the pion’s light-cone momentum, supporting theoretical expectations that gluonic degrees of freedom become increasingly dominant as N_c increases.

Abstract: We compute the pion quark momentum fraction in the large-$N_c$ limit using the Twisted Eguchi-Kawai (TEK) reduced model. Gauge configurations for different values of the lattice spacing are generated with the pure-gauge TEK action, while the pion correlators are computed for different quark masses with maximally twisted mass fermions. The bare matrix element corresponding to the quark momentum fraction is obtained using the Feynman-Hellmann method, and we use perturbative renormalization factors to connect to the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ scheme at $2\,\mathrm{GeV}$. A global analysis at several lattice spacings, pion masses, and values of $N_c$ yields $\langle x\rangle_q^{\overline{\mathrm{MS}}}(2\,\mathrm{GeV}) = 0.267(105)$ at the physical pion mass in the large-$N_c$ limit. This value is significantly smaller than existing $N_c=3$ determinations from phenomenology and lattice QCD, indicating that, in the large-$N_c$ limit, the pion's light-cone momentum is carried predominantly by gluons.

</details>


### [122] [A regularization-independent Information Criterion for correlated data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12877)
*Davide Laudicina*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Regularization-Independent Akaike Information Criterion (RIAIC), a generalization of AIC for regularized fits on correlated data—especially useful when covariance matrix regularization is applied, as in lattice QCD. Unlike prior generalizations, RIAIC estimates the model-dependent part of the correlated Kullback–Leibler divergence and is, on average, independent of the specific regularization used. Its Bayesian counterpart is also derived, and empirical validation on lattice QCD data confirms its consistency with standard correlated AIC under statistical fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard AIC generalizations for regularized fits depend on the specific form of covariance regularization, limiting their robustness and transferability across methods; there is a need for a regularization-independent criterion to enable reliable model and subset selection in correlated-data settings (e.g., lattice QCD) where covariance regularization is unavoidable.

Method: The authors derive RIAIC by redefining the information criterion as an unbiased estimator of the model-dependent contribution to the correlated Kullback–Leibler divergence, decoupling it from the regularization scheme applied to the data covariance matrix. They also formulate its Bayesian extension (BRIAIC). Validation is performed via regularized fits on synthetic and real lattice QCD data, comparing RIAIC-based selection against standard correlated AIC.

Result: RIAIC reproduces the model and subset selection behavior of the standard correlated AIC on lattice QCD data, with fluctuations consistent with expected statistical uncertainties; it demonstrates regularization independence empirically and theoretically, and its Bayesian extension is formally established.

Conclusion: RIAIC provides a robust, regularization-independent framework for model and data subset selection in correlated-data regression with covariance regularization—enabling principled inference in domains like lattice QCD where such regularization is routine.

Abstract: We generalize the Akaike Information Criterion (AIC) to allow model and data subset selection when regularized fits are performed on correlated data. While the standard generalization to regularized fits is inherently dependent on the type of regularization applied to the data covariance matrix, our definition, the RIAIC, is, on average, regularization-independent. The regularization-independence is achieved by defining the new Information Criterion as an estimator of the model-dependent contribution to the correlated Kullback--Leibler divergence. The Bayesian extension is derived as well. We test the new criterion on lattice QCD data by performing regularized fits and show that it reproduces the selection properties of the standard correlated AIC, with fluctuations which are compatible with the expected statistical uncertainties. These results enable the application of subset and model selection techniques to cases where regularizations are applied to the covariance matrix of the fitted dataset.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [123] [Pion and kaon electromagnetic form factors in a flavor-dressed contact interaction: the role of Nf-screening on dynamical chiral symmetry breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10166)
*Aftab Ahmad,Muhammad Abid,Niaz Hussain,Mumtaz Khan,Tariq Mahmood,Alfredo Raya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes electromagnetic form factors of pion and kaon using a symmetry-preserving contact interaction with flavor-dependent coupling, within rainbow-ladder truncation and proper-time regularization; it finds that charge radii increase monotonically with number of light flavors Nf, driven primarily by the quark core, not vector-meson dressing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how dynamical chiral symmetry breaking (DCSB) is screened as the number of light quark flavors Nf increases toward the chiral transition, and to assess the impact of flavor dependence on meson electromagnetic structure—particularly charge radii—within a controlled, symmetry-preserving framework.

Method: Uses a flavor-dependent symmetry-preserving contact interaction in the rainbow-ladder truncation of the Schwinger-Dyson and Bethe-Salpeter equations, with proper-time regularization; includes the dressed quark-photon vertex solved from its inhomogeneous Bethe-Salpeter equation; computes pion and kaon electromagnetic form factors and charge radii across Nf = 2–6; compares to NA7 data and one-loop chiral perturbation theory for Q² ≤ 0.6 GeV².

Result: Both pion and kaon charge radii increase monotonically with Nf; the bare-vertex (quark-core) contribution to ⟨r²π⟩ rises from 0.097 to 0.226 fm², while the vector-meson dressing contribution only modestly increases from 0.102 to 0.127 fm² despite rho mass dropping from 0.93 to 0.70 GeV; strange-quark mass moderately suppresses kaon’s response; results are robust under variations of screening function and infrared confinement scale.

Conclusion: The growth of meson charge radii with Nf is a robust signature of diminishing DCSB, dominantly encoded in the quark core—not meson dressing—highlighting the critical role of flavor-dependent confinement and chiral dynamics in hadron structure.

Abstract: We compute the electromagnetic form factors of the pion and the kaon in a symmetry-preserving contact interaction whose effective coupling depends on the number of active light flavors Nf , so that dynamical chiral symmetry breaking is screened as Nf grows toward the chiral transition. The calculation uses the rainbow-ladder truncation of the Schwinger-Dyson and Bethe-Salpeter equations with proper-time regularization, and it includes the dressed quark-photon vertex obtained from its own inhomogeneous Bethe-Salpeter equation in the same truncation. At Nf = 2 the model reproduces the established contact interaction values of the meson masses, decay constants and condensates, and the charge radii rpi = 0.447 fm and rK = 0.417 fm, which undershoot experiment by about one third, as is known for this class of interactions. We therefore restrict every comparison to Q2 less then or equal to 0.6 GeV2, confront it with the NA7 data and with one-loop chiral perturbation theory, and focus on the response to Nf . Both radii grow monotonically with Nf , and the growth survives every variation of the screening function and of the infrared confinement scale that we test. The growth is carried by the quark core rather than by the vector-meson dressing: between Nf = 2 and 6 the bare-vertex contribution to <r2pi> rises from 0.097 to 0.226 fm2, while the dressing contribution moves from 0.102 to 0.127 fm2 even though the rho pole drops from 0.93 to 0.70 GeV. The finite strange mass slows the kaon response only moderately.

</details>


### [124] [Evidence for quark+quark pairing in proton formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10259)
*Hui-Yu Xing,Dan-Dan Cheng,Craig D. Roberts,Jose Rodriguez-Quintero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quark pairing in baryons via nucleon fragmentation functions derived from parton distribution functions and jet cascade equations; it finds strong evidence (9σ) for diquark correlations, especially in d→p fragmentation, inconsistent with no-pairing models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether diquark correlations exist inside baryons by examining their imprint on nucleon fragmentation functions in high-energy collisions.

Method: Derives nucleon fragmentation functions using the Drell-Levy-Yan relation from nucleon parton distribution functions, then solves coupled pion/kaon/nucleon jet cascade equations; compares predictions with global fits and proton multiplicity data.

Result: Strong empirical evidence for diquark correlations, most prominently in d→p fragmentation; the z-profile from a diquark-containing proton wave function matches proton multiplicity data, while the no-pairing model is excluded at 9σ.

Conclusion: Diquark correlations are essential for describing nucleon fragmentation, particularly in down-quark-to-proton transitions, and are strongly supported by momentum-dependent hadron multiplicity data.

Abstract: The question of pairing between quarks inside baryons is addressed by calculating the fragmentation functions that describe production of nucleons (protons and neutrons) in high-energy collisions and comparing attendant predictions with phenomenological global fits to available data and directly with data on hadron multiplicities. The fragmentation functions are obtained from nucleon parton distribution functions using the Drell-Levy-Yan relation and subsequently solving a coupled set of pion, kaon, and nucleon jet cascade equations. Strong empirical evidence is found for the presence of diquark correlations. In comparison with global fits, $d$ (down) quark to proton fragmentation ($d\to p$) exhibits the clearest signals. The strongest evidence is found in comparisons with the momentum-dependent proton multiplicity distribution: FFs built from a proton wave function that contains diquark correlations deliver a $z$-profile that is fully consistent with available data, whereas that obtained from a no-pairing wave function is disfavoured by data at the $9\sigma$ level.

</details>


### [125] [Heavy-quark spin symmetry and Pauli exclusion in pentaquarks with three to five heavy quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12318)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates negative-parity hidden-flavor pentaquarks with multiple heavy quarks (c/b) using QCD sum rules and S-wave baryon–meson currents; it analyzes spin-dependent mass splittings, identifies dominance of two-hadron correlator contributions, reveals non-decreasing $5/2^-$–$3/2^-$ splitting in fully heavy systems due to Pauli blocking, and shows factorizability limits for flavor-separated currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand spin–spin and exchange-driven mass splittings in exotic multiquark states with three to five heavy quarks, disentangling heavy-quark spin symmetry effects from Pauli exclusion and hyperfine dynamics, while mitigating scheme- and width-dependent uncertainties via relative mass differences.

Method: QCD sum rules with complete S-wave baryon–meson interpolating currents ($J^P = 1/2^-, 3/2^-, 5/2^-$), operator product expansion up to dimension 10 (for $QQ\bar{Q}qq$), 9 ($QQQ\bar{Q}q$), and 6 ($QQQQ\bar{Q}$); decomposition of correlators into factorizable two-hadron and connected quark-exchange parts; comparison across charm and bottom sectors to isolate scaling behavior.

Result: 1) Spin splittings decrease as $\bar m_c / \bar m_b$ only for configurations with uniquely coupled light degrees of freedom; 2) Splittings persist unchanged when two light quarks reside in separate hadrons—consistent with heavy-quark spin symmetry; 3) In $cccc\bar c$ and $bbbb\bar b$, $5/2^-$ lies 126–228 MeV above $3/2^-$, with no $c\to b$ reduction—indicating Pauli blocking, not hyperfine interaction; 4) Quark-exchange contribution scales with heavy-quark mass only when heavy quarks are exchanged; 5) Flavor-segregated currents are exactly factorizable and blind to binding.

Conclusion: Mass splittings in multiquark pentaquarks are governed by light-quark spin coupling topology and identical-quark exchange symmetry—not universal hyperfine interactions; fully heavy $J^P=5/2^-$ states are robustly higher due to Pauli principle; QCD sum rule analysis confirms dominance of molecular (two-hadron) structure, with binding signatures requiring non-factorizable, flavor-overlapping currents.

Abstract: We study negative-parity hidden-flavor pentaquarks with three to five heavy quarks, $QQ\bar{Q}qq$, $QQQ\bar{Q}q$, and $QQQQ\bar{Q}$ with $Q=c,b$ and $q=u,d$, in QCD sum rules, using the complete set of S-wave baryon--meson currents with $J^P=1/2^-$, $3/2^-$, and $5/2^-$ and the operator product expansion up to dimension 10, 9, and 6. We focus on the splittings between states of the same baryon--meson configuration, which, unlike the absolute masses, are only weakly affected by the choice of the heavy-quark mass scheme and by a finite width. Configurations whose light degrees of freedom have a unique spin are nearly degenerate or show splittings that decrease from charm to bottom by a factor compatible with $\bar m_c/\bar m_b$, whereas configurations with two light quarks in different hadrons show splittings that are not reduced, as expected from heavy-quark spin symmetry. A decomposition of the correlators into factorizable two-hadron and connected quark-exchange contributions shows that the correlators are dominated by the two-hadron part and that this pattern holds separately for both parts: the quark-exchange contribution decreases from charm to bottom when a heavy quark is exchanged and is not reduced when a light quark is exchanged. In the fully heavy systems the $1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ states are degenerate within about $20$~MeV, while the $5/2^-$ state lies $126$--$139$~MeV ($cccc\bar c$) and $183$--$228$~MeV ($bbbb\bar b$) above the $3/2^-$ state. This splitting is generated by the exchange terms between the identical heavy quarks and does not decrease from charm to bottom, which points to the Pauli principle rather than to a hyperfine effect. Currents whose baryon and meson share no quark flavor are exactly factorizable at this order and cannot distinguish bound states from two-hadron states.

</details>


### [126] [Magnetic moments of the $Σ_c$ and $Σ_b$ baryons from external-field QCD sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12373)
*Halil Mutuk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes magnetic moments of Σ_c and Σ_b baryon isotriplets using external-field QCD sum rules, incorporating full heavy-quark mass dependence and photon/gluon couplings; it normalizes magnetic sum rules by mass sum rules to eliminate residue dependence, fixes the quark-condensate magnetic susceptibility χ via the proton’s magnetic moment, and reports results with comprehensive uncertainty quantification (Monte Carlo + systematic), finding light-diquark dominance and small, scheme-sensitive heavy-quark contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably compute magnetic moments of charmed and bottom Σ baryons—key probes of heavy-light dynamics in QCD—while controlling dominant theoretical uncertainties, especially from the quark-condensate magnetic susceptibility χ and interpolating-field dependence.

Method: External-field QCD sum rules with full heavy-quark propagator (including mass dependence and couplings to photon, soft gluons, and both); normalization of magnetic sum rules by corresponding mass sum rules to cancel the baryon residue; calibration of χ using the proton magnetic moment computed with the same interpolating field; Monte Carlo analysis for QCD input uncertainties; inclusion of systematic uncertainties from Borel window, scale choice, heavy-quark mass scheme, and normalization.

Result: Magnetic moments (in nuclear magnetons): μ_Σc⁺⁺ = 2.37±0.65, μ_Σc⁺ = 0.50±0.15, μ_Σc⁰ = −1.39±0.37; μ_Σb⁺ = 2.16±0.65, μ_Σb⁰ = 0.55±0.16, μ_Σb⁻ = −1.06±0.32. The light diquark carries most of the moment; heavy-quark contributions are −0.13±0.02 μ_N (Σ_c) and +0.013±0.003 μ_N (Σ_b). Results show significant sensitivity to heavy-quark mass scheme (MS̄ used).

Conclusion: The magnetic moments of Σ_c and Σ_b baryons are dominantly determined by the light diquark subsystem; heavy-quark contributions are small but non-negligible and scheme-dependent. The adopted normalization and χ calibration strategy substantially reduce model dependence, yielding predictions with ~20–30% total uncertainty—enabling meaningful comparison with future lattice QCD or experimental measurements.

Abstract: We compute the magnetic moments of the $\Sigma_c$ and $\Sigma_b$ isotriplets with external-field QCD sum rules. The heavy-quark propagator is kept with its full mass dependence and with its couplings to the photon, to soft gluons and to both simultaneously, and the magnetic sum rule is normalized by the mass sum rule of the same interpolating field, which removes the residue and gives the baryon masses within a few percent. The quark-condensate magnetic susceptibility $\chi$, which otherwise dominates the uncertainty, is fixed by the proton magnetic moment computed with the same interpolating field; the neutron moment then follows to about $5\%$. Uncertainties from the QCD inputs are evaluated with a Monte Carlo analysis, and those from the interpolating field, the Borel window, the scale of the heavy-quark mass, the calibration and the normalization are added. With $\overline{\rm MS}$ heavy-quark masses we obtain $\mu_{\Sigma_c^{++}}=2.37\pm0.65$, $\mu_{\Sigma_c^{+}}=0.50\pm0.15$, $\mu_{\Sigma_c^{0}}=-1.39\pm0.37$, $\mu_{\Sigma_b^{+}}=2.16\pm0.65$, $\mu_{\Sigma_b^{0}}=0.55\pm0.16$ and $\mu_{\Sigma_b^{-}}=-1.06\pm0.32$ in nuclear magnetons; at this order the results depend sizably on the heavy-quark mass scheme. The moments are carried by the light diquark; the heavy-quark contribution, whose separation depends on the interpolating field, is $-0.13\pm0.02\,\mu_N$ for $\Sigma_c$ and $+0.013\pm0.003\,\mu_N$ for $\Sigma_b$.

</details>


### [127] [Untangling flow-harmonic correlations in OO and Pb--Pb collisions at the LHC using event-shape engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10914)
*Suraj Prasad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how event-shape engineering (ESE) via q2 and q3 selections affects symmetric cumulants SC(m,n) — quantifying correlations between flow harmonics vm and vn — in oxygen-oxygen (OO) and lead-lead (Pb–Pb) collisions at 5.36 TeV, using EPOS4 simulations. It decomposes SC(m,n) via the law of total covariance into 'selected' (between qn classes) and 'residual' (within classes) components, revealing distinct ESE efficacy: in OO collisions, v2–v3 and v2–v4 correlations reside almost entirely within q2 classes (limiting ESE control), whereas in Pb–Pb, q2/q3 selections independently modulate the two factors of v5’s nonlinear coupling — enabling experimental isolation of nonlinear response contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
