Motivation: To assess the effectiveness of event-shape engineering (ESE) — specifically q2 and q3 selections — in controlling nonlinear hydrodynamic response contributions to symmetric cumulants SC(m,n) in small (OO) vs. large (Pb–Pb) collision systems, where limited forward multiplicity in light-ion collisions constrains ESE power.

Method: Uses EPOS4 Monte Carlo simulations with ATLAS-like acceptance for OO and Pb–Pb collisions at √sNN = 5.36 TeV; applies event-shape engineering via q2 and q3 selection; decomposes symmetric cumulants SC(m,n) using the law of total covariance into inter-class ('selected') and intra-class ('residual') components to quantify ESE sensitivity.

Result: In OO collisions, v2–v3 and v2–v4 correlations are predominantly residual (i.e., persist within q2 classes), limiting ESE control; however, smallest-q2 central OO events show weakened v2–v3/v2–v4 correlations, linked to v4 approaching linear response. In Pb–Pb collisions, q2 and q3 selections act separately on the two factors of v5’s nonlinear coupling, allowing experimental disentanglement of those contributions.

Conclusion: ESE efficacy for isolating nonlinear flow response is system-size dependent: it is strongly constrained in small OO systems due to dominant intra-class correlations, but enables factorized control and experimental isolation of nonlinear coupling terms in larger Pb–Pb systems — validating ESE as a tool for probing hydrodynamic nonlinearity in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: Symmetric cumulants $\mathrm{SC}(m,n)$ measure the correlation between the event-by-event fluctuations of the flow harmonics $v_m$ and $v_n$, which, for higher harmonics, is influenced by the nonlinear response to the elliptic ($v_2$) and triangular ($v_3$) flow. Event-shape engineering, which selects events by the reduced flow vector $q_n$, is expected to control this contribution. However, its power is limited by the small forward multiplicity in light-ion collisions. In this work, we study the effect of the $q_2$ and $q_3$ selections on the symmetric cumulants in OO and Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=5.36$~TeV using EPOS4 with an ATLAS-like acceptance. Using the law of total covariance, the symmetric cumulant is separated into a selected part, which follows the differences between the $q_n$ classes, and a residual part within them. It is observed that in OO collisions the correlations of $v_2$ with $v_3$ and $v_4$ remain almost entirely within the $q_2$ classes, unlike in Pb--Pb collisions. Nevertheless, events with the smallest $q_2$ in central OO collisions show weaker correlations of $v_2$ with $v_3$ and $v_4$, which is attributed to $v_4$ approaching its linear response. In Pb--Pb collisions, the $q_2$ and $q_3$ selections act separately on the two factors of the nonlinear coupling of $v_5$, which establishes a way to isolate them experimentally.

</details>


### [128] [Precision Fragmentation Functions from Modern COMPASS SIDIS Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11237)
*Maryam Soleymaninia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents three global determinations of collinear fragmentation functions (FFs) for pions, kaons, and unidentified charged hadrons, combining $e^+e^-$ annihilation and charge-separated SIDIS data—including new COMPASS 2025 proton-target and revised 2026 isoscalar-target results—using neural-network parametrizations, Monte Carlo replicas, and NLO/NNLO QCD frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision and flavor separation of collinear fragmentation functions—especially for light-quark and gluon channels—by leveraging newly available high-statistics, charge-separated SIDIS data and state-of-the-art theoretical and statistical tools.

Method: Global QCD analysis using flexible neural-network parametrizations of FFs; inclusion of $e^+e^-$ annihilation and charge-separated SIDIS data (including 2025 COMPASS proton-target and revised 2026 isoscalar-target datasets); Monte Carlo replica method for uncertainty quantification; NLO and NNLO perturbative QCD calculations.

Result: HAPS-PiFF1.0, HAPS-KaFF1.0, and HAPS-hFF1.0 FF sets are produced; proton and isoscalar SIDIS data significantly enhance flavor discrimination (e.g., u/d/s quark fragmentation), while gluon fragmentation remains poorly constrained; NNLO corrections yield measurable shifts in central values and uncertainties, especially at intermediate $z$.

Conclusion: The new analyses represent a significant advance in global FF determinations, with improved flavor sensitivity from modern SIDIS data; however, gluon fragmentation remains a key limitation, calling for future measurements (e.g., jet substructure in $ep$ or $pp$ collisions, polarized SIDIS, or $e^+e^-$ event shapes) to break degeneracies.

Abstract: We present a comparative account of three global determinations of collinear fragmentation functions (FFs) for charged pions, charged kaons, and unidentified charged hadrons. The HAPS-PiFF1.0, HAPS-KaFF1.0, and HAPS-hFF1.0 analyses combine single-inclusive $e^+e^-$ annihilation with charge-separated semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (SIDIS), including the 2025 COMPASS proton-target measurements and the revised 2026 isoscalar-target results. Flexible neural-network parametrizations, Monte Carlo replicas, and NLO/NNLO perturbative setups are employed. The proton and isoscalar samples sharpen the flavor sensitivity of SIDIS, while gluon fragmentation remains comparatively weakly constrained. We discuss the effects of perturbative accuracy, the principal limitations, and prospects for future measurements.

</details>


### [129] [Probing Exotic Electron-Electron Interactions via Adiabatic Spin Inversion in MRFM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11367)
*Chunlong Li,Shien Yang,Yiwei Huang,Yu Zhang,Fei Xue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an MRFM-based method using OSCAR to detect short-range spin-dependent interactions mediated by light bosons, deriving measurable mechanical signals (resonance shifts, second-harmonic force) and estimating improved sensitivity—especially for the V₂ interaction—at submicrometer ranges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable sensitive, short-distance searches for exotic spin-dependent electron–electron interactions mediated by light bosons beyond the Standard Model, which are poorly constrained at submicrometer scales.

Method: A magnetic resonance force microscopy (MRFM) scheme employing oscillating-cantilever-driven adiabatic reversals (OSCAR) of electron spins; uses a ferromagnetic tip as both polarized-spin source and field-gradient generator; models coupled spin-mechanical dynamics linearly; analyzes resonance-frequency shifts (V₂, V₃) and second-harmonic force (V₈); implements interrupted-OSCAR differential detection and distance-dependent measurements to suppress magnetic backgrounds.

Result: Derives analytically distinct mechanical signatures for V₂, V₃, and V₈ interactions; demonstrates background discrimination via differential and distance-dependent protocols; projects enhanced sensitivity—particularly for V₂—over submicrometer ranges, surpassing existing constraints in select regimes using realistic ensemble and single-spin MRFM parameters.

Conclusion: Adiabatic spin-inversion MRFM with OSCAR is a viable and complementary platform for probing short-range spin-dependent forces, offering a pathway to test new physics in unexplored parameter space.

Abstract: Light bosons beyond the Standard Model can mediate exotic spin-dependent interactions between electrons, motivating sensitive searches at short distances. We propose a magnetic resonance force microscopy (MRFM) scheme that exploits oscillating-cantilever-driven adiabatic reversals (OSCAR) of electron spins to convert these interactions into measurable mechanical signals. The ferromagnetic tip serves as both a dense polarized-spin source and a source of magnetic-field gradients, enabling force detection without magnetic shielding between the source and sensor. Within a linearized treatment of the coupled spin-mechanical dynamics, we derive resonance-frequency shifts associated with the $V_2$ and $V_3$ interactions and a second-harmonic force signal arising from the velocity-dependent $V_8$ interaction. We further investigate interrupted-OSCAR differential detection and distance-dependent measurements to discriminate exotic signals from conventional magnetic backgrounds. Using representative parameters from ensemble and single-spin MRFM experiments, we estimate the projected sensitivity under specified noise and statistical assumptions. The results suggest that the proposed scheme could provide complementary sensitivity to short-range spin-dependent interactions, with the potential to improve existing constraints on $V_2$ over selected submicrometer interaction ranges. Our study establishes adiabatic spin-inversion MRFM as a promising approach to probing exotic electron-electron forces.

</details>


### [130] [How to isolate charm production mechanisms using charm-anticharm rapidity and azimuthal angle differences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11422)
*Marcus O'Riordan,Peter Christiansen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses PYTHIA simulations to disentangle charm production mechanisms in hadronic collisions via $D^0\\bar{D}^0$ correlations, showing that $\Delta y$ selection isolates final-state splitting contributions; it motivates differential LHC analyses and extension to heavy-ion collisions to probe QGP interactions of charm vs. gluon-originated charm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Charm production involves multiple overlapping processes (hard scattering, final-state splitting, initial-state shower), making first-principles calculation difficult; experimental separation is needed to constrain theory and study QGP effects in heavy-ion collisions.

Method: PYTHIA 8 event generator simulations to model and classify charm-anticharm hadron production mechanisms; analysis of rapidity ($\\Delta y$) and azimuthal-angle ($\\Delta \\phi$) correlations between $D^0$ and $\\bar{D}^0$; comparison with LHCb Run 1 $D^0\\bar{D}^0$ correlation data.

Result: Hard-process, final-state splitting, and initial-state-shower charm populate distinct ($\\Delta y$, $\\Delta \\phi$) regions; near-side ($\\Delta \\phi \\approx 0$) correlations are dominated by final-state splitting, strongly modulated by $\\Delta y$ cuts; existing LHCb data show qualitative agreement but lack differential precision; heavy-ion extension would separate direct hard-scattered charm from gluon-splitting charm via medium-modified correlations.

Conclusion: Rapidity-differential $D^0\\bar{D}^0$ correlations provide a robust experimental observable to isolate charm production mechanisms in pp collisions and serve as a novel probe of charm–QGP and gluon–QGP interactions in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: Charm production in hadronic collisions proceeds via many different processes, which makes it hard to calculate from first principles. Using PYTHIA simulations, we study correlations between charm and anticharm hadrons and show that hard-process charm, final-state splitting charm, and initial-state-shower charm populate markedly different regions of rapidity and azimuthal-angle difference. In particular, selections on the pair rapidity separation, $\Delta y$, can strongly enrich or suppress the near-side final-state splitting contribution and thereby provide experimental sensitivity to the different production processes. A comparison with existing LHCb $D^0\bar{D}^0$ correlation measurements from Run 1 motivates more differential studies using the substantially larger LHC data sets now available. Extending such measurements to heavy-ion collisions could further distinguish medium modifications of charm produced directly in the hard scattering from charm originating from a parent gluon, providing a new handle on the different interactions of heavy quarks and gluons with the quark-gluon plasma.

</details>


### [131] [Spin entanglement in $pp$ and $pA$ collisions: high-energy TMD effects and fragmentation probes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11568)
*Michael Fucilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies spin-spin correlations of quark-antiquark pairs in pp/pA collisions using k_T-factorization and shockwave formalism, deriving full spin-density matrices for inclusive and diffractive channels; it reveals cos(2φ)-modulated quantum entanglement measures (concurrence, Rényi entropy, discord) driven by linearly polarized gluons and six small-x TMDs, with BFKL enhancement and saturation suppression in inclusive production, while diffraction yields robust, phase-space-universal entanglement and Bell nonlocality due to rank-2 eikonal Pomeron exchange; experimental access is proposed via Collins/dihadron fragmentation functions and double-Collins asymmetries as entanglement witnesses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum entanglement and spin correlations of quark-antiquark pairs in high-energy hadronic collisions as novel probes of small-x QCD dynamics—particularly linearly polarized gluon distributions and saturation effects—and to establish connections between quantum information measures (concurrence, discord, Rényi entropy) and QCD factorization in both inclusive and diffractive regimes.

Method: Uses k_T-factorization within the shockwave (CGC) formalism to compute the full spin-density matrix for back-to-back q\bar{q} production in unpolarized pp/pA collisions; distinguishes inclusive and diffractive channels; analytically constructs density matrices under eikonal Pomeron exchange for diffraction; identifies rank-2 structure with maximally mixed marginals; computes concurrence, stabilizer Rényi entropy, and quantum discord; analyzes cos(2φ) modulations arising from linearly polarized gluon TMDs; contrasts BFKL (dilute) vs. saturation (dense target) limits.

Result: In inclusive production: cos(2φ) modulation of entanglement measures emerges, governed by six small-x gluon TMDs; maximal at q = 0 and q-independent in BFKL limit, strongly suppressed by saturation; in diffraction: q\bar{q} pair is always entangled with identical entanglement signatures as in diffractive DIS, due to rank-2 spin-density matrix with maximally mixed marginals—ensuring equivalence of entanglement and Bell nonlocality even for mixed states across full phase space; double-Collins asymmetries for massless quarks serve as entanglement witnesses.

Conclusion: Spin-spin correlations of q\bar{q} pairs encode fundamental aspects of small-x gluon polarization and saturation physics; entanglement is a robust, measurable signature distinguishing dilute (BFKL) and saturated (CGC) regimes; diffraction provides a clean, universal testbed for QCD-induced quantum correlations due to its exclusive eikonal nature; proposed observables (double-Collins asymmetries, dihadron fragmentation) open a new avenue for quantum information–QCD synergy in collider experiments.

Abstract: Spin-spin correlations of a quark-antiquark pair produced in proton-proton or proton-Ion collisions are investigated in $k_T$-factorization within the shockwave formalism. We construct the full spin-density matrix of both inclusive and diffractive channels in the limit in which the two quarks are back-to-back. In the inclusive channel, due to linearly polarized gluons, we show that a $\cos (2 \phi)$-modulation of the concurrence, stabilizer Rényi entropy, and quantum discord emerges and depends on the interplay between six small-$x$ gluon TMDs. The modulation is predicted to be maximally strong and independent of the transverse momentum imbalance, $q$, from a dilute (BFKL) perspective, while it is considerably suppressed by saturation effects. In diffraction, we observe that the pair is always entangled and exhibits the same properties found in diffractive DIS. We explain how these properties follow directly from the exclusive and eikonal nature of the Pomeron exchange. We trace this behavior to a spin-density matrix of rank at most two with maximally mixed marginals. For this class of states, entanglement and Bell nonlocality coincide even when the state is mixed, while the explicit form of the light-front helicity amplitudes ensures that both persist in the whole phase space. We suggest accessing the spin correlations in unpolarized hadron collisions through the Collins and dihadron fragmentation functions. We point out that, in inclusive production, for massless quarks, suitably chosen double-Collins asymmetries provide promising entanglement witnesses.

</details>


### [132] [HEART: a resonance-cascade model of soft hadron production in p+p collisions at 158 GeV/c](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11570)
*Ondrej Chvala*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the HEART model, an event-level implementation of Fischer et al.'s two-component (target/projectile) interpretation of NA49 p+p data at 158 GeV/c, modeling hadronic excitation and resonance-driven de-excitation with exact conservation laws and one-pion exchange for leading neutrons; it achieves superior χ² fit to identified-hadron spectra vs. EPOS-LHC, UrQMD, PYTHIA 8, requires no central source, and identifies key fragmentation-region observables distinguishing cascade dynamics from string/fireball paradigms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an event-level realization of Fischer et al.'s two-component (target/projectile excited-system) interpretation of NA49 identified-hadron spectra in p+p collisions at 158 GeV/c, and to test whether a resonance-based cascade picture—without a central fireball or string fragmentation—can quantitatively describe the full set of double-differential cross sections and neutron data.

Method: Developed HEART (Hadronic Excitations And Resonance Transitions): color-singlet excited systems formed via single/double diffraction and non-diffractive exchange; below ~1.7–2.2 GeV, systems are catalogued resonances; above, resonant continuum; de-excitation proceeds via successive two-body transitions obeying exact conservation laws (energy, momentum, charge, baryon number, strangeness, isospin); leading neutrons generated by one-pion exchange; 14 parameters fitted to 1254 identified-hadron (π±, K±, p, p̄) double-differential cross section points + 8 neutron points under a correlated systematic error model with 5% floor.

Result: HEART achieves χ² = 2263 / 1248 d.o.f.; outperforms EPOS-LHC, UrQMD, PYTHIA 8 (χ² 4.2–14.4× higher); reproduces forward K⁺ deficit, K*⁰ excess, flat Λ xF, and Ξ⁻ yield (61% of NA61/SHINE); reveals linear charged-multiplicity growth with excited-system mass (steeper than e⁺e⁻); identifies fragmentation-region observables (e.g., hyperon production kinematics, leading-particle correlations) as discriminants between cascade and string/fireball models.

Conclusion: The HEART model validates a purely hadronic, resonance-cascade picture of high-energy p+p collisions without invoking a central source, string fragmentation, or thermal fireballs; its success suggests that fragmentation-region dynamics—governed by quantum-number-conserving resonance transitions and meson exchange—are sufficient to explain a broad range of identified-hadron spectra, challenging prevailing Monte Carlo paradigms.

Abstract: Fischer and co-workers interpret the NA49 identified-hadron spectra in p+p collisions at 158 GeV/c as the sum of a target and a projectile component, each the decay of an excited hadronic system. This paper supplies an event-level realization of that picture, the HEART model (Hadronic Excitations And Resonance Transitions), and tests it on the published NA49 data. Single diffraction, double diffraction, and non-diffractive exchange produce color-singlet excited systems. Each system is a catalogued resonance below a duality boundary of 1.7 to 2.2 GeV or a member of a resonant continuum above it. It de-excites by successive two-body transitions with exact conservation laws, and one-pion exchange produces the leading neutrons. Fourteen parameters are fitted to 1,254 points of the $\pi^\pm$, $K^\pm$, $p$, and $\bar{p}$ double-differential cross sections and eight neutron points. Under an error model that profiles the correlated systematic uncertainties, with a declared 5% model floor, the fit yields $\chi^2 = 2263$ on 1248 degrees of freedom. On the same points, EPOS-LHC, UrQMD, and PYTHIA 8 at their published tunes give $\chi^2$ values 4.2 to 14.4 times higher. The model needs no central source. The forward $K^+$ deficit, the forward $K^*(892)^0$ excess, and the flat $\Lambda$ $x_F$ distribution point to the momentum sharing at the vertex where the leading baryon turns into a hyperon. The $\Xi^-$ reaches 0.61 of the NA61/SHINE yield. The charged multiplicity of a heavy excited system grows linearly with its mass, faster than in $e^+e^-$ annihilation. The observables that distinguish a cascade from string and fireball models lie in the fragmentation region, where wide-acceptance fixed-target spectrometers can measure them.

</details>


### [133] [A possible signature of neutrino spin precession in the J-PARC muon $g-2$ experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12749)
*S. Carneiro,F. C. Sobrinho*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the angular momentum of undetected neutrino pairs in muon decay contributes to the uncertainty in the muon magnetic anomaly (g−2) measurement, comparing Fermilab and J-PARC E34 experiments; it finds a statistically distinguishable correction difference (~2.36σ) between them, and proposes a higher-sensitivity test via varying muon beam energy at J-PARC (~4.88σ).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and test a previously proposed neutrino angular-momentum contribution to the muon g−2 anomaly uncertainty—distinct from hadronic contributions—by exploiting differences in decay kinematics between high-energy (Fermilab) and low-energy (J-PARC) muon beams.

Method: Explicit integration of the longitudinal neutrino-pair momentum using the theoretical weak-decay positron energy spectrum and asymmetry; consistent event-weighting N(E)A(E); comparison of corrections across beam energies and positron energy windows; statistical significance estimation under optimistic E34 precision assumptions.

Result: The neutrino angular-momentum correction is smaller at J-PARC by 0.415 ± 0.176 ppm relative to Fermilab for their respective positron energy ranges; this yields 2.36σ significance. A dedicated J-PARC measurement at 245 MeV muon energy (vs. nominal) with 200 MeV positron threshold yields up to 4.88σ sensitivity to the effect.

Conclusion: The kinematic dependence of the neutrino angular-momentum correction enables an experimental test independent of hadronic uncertainties; J-PARC E34—especially with optimized muon energy—offers a clean probe of this subleading Standard Model effect in muon decay.

Abstract: We examine a possible signature of the neutrino angular-momentum contribution to the muon magnetic anomaly measurement in the J-PARC E34 experiment. In previous papers we argued that the angular momentum carried by the unobserved neutrino pair may leave an uncertainty in the anomaly inferred from the time modulation of detected positrons. At Fermilab the highly relativistic muons make the forward-emission approximation suitable for this estimate. The lower muon momentum at J-PARC requires the decay kinematics to be treated explicitly. We integrate the longitudinal momentum of the neutrino pair using the positron energy distribution and asymmetry derived from the theoretical weak-decay spectrum, and the same event-counting weight $N(E)A(E)$ is employed for both experiments. For positron energies between $1.5$ and $2.9$ GeV at Fermilab and between $200$ and $275$ MeV at J-PARC, the proposed correction is smaller at J-PARC by $0.415\pm0.176$ ppm, when the most optimistic E34 precision is assumed. The expected difference amounts to $2.36$ standard deviations if the future measurement is centred on this value. The calculation thus identifies an experimental comparison which may test the proposed effect independently of the hadronic contribution to the Standard Model prediction. We also examine the possibility of testing the effect by comparing measurements at two different muon momenta. With a fixed positron energy threshold of 200 MeV, the maximum sensitivity is obtained at a muon beam energy of 245 MeV. The predicted difference relative to the nominal J-PARC configuration has significance of $4.88\sigma$ for the most optimistic experimental sensitivity.

</details>


### [134] [Proof of multiloop high-multiplicity integrand reduction coefficients in the 't Hooft-Veltman scheme](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10065)
*Shengli Yi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides two independent proofs—recursive decomposition with induction and the multiple cut method using Cramer's rule—for a compact determinantal formula that computes coefficients in a partial-fraction reduction of Feynman integrands in the 't Hooft-Veltman scheme, and analyzes its behavior under kinematic degeneracies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish and understand a recently proposed compact determinantal formula for partial-fraction coefficients in high-multiplicity Feynman integrands within the 't Hooft-Veltman scheme, where affine-linear relations among propagator denominators enable structural simplifications.

Method: Two distinct mathematical approaches: (1) recursive partial-fraction decomposition combined with induction on the number of affine-linear relations among denominators; (2) multiple cut technique—clearing the common denominator, imposing vanishing conditions on complementary denominators, and applying Cramer’s rule to solve for the coefficient. Additional analysis includes examination of special kinematic limits and basis degeneracy.

Result: Both proofs independently confirm the validity of the determinantal coefficient formula; the paper further demonstrates that singular coefficients can cancel in combinations to preserve finite polynomial identities even when the denominator-product basis becomes degenerate.

Conclusion: The determinantal formula is mathematically robust across generic and special kinematics; its validity is secured by two complementary proof strategies rooted in algebraic recursion and linear-algebraic residue extraction, enhancing confidence in its use for automated amplitude computations.

Abstract: In the 't Hooft-Veltman (tHV) scheme, external momenta lie in a fixed integer-dimensional space, and the propagator denominators of high-multiplicity Feynman integrands obey affine-linear relations. Previous work used these relations to construct a partial-fraction reduction with a compact determinantal formula for its coefficients. We present two independent proofs of this formula. The first proof uses recursive partial-fraction decomposition and induction on the number of affine relations. The second adopts the multiple cut method. After clearing the common denominator, setting the complementary denominators to zero isolates one product, and Cramer's rule gives the complete coefficient. We also discuss the behavior of the formula at special kinematics and give an example in which terms with singular coefficients combine to preserve a finite polynomial identity as the denominator-product basis degenerates.

</details>


### [135] [Phenomenological explorations of a quasi-degenerate $Z'$ boson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10075)
*C. S. Kim,Donghun Lee,Gyeonghyun Min,Sechul Oh,Dibyakrupa Sahoo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a model-independent analysis of a quasi-degenerate Z′ boson near the SM Z mass, showing its impact on e⁺e⁻ → f f̄ cross-sections and forward-backward asymmetries—especially off the Z-pole—and derives constraints from N_ν measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate phenomenological consequences of a Z′ boson nearly degenerate with the SM Z boson—a scenario not excluded by current data but potentially impactful on precision electroweak observables.

Method: Tree-level calculation of e⁺e⁻ → f f̄ (f ≠ e⁻) cross-section and forward-backward asymmetry in the presence of a quasi-degenerate Z′; derivation of constraints using LEP/SLD measurement of the effective number of light neutrino species (N_ν).

Result: A quasi-degenerate Z′ significantly modifies both cross-section and forward-backward asymmetry, with the latter exhibiting enhanced sensitivity away from the Z-pole; N_ν measurements impose stringent upper bounds on the Z′–Z mixing angle and coupling strength.

Conclusion: Even without specifying a UV-complete model, a Z′ near the Z mass is tightly constrained by precision Z-pole and off-pole electroweak data—particularly N_ν—highlighting the power of model-independent phenomenology.

Abstract: We do a general model-independent phenomenological analysis of the curious case of a $Z'$ boson with mass close to that of the Standard Model (SM) $Z$ boson. From tree-level calculation alone we show that presence of such a quasi-degenerate or `practically' degenerate $Z'$ boson affects both the cross-section and forward-backward asymmetry of the process $e^+ e^- \to f \bar{f}$ where $f$ (with $f\neq e^-$) can be a lepton or quark. Most importantly the effect on forward-backward asymmetry is found to be more prominent away from the $Z$-pole. We provide constraints on such a $Z'$ from the measurement of $N_{\nu}$, the number of light neutrino species.

</details>


### [136] [Critical Temperature in Nonextensive Hadron Models: A Pressure-based Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10079)
*M. A. A. Ahmed,H. Zainuddin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how nonextensive statistical mechanics (via Tsallis entropy) affects the critical temperature $T_c$ of the hadron–quark phase transition in QCD matter, showing that $T_c$ depends sensitively on the nonextensivity parameter $q$ and the underlying hadron model (MIT, Dual, Fuzzy), with a physically robust estimate $T_c^w \approx 102\ \text{MeV}$ near $q=1$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile deviations from standard Boltzmann-Gibbs statistics—arising from intrinsic fluctuations, memory effects, and long-range color interactions in heavy-ion collisions—with reliable extraction of the QCD critical temperature $T_c$.

Method: Uses a pressure-based equation of state derived from Tsallis nonextensive entropy; solves for $T_c$ as the positive root of the pressure equation across varying $q$ and three hadronic models (MIT bag, Dual QCD, Fuzzy bag); analyzes monotonicity, root multiplicity, and model dependence.

Result: For $q=1$, analytic $T_c$ values are obtained: $102.3{-}106.1\ \text{MeV}$ (MIT), $86.5{-}87.8\ \text{MeV}$ (Dual), $113.1{-}121.1\ \text{MeV}$ (Fuzzy); at $q=0$, $T_c \sim 55\ \text{MeV}$ corresponds to nuclear liquid-gas transition; weighted composite estimate yields $T_c^w \approx 102\ \text{MeV}$; physically consistent domain is $0.99 \leq q \leq 1.002$.

Conclusion: Nonextensivity significantly modulates $T_c$, but a narrow $q$-window around unity supports a robust, model-informed consensus value near $102\ \text{MeV}$, bridging non-Markovian dynamics and equilibrium QCD thermodynamics.

Abstract: The determination of the critical temperature $T_c$ in strongly interacting matter is central to understanding the transition between confined hadronic matter and deconfined quark-gluon degrees of freedom. In high energy nuclear collisions, intrinsic fluctuations, memory effects, and long-range color interactions can drive the fireball evolution away from purely Markovian thermalization, making nonextensive statistical descriptions relevant near equilibrium. We investigate the critical temperature of nonextensive hadron models using a pressure-based formulation derived from Tsallis entropy. Here we show that the qualitative structure of the pressure equation is controlled by both the nonextensive parameter $q$ and the model-dependent bag contribution $B^{\text{model}}$. For $q=0$, the pressure equations are monotonic and admit a unique positive root, whereas for $q=1$ the seventh-order term vanishes and analytic solutions can be obtained. For $q>1$, the negative $T^7$ contribution modifies the pressure behavior and can generate multiple positive roots at finite volume. %Cosmological bounds require $q \approx 1$, meaning $q \simeq 1.001$ in proton-proton phenomenology is a system-dependent fit parameter. For $q\geq1$, $T^{\text{model}}_c$ is $\left(102.3\leq T_c^{\text{MIT}}\leq106.1,\ 86.5\leq T_c^{\text{Dual}}\leq 87.8,\ \text{and} \ 113.1\leq T_c^{\text{Fuzzy}}\leq 121.1\right)$ MeV. At $q=0$, $T^{\text{model}}_c \sim 55$ MeV marks the nuclear liquid-gas transition. A weighted comparison constrained by the MIT bag model at $q=1$ gives $(T_c^{\text{w}}\approx102)\ \text{MeV}$, with Fuzzy and Dual QCD terms entering as model-propagation corrections. The interval $(0.99\leq q\leq 1.002)$ therefore provides the most reliable domain for a common physical interpretation and a composite estimate of the critical temperature.

</details>


### [137] [Collider-observable curvaton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10114)
*David Cyncynates,Takuya Okawa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Higgs-portal curvaton model, computing quantum and thermal corrections to its potential and showing they remain controlled; it identifies viable parameter space where the curvaton mass and Higgs-portal coupling are comparable—within reach of upcoming light scalar searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize the curvaton mechanism in a technically natural way via superrenormalizable Higgs-portal interactions, while ensuring radiative stability of the curvaton potential across cosmological evolution.

Method: Computation of quantum (loop) and finite-temperature corrections to the curvaton effective potential; analysis of curvaton decay dynamics—including off-shell dissipation in the SM plasma—and mapping of viable parameter space consistent with observational constraints.

Result: Quantum and thermal corrections remain perturbatively controlled; for high-scale inflation, curvaton decay proceeds dominantly via off-shell SM processes; broad viable parameter space exists, with a phenomenologically accessible region where curvaton mass ~ Higgs-portal coupling scale.

Conclusion: The Higgs-portal curvaton scenario is radiatively stable and observationally testable—light Higgs-mixed scalars in the relevant mass–coupling regime are within reach of next-generation experiments.

Abstract: We study a technically natural realization of the curvaton mechanism, in which decay is mediated through the superrenormalizable interaction with the Higgs. We compute the quantum and thermal corrections to the curvaton potential and show that they remain under control throughout its cosmological evolution. For high inflationary scales, the curvaton dissipates through off-shell processes in the Standard Model plasma. The viable parameter space is broad, and the region in which the curvaton mass and dimensionful Higgs-portal coupling are closest in scale lies within the discovery reach of proposed searches for light Higgs-mixed scalars.

</details>


### [138] [Real Intermediate States in Dark Matter Freeze-In from First Principles: Single Mediator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10204)
*Jannis Brirup,Adrian Finke,Michael Klasen,Luca Paolo Wiggering*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a first-principles Closed-Time-Path (CTP) quantum field-theoretic framework to correctly treat real intermediate states (RIS) in s-channel freeze-in dark matter production with unstable mediators, eliminating double-counting artifacts present in semi-classical Boltzmann approaches and incorporating full thermal corrections beyond the Hard Thermal Loop approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the double-counting issue between decay and annihilation collision terms in semi-classical Boltzmann treatments of s-channel freeze-in with unstable mediators, where finite-width regularization of resonances leads to unphysical overcounting of real intermediate states (RIS); no first-principles solution existed previously.

Method: Derives a consistent CTP-based non-equilibrium QFT formalism for s-channel freeze-in of scalar, fermion, or vector DM; uses exact resummed propagators for unstable mediators (including full mass and momentum dependence) via 1PI self-energy diagrams induced by thermal bath particles; derives separate evolution equations for scalar and vector mediators in thermal equilibrium.

Result: Demonstrates numerically that CTP-predicted relic densities deviate significantly from those obtained using common ad-hoc RIS-subtraction schemes, confirming the phenomenological importance of a unitary, first-principles treatment; captures thermal corrections systematically, beyond the Hard Thermal Loop limit.

Conclusion: The CTP formalism provides a unitary, self-consistent, and phenomenologically relevant description of s-channel freeze-in with unstable mediators, superseding approximate RIS-subtraction methods and enabling accurate predictions for DM relic abundance in such models.

Abstract: We investigate the treatment of real intermediate states (RIS) in freeze-in production of $s$-channel Dark Matter (DM) with unstable mediators, as such models feature a double-counting issue when decay and annihilation collision terms are simply added within the semi-classical Boltzmann approach and a finite width is used for the regularization of the resonance. While several ad-hoc RIS-subtraction schemes are available to mitigate this problem, a first-principles treatment in this context is so far missing. We therefore start from the Closed-Time-Path (CTP) formulation of non-equilibrium Quantum Field Theory and present a CTP-based description of $s$-channel freeze-in pair production of a single scalar, fermion or vector DM species. In addition to being intrinsically free of non-unitary overcounting, the CTP ansatz also has the advantage of systematically capturing thermal corrections known to be important for DM freeze-in. Separate evolution equations assuming a single scalar or vector mediator in thermal equilibrium with the surrounding bath are presented. The exact propagators of the unstable mediators are approximated by resumming one particle irreducible (1PI) self-energy diagrams generated by bath particles and are evaluated with their full mass and momentum dependence in contrast to the commonly adopted Hard Thermal Loop limit. The CTP relic densities are compared numerically with those obtained using the most common RIS-subtraction schemes for two example $s$-channel freeze-in models, demonstrating the phenomenological relevance of a consistent RIS treatment.

</details>


### [139] [$B\to$ invisible decays as probes of light new physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10218)
*Martín Novoa-Brunet*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Belle II excess in B+→K+Emiss decays, favoring a two-body contribution near 2.1 GeV; explores scalar/fermion pair alternatives (0.4–0.6 GeV); proposes an aligned Higgsed U(1)′ model with a vector-like top partner explaining the signal via a light Z′, whose visible dimuon decays are suppressed by adding a light dark fermion that also serves as resonant thermal dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain the Belle II excess in B+ → K+ Emiss using new physics beyond the Standard Model, distinguishing between on-shell production of light states and heavy particle contributions to b → s νν̄.

Method: Fit missing-mass distribution; compare scalar/fermion pair hypotheses; construct and analyze an aligned Higgsed U(1)′ model with vector-like top partner and light dark fermion; assess constraints from B → K* Emiss, longitudinal K* polarization, and invisible Bs decays; evaluate Z′ phenomenology including loop-induced Z–Z′ mixing and dimuon search limits.

Result: Two-body contribution near 2.1 GeV best fits the missing-mass spectrum; scalar/fermion pairs at ~0.5 GeV remain viable; the U(1)′ model realizes a light Z′ via top-partner loops, requiring a light dark fermion to suppress dimuon decays and simultaneously account for observed dark matter relic density via resonant annihilation near the Z′ pole.

Conclusion: The Belle II B+ → K+ Emiss excess points to new light on-shell states or modified b → s νν̄; a minimal U(1)′ extension with vector-like top partner and keV–MeV-scale dark fermion consistently explains the signal, evades current collider bounds, and satisfies cosmological dark matter requirements.

Abstract: Rare $B$ decays with missing energy probe heavy particles contributing to $b\to s\nu\bar\nu$ and new light states produced on shell. We discuss both possibilities in light of the Belle II excess in $B^+\to K^+\Emiss$. Fits to the missing-mass distribution favour a two-body contribution near $2.1\,\GeV$, while scalar and fermion pairs of masses around $0.4$-$0.6\,\GeV$ remain viable alternatives. Extracting their couplings would profit from additional measurements, including the $B\to K^*\Emiss$ rate and longitudinal $K^*$ polarization, together with invisible $B_s$ decays which constrain scalar-pair interactions. We discuss how an aligned Higgsed $U(1)'$ model can realize the single-vector explanation through a vector-like top partner. Loop-induced electroweak mixing makes visible $Z'$ decays unavoidable, requiring an additional invisible channel to evade dimuon searches. We show that a light dark fermion can provide this channel and also account for the observed dark matter abundance through resonant thermal annihilation.

</details>


### [140] [Constraints on quirks from a Geant4-based reinterpretation of the CMS search for heavy stable charged particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10273)
*Tamas Almos Vami*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets CMS heavy stable charged particle (HSCP) search data to constrain quirk models—exotic heavy fermions bound by a low-scale confining force—finding mass exclusions up to ~990 GeV depending on the confining scale Λ and quirk representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend constraints on quirk models—new heavy fermions with electroweak charges and low-scale confinement—beyond existing collider and dedicated experiments (e.g., FASER), by repurposing CMS HSCP search data with realistic detector simulation and likelihood-based statistical analysis.

Method: CMS 13 TeV, 101 fb⁻¹ Drell-Yan HSCP search is reinterpreted for quirks: quirk pair production, string-mediated dynamics, and ionization signatures are simulated using Geant4 in a parametric CMS-like detector; event selection is emulated and combined with public CMS likelihoods; validation performed against published heavy lepton limits (11% agreement).

Result: For SU(2)-doublet quirks: 870–970 GeV excluded at Λ ≲ 70 eV (independent HSCP regime); 280–320 GeV excluded at Λ ≳ 10 keV (doubly ionizing regime); no sensitivity in intermediate Λ (~70 eV–10 keV) due to turn-around inside detector. For SU(2)-singlet (FASER benchmark, two infracolors): 990 GeV excluded below 100 eV (where FASER is insensitive); 300 GeV excluded above 10 keV (beyond FASER’s 175 GeV limit).

Conclusion: CMS HSCP search provides complementary, often stronger, constraints on quirks compared to FASER in specific Λ regimes, highlighting the importance of reinterpretation across detector geometries and ionization signatures; the 'turn-around' region remains experimentally inaccessible to both setups.

Abstract: Quirks are new heavy fermions with electroweak charges that are also bound by a confining force whose scale $\Lambda$ is far below their mass. A produced pair therefore stays connected by a macroscopic string. We reinterpret the CMS search for heavy stable charged particles in 101 fb$^{-1}$ of 13 TeV proton-proton collisions for Drell-Yan produced quirks. The quirk dynamics is simulated with Geant4 in a parametric CMS-like detector, and the event selection is emulated and combined with the public likelihoods. The emulation reproduces the published limits on a heavy charged lepton within 11%. For an $SU(2)$-doublet quirk with one (two) infracolors, masses up to 870 (970) GeV are excluded for $\Lambda\lesssim70$ eV, where the quirks behave as independent heavy stable charged particles. For $\Lambda\gtrsim10$ keV, where the pair moves as one doubly ionizing object, the limit is 280 (320) GeV. In between, the quirks turn around inside the detector and the search has no sensitivity. For the $SU(2)$-singlet benchmark of the FASER search, with two infracolors, the limits reach 990 GeV below 100 eV, where FASER has no sensitivity, and 300 GeV above 10 keV, beyond the FASER exclusion of 175 GeV.

</details>


### [141] [Quantum Open Effective Field Theory: anomalous decoherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10287)
*Gabriela Barenboim,Daniel Boyanovsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum decoherence of a scalar field interacting with 'anomalous' critical environments using Schwinger-Keldysh formalism and second-order quantum master equation, revealing non-Markovian transient dynamics, power-law or logarithmic decay of the decoherence function Φ(t), and resonant enhancement at critically slow quasiparticles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond standard Markovian approximations (e.g., Fermi's golden rule) and study decoherence in strongly correlated, scale-invariant, or threshold-dominated environments where conventional spectral densities fail—specifically unparticle physics, infrared/kinematic thresholds, and critical baths.

Method: Schwinger-Keldysh closed-time-path formalism to derive an exact influence functional and Langevin equation; second-order time-local quantum master equation (Lindblad form) with explicitly time-dependent decoherence rates; spectral analysis of bath correlation functions with anomalous scaling dimensions and critical slowing down (dynamical exponent z).

Result: Decoherence function Φ(t) exhibits either Φ(t) ∝ t⁻μ (μ > 0) governed by spectral density near threshold and anomalous scaling, or Φ(t) ∝ ln(t) for infrared enhancement; for critical environments, a new dynamical scaling exponent emerges and resonant decoherence enhancement occurs at wavevector q* ∝ ω_q^{1/z} corresponding to critically slow quasiparticles.

Conclusion: Anomalous environments induce rich non-Markovian decoherence dynamics unattainable in standard weak-coupling treatments; time-dependent rates and scaling exponents are essential for capturing transient and asymptotic behavior, offering diagnostic tools for criticality and conformal sectors via probe decoherence.

Abstract: We study decoherence of a scalar field coupled to ``anomalous'' environments with critical behavior with the Schwinger-Keldysh including the influence functional, and the quantum master equation (QME) up to second order in the coupling. The Schwinger-Keldysh formulation yields a Langevin equation and the Lindblad (QME) includes \emph{time dependent rates} allowing to study transient dynamics. We focus on three examples featuring non-Markov dynamics that cannot be described with S-matrix/Fermi's golden rule rates \textbf{i) Unparticle physics:} a conformal sector beyond the Standard Model at an infrared fixed point featuring correlation functions with anomalous scaling dimensions, \textbf{ii) Threshold and infrared enhancement:} when the mass shell of the field coincides with or is near to a kinematic threshold of the spectral density of the bath, \textbf{iii) Critical environments:} the bath is at a critical point, correlation functions scale with anomalous dimensions and long-wavelength fluctuations feature critical slowing down. In all cases the reduced density matrix undergoes decoherence in the energy basis of the probe, and off-diagonal matrix elements decay as $e^{-\Phi(t)}$ with $\Phi(t)>0 $ the \emph{decoherence function} and $\Gamma(t) = \dot{\Phi}(t)$ the decoherence rate. In all cases we find that either transient or asymptotically $\Phi(t) \propto t^{-\mu}~;~ \mu>0$ with $\mu$, determined by the behavior of the spectral density at threshold and the anomalous scaling dimensions of the correlation functions, or $\Phi(t) \propto \ln(t)$ for the infrared case. For a critical environment there is a new dynamical scaling exponent. A probe of frequency $\omega_q$ is resonant with a \emph{critically slow} quasiparticle for $q_*\propto \omega^{1/z}_q$ at which decoherence is strongly \emph{enhanced}.

</details>


### [142] [A comparative analysis of semileptonic $B_{(s)}\to D_{(s)}$ and $B_c\to(η_c,B,B_s)$ decays in the light-front quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10407)
*Ho-Meoyng Choi,Bhoomika Pandya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a global analysis of semileptonic B-meson decays (B_(s), B_c → D_(s), η_c, B, B_s) using five light-front quark models within a unified Bakamjian–Thomas framework; it computes form factors via analytic continuation in complex transverse momentum space—bypassing q² parametrizations—and reports model-averaged predictions for R(D) and R(D_s) with uncertainty bands.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess systematic uncertainties in QCD-based potential-model predictions for B→D(∗) semileptonic transitions—particularly R(D) and R(D_s)—by isolating effects of confinement, radial mixing, and hyperfine interactions under a consistent current operator and spectroscopic input.

Method: Five light-front quark models implemented within the Bakamjian–Thomas relativistic Hamiltonian formalism; weak current constructed consistently across models; form factors computed via direct analytic continuation of spacelike overlap integrals in the q⁺=0 frame using a mass-weighted contour in complex kₜ space; no q² parametrization; external kinematics set by physical meson masses, internal Lorentz prefactors from free on-shell spinors.

Result: All five models yield consistent f₋(q²) extractions from (+,⊥) and (+,−) current components; form factors and differential decay rates reported as equal-weight model averages with pointwise min–max bands; light-lepton-averaged R(D) = 0.282–0.301, R(D_s) = 0.287–0.303.

Conclusion: The spread in R(D) and R(D_s) reflects residual model dependence in confinement and spin–spin dynamics—not current construction—validating the robustness of the analytic continuation method and highlighting the need for refined spectroscopic inputs to reduce theoretical uncertainty in LFU tests.

Abstract: We present a global analysis of the semileptonic transitions $B_{(s)}\to D_{(s)}$ and $B_c\to(\eta_c,B,B_s)$ by comparing five potential-model implementations of the QCD-motivated light-front quark model. We use the same fully Bakamjian--Thomas-based prescription for the weak current in all five models and keep their respective spectroscopic parameters fixed. We examine how confinement, radial mixing, and the treatment of hyperfine interactions jointly affect the form factors, differential decay rates, branching fractions, and lepton-flavor ratios. The internal Lorentz prefactors use the same free quark--antiquark kinematics as the on-shell constituent spinors in the weak-current kernels, while the physical meson masses determine the external kinematics. For each model and decay channel, the two independent extractions of $f_-(q^2)$ using the $(+,\perp)$ and $(+,-)$ current-component combinations agree within numerical accuracy. We perform a direct analytic continuation of the spacelike overlap integrals in the $q^+=0$ frame using a mass-weighted contour in complex transverse momentum space. This yields the form factors throughout the physical timelike region, including zero recoil, without parametrizing their $q^2$ dependence. Form factors and differential decay rates are presented as equal-weight model averages with pointwise minimum--maximum bands. The light-lepton-averaged ratios range from $0.282$ to $0.301$ for $R(D)$ and from $0.287$ to $0.303$ for $R(D_s)$.

</details>


### [143] [Search sensitivity for type-III seesaw heavy leptons in simulated \texorpdfstring{$pp$}{pp} collisions at \texorpdfstring{$\sqrt{s}=13.6$}{sqrt(s)=13.6} TeV at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10753)
*A.M.M. Elsayed,Yusheng Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper searches for TeV-scale heavy leptons from the type-III seesaw mechanism using 500 fb⁻¹ of simulated √s = 13.6 TeV pp collision data, modeling ATLAS with Delphes; nine lepton/jet-based channels and multivariate analysis yield a 95% CL mass exclusion limit of ~1.6 TeV under flavor-democratic mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the type-III seesaw mechanism—a theoretical extension of the Standard Model that explains neutrino masses—by searching for its predicted TeV-scale heavy leptons via pair production at the LHC.

Method: Simulated signal and SM background events corresponding to 500 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13.6 TeV are analyzed using Delphes to model the ATLAS detector response; events are split into nine mutually exclusive channels based on final-state lepton multiplicity and large-R jets; multivariate analysis is used for signal-background discrimination.

Result: Under flavor-democratic mixing (ℬₑ = ℬ_μ = ℬ_τ = 1/3), the expected 95% confidence level upper exclusion limit on the heavy lepton mass is approximately 1.6 TeV.

Conclusion: The analysis sets a competitive mass exclusion bound on type-III seesaw heavy leptons, constraining the parameter space of this neutrino mass generation mechanism at the TeV scale.

Abstract: A search for the pair production of TeV-scale heavy leptons as predicted by the type-III seesaw mechanism is presented. The analysis is based on a simulated signal and Standard Model background data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $500~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV. The ATLAS detector response is modeled with Delphes. Events are categorized based on the number of final state leptons and large-$R$ jets into nine mutually exclusive search channels. Multivariate analysis techniques are employed to separate the signal from the Standard Model backgrounds. Under the flavor-democratic mixing scenario ($\mathcal{B}_e=\mathcal{B}_\mu=\mathcal{B}_\tau=1/3$), the expected 95\% confidence level upper exclusion limit on the heavy lepton mass is approximately $1.6$ TeV.

</details>


### [144] [Sensitivity of jet and dijet observables to medium effects in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV using X-SCAPE/JETSCAPE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11012)
*Lakshitha Fernando,Claude A. Pruneau,Joern Putschke,Abhijit Majumder,Victor Gonzalez,Oveis Sheibani,Akash Raj,Dana Abdallah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates jet quenching signatures in high-multiplicity pp collisions at 13 TeV using JETSCAPE/X-SCAPE, comparing simplified (Bjorken brick) and realistic (event-by-event hydro + fluctuations) medium scenarios against vacuum baselines; dijet invariant mass shows the clearest medium response in the brick model, while realistic simulations show only subtle, localized deviations—especially in dijet asymmetry and groomed observables—highlighting limited sensitivity of current observables to quenching in small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish whether jet quenching—a hallmark of QGP formation—occurs in small systems like high-multiplicity pp collisions, where expected modifications are tiny and confounded by selection biases, MPI, and multijet backgrounds.

Method: Uses JETSCAPE/X-SCAPE framework to simulate jets in: (i) a simplified Bjorken-expanding 'brick' medium with varying length and transport coefficient (q̂), versus vacuum; (ii) realistic event-by-event hydrodynamic evolution with fluctuating initial conditions, comparing top-20% activity events in medium vs. vacuum. Full anti-kT jets (R=0.2, 0.4) reconstructed; observables include dijet mass, pT asymmetry, Δφ, groomed radius, and groomed mass.

Result: In brick model: dijet invariant mass spectrum exhibits strongest, ordered medium response (increasing with brick length/q̂ and more pronounced for R=0.2); pT and asymmetry changes are smaller; no azimuthal broadening observed. In realistic simulations: medium-to-vacuum ratios ≈ 1 for ungroomed observables, with only modest ⟨A_J⟩ increase and localized kinematic differences; groomed radius/mass show transient but non-persistent narrowing/mass shifts.

Conclusion: Dijet invariant mass is the most robust observable for detecting medium-induced modifications in pp within current modeling; however, all studied observables exhibit limited sensitivity in realistic small-system simulations, suggesting that unambiguous jet quenching identification in pp remains challenging with present tools and statistics.

Abstract: Establishing jet quenching in small collision systems, such as high-multiplicity proton-proton collisions at the LHC, is challenging because the expected modifications are small and can be obscured by event-selection effects, multiparton interactions, and multijet production. We examine the response of several jet and dijet observables to medium-induced changes in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV using the JETSCAPE/X-SCAPE framework. First, a simplified Bjorken-expanding brick medium is compared with a vacuum reference for several brick lengths and strengths of transport coefficients. Second, separately generated realistic vacuum and medium samples with fluctuating initial conditions and event-by-event hydrodynamic evolution are compared after selecting the highest-activity 20% of events. Full anti-$k_T$ jets are reconstructed with $R=0.2$ and $R=0.4$. The observables are dijet invariant mass, leading- and subleading-jet transverse momentum, folded azimuthal separation, dijet asymmetry, Soft-Drop groomed jet radius, and groomed mass. In the brick calculation, the dijet-mass spectrum shows the clearest ordered response: departures from the vacuum reference generally increase with brick length and strength of transport coefficient and are more visible for the narrower jet radius, while the jet-$p_T$ and dijet-asymmetry changes are smaller and no clear azimuthal broadening is resolved. In the selected realistic events, the ungroomed medium-to-vacuum ratios remain close to unity but contain localized kinematic differences, together with a modest increase of the mean dijet asymmetry. The groomed-radius and groomed-mass ratios show localized differences but do not exhibit a persistent angular narrowing or mass redistribution. These findings quantify the sensitivity of the studied observables to medium-induced jet modifications in the present pp calculations.

</details>


### [145] [Quark-level CPT violation in neutral-meson systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11033)
*Ralf Lehnert,Agnes Roberts*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies CPT and Lorentz violation in neutral-meson interferometry using the nonminimal Standard-Model Extension (SME) at mass dimension d=5, linking quark-level SME coefficients to meson-level effective Lagrangians via chiral perturbation theory, and derives new experimental constraints—up to 18 orders of magnitude tighter than prior bounds—using existing data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret CPT-violating neutral-meson oscillation experiments within a rigorous, renormalizable, and theoretically consistent framework—the nonminimal SME—at mass dimension d=5, bridging quark-level fundamental parameters and observable meson-level phenomena.

Method: Chiral perturbation theory is applied to match the d=5 quark-sector SME Lagrangian onto an effective pseudoscalar meson Lagrangian; detector geometry is explicitly incorporated to model interference observables in neutral-meson interferometry (e.g., K⁰–K̄⁰, B⁰–B̄⁰ systems).

Result: First phenomenological constraints on two d=5 SME coefficients (a_q-type or c_q-type, depending on operator structure) are extracted from existing neutral-meson oscillation data; projected sensitivities improve upon published bounds by up to 18 orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Neutral-meson interferometry provides an ultra-sensitive probe of d=5 CPT- and Lorentz-violating operators in the quark sector; the derived mapping enables direct, quantitative interpretation of precision meson experiments in the nonminimal SME framework.

Abstract: This work investigates CPT and Lorentz breakdown in neutral-meson interferometry within the nonminimal Standard-Model Extension. Employing methods of chiral perturbation theory for mass dimension d=5, a connection between the quark-level and the meson effective pseudoscalar Lagrangian is established. The resulting phenomenological signatures in neutral-meson oscillations with particular focus on the detector geometry are explored. This analysis permits the interpretation of such CPT tests within the d=5 quark sector of the Standard-Model Extension. Existing data are used to extract first constraints on two coefficients and to estimate achievable sensitivity gains over published bounds of up to 18 orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [146] [Quark- and hadronic-level CPT violation phenomenology of neutral-mesons at at $d=5$ of the SME](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11047)
*Agnes Roberts*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a first-order correspondence between hadron-level scalar effective field theory and quark-level d=5 nonminimal SME for Lorentz/CPT violation in neutral-meson oscillations, linking the quark-level a^(5) coefficient to mesonic CPTV observables—especially for kaons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between hadron-level effective descriptions of Lorentz/CPT violation in neutral-meson mixing and the underlying quark-level Standard Model Extension (SME) framework, ensuring consistency with C, P, T symmetries and spontaneously broken chiral symmetry of QCD.

Method: Analytical matching of effective Lagrangians to first order in CPT violation; imposition of symmetry constraints (C, P, T, chiral symmetry) to identify correspondence between the quark-level SME coefficient a^(5) and hadronic scalar EFT parameters.

Result: A concrete mapping is derived between the quark-level d=5 SME coefficient a^(5) and the phenomenological CPT-violating parameter in neutral-meson oscillations; a general expression applicable to kaon systems is provided.

Conclusion: The hadronic scalar EFT description of CPTV in neutral-meson oscillations is consistently embeddable into the quark-level d=5 nonminimal SME, enabling direct interpretation of experimental kaon results in terms of fundamental SME coefficients.

Abstract: This article summarizes an investigation of the connection between the hadron-level scalar effective field theory treatment of Lorentz symmetry violation in neutral-meson oscillations and the corresponding quark level $d=5$ nonminimal SME approach. The correspondence between the quark-level $a^{(5)}$ term is identified to first order in CPT violation (CPTV) based on the expectation that C, P, T symmetries and the spontaneously broken chiral symmetry of QCD should hold for both formalism. These can be related to the phenomenological parameter describing possible CPTV in the meson oscillations. A general result facilitating searches for kaons is given.

</details>


### [147] [Study of the weak radiative decay $B_c^+\to B_u^{\ast+}γ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11095)
*Jia Zhao,Tianhong Wang,Xi-Yu Ni,Zhi-Hui Wang,Guo-Li Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the rare radiative decay $B_c \to B_u^*\gamma$ in the Standard Model using the instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter approach, incorporating short-distance penguin, weak annihilation, and long-distance vector meson dominance contributions; it finds the LD contribution highly sensitive to the $\rho$ meson width, and the SD contribution dominates over annihilation, making this channel valuable for probing charm-sector FCNCs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe flavor-changing neutral current (FCNC) processes involving charm quarks via the rare radiative decay $B_c \to B_u^*\gamma$, which is forbidden at tree level and thus sensitive to loop-level SM dynamics and potential new physics.

Method: Instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter (BS) approach; includes short-distance $c \to u\gamma$ penguin, weak annihilation, and long-distance vector meson dominance (VMD) contributions; two treatments of $\rho$-meson width ($\Gamma_\rho = 0$ vs. experimental value) for LD evaluation.

Result: LD branching ratios: $5.0\times10^{-9}$ ($\Gamma_\rho=0$) and $7.3\times10^{-8}$ ($\Gamma_\rho=\mathrm{exp}$); SD contribution comparable to LD but one order larger than annihilation; overall sensitivity to $\Gamma_\rho$ underscores importance of resonance treatment in LD calculations.

Conclusion: The $B_c \to B_u^*\gamma$ decay is a theoretically clean and experimentally promising channel for studying charm-quark FCNCs within and beyond the Standard Model; precise treatment of long-distance effects—especially $\rho$ width—is essential for reliable predictions.

Abstract: We investigate the rare radiative decay $B_c \to B_u^\ast\gamma$ within the Standard Model by using the instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter (BS) approach. The short distance (SD) $c \to u\gamma$ penguin, weak annihilation, and long distance (LD) vector meson dominance (VMD) contributions are all included. In the calculation of LD contribution, we use two scenarios, namely the decay width of $\rho$ being set to zero or kept as the experimental value, which gives $\mathrm{Br}_{\rm LD}=5.0\times10^{-9}$ and $7.3\times10^{-8}$, respectively, indicating an important role of $\Gamma_\rho$ in the calculation. The SD contribution is comparable in magnitude to the LD contributions, but one order larger than the annihilation contribution. This shows the $B_c \to B_u^\ast\gamma$ channel can provide an important way to study the flavor changing neutral current processes of charm quark.

</details>


### [148] [Holographic dissociation of tetraquark and hybrid meson in the spinning plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11202)
*Zhenqiang Ren,Xiang Gao,Zhou-Run Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a holographic framework based on a spinning Myers-Perry black hole to study the dissociation of exotic hadrons — the tetraquark $Z_c$ and hybrid meson $\pi_1$ — in a rotating quark-gluon plasma; spectral functions in longitudinal/transverse channels show distinct, state-dependent melting patterns (2S before 1S, transverse more sensitive), enabled by a non-quadratic dilaton tuned to experimental mass spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified holographic description of exotic hadron dissociation (specifically tetraquark $Z_c$ and hybrid meson $\pi_1$) in a rotating quark-gluon plasma, avoiding computationally expensive dynamical binding energy calculations while capturing channel- and state-dependent thermal behavior.

Method: Holographic QCD approach using the planar limit of a spinning Myers-Perry black hole to model a spinning quark-gluon plasma; non-quadratic dilaton profile calibrated to $Z_c$ and $\pi_1$ mass spectra; spectral functions computed in longitudinal and transverse vector channels via gauge-gravity duality.

Result: Both boost parameter and temperature suppress peak height and broaden width of spectral peaks, more strongly in transverse channel; 2S states dissociate at lower temperatures than 1S states in both channels; $Z_c$ and $\pi_1$ exhibit quantitatively distinct dissociation patterns despite shared framework.

Conclusion: A single consistent holographic setup can differentiate exotic hadron dissociation behaviors without explicit binding energy computation, validating its utility for comparative thermal spectroscopy of multiquark and hybrid states in rotating QGP.

Abstract: We explore the dissociation of the tetraquark \(Z_c\) and the hybrid meson \(\pi_1\) in a spinning quark-gluon plasma described by the planar limit of a spinning Myers-Perry black hole. Both states are treated within one holographic framework, using a non-quadratic dilaton profile whose parameters are fixed by the \(Z_c\) and \(\pi_1\) mass spectra. Spectral functions are computed in the longitudinal and transverse channels. The boost parameter and temperature both lower the peak height and broaden the width, and the effect is stronger in the transverse channel. In both channels, the heavier 2S states melt before the 1S states. Furthermore, the two exotic channels display distinct dissociation behaviors within the same effective framework. This provides a unified holographic approach to compare how different exotic states behavior, without requiring a dynamical calculation of their binding energies.

</details>


### [149] [The first-order phase transitions and gravitational waves from an effective theory inspired by four-stack intersecting D6-brane models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11211)
*Haotian Huangfu,Tianjun Li,Qi Sun,Rui Sun,Wenxing Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational-wave (GW) production from a strongly supercooled first-order phase transition associated with SU(2)_{R1} × SU(2)_{R2} → SU(2)_R symmetry breaking in a Pati–Salam-inspired string-derived model; using finite-temperature effective potential, it finds sound-wave-dominated GW signals peaking at ~0.6 Hz with amplitude ~10^{-11}, within reach of deci-Hz detectors (DECIGO/BBO), and analyzes parameter dependencies and detectability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the stochastic gravitational-wave background from a theoretically motivated, UV-complete early-Universe phase transition—specifically right-handed gauge symmetry breaking in a Pati-Salam framework derived from intersecting D6-brane models—and assess its observability by upcoming deci-Hz interferometers.

Method: Computes the finite-temperature one-loop effective potential with ring resummation; models the first-order phase transition dynamics (nucleation, percolation, completion, reheating); evaluates bubble wall velocity, friction, and GW source contributions (sound waves, turbulence); performs parameter scans over wall speed, renormalization scale, and thermal screening; compares signal spectra to noise curves of DECIGO, BBO, and other proposed detectors.

Result: The transition is strongly supercooled (~few TeV), non-runaway (friction prevents wall acceleration), leading to sound-wave-dominated GW emission; peak frequency f_peak = 0.611 Hz, h²Ω_GW,peak = 1.232×10⁻¹¹; finite acoustic lifetime suppresses peak amplitude; signal is robust across reasonable variations in wall speed, renormalization scale, and thermal screening.

Conclusion: This string-motivated Pati-Salam symmetry breaking transition produces a detectable deci-Hz stochastic gravitational-wave background, offering a concrete, testable signature for next-generation space-based interferometers and bridging top-down UV model building with observable GW phenomenology.

Abstract: First-order phase transitions in the early Universe can generate stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds in frequency bands targeted by proposed deci-Hz observatories. We study the gravitational-wave consequences of the right-handed gauge symmetry breaking $SU(2)_{R_1}\times SU(2)_{R_2}\to SU(2)_R$ in a Pati-Salam effective theory inspired by four-stack intersecting D6-brane models. The ultraviolet construction fixes the gauge structure and particle multiplicities, while the scalar masses and quartic couplings are treated as renormalized inputs below the supersymmetry-breaking scale. Using a one-loop finite-temperature potential with ring resummation, we follow a strongly supercooled transition at the few-TeV scale through nucleation, percolation, completion, and reheating. The leading-order friction diagnostic does not support a runaway wall, so sound waves dominate the gravitational-wave signal, with turbulence as a sub-leading contribution; the finite acoustic lifetime suppresses the peak. For the fast-wall reference case, the spectrum peaks at $f_{\rm peak}=0.611$ Hz with $h^2\Omega_{\rm GW,peak}=1.232\times10^{-11}$, in the deci-Hz band targeted by DECIGO and BBO. We present its dependence on the wall speed, renormalization scale, and thermal screening, together with a noise-only comparison for several proposed detector configurations.

</details>


### [150] [An $SU(5)$ GUT with non-holomorphic modular $S'_4$ symmetry: fermion masses, mixing and leptogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11241)
*Xianshuo Zhang,Yakefu Reyimuaji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a non-supersymmetric SU(5) grand unified theory with S'_4 modular flavor symmetry and U(1)_PQ symmetry, using polyharmonic Maass forms for Yukawa couplings; it successfully fits all fermion masses, mixings, and neutrino oscillation data (NO/IO), predicts unmeasured neutrino parameters, and realizes thermal leptogenesis with baryon asymmetry matching observation via a single RH neutrino mass scale parameter b.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build a predictive, non-SUSY Grand Unified Theory that simultaneously explains fermion flavor structure (including neutrino masses and mixing) and the baryon asymmetry of the universe via leptogenesis, without introducing arbitrary Yukawa textures.

Method: Constructs an SU(5) GUT with non-holomorphic S'_4 modular symmetry and global U(1)_PQ symmetry; employs polyharmonic Maass forms as modular forms to generate constrained, predictive Yukawa couplings; performs global χ² fit to charged-fermion and neutrino oscillation data; solves density matrix equations for thermal leptogenesis; analyzes correlations between high-scale parameter b and low-energy observables.

Result: Achieves excellent agreement with all fermion mass and mixing data (|Pull| < 0.8 for all observables) for both neutrino mass orderings; predicts absolute neutrino masses, δ_CP, Majorana phases, and m_ββ; reproduces observed baryon asymmetry η_B ≃ 6.12×10⁻¹⁰ via thermal leptogenesis controlled solely by RH neutrino mass scale parameter b; identifies testable correlations between b and low-energy neutrino observables.

Conclusion: A fully predictive, non-supersymmetric SU(5) GUT is realized where modular flavor symmetry, PQ symmetry, and leptogenesis are consistently unified; the single parameter b links high-scale physics (RH neutrino masses) to both flavor phenomenology and cosmology, offering falsifiable predictions for next-generation neutrino experiments.

Abstract: A non-supersymmetric $SU(5)$ grand unified model is constructed based on the non-holomorphic modular $S'_4$ flavor symmetry, supplemented by a global Peccei--Quinn $U(1)_{PQ}$ symmetry that constrains the Yukawa couplings. Constructed from polyharmonic Maaß forms, these couplings allow the model to reproduce the observed charged-fermion masses and mixing as well as the neutrino oscillation parameters for both normal ordering (NO) and inverted ordering (IO), with all Pull values satisfying $|\mathcal{P}| < 0.8$. The model further yields specific predictions for yet-unmeasured parameters, including the absolute neutrino masses, the Dirac CP phase, the Majorana phases, and the effective Majorana mass $m_{\beta\beta}$. We then study thermal leptogenesis by solving the density matrix equations. For a given benchmark point, the final baryon asymmetry depends solely on the single free parameter $b$ that sets the right-handed neutrino mass scale, and an appropriate choice of this parameter reproduces the observed value $\eta_B \simeq 6.12 \times 10^{-10}$. Finally, the correlation between this scale parameter and specific low-energy observables provides a potential bridge between the fermion flavor structure and early-Universe cosmology.

</details>


### [151] [Direct Light-Cone Reconstruction of the $B$-Meson Shape Function in HQET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11246)
*Ao-Sheng Xiong,Shu-Man Hu,Ji Xu,Fu-Sheng Yu,Shuai Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quasi-shape function for the B-meson directly within HQET and computes its one-loop matching to the light-cone shape function, enabling a direct lattice HQET determination without intermediate full-QCD matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable robust lattice QCD calculations of the B-meson shape function when the heavy quark mass is comparable to or exceeds the inverse lattice spacing, leveraging HQET as a natural Wilsonian effective theory framework.

Method: Constructs a B-meson quasi-shape function in HQET; computes its one-loop matching to the light-cone shape function; derives renormalization kernels and matching coefficients in both \overline{MS} and RI/MOM schemes for the quasi-shape function, while retaining \overline{MS} for the light-cone shape function; works at leading power in the large-v^z expansion and ensures infrared regulator independence.

Result: Complete one-loop renormalization kernels and matching coefficients for the HQET-based quasi-shape function onto the light-cone shape function, valid in \overline{MS} and RI/MOM schemes, enabling first-principles lattice extraction of the B-meson shape function.

Conclusion: The proposed HQET-based quasi-shape function and its perturbative matching provide a theoretically consistent and practical pathway for nonperturbative lattice determination of the B-meson shape function, circumventing complications from full-QCD intermediate steps.

Abstract: The $B$-meson shape function encodes the nonperturbative residual-momentum dynamics of the heavy quark within heavy quark effective theory (HQET). From a Wilsonian perspective, HQET provides a natural framework for lattice calculations when the heavy quark mass is comparable to or exceeds the inverse lattice spacing. Motivated by this observation, we introduce a $B$-meson quasi-shape function directly in HQET and calculate its one-loop matching to the light-cone shape function, avoiding an intermediate matching step involving a full-QCD light-cone distribution. We determine the one-loop renormalization kernels and matching coefficients in both the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ and regularization-independent momentum-subtraction (RI/MOM) schemes for the quasi-shape function, while keeping the light-cone shape function in the $\overline{\mathrm{MS}}$ scheme. The matching coefficients are obtained at leading power in the large-$v^z$ expansion and are independent of the infrared regulator. Our results provide the perturbative ingredients for a direct lattice HQET determination of the $B$-meson shape function.

</details>


### [152] [Probing the Supersymmetric Grand Unified Theories with Anomaly Mediation at the Future Proton-Proton Colliders and Hyper-Kamiokande Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11254)
*Waqas Ahmed,Tianjun Li,Shabbar Raza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores SUSY GUTs with minimal AMSB under the 'grand desert' hypothesis, deriving upper mass bounds for gluinos (8 TeV) and light squarks (6 TeV) at high-scale GUTs (MGUT ≥ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV), making them accessible at 100 TeV colliders (FCC-hh/SppC); it also links lower MGUT to testable dimension-six proton decay lifetimes (< ~5×10³⁴ yr) at Hyper-Kamiokande, and provides benchmark points for both regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
