Motivation: To systematically probe SUSY GUTs in the grand desert scenario using next-generation experiments—specifically, 100 TeV hadron colliders (FCC-hh, SppC) for colored sparticles and Hyper-Kamiokande for proton decay—by connecting GUT scale, sparticle mass spectra, and observable signatures within minimal AMSB.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of minimal AMSB within SUSY GUTs under the grand desert hypothesis; derivation of sparticle mass bounds via renormalization group evolution and GUT-scale boundary conditions; computation of dimension-six proton decay lifetime from heavy gauge boson exchange; identification of benchmark points satisfying MGUT ≥ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV (collider-accessible) and MGUT ≤ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV (proton-decay-testable).

Result: For MGUT ≥ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV: gluino mass ≤ 8 TeV, first-two-gen squark mass ≤ 6 TeV — detectable at FCC-hh/SppC; full mass spectra for colored sparticles, sleptons, pseudo-scalar Higgs, and electroweakinos are presented; search strategies for gluino and winos outlined. For MGUT ≤ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV: dimension-six proton decay lifetime ≤ ~5×10³⁴ yr — within Hyper-Kamiokande sensitivity. Two representative benchmark points are provided.

Conclusion: The grand desert SUSY GUT framework with minimal AMSB is highly testable: collider and proton-decay experiments jointly constrain the GUT scale, offering complementary discovery potential; the critical MGUT threshold (~0.84×10¹⁶ GeV) demarcates which experimental channel dominates sensitivity.

Abstract: With the grand desert hypothesis, we investigate the Supersymmetric (SUSY) Grand Unified Theories (GUTs) with the minimal anomaly mediated supersymmetry breaking (AMSB) as a continuation of our program of studying SUSY GUTs at the future 100 TeV proton-proton Collider such as $\rm FCC_{hh}$ and SppC as well as proton-decay experiment such as Hyper-Kamiokande experiment. We show that for the GUT scale $M_{GUT} \ge 0.84 \times 10^{16}$ GeV, the upper mass bounds on the gluino and first two generation squarks are 8 TeV and 6 TeV, respectively. Therefore, the gluino and first two generation squarks can be probed at the $\rm FCC_{hh}$ and SppC. In addition, we discuss the mass spectra for the colored sparticles, sleptons, pseudo-scalar Higgs and electroweakinos. We also shed light on the possible search strategies of gluino and winos at the future 100 TeV colliders. Moreover, for $\rm M_{\rm GUT} \le 0.84 \times 10^{16}$ GeV, the proton lifetime for dimension-six proton decay via heavy gauge boson exchange is less than or around $5 \times 10^{34}$ years, and thus we can probe it at the Hyper-Kamiokande experiment. To be concrete, we present a couple of benchmark points representing the above two mentioned GUT conditions on $\rm M_{\rm GUT}$.

</details>


### [153] [Rapid, High-Precision Numerical Evaluation of Multiloop Feynman Diagrams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11292)
*Richard Easther*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Sinc function approximation method to numerically evaluate multiloop Feynman diagrams, demonstrating superior speed and precision over Monte Carlo for electron g-2 calculations beyond ~1% accuracy—e.g., six-digit 3-loop results in minutes vs. years for Monte Carlo—and shows feasibility for 5-loop integrals and broader applicability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the computational inefficiency of Monte Carlo methods for high-precision evaluation of multiloop Feynman diagrams, especially in quantum electrodynamics (QED) g-2 calculations where convergence is slow and resource-intensive.

Method: Uses Sinc function approximations to convert multidimensional Feynman parameter integrals into rapidly convergent sums; applies this to electron anomalous magnetic moment (g-2) diagrams up to five loops; benchmarks against Monte Carlo integration; explores extrapolation techniques for acceleration.

Result: Sinc-based evaluation matches Monte Carlo speed at ~1% precision for full 3- and 4-loop g-2 sets; achieves six-digit precision for 3-loop integrals in minutes (vs. years for Monte Carlo); successfully computes individual 5-loop g-2 integrals; demonstrates scalability and generalizability to other diagram topologies.

Conclusion: The Sinc function method is a highly efficient, high-precision alternative to Monte Carlo for multiloop Feynman integral evaluation, particularly advantageous beyond percent-level accuracy, with promising extensions to higher loops and broader classes of diagrams.

Abstract: We numerically evaluate multiloop Feynman diagrams by using Sinc function approximations to transform the integrals into rapidly convergent, multidimensional sums. We compare the efficiency of this approach to Monte Carlo evaluations of electron g-2 terms. We show that the two strategies reach parity at precisions of around 1% for the full sets of three and four loop diagrams. Beyond this level Sinc function methods are dramatically faster - computing 3-loop integrals to six digit precision takes minutes whereas Monte Carlo timings map to years. We show that the method can evaluate individual 5-loop g-2 integrals and discuss extrapolation techniques that would further improve performance. We note that the method appears to be applicable to more general diagrams.

</details>


### [154] [After the Higgs: Looking for the Universe Behind the Particle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11458)
*Veronica Sanz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The Higgs boson has transitioned from a discovery target to a precision probe of fundamental physics, vacuum structure, and cosmology; the paper advocates for a strategy of open-ended precision measurement, experimental diversity, theoretical risk-taking, and independent justification of fundamental science in the absence of guaranteed new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The shift in the Higgs boson's role—from discovery target to precision probe—coincides with the absence of theoretical guarantees for new physics within experimental reach and growing challenges to long-term funding for fundamental science.

Method: Conceptual and strategic analysis: revisiting the historical path from motivated discovery to precision physics; highlighting the Higgs field’s unique quantum properties (e.g., non-zero vacuum expectation value, scalar nature, coupling universality); arguing for an exploratory framework grounded in epistemic humility and institutional resilience.

Result: A coherent rationale for treating the Higgs as a gateway to cosmology and hidden sectors, coupled with concrete recommendations for sustaining high-energy physics: agnostic precision measurements, diversified experiments, protected theoretical innovation, and public advocacy rooted in intrinsic scientific value.

Conclusion: In the absence of a 'guarantee theorem' for new physics, the future of fundamental physics depends not on preordained targets but on cultivating robust, flexible, and intellectually diverse exploration—centered on the Higgs as both a tool and a compass.

Abstract: Fourteen years after its discovery, the Higgs boson has changed roles. It was once the object of the search, but today it is an instrument with which we interrogate Nature's vacuum, test quantum field theory and look for new principles. This shift comes at a time when, unlike before the Higgs discovery, we have no theoretical argument guaranteeing new physics within experimental reach. At the same time, securing long-term support for fundamental science is becoming increasingly challenging. I revisit the path from a theoretically motivated discovery to a precision programme, explain why the Higgs is such an unusual quantum field, and argue that this same peculiarity makes it a gateway to cosmology and other sectors. I then turn to a less technical question: how should a field plan for exploration when no theorem guarantees the next discovery? My answer is to pursue precision without pretending to know in advance which measurement will matter, sustain a diverse experimental portfolio, protect theoretical risk-taking, and make the public case for fundamental exploration on its own terms.

</details>


### [155] [Short-range dynamics of $X(3872)$ and $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ in a coupled-channel framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11472)
*Fang-Zheng Peng,Li-Sheng Geng,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the short-range dynamics of X(3872) and Tcc(3875)+ using a coupled-channel approach with contact interactions, pion exchange, and D* self-energy; it finds that pure molecular descriptions are insufficient for Tcc, suggesting mixing with a compact ccūd̄ state, and quantifies small but non-negligible short-distance (charmonium/tetraquark) components in both states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve residual tension between pure-molecular interpretations of X(3872) and Tcc(3875)+, and to assess the role of short-distance physics (e.g., compact tetraquark or charmonium components) versus long-range meson-exchange dynamics in these near-threshold exotic hadrons.

Method: Coupled-channel Lippmann-Schwinger equations including D*D̄/DD* channels, explicit pion exchange (with local term subtracted), D* self-energy (to incorporate D D̄ π / D D π three-body dynamics), scalar/vector-meson exchange saturation, Feshbach projection for channel coupling analysis, and Born–Oppenheimer EFT for spatial structure decomposition.

Result: The X(3872) pole remains 5–12 MeV below threshold even with full vector-meson local term; pion exchange moderately affects binding but strongly enhances isospin violation; Feshbach analysis shows Tcc(3875)+ cannot be explained by DD* interaction alone — it requires mixing with a compact ccūd̄ component; BO-EFT yields short-distance weights of 3.0–9.7% (X(3872)) and 1.3–14.4% (Tcc(3875)+).

Conclusion: A joint description incorporating both molecular and compact degrees of freedom is essential for Tcc(3875)+, while X(3872) remains dominantly molecular but with measurable short-distance contributions; retaining the local vector-exchange term within saturation is physically favored.

Abstract: We study the short-range dynamics in the coupled-channel description of the $S$-wave $D \bar{D}^*$ and $DD^*$ systems for $X(3872)$ and $T_{cc}(3875)^+$, respectively. Contact interactions are related through scalar- and vector-meson exchange saturation, while pion exchange is included explicitly with its local term subtracted. The coupled Lippmann-Schwinger equations incorporate the self-energy of the unstable $D^*$ mesons, thereby accounting for the three-body $D \bar{D} \pi$ ($D D\pi$) dynamics. Fixing the contact interaction from the $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ pole, we trace the predicted $X(3872)$ pole as the local magnetic dipole term from vector-meson exchange is varied. Retaining this term in full produces the shallowest poles, which remain approximately $5$--$12~\mathrm{MeV}$ below the observed $X(3872)$ mass. It is found that the pion exchange has a moderate effect on the binding energy and a stronger effect on the isospin violation for the $X(3872)$ state. The residual tension between the pure-molecular bound descriptions of $X(3872)$ and $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ is analyzed with the Feshbach projection. This analysis indicates a $DD^*$ interaction weaker than that implied by the purely molecular fits, and shows that the $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ state instead arises from the mixing of the $DD^{*}$ channel with a compact $cc\bar{u}\bar{d}$ state. We further examine the spatial structure using Born--Oppenheimer effective field theory. For separation radii of $0.5$--$1.5~\mathrm{fm}$, the combined charmonium and short-distance tetraquark weight in $X(3872)$ is $3.0$--$9.7\%$, while the short-distance weight in $T_{cc}(3875)^+$ is $1.3$--$14.4\%$. These results favor retaining the local vector-exchange contribution within the saturation framework and motivate a joint treatment of molecular and compact dynamics for the $T_{cc}(3875)^+$.

</details>


### [156] [Next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to radiative decays of fully heavy tetraquarks into quarkonia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11511)
*Yu-Dong Zhang,Zai-Fu Jiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to electromagnetic decays of tetraquark states $T_{4Q}=QQ\bar Q\bar Q$ ($Q=c,b$) into quarkonium + photon, deriving analytic short-distance coefficients at $\mathcal{O}(\alpha\alpha_s^5)$, quantifying sizable NLO enhancements (~1.8–2.9×), and estimating observable yields at LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions for electromagnetic decays of fully-heavy tetraquarks $T_{4Q}$, which are promising candidates for experimental discovery at hadron colliders; to assess NLO QCD corrections that significantly affect decay widths and reduce renormalization-scale dependence.

Method: Uses nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization; computes NLO QCD corrections to $T_{4Q}^{J^{PC}} \to V_Q/\eta_Q + \gamma$ decays; handles ultraviolet divergences via dimensional regularization and renormalization; includes interference between color configurations for $0^{++}$ states; derives analytic short-distance coefficients through $\mathcal{O}(\alpha\alpha_s^5)$ in the heavy-quark velocity expansion.

Result: NLO corrections enhance $T_{4Q}^{2^{++}} \to V_Q+\gamma$ by ~2.94 (c) / 2.33 (b) and $T_{4Q}^{1^{+-}} \to \eta_Q+\gamma$ by ~2.22 (c) / 1.83 (b); renormalization-scale dependence is reduced but remains non-negligible; predicted $pp$ yields at $\sqrt{s}=13~\mathrm{TeV}$: $\mathcal{O}(10^6\text{--}10^7)$ for $T_{4c}\to J/\psi+\gamma$, $\mathcal{O}(10^4)$ for $T_{4c}\to \eta_c+\gamma$.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are essential for reliable predictions of $T_{4Q}\to\text{quarkonium}+\gamma$ decays; the $J/\psi+\gamma$ channel offers a clean experimental signature via $\ell^+\ell^-\gamma$ invariant mass, complementary to di-charmonium searches.

Abstract: Within nonrelativistic QCD factorization, we calculate the next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to the decays of compact $T_{4Q}=QQ\bar Q\bar Q$ states ($Q=c,b$) into an $S$-wave quarkonium and a photon. Conservation of charge conjugation allows $T_{4Q}^{0^{++}}$ and $T_{4Q}^{2^{++}}$ to decay into $V_Q+\gamma$, with $V_c=J/\psi$ and $V_b=\Upsilon$, while $T_{4Q}^{1^{+-}}$ can decay into $\eta_Q+\gamma$. At leading order in the heavy quark velocity expansion, we derive the short-distance coefficients for these decay widths in analytic form through $\mathcal{O}(\alpha\alpha_s^5)$, including the interference contribution between the two color configurations of $T_{4Q}^{0^{++}}$. All ultraviolet divergences at NLO are exactly canceled by renormalization. The NLO QCD corrections enhance the decay widths for $T_{4Q}^{2^{++}}\to V_Q+\gamma$ and $T_{4Q}^{1^{+-}}\to\eta_Q+\gamma$ by factors of approximately $2.94$ ($2.33$) and $2.22$ ($1.83$), respectively. The dependence on the renormalization scale $\mu_R$ is reduced at NLO in most channels, although it remains sizable. Based on the NLO branching fractions, we estimate yields of order $10^6$--$10^7$ for $T_{4c}\to J/\psi+\gamma$ and $10^4$ for $T_{4c}\to\eta_c+\gamma$ in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13~\mathrm{TeV}$ with an integrated luminosity of $100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$. The $J/\psi+\gamma$ channels can be investigated through $J/\psi\to\ell^+\ell^-$ in the $\ell^+\ell^-\gamma$ invariant-mass spectrum, complementing di-charmonium searches.

</details>


### [157] [Hadronic final-state effects on open-charm production at LHC energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11569)
*Taesoo Song,Elena Bratkovskaya,Krzysztof Redlich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the PHSD transport model to study how hadronic final-state interactions affect open-charm observables (e.g., D-meson ratios and elliptic flow) in pp and Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies; it finds D-meson yields/ratios are insensitive to hadronic rescattering, but transverse momentum spectra and v₂ are significantly modified, underscoring the need to include hadronic evolution when inferring QGP properties from differential charm observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the role of hadronic final-state interactions—often neglected—in shaping open-charm observables, especially for extracting reliable QGP properties from differential measurements like pT spectra and elliptic flow.

Method: Microscopic transport simulations using the Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) approach, comparing scenarios with and without charm-hadron rescattering in hadronic phase; benchmarked against ALICE data and Statistical Hadronization Model predictions.

Result: Integrated D-meson ratios (e.g., D_s^+/D^0) show weak sensitivity to hadronic rescattering but reproduce system-size trends (e.g., D_s^+/D^0 increase from pp to Pb+Pb); in contrast, low-to-intermediate-pT spectra and elliptic flow (v₂) are strongly modified by hadronic rescattering.

Conclusion: Hadronic evolution significantly impacts differential open-charm observables (pT spectra, v₂) but not integrated yields/ratios; thus, neglecting hadronic rescattering introduces bias in QGP property extraction from such differential probes.

Abstract: We investigate the influence of hadronic final-state interactions on open-charm observables in pp and Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies within the microscopic Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) transport approach. Calculations with and without charm-hadron rescattering are compared with ALICE measurements and predictions of the Statistical Hadronization Model for charm. The relative integrated $D$-meson multiplicities are essentially insensitive to hadronic interactions. PHSD reproduces the weak system dependence of the nonstrange $D$-meson ratios and the increase of $D_s^+/D^0$ from pp to Pb+Pb collisions, consistent with the statistical-model trend from strangeness undersaturation towards equilibrium. In contrast, hadronic rescattering redistributes the transverse momentum spectra at low and intermediate momenta and enhances the elliptic flow. These results demonstrate the importance of accounting for the hadronic evolution when extracting QGP properties from differential open-charm observables.

</details>


### [158] [Threshold resummation for top-quark pair production in association with a jet at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11600)
*Bakar Chargeishvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies threshold resummation for the invariant-mass distribution of tt̄+jet at the LHC at NNLL accuracy in SCET, matched to exact NLO. It identifies limitations of the leading-power soft-collinear kernel—especially from missing initial-state contributions and mass-ratio dependence—and finds the resummed correction beyond NLO is large (up to ~33%) and not perturbatively controlled; thus, NLO remains the central prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for tt̄+jet invariant-mass distributions near threshold at the LHC, where large logarithms require resummation, but existing kernels may break down due to kinematic and mass-scale effects.

Method: Performs NNLL threshold resummation in soft-collinear effective theory (SCET), treating jet-radius logarithms at fixed order and matching to exact NLO. Validates hard functions and corrects published one-loop soft anomalous-dimension matrices. Compares leading-power kernel against exact real emission (including Drell-Yan validation) and analyzes deviations as function of τ = M²/S and M/mₜ.

Result: The leading-power kernel accurately models real emission only near strict threshold (emitted energy < 100–200 GeV); it fails for larger emissions due to missing initial-state contributions scaling with τ and growing sensitivity to M/mₜ. Resummed corrections beyond NLO range from 1–2% to 23–33%, depending on jet threshold (10–20% of M), and are not perturbatively stable. Hence, NLO remains the reliable central prediction.

Conclusion: Threshold resummation for tt̄+jet invariant mass at NNLL+NLO is theoretically compromised by unresummed power-suppressed initial-state effects and top-mass hierarchy dependence, rendering the resummed correction unstable and not under perturbative control; NLO should be used as the central result, with resummation offering no robust improvement over scale uncertainty or known NNLO/NLO+PS predictions.

Abstract: We study threshold resummation for the invariant-mass distribution of a top-quark pair and a jet at the LHC. We perform the resummation at next-to-next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy in soft-collinear effective theory, with the logarithms of the jet radius treated at fixed order, and match it to the exact next-to-leading-order (NLO) cross section. We correct the one-loop soft anomalous-dimension matrices in the literature and validate the hard functions. We compare the leading-power kernel with exact real emission, and validate this comparison on Drell-Yan production. At invariant masses up to about 1 TeV with a fixed jet threshold the method does not apply. At 1-4 TeV with a jet threshold of 15% of the invariant mass, the kernel describes the exact real-emission density close to threshold but fails once the emitted energy exceeds about 100-200 GeV. We trace part of this failure to initial states absent from the kernel, which depend on $\tau=M^2/S$, with $M$ the invariant mass and $\sqrt{S}$ the hadronic centre-of-mass energy. In addition, at fixed $\tau$ the difference between the kernel and the exact result grows with the ratio $M/m_t$ of the invariant mass to the top-quark mass. As a consequence, the resummed correction beyond NLO, for a scale equal to the invariant mass, ranges from 1-2% to 17-23% for different treatments of this region. The upper end becomes 13-16% for a jet threshold of 20% of the invariant mass and 23-33% for 10%. The range is not a bound, and the correction is not under perturbative control. We therefore keep NLO as the central prediction. We compare the possible effect of resummation with the NLO scale uncertainty, with the known next-to-next-to-leading-order corrections, and with NLO matched to a parton shower.

</details>


### [159] [A Poisson-gas approach to medium-induced gluon radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11606)
*Manoel Rodríguez Calvo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a new analytical and computational framework for calculating medium-induced gluon radiation in jet quenching, using a Laplace decomposition of the Yukawa dipole cross section to express the BDMPS-Z spectrum as a Poisson-averaged sum over Gaussian scattering configurations—enabling efficient, parallelizable Monte Carlo evaluation for arbitrary static or expanding QCD media.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The precision era of jet-quenching phenomenology demands quantitative control over medium-induced gluon radiation; existing approaches lack both analytical tractability and numerical robustness—especially under transverse momentum ($k_\perp$) cuts or for non-uniform/expanding media.

Method: The authors decompose the Yukawa (Gyulassy-Wang) dipole cross section via Laplace transform into a superposition of Gaussian potentials; then recast the fully resummed BDMPS-Z radiation spectrum as an ensemble average over Poisson-distributed Gaussian scattering centers, each admitting closed-form solutions; final evaluation uses Monte Carlo sampling of configurations.

Result: A master formula is derived—valid differentially in $k_\perp$ and for $k_\perp$-integrated spectra with arbitrary cuts—for static and expanding media with arbitrary density profiles; Monte Carlo evaluation is efficient and parallelizable; reproduces known first-order opacity results without cuts, but reveals significant deviations (relative) when $k_\perp$ cuts are imposed.

Conclusion: The method provides a unified, flexible, and computationally scalable foundation for jet-quenching calculations; it naturally generalizes to more complex in-medium parton cascade elements (e.g., finite energy fractions, color antenna emissions), and serves as a basis for future analytical approximations and precision phenomenology.

Abstract: The precision era of jet-quenching phenomenology requires a quantitative control of medium-induced gluon radiation. We consider the BDMPS-Z spectrum in the soft-gluon limit for a Yukawa (Gyulassy-Wang) potential. Writing the Yukawa dipole cross section as a Laplace superposition of Gaussians, we express the fully resummed spectrum as an average over Poisson-distributed configurations of Gaussian scattering centers, each of which is solved in closed form. This yields a master formula for the spectrum both differential in the transverse momentum $k_\perp$ and integrated with a cut on $k_\perp$, valid for static and expanding media with arbitrary profiles. Its evaluation by Monte Carlo sampling of the configurations is efficient and trivially parallelizable. The method reproduces the first order in opacity and published results without kinematic cuts, while with a cut we find sizeable relative deviations from previous results. We argue that the same construction extends to the more complex building blocks of an in-medium parton cascade, such as finite energy fraction and emissions off color antennas, and provides a natural starting point for analytical approximations. The code is publicly available.

</details>


### [160] [The $ϕ$-$η$ Puzzle and the Five-Layer Effective-Temperature Hierarchy in $pp$ Collisions at 2.76 and 7 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11612)
*M. Waqas,Wolfgang Bietenholz,Zhen-Yan Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a robust five-layer hierarchy in effective freeze-out temperatures of identified particles in pp collisions at 2.76 and 7 TeV, revealing sequential kinetic decoupling governed by strangeness content, cross-sections, and OZI suppression—not mass alone; the φ(1020) and (at 7 TeV) η mesons occupy the highest-temperature layer, challenging conventional hadronization models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the dynamics of final-stage strongly interacting matter via sequential kinetic freeze-out, motivated by the recent quadruple freeze-out scenario and the need to understand how particle identity (e.g., strangeness, OZI suppression) governs decoupling order in small systems.

Method: Thermodynamic analysis of transverse momentum spectra using a modified Tsallis-Pareto distribution to extract effective temperature (T_eff) and non-extensivity parameter (q) for identified particles in pp collisions at √s = 2.76 and 7 TeV; statistical significance of layer separation assessed via σ-levels.

Result: A statistically significant five-layer hierarchy in T_eff is observed at both energies: (1) non-strange hadrons, (2) singly-strange hadrons, (3) multi-strange hadrons, (4) light nuclei, (5) φ(1020); at 7 TeV, η joins φ in layer 5; all inter-layer separations exceed 3σ, top separation >7σ; T_eff increases with energy but hierarchy persists.

Conclusion: Kinetic freeze-out is sequentially ordered not by mass but by interaction strength—modulated by strangeness, hadronic cross-sections, and OZI suppression—implying that even in pp collisions, the medium exhibits QGP-like collective evolution sensitive to internal particle structure; the η–φ coincidence at 7 TeV poses an open theoretical puzzle.

Abstract: Sequential kinetic freeze-out in high-energy collisions probes the dynamics governing the final stages of strongly interacting matter. Inspired by the recently established quadruple freeze-out scenario, we present a thermodynamic analysis of transverse momentum spectra for a set of identified particles produced in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 2.76$ and $7$ TeV. Using a modified Tsallis-Pareto function, we extract the effective temperature and the non-extensivity parameter for each species. We observe a robust five-layer hierarchy in the effective temperature. At both energies, the layers are: non-strange hadrons, singly-strange hadrons, multi-strange hadrons, light nuclei, and the $\phi(1020)$ meson. At $7$~TeV, the $\eta$ meson joins the $\phi(1020)$ meson in the highest-temperature layer. At both energies, each pair of neighboring layers is separated by more than $ 3\sigma$, and the separation at the top exceeds $7\sigma$. The grouping persists at both energies, while the temperatures shift upward at 7 TeV. The observed pattern shows that the decoupling sequence is driven by an interplay of strangeness, cross-sections, and the OZI rule rather than by particle mass alone. Notably, the $\phi(1020)$ meson has a mass close to the proton, but a significantly higher temperature due to its OZI-suppressed coupling to the hadronic medium. The unexpected coincidence of $\eta$ and $\phi(1020)$ at 7 TeV presents a puzzle that may point to a common early production mechanism or in-medium effects, which are not yet understood. The five-layer hierarchy provides new constraints on theoretical models of hadronization. It suggests that the sequential decoupling of particles from the evolving medium is even more sensitive to their internal structure and interaction than previously assumed, with implications for the QGP-like matter formation in small systems.

</details>


### [161] [Nanohertz gravitational waves and asymmetric dark matter from a supercooled dark sector phase transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11694)
*Wonsub Cho,Ki-Young Choi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper links asymmetric dark matter to nanohertz gravitational waves from a supercooled first-order phase transition in a classically conformal dark sector, calculating thermal nucleation with NLOdet corrections and assessing compatibility with pulsar timing array data and Big Bang nucleosynthesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed dark-matter abundance and reconcile it with nanohertz gravitational-wave signals detected by pulsar timing arrays, via a thermally coupled dark–visible sector with radiative symmetry breaking and Affleck–Dine-generated asymmetry.

Method: Computes finite-temperature thermal nucleation rate in 3D effective theory including finite-density corrections, next-to-leading-order (NLO) contributions, and fluctuation determinants (NLOdet); models coupled thermal evolution of dark and visible sectors; performs parameter scans and compares GW spectra against PTA free-spectrum posteriors and BBN constraints.

Result: A MeV-scale supercooled phase transition in the dark sector produces nanohertz gravitational waves compatible with current PTA sensitivity for benchmark parameters; relic asymmetry and entropy dilution jointly fix dark-matter density; BBN constraints are satisfied under adopted thermal assumptions.

Conclusion: Asymmetric dark matter in a classically conformal sector with radiative symmetry breaking and delayed reheating can simultaneously explain the dark-matter abundance and generate detectable nanohertz gravitational waves—offering a testable link between particle physics, cosmology, and gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: We study the connection between asymmetric dark matter and nanohertz gravitational waves from a supercooled first-order phase transition in a classically conformal dark sector. The dark sector contains a real scalar, a complex scalar and a Dirac fermion, whose mass arises from radiative symmetry breaking. An additional Affleck--Dine condensate generates a fermion asymmetry and heats the dark bath before nucleation. The conserved dark charge fixes the asymmetry between fermions and antifermions, while entropy production during delayed visible-sector reheating dilutes the relic and determines the redshift of the gravitational-wave spectrum. We calculate the thermal nucleation rate in a three-dimensional effective theory with finite-density corrections, next-to-leading-order contributions and fluctuation determinants (NLOdet). We follow the coupled thermal evolution of the dark and visible sectors. For a benchmark chosen to match the observed dark-matter abundance, the transition completes at MeV-scale temperatures and produces a nanohertz signal under the adopted dynamical and thermal assumptions. We compare the predicted collision-dominated gravitational-wave spectra with free-spectrum posteriors from pulsar timing arrays. For a benchmark point and variations of the dark-sector parameters, the gravitational-wave spectra and dark matter relic abundances are compared, and compatibility with Big Bang nucleosynthesis is assessed.

</details>


### [162] [Origin of Tensor Polarized Structure of the Deuteron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11749)
*Satvir Kaur,Chandan Mondal,Xingbo Zhao,Chueng-Ryong Ji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that the deuteron's tensor-polarized structure function $b_1(x)$ is dominantly generated by hidden-color components—not the conventional $D$-wave—within a QCD-based light-front framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional nuclear models fail to reproduce the magnitude and momentum dependence of the HERMES-measured $b_1$, prompting investigation beyond nucleonic degrees of freedom.

Method: QCD-based light-front formalism incorporating hidden-color components and relativistic spin–momentum correlations; comparison of contributions from $D$-wave vs. colored-cluster $S$-wave sectors.

Result: A small hidden-color admixture generates substantial $b_1(x)$; the empirically constrained $D$-wave (0.85%) alters $b_1$ by only 1.7%; dominant contribution comes from the colored-cluster $S$-wave sector.

Conclusion: The tensor-polarized structure of the deuteron is driven primarily by hidden-color dynamics, not the $D$-wave component.

Abstract: The microscopic origin of the deuteron tensor polarized structure function $b_1$ remains unresolved. Conventional nuclear models attribute a nonzero $b_1$ mainly to the $D$-wave component of the deuteron wave function, but generally fail to reproduce the magnitude and momentum-dependence observed by the HERMES experiment. Using a QCD-based light-front framework with degrees of freedom beyond the conventional nucleonic picture, in particular including hidden-color components and relativistic spin--momentum correlations, we find that a small but nonzero estimated hidden-color admixture can be sufficient to generate a substantial tensor response ($b_1(x)$), while it is essentially insensitive to the $D$-wave component. The physical $D$-wave probability of $0.85\%$, fixed by the empirical quadrupole moment, changes $b_1$ by only $1.7\%$, with the dominant contribution arising from the colored-cluster $S$-wave sector. These results demonstrate that the tensor-polarized structure of the deuteron is driven primarily by hidden-color dynamics rather than by the $D$-wave component.

</details>


### [163] [Probing the inhomogeneous phases in magnetized quark matter via the bosonic two-point function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11795)
*Kang Deng,Bo Feng,Tianle Wei,Shuai Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes chiral condensate stability in magnetized two-flavor quark matter using the NJL model, revealing Peierls-type instabilities from Landau-level–driven 1D nesting, magnetic-field–induced negative 'canyons' in the bosonic two-point function, and a moat-like dispersion signaling precursor inhomogeneous chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability of homogeneous chiral condensates and possible emergence of inhomogeneous phases in dense, magnetized quark matter—relevant for neutron star interiors and heavy-ion collisions.

Method: Analytical derivation of the momentum-dependent bosonic two-point function (effective potential quadratic kernel) to quadratic order in fluctuations within the 2-flavor NJL model under a constant magnetic field; includes Landau quantization, zero- and finite-temperature analysis, and numerical mapping of the two-point function's momentum-space structure.

Result: At T = 0, sharp logarithmic divergences (Peierls instabilities) appear at each Landau-level Fermi momentum due to 1+1D nesting; strong B fields induce deep negative regions ('canyons') in the two-point function; thermal effects suppress but do not eliminate negativity at intermediate T; at large μ and moderate T, a non-monotonic 'moat' dispersion emerges—a flat positive basin at non-zero momentum—indicating residual tendency toward inhomogeneous chiral symmetry breaking.

Conclusion: Homogeneous chiral condensation is destabilized by magnetic-field–enhanced dimensional reduction and Fermi surface nesting; the persistence of negative modes and the emergence of moat dispersion robustly signal that inhomogeneous chiral phases are likely precursors or competitors in magnetized, dense QCD matter.

Abstract: We investigate the stability of homogeneous chiral condensates in magnetized quark matter within the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model. By expanding the effective action to quadratic order, we derive the full momentum-dependent bosonic two-point function in a constant magnetic field. At zero temperature, our analytical results show that each occupied Landau level produces a sharp logarithmic divergence at its corresponding Fermi momentum. This severe Peierls instability is driven by one-dimensional Fermi surface nesting within the effectively 1+1-dimensional subsystems created by Landau quantization. Numerically, we map out the momentum-space topography of the two-point function across varying magnetic fields and temperatures. We find that strong magnetic fields induce deep negative canyons. While thermal fluctuations smooth these canyons into shallower valleys as the temperature increases, the negative regions remain robust at intermediate temperatures and only vanish entirely at sufficiently high temperatures. Intriguingly, at larger chemical potentials where the system is strictly stable, a broad positive basin with a flat curvature at the origin persists at non-zero momentum. This non-monotonic dispersion is the hallmark of a moat regime, acting as a precursor state that strongly signals a residual tendency toward inhomogeneous chiral symmetry breaking. Our findings provide valuable insights into the phase structure of dense, magnetized quark matter.

</details>


### [164] [Interplay of Scalar and Pseudo-Scalar Mesonic Channels in $Λ\barΛ$ Spin Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11882)
*Kun Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates ΛΛ̄ spin correlations in heavy-ion collisions via s̄s production through scalar (0⁺) and pseudo-scalar (0⁻) mesonic channels; using the 3-flavor NJL model at RPA level, it finds dominant pseudo-scalar contribution yielding net negative spin correlation, angular dependence peaking at θ = 0, and collision-energy dependence sensitive to proximity to the chiral phase boundary—offering a novel probe of the chiral transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the chiral phase structure of QCD matter via spin correlations of ΛΛ̄ pairs, leveraging their sensitivity to the underlying s̄s production mechanism and its connection to scalar/pseudo-scalar meson dynamics near the chiral phase boundary.

Method: Analytical decomposition of ΛΛ̄ spin correlation based on parity and angular momentum constraints for J=0 meson exchange; implementation within the three-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model at the Random-Phase-Approximation (RPA) level to compute scalar and pseudo-scalar spectral weights; evaluation of momentum-integrated spin correlation along the freeze-out line as a function of collision energy.

Result: Pseudo-scalar channel dominates, yielding net negative spin correlation fraction; angular dependence shows strongest correlation at opening angle θ ≈ 0 and weakest at θ = π; collision-energy dependence exhibits non-monotonic behavior when the freeze-out trajectory passes near the chiral crossover region.

Conclusion: The ΛΛ̄ spin correlation is a sensitive, model-based observable for mapping the QCD phase diagram—particularly the location and nature of the chiral phase transition—via its distinct signatures in meson-channel dominance, angular modulation, and non-monotonic beam-energy dependence.

Abstract: The spin correlation of $\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}$ hyperon pairs produced in heavy-ion collisions is investigated through scalar and pseudo-scalar mesonic channels that mediate the creation of $s\bar{s}$ pairs. Because a $J=0$ meson forces $L=S$ while parity requires $(-1)^{L+1}=+/-1$, the two vertices project the pair onto distinct total-spin states: a scalar ($0^+$) vertex selects the $L=S=1$ configuration with a spin weight of $+1$, whereas a pseudo-scalar ($0^-$) vertex selects the $L=S=0$ configuration with a weight of $-3$; these weights are the traces of the correlation tensor, corresponding to $P_{s\bar{s}}=+1/3$ and $-1$, respectively. Implementing this framework within the three-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model at the Random-Phase-Approximation level, we compute the scalar and pseudo-scalar spectral weights. Our results show that the pseudo-scalar channel dominates, resulting in a negative net correlation fraction throughout. And the spin correlation also depends on the opening angle: strongest spin correlation at around $\theta=0$ while weakest at $\theta=\pi$. Furthermore, we show that the collision-energy dependence of the momentum-integrated spin correlation along the freeze-out line is sensitive to the trajectory's path relative to the chiral phase boundary. Trajectories passing near the crossover region exhibit a non-monotonic structure, providing a sensitive probe for the location of the chiral phase transition.

</details>


### [165] [Axion-Photon Conversion in Variable-Domain Magnetic Fields and the Highest-Energy Photons from GRB 221009A](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11983)
*Rick Sundar Garai,Tanmoy Kumar,Sourov Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes photon-axion-like particle (ALP) conversion as a unified explanation for ultra-high-energy photons detected from GRB 221009A (by LHAASO) and a ~300 TeV photon (by Carpet-3), using stochastic magnetic field modeling across cosmic environments; the best-fit ALP parameters evade current CAST constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain two anomalous high-energy photon observations—LHAASO's UHE photons from GRB 221009A and Carpet-3's isolated ~300 TeV photon—via photon-ALP oscillations, which could resolve tension with standard photon propagation expectations.

Method: Numerical propagation of the photon-ALP system through three magnetic environments: host galaxy (modeled as stochastic domains with random field orientations and variable lengths), intergalactic medium (uniform or turbulent fields), and Milky Way (using realistic field models); spectral fitting to combined LHAASO + Carpet-3 data via chi-square minimization.

Result: A broad domain-length distribution in the host galaxy significantly enhances photon survival probability above ~100 TeV, enabling simultaneous fit to both datasets; best-fit ALP mass m_a = 3.85×10⁻⁷ eV and coupling g_aγ = 3.07×10⁻¹¹ GeV⁻¹, with 1σ/2σ regions partially overlapping CAST exclusion limits.

Conclusion: Photon-ALP conversion in a realistically structured host-galaxy magnetic field provides a viable, self-consistent explanation for both ultra-high-energy photon detections without requiring exotic astrophysical sources or new propagation physics beyond ALPs; the inferred parameter space is testable by next-generation helioscopes and gamma-ray observatories.

Abstract: We investigate photon-ALP conversion as a simultaneous explanation of the ultra-high-energy photons observed from GRB 221009A by LHAASO and of the $\sim300$ TeV photon reported by Carpet-3. We model the magnetic field of the host galaxy as a set of domains with randomly oriented field directions and variable domain lengths, and propagate the photon-ALP system through the host galaxy, the intergalactic medium and the Milky Way. Fitting the combined LHAASO and Carpet-3 spectra, we find that a broad distribution of domain lengths raises the photon survival probability at the highest energies and accommodates the Carpet-3 point with photon-ALP conversion alone. The best fit, $m_a=3.85\times10^{-7}$~eV and $g_{a\gamma}=3.07\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$, lies below the CAST bound, and parts of the $1\sigma$ and $2\sigma$ regions are also compatible with it.

</details>


### [166] [Required spectral contributions in glueball QCD sum rules beyond single-pole fits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12073)
*Yong-Qi Kang,Zi-Yan Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a continuously verified diagnostic to distinguish between single-pole fits and broader mass-region contributions required by QCD sum-rule moments; it shows that even when narrow candidate mass bands (e.g., around X(2370)) are empty, broader bands (e.g., 30% width) are still required by the data—highlighting that pole fits do not imply necessity of their fitted mass region, and exclusion of all single poles does not eliminate evidence for broader spectral support.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in interpreting QCD sum-rule analyses: a successful single-pole fit is often misinterpreted as evidence for a physical state at that mass, whereas sum-rule moments may instead require broader spectral support—even if narrow candidate regions are vacant.

Method: Develops a diagnostic based on continuous verification of nonnegativity constraints on the spectral function under Borel-transformed sum rules; applies it to 14 two- and three-gluon glueball currents using central fixed-scale inputs, undeformed continua, and 1% matching tolerance; classifies outcomes into five diagnostic categories based on compatibility of single-pole fits and necessity/emptiness of 30% mass bands.

Result: Of 12 nonempty current inputs, all allow an empty narrow candidate band, yet 9 require a broader (30%) contribution; 4 of those 9 lower bounds persist on subwindows satisfying fixed-scale criteria; for the $GG,0^{-+}$ current (closest to X(2370)), the 20% band around 2370 MeV can be empty, but the 30% band requires ~44.2% of the low-energy Borel integral—indicating mandatory broad support; two currents admit no nonnegative spectrum.

Conclusion: Single-pole fits are insufficient to establish the existence or mass of a physical glueball state; sum-rule moments can mandate broad spectral contributions independently of narrow resonances—thus, claims about states like X(2370) based solely on pole fits are unwarranted without additional theoretical and experimental validation.

Abstract: We provide a continuously verified diagnostic that distinguishes a compatible single-pole fit from a mass region required by QCD sum-rule moments. For specified glueball sum-rule inputs, the moments can require contributions in broad mass bands even when narrower candidate bands are empty. We compare fourteen two- and three-gluon currents at central fixed-scale inputs, undeformed continua and 1\% matching tolerance. All twelve nonempty inputs permit an empty candidate band, yet nine still require a broader contribution. This separates two statements that a pole fit conflates: a successful pole fit does not make its mass region necessary, and excluding every single pole does not remove the information that a broader region must contribute. The five diagnostic classes of the survey organize these outcomes by whether a pole is compatible and whether the 30\% band is required or can be empty; two inputs admit no nonnegative spectrum. Four of the nine positive bounds persist on subwindows satisfying the fixed-scale input criteria. For the $GG,0^{-+}$ current, closest to the nominal $X(2370)$ mass, the 20\% band containing $X(2370)$ can be empty while the 30\% band requires about 44.2\% of each spectrum's own low-energy Borel integral. Determining the physical nature of $X(2370)$ requires further theoretical and experimental progress.

</details>


### [167] [Theoretical constraints on beyond Standard Model extensions of the Higgs potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12105)
*Javier E. Pena-Vico,Carlos Quezada-Calonge,Juan J. Sanz-Cillero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes theoretical constraints ensuring the boundedness-from-below (BFB) of Higgs potentials in extended sectors (RSE and 2HDM), derives and compares BFB conditions, provides a public tool for testing positivity in complex potentials, and uses these constraints to bound masses of new heavy scalars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure consistency of quantum field theories, the Higgs potential must be lower-bounded; this work addresses the challenge of deriving robust, complete, and practically applicable boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions for extended Higgs sectors where analytical treatment is nontrivial.

Method: Analytical derivation and systematic comparison of necessary and sufficient boundedness-from-below conditions for the Real Singlet Extension and both Z₂-conserving and Z₂-violating Two-Higgs-Doublet Models; development of a publicly available numerical code for positivity testing in general scalar potentials; translation of BFB constraints into bounds on physical scalar masses.

Result: Complete and improved BFB conditions for Z₂-violating 2HDM (previously incomplete in literature); validated agreement with prior results in Z₂-conserving cases; public code released; derived concrete upper and lower mass bounds for new heavy scalars in both models.

Conclusion: Boundedness-from-below constraints are highly restrictive and model-dependent, especially in Z₂-violating scenarios; rigorous enforcement of these conditions is essential for viable phenomenology and parameter-space scans, and the provided tools enable reliable application across broad classes of multi-scalar models.

Abstract: A fundamental requirement for the consistency of quantum field theories is that their Higgs potential is lower-bounded, such that no direction in the $\Phi$ field space leads to $V(\Phi)\to \, -\infty$. This work presents a comprehensive analysis of the various theoretical constraints on potentials in extended Higgs sectors, highlighting their relative impact and restrictiveness. We illustrate our study on the potential parameters with two particular Higgs sector extensions: the Real Singlet Extension (RSE) and the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (2HDM), separating $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$-conserving and $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$-violating cases. In order to do this, we derive and compare these conditions to other studies found in the literature. Additionally, we developed a publicly available code capable of testing positivity conditions in complex potentials, including particularly challenging cases such as the general $\mathbb{Z}_2$-violating 2HDM. Finally, we apply those restrictions to the physical parameters and deduce an upper and a lower bound on the masses of new heavy scalars.

</details>


### [168] [SuperWIMPs in the Kination Era: Imprints on $N_{\rm eff}$ and CMB Spectral Distortions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12148)
*Geneviève Bélanger,Abhishek Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how late-time decays of unstable particles into dark matter (N₁) and neutrinos in the scotogenic model—enhanced by a kination-dominated era—can simultaneously explain DM relic density, generate CMB μ-distortions and ΔN_eff, and yield LHC-detectable signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile underabundant dark matter production in standard cosmology within the scotogenic model by invoking non-standard early-universe expansion (kination) and to identify correlated, testable imprints on both cosmological observables (CMB spectral distortions, N_eff) and collider experiments (LHC).

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of N₁ relic abundance via superWIMP production from late decay of lightest inert doublet scalar; calculation of energy injection effects on CMB spectral distortions (μ-type) and relativistic degrees of freedom (ΔN_eff); mapping of viable parameter space (200–500 GeV inert scalars) onto LHC phenomenology (e.g., displaced vertices, missing energy + leptons).

Result: Kination enables correct N₁ relic density where standard cosmology fails; late decays produce observable μ-distortions and/or shifts in N_eff; 200–500 GeV inert scalars yield detectable LHC signatures (e.g., soft leptons + MET); strong complementarity between CMB-S4/LiteBIRD sensitivity and HL-LHC reach.

Conclusion: Late decays to DM + neutrinos in non-standard thermal histories generate multi-messenger signatures: CMB spectral distortions and N_eff provide cosmological probes of DM genesis, while colliders test the parent particle directly—enabling joint inference on dark matter origin and early-universe dynamics.

Abstract: We investigate how the late-time decays of unstable particles into dark matter and neutrinos can leave measurable imprints on both cosmological observables and collider experiments. We study this mechanism within the scotogenic model, where the lightest singlet fermion $N_1$ serves as the dark matter candidate and is produced via the late decay of the lightest inert doublet scalar via the superWIMP mechanism. By incorporating a kination-dominated era in the early Universe, the altered expansion rate allows to match the observed $N_1$ relic density in parameter regions that would be underabundant in standard cosmology. The energy exchange between the injected neutrinos and photons resulting from the late-time decay can produce CMB spectral $\mu$-distortions, detectable shifts in the effective number of relativistic species $N_{\rm eff}$, or both simultaneously. Concentrating on the lightest component of inert doublet scalars with masses between $200$ and $500~\text{GeV}$, we find that these cosmological signals are accompanied by complementary signatures accessible at the LHC. Although our analysis is derived within the scotogenic framework, our findings apply more broadly to scenarios in which the unstable particle undergoes late-time out-of-equilibrium decay to produce dark matter and non-thermal neutrinos, offering how future CMB measurements and collider searches can jointly probe dark matter production and the early Universe's thermal history.

</details>


### [169] [FORM 5.0 and further developments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12157)
*Ana Costa Pereira,Joshua Davies*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews FORM version 5.0, a computer algebra system essential for symbolic computations in theoretical high-energy physics, and highlights recent performance improvements post-release.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To present the new features of FORM 5.0 and address the growing demand for efficient large-scale symbolic manipulation in high-energy physics calculations.

Method: Review and analysis of FORM 5.0's features and subsequent performance-oriented developments, likely including benchmarking and optimization techniques.

Result: Documentation and characterization of FORM 5.0’s capabilities and identification of key performance enhancements made after its release.

Conclusion: FORM 5.0 significantly advances symbolic computation infrastructure for theoretical high-energy physics, and ongoing optimizations further strengthen its utility for large-scale calculations.

Abstract: In these proceedings we review the features of the recently-released version 5.0 of the FORM computer algebra system, which is one of the core software packages for the large-scale symbolic manipulations required by calculations in theoretical high-energy physics. We also discuss developments since the release of version 5.0, focussing in particular on recent performance improvements.

</details>


### [170] [Constraints on the Real Singlet Scalar Dark Matter Model from Higgs Self-Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12234)
*Hao-Yu Luo,Kun-Peng Chen,Han Hu,Yun Wei,Ming Zhong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes constraints on the real singlet scalar dark matter model using Higgs trilinear coupling ($\kappa_3$), perturbative unitarity, and Higgs invisible decay ($\mathrm{BR}_{\mathrm{inv}}$); finds current $\kappa_3$ measurements weaker than unitarity and especially $\mathrm{BR}_{\mathrm{inv}}$ limits; shows decoupling of $\kappa_3$, $\kappa_4$ above ~60 TeV; concludes future $\kappa_3$ precision at $\mathcal{O}(0.1\%)$ could meaningfully constrain high-mass WIMP parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the comparative constraining power of Higgs trilinear coupling measurements versus established theoretical (perturbative unitarity) and experimental (invisible decay, relic density, direct/indirect detection) bounds on the real singlet scalar dark matter model.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of parameter dependencies of Higgs trilinear ($\kappa_3$) and quartic ($\kappa_4$) coupling modifiers in the real singlet scalar model; comparison of exclusion contours from $\kappa_3$ measurement precision, perturbative unitarity, and $\mathrm{BR}_{\mathrm{inv}} \leq 0.107$; asymptotic analysis of decoupling behavior; sensitivity projection for future $\kappa_3$ measurements.

Result: Current best $\kappa_3$ constraint is weaker than perturbative unitarity, which in turn is weaker than $\mathrm{BR}_{\mathrm{inv}}$ limit; $\kappa_3$ and $\kappa_4$ decouple monotonically, vanishing asymptotically for $m_S \gtrsim 60\,\mathrm{TeV}$; no existing or near-future collider $\kappa_3$ measurement surpasses sensitivity of standard DM probes—except potentially at $\mathcal{O}(0.1\%)$ $\kappa_3$ precision, which would constrain part of the high-mass WIMP regime.

Conclusion: Higgs self-coupling measurements are subdominant to conventional DM constraints in the real singlet model under current and foreseeable collider sensitivities; only extreme $\kappa_3$ precision ($\lesssim 0.1\%$) could yield novel, complementary constraints on heavy WIMP parameter space.

Abstract: Taking the real singlet scalar dark matter model as a representative test case, we examine the constraints imposed on the model's parameter space by Higgs trilinear coupling measurements and perturbative unitarity requirements. For the low dark matter mass regime, the constraining strength delivered by the current highest-precision $\kappa_3$ measurement is notably inferior to the exclusion limit derived from perturbative unitarity requirements, while the perturbative unitarity bound itself remains considerably less restrictive than the constraints extracted from the extant experimental upper limit on the Higgs invisible decay branching ratio $\mathrm{BR}_{\mathrm{inv}} \leq 0.107$. Both the coupling modifiers $\kappa_3$ and $\kappa_4$ exhibit a well-defined decoupling behavior, with their respective magnitudes monotonically decreasing as the dark matter mass $m_S$ increases. As a direct consequence, the corrections to both couplings asymptotically approach zero for dark matter masses in excess of 60 TeV, when the parameter $a_2$ remains within its perturbative validity domain. Neither existing collider measurements of the Higgs trilinear coupling nor measurements targeted for planned future collider facilities possess the requisite sensitivity to impose more stringent constraints on the model's viable parameter space than the well-established bounds derived from experimental probes of dark matter relic density, invisible Higgs decay signatures, dark matter direct detection, and dark matter indirect detection. Nevertheless, under the scenario that the experimental measurement uncertainty of $\kappa_3$ reaches the $\mathcal{O}(0.1\%)$ scale or lower, non-negligible constraints acting on a subset of the large mass parameter space associated with the WIMP dark matter scenario can be expected.

</details>


### [171] [NLO QCD corrections to $W^{+}$-boson decays into doubly heavy baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12253)
*Hong-Hao Ma,Xu-Chang Zheng,Juan-Juan Niu,Xing-Gang Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to inclusive W⁺ → doubly heavy baryon + light antiquarks decays in NRQCD, finding large K-factors (2.27–2.95), significant scale/mass uncertainties, kinematic shifts toward lower energy fractions, and provides NLO-predicted branching ratios for Ξ_cc and Ξ_bc.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for production of doubly heavy baryons (Ξ_cc, Ξ_bc) in W⁺ decays by including indispensable NLO QCD corrections within NRQCD, enabling reliable comparison with future LHC or FCC-ee data.

Method: NRQCD factorization applied to inclusive decays W⁺ → Ξ_cc + c̄ + s̄ + X and W⁺ → Ξ_bc + b̄ + s̄ + X; full NLO QCD corrections computed for both color-antitriplet (3̄) and color-sextet (6) S-wave states; decay widths, kinematic distributions (invariant mass, energy fraction), and parametric uncertainties (m_c, m_b, renormalization scale μ_R) systematically analyzed.

Result: K-factors = 2.27 (Ξ_cc) and 2.95 (Ξ_bc) at central masses (m_c=1.8 GeV, m_b=5.1 GeV); individual-channel K-factors range 1.94–5.53 (max in [³S₁]₆ for Ξ_bc); NLO enhances low invariant-mass region and softens baryon energy spectrum; inclusive BR(W⁺→Ξ_cc) = 8.86×10⁻⁵, BR(W⁺→Ξ_bc) = 3.63×10⁻⁶ (using Γ_W = 2.085 GeV).

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are essential—enhancing LO widths by >100%—and strongly channel-dependent; the computed NLO branching ratios serve as robust benchmarks for experimental searches of Ξ_cc and Ξ_bc at high-energy colliders.

Abstract: The next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections have been investigated for the production of doubly heavy baryons via the inclusive $W^{+}$-boson decays $W^{+}\to\Xi_{cc}+\bar{c}+\bar{s}+X$ and $W^{+}\to\Xi_{bc}+\bar{b}+\bar{s}+X$ within the nonrelativistic QCD framework. The decay widths, the kinematic distributions, and the uncertainties induced by the heavy-quark masses and the renormalization scale have been analyzed for both the color-antitriplet ($\bar{\mathbf{3}}$) and the color-sextet ($\mathbf{6}$) $S$-wave states. With the central input parameters $m_{c}=1.8~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $m_{b}=5.1~\mathrm{GeV}$, the $K$ factors are $2.27$ for $\Xi_{cc}$ and $2.95$ for $\Xi_{bc}$, i.e., the NLO corrections increase the corresponding LO widths by about $127\%$ and $195\%$, which shows that the NLO corrections are indispensable for quantitative predictions. The individual-channel $K$ factors range from $1.94$ to $5.53$, with the largest one occurring for $\Xi_{bc}$ in the $[{}^{3}S_{1}]_{\mathbf{6}}$ channel. The distributions show that the NLO corrections enhance the low invariant-mass region and shift the baryon energy spectrum toward smaller energy fractions. With $\Gamma_{W}=2.085~\mathrm{GeV}$, the inclusive branching ratios are $8.86\times10^{-5}$ and $3.63\times10^{-6}$ for $\Xi_{cc}$ and $\Xi_{bc}$, respectively, which provide an NLO benchmark for future experimental searches.

</details>


### [172] [A Self-Interacting Dark-Matter Route to Supermassive Kerr Black Holes via Super-Eddington Bondi Accretion: Mass-Spin Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12282)
*Chian-Shu Chen,Feng-Li Lin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on Schwarzschild black holes to Kerr black holes, showing that relativistic Bondi accretion of self-interacting dark matter remains an efficient super-Eddington growth mechanism—even with rotation—enabling rapid, joint mass and spin growth of early stellar-mass black hole seeds into supermassive Kerr black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain rapid black-hole growth observed in the early Universe (e.g., by JWST), particularly how stellar-mass seeds could become supermassive Kerr black holes within cosmological timescales.

Method: Analyzes stationary, equatorial, quasi-spherical critical relativistic Bondi accretion onto Kerr black holes, incorporating repulsively self-interacting dark matter and rotating inflow; determines existence conditions for smooth critical solutions using marginally bound capture angular momentum limits.

Result: Critical accretion solutions exist for a finite range of inflow angular momentum (bounded by Kerr marginally bound capture values); rotation modifies sonic structure and efficiency but preserves key features (e.g., critical density cancellation, halo matching); joint mass–spin growth is feasible until halo supply depletes.

Conclusion: Relativistic Bondi accretion of self-interacting dark matter provides a robust, super-Eddington pathway for forming early supermassive Kerr black holes from stellar-mass seeds—including simultaneous spin-up—without requiring fine-tuned initial conditions or exotic physics.

Abstract: Recent JWST observations motivate mechanisms for rapid black-hole growth in the early Universe. Extending our previous Schwarzschild study, we show that critical relativistic Bondi accretion of repulsively self-interacting dark matter remains an efficient super-Eddington channel for Kerr black holes with rotating inflow. Within a stationary equatorial quasi-spherical framework, smooth critical solutions exist over a finite range of inflow angular momentum set by the Kerr marginally bound capture values. Rotation changes the sonic structure and accretion efficiency, while the critical density cancellation and dark-sector/halo matching retain their Schwarzschild form. The resulting rapid growth persists until the finite halo supply becomes limiting. Because the inflow also carries angular momentum, stellar-mass seeds can grow and spin up simultaneously; illustrative population evolutions therefore provide a joint mass--spin route to early supermassive Kerr black holes.

</details>


### [173] [Naturalness in the Past from a Time-Dependent Supersymmetry Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12363)
*Tony Gherghetta,Kunio Kaneta,Wenqi Ke*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an epoch-dependent electroweak naturalness mechanism in no-scale supergravity, where a rolling quintessence modulus dynamically sets the SUSY-breaking scale—lighter in the early universe, heavier today—while suppressing fifth forces and varying constants via modular weight correlations; it requires tuning for radiative stability and yields testable consequences for phase transitions, relic densities, and gravitational waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the electroweak hierarchy problem by relaxing naturalness constraints across cosmic time, leveraging cosmological evolution rather than static fine-tuning.

Method: Constructs a no-scale supergravity model with a dynamical quintessence modulus coupled to the visible sector via modulus-dependent superpotential Yukawa couplings and Kähler metric with correlated modular weights; analyzes suppression of fifth-force/varying-constant effects and radiative stability of the quintessence potential.

Result: A viable epoch-dependent naturalness scenario is realized: superpartner masses vary cosmologically; fifth-force and varying-alpha constraints are evaded; radiative corrections to the quintessence potential necessitate modest tuning of field-dependent vacuum energy; predicts modified thermal history, altered phase transition dynamics, non-standard relic abundances, and distinctive stochastic gravitational-wave spectra.

Conclusion: Electroweak naturalness need not be time-invariant; embedding it in dynamical moduli cosmology offers a theoretically consistent, phenomenologically rich alternative to conventional SUSY naturalness, with falsifiable signatures in early-universe observables.

Abstract: We consider the possibility that electroweak naturalness may be epoch dependent. In no-scale supergravity, a rolling quintessence modulus can control the supersymmetry-breaking scale, allowing superpartners to be much lighter in the early universe while remaining heavy today. Fifth-force and varying-constant effects can be suppressed via correlated modular weights in the visible-sector Kähler metric and modulus-dependent superpotential Yukawa couplings, while radiative stability of the quintessence potential requires additional tuning of the field-dependent vacuum energy. The resulting effective theory has distinctive implications for early-universe phase transitions, relic abundances, and gravitational waves.

</details>


### [174] [From oxygen to lead: testing a common QGP description with high-$p_\perp$ probes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12404)
*Bithika Karmakar,Dusan Zigic,Magdalena Djordjevic*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests whether a unified quark-gluon plasma (QGP) description can simultaneously explain jet quenching (via $R_{AA}$) and elliptic flow ($v_2$, $v_3$) across light-ion (O+O, Ne+Ne) to heavy-ion (Xe+Xe, Pb+Pb) collisions, using event-by-event hydrodynamics coupled to the DREENA energy loss model with fixed microscopic parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Suppression ($R_{AA}$) and finite high-$p_\perp$ elliptic anisotropy ($v_2$) in small systems (O+O, Ne+Ne) challenge whether a QGP forms and whether its properties scale consistently across system sizes — motivating a unified, parameter-consistent theoretical framework to assess QGP universality.

Method: Event-by-event viscous hydrodynamic bulk evolution constrained by soft-sector data, coupled to DREENA for radiative + collisional parton energy loss; same microscopic parameters applied uniformly across O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb; predictions include $R_{AA}$, $v_2^{SP}$, $v_3^{SP}$, $v_2\{4\}/v_2\{2\}$, and flavor-differential (D/B meson) observables.

Result: The model reproduces observed suppression ordering and predicts comparable $v_2$ magnitudes across all four systems in 10–20% centrality; predicts distinct centrality trends for $v_2^{SP}$ (modest decrease) and $v_3^{SP}$ (stronger decrease) in light ions; finds $v_2^{SP}/(1-R_{AA})$ nearly $p_\perp$-independent at high $p_\perp$; and shows $v_2\{4\}/v_2\{2\}$ weakly $p_\perp$-dependent, matching soft-sector behavior — extended to D/B mesons.

Conclusion: A common QGP description — with consistent initial-state fluctuations, medium evolution, and parton energy loss — quantitatively accounts for both suppression and anisotropic flow across system sizes from oxygen to lead, supporting QGP formation even in light-ion collisions and providing falsifiable predictions for harmonic structure, fluctuation scaling, and flavor dependence.

Abstract: The observation of suppression and finite high-$p_\perp$ elliptic anisotropy in O+O and Ne+Ne raises the question of whether a common quark--gluon plasma (QGP) description can simultaneously account for both observables across systems from oxygen to lead. We present predictions using event-by-event bulk evolution constrained by soft-sector observables and coupled to DREENA radiative and collisional energy loss, with the same microscopic parameter set for O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb. The calculation reproduces the observed ordering of suppression and predicts elliptic anisotropies of comparable magnitude across the four systems in 10--20$\%$ collisions. We predict a modest centrality dependence of $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}$ and a more pronounced decrease of $v_3^{\mathrm{SP}}$ toward peripheral light-ion collisions, in contrast to the corresponding trends in larger systems. In light ions, we predict that $v_2^{\mathrm{SP}}/(1-R_{AA})$ is nearly independent of momentum at high $p_\perp$. We further predict that the differential ratio $v_2\{4\}/v_2\{2\}$ depends only weakly on momentum at high $p_\perp$, with magnitudes and centrality trends broadly comparable to those of the measured soft-sector ratios, suggesting that the soft-sector fluctuation pattern persists in hard--soft correlations. Predictions for D and B mesons extend the comparison to probes of different flavors. Taken together, these system-size, harmonic, fluctuation, and flavor dependences provide experimental tests of how far a common QGP description extends.

</details>


### [175] [A singlet framework for the mu problem and type-II seesaw in a generalized NMSSM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12625)
*Xiaokang Du,Yusi Pan,Fei Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a generalized NMSSM where a gauge singlet connects the Higgs bilinear to the type-II seesaw scale, enabling natural generation of μ_eff and vectorlike triplet masses; integrating out triplets yields the Weinberg operator, and neutralino dark matter scenarios (bino-/singlino-like) with high-scale triplets (~10^15 GeV) remain viable under relic density and direct-detection constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile naturalness of the μ-term in the NMSSM with high-scale neutrino mass generation (type-II seesaw) while maintaining viable electroweak-scale dark matter and satisfying phenomenological constraints.

Method: Constructs a generalized NMSSM with a gauge singlet coupling the Higgs bilinear to a type-II seesaw sector; computes μ_eff from singlet VEV and soft SUSY-breaking contributions; integrates out vectorlike SU(2)_L triplets to generate the Weinberg operator; analyzes neutralino dark matter candidates (bino- and singlino-like), relic abundance via funnels/coannihilation, and direct-detection limits.

Result: μ_eff remains natural due to the supersymmetric non-renormalization theorem despite dependence on soft parameters and threshold effects; triplet scales up to ~10^15 GeV are compatible with TeV-scale neutralino DM; viable spectra include heavy-Higgs funnels and coannihilation with gluinos or winos, all satisfying Planck-measured relic density and XENON/LUX direct-detection bounds.

Conclusion: The model successfully unifies natural μ-term generation, high-scale neutrino mass, and viable WIMP dark matter within a supersymmetric framework, offering testable signatures at colliders and direct-detection experiments.

Abstract: We study a generalized NMSSM in which a gauge singlet links the Higgs bilinear to the type-II seesaw scale. The singlet vacuum expectation value contributes to $\mu_{\rm eff}$ and generates the supersymmetric mass of a vectorlike triplet pair. Integrating out the triplets gives the Weinberg operator, and their gauge charges admit a possible messenger interpretation. Large cancellation between the bare $\mu_0$ term and the singlet VEV generated bilinear Higgs term that keeps $\mu_{\rm eff}$ near the soft scale is still natural in the supersymmetric limit (by the supersymmetric non-renormalization theorem), although the concrete value of $\mu_{\rm eff}$ also depends slightly on the soft SUSY breaking parameters and finite threshold matching. The studied bino-like and singlino-like neutralino solutions accommodate triplet input scales approaching $10^{15}$ GeV together with electroweak-to-TeV dark matter. Representative spectra consistent with heavy-Higgs funnels or coannihilation with gluinos or winos yield allowed relic abundances while satisfying direct-detection constraints.

</details>


### [176] [Angular analysis of the purely baryonic decays $Λ_b^0\to n p\bar p$ and $Λ_b^0\toΛp\bar p$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12630)
*Chao-Qiang Geng,Chao Han*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs an angular analysis of Λb⁰→np̄p and Λb⁰→Λp̄p decays using a phenomenological generalized-factorization model with coherent interference of two current contributions. It computes pair-angle distributions, forward-backward asymmetries, Legendre moments, and Λ polarization; finds small n̄p-frame asymmetry due to cancellation across mass bins, and significant shift in Λ polarization under p̄p mass cut — highlighting sensitivity to interference and phase-space selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dynamics of purely baryonic Λb⁰ decays—rare processes sensitive to QCD factorization, final-state interactions, and interference effects—and to establish baseline angular/spin observables for theory–experiment comparison.

Method: Phenomenological generalized-factorization model incorporating coherent interference of two distinct weak current contributions yielding identical final states; full angular analysis including pair-angle distributions, forward-backward hemisphere asymmetries (in n̄p and p̄p frames), Legendre polynomial moments, and Λ polarization; integration over invariant mass bins (e.g., m(p̄p) < 2.85 GeV).

Result: In Λb⁰→np̄p: FB asymmetry = 4.46×10⁻⁴ (n̄p frame) and 0.856 (p̄p frame) at Nc^eff = 3 and zero relative phase; small n̄p asymmetry arises from sign compensation across mass bins. In Λb⁰→Λp̄p: applying m(p̄p) < 2.85 GeV modifies angular distributions and shifts rate-weighted longitudinal Λ polarization from −0.803 to −0.954.

Conclusion: Angular and spin observables in these baryonic Λb⁰ decays are highly sensitive to the relative strength and phase of interfering current contributions, as well as to phase-space cuts—making them powerful probes of nonleptonic decay dynamics; this model-dependent study serves as a foundational benchmark for future theoretical refinements and experimental measurements.

Abstract: An angular analysis of the purely baryonic decays $\Lambda_b^0\to np\bar p$ and $\Lambda_b^0\to\Lambda p\bar p$ is presented within a phenomenological generalized-factorization model. Within this model, two current contributions leading to the same final state are combined coherently. Pair-angle distributions, forward-backward hemisphere asymmetries, and Legendre moments are calculated in both channels, together with the polarization of the produced $\Lambda$ for an unpolarized parent. For the neutron channel, the full-region forward-backward asymmetries are $4.46\times10^{-4}$ in the $n\bar p$ frame and $0.856$ in the $p\bar p$ frame at $N_c^{\mathrm{eff}}=3$ and zero additional relative phase. From mass-bin integration, the small first asymmetry is found to arise from compensation between signed contributions from different regions. In the $\Lambda$ channel, the angular distributions are modified by the selection $m(p\bar p)<2.85\,\mathrm{GeV}$, and the rate-weighted longitudinal polarization along the $\Lambda$ flight direction is shifted from $-0.803$ to $-0.954$. These results show how angular and spin observables depend on the relative current contributions and the selected phase-space region. The model-dependent analysis is intended as a first step toward understanding the dynamics of these decays and as a basis for further theoretical studies and comparisons with measurements.

</details>


### [177] [Pentagon Functions for Two-Loop Leading-Colour $pp\to t\bar{t}H$ Amplitudes with a Light-Quark Loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12661)
*Simone Devoto,Manfred Kraus,Ben Page*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a numerically stable, efficient C++ implementation of planar two-loop master integrals for $pp \to t\bar{t}H$ with a light-quark loop, using analytic expressions (logarithms, dilogarithms) at weights one and two, and adaptive Chebyshev approximation for higher-weight integrals involving nested square roots; it extends the PentagonFunctions++ library and is validated for NNLO phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
