Motivation: To enable precise NNLO QCD predictions for $t\bar{t}H$ production at the LHC, which requires fast, accurate, and numerically stable evaluation of planar two-loop master integrals with a closed light-quark loop—particularly challenging due to nested square-root branch structures in the physical region.

Method: Constructed a basis of special functions with analytic expressions up to transcendental weight two (logarithms, dilogarithms); represented higher-weight functions as one-fold integrals; analyzed analytic structure of nested roots; built numerically stable $\mathrm{d}\log$ forms and letters; used adaptive Chebyshev approximation for non-logarithmic antiderivatives; implemented in C++ as an extension of PentagonFunctions++.

Result: A robust, high-precision, and fast C++ library for evaluating the required two-loop master integrals across the physical phase space; benchmarked performance confirms suitability for phenomenological use in NNLO calculations.

Conclusion: The developed implementation provides a critical, ready-to-use computational building block for state-of-the-art NNLO predictions of $t\bar{t}H$ production, resolving numerical instabilities from nested radicals and enabling future precision studies at the LHC.

Abstract: We present a basis of special functions and an efficient numerical implementation for the planar two-loop master integrals that enter the leading-colour amplitudes for $pp\to t\bar{t}H$ with a closed light-quark loop. We provide analytic results for the special functions at transcendental weight one and two in terms of logarithms and dilogarithms, while higher weights are evaluated as one-fold integrals. We focus on the nested roots that arise, studying their analytic structure on the physical region and constructing numerically stable representations of letters and $\mathrm{d}\log$ forms that involve them. We find that a number of $\mathrm{d}\log$ forms have an antiderivative that cannot be represented by a single logarithm over the physical region and tackle them numerically with adaptive Chebyshev approximation. We implement the functions in \texttt{C++} as an extension of the \texttt{PentagonFunctions++} library. We study the performance of our implementation over physical phase-space points and find that the evaluation is accurate and fast enough for phenomenological application. This provides an important building block for NNLO studies of $t\bar{t}H$ production at the LHC.

</details>


### [178] [Amplitudes separation and strong-electromagnetic phases determination in the $\boldsymbol{J/ψ}$ decays into baryon-antibaryon pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12673)
*Chiara Alice,Antonio Amoroso,Matteo Barbagiovanni,Monica Bertani,Diego Bettoni,Fabrizio Bianchi,Alberto Bortone,Damiano Cabiati,Alessandro Calcaterra,Gianluigi Cibinetto,Fabio Cossio,Marco Destefanis,Francesca De Mori,Emma Di Fiore,Luciano Fava,Sara Garbolino,Isabella Garzia,Michela Greco,Marco Maggiora,Simonetta Marcello,Federico Matias Melendi,Giulio Mezzadri,Piero Patteri,Simone Pacetti,Angelo Rivetti,Manuel Dionisio Da Rocha Rolo,Francesco Rosini,Marco Scodeggio,Stefano Sosio,Stefano Spataro,Umberto Zarantonello*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extracts the relative phase between strong and electromagnetic amplitudes in vector charmonium decays to spin-1/2 baryon–antibaryon pairs using a flavour-SU(3) effective Lagrangian with vector and tensor currents; it derives electric/magnetic phases, integrated overlap phase, and effective rate angle from partial-wave parametrization, yielding two viable solutions consistent with Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ scan data but in tension with several EM production phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the relative strong–electromagnetic phase in vector charmonium → baryon–antibaryon decays, which is essential for understanding QCD dynamics, final-state interactions, and testing SU(3) symmetry in hadronic transitions.

Method: Constructs a flavour-SU(3)-invariant effective Lagrangian incorporating vector and tensor currents; parametrizes decay amplitudes via partial-wave expansion; analytically derives electric, magnetic, integrated overlap, and effective rate angles from the same complex amplitude structure.

Result: Two discrete solutions for the relative phase are obtained; both reproduce the measured Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ scan phase within experimental uncertainty, but exhibit statistically significant tension with published electric–magnetic production phase measurements in related channels.

Conclusion: The analysis confirms theoretical consistency of the SU(3) framework for these decays but reveals discrepancies with existing EM phase extractions—suggesting either underestimated systematic uncertainties in prior measurements or limitations in the current effective description (e.g., omitted higher-order SU(3) breaking or coupled-channel effects).

Abstract: We extract the relative phase between strong and electromagnetic amplitudes of the decays of a vector charmonium into spin-$1/2$ baryon-antibaryon pairs. Starting from a flavour-SU(3) effective Lagrangian with vector and tensor currents, we construct a parametrization in terms of partial waves coefficients. The electric and magnetic phases, the phase of the integrated complex overlap and the effective rate angle are derived from the same source amplitudes. We retain two possible solutions and both of them describe the scalar $\Sigma^+\bar\Sigma^-$ scan phase within its uncertainty, while presenting tension with several electric-magnetic production phases.

</details>


### [179] [Nucleon transversity from dihadron data and x-dependent lattice QCD constraint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12745)
*Hongxin Dong,Yuan-Yuan Huang,Tianbo Liu,Xiaomin Shen,Peng Sun,Xiaonu Xiong,Jian-Hui Zhang,Yuxiang Zhao,Yiyu Zhou,Ruilin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a joint neural-network analysis of collinear transversity distributions and dihadron fragmentation functions using data from e+e−, SIDIS, and polarized pp collisions, augmented by lattice QCD constraints on isovector transversity; inclusion of lattice input modifies down-valence (more negative) and up-valence (enhanced at large x) transversity, with the central down-valence result violating Soffer bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on collinear transversity distributions—particularly flavor-separated quark/antiquark contributions—which are poorly determined from experimental data alone due to chiral-odd nature and lack of direct observables; lattice QCD provides complementary, first-principles input on the isovector combination.

Method: Joint global neural-network fit incorporating experimental data from three processes: dihadron production in e+e− annihilation, semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (SIDIS), and polarized proton-proton collisions; lattice QCD results for the isovector transversity h1^u−d(x) are included as external constraints in the fit; matched fits with and without lattice input are performed and compared.

Result: Lattice input shifts down-valence transversity toward more negative values and enhances up-valence transversity at large x; the central down-valence distribution exceeds the Soffer bound |h1^d_v(x)| ≤ (1 + g1^d_v(x))/2, indicating potential tension with positivity constraints; both fits (with/without lattice) describe experimental data comparably well.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD provides valuable, complementary constraints on transversity that reshape flavor-separated distributions, especially at large x; observed violation of Soffer bounds for down-valence transversity calls for scrutiny—either of theoretical assumptions, lattice systematics, or experimental inputs—and motivates further tests of positivity in transverse spin structure.

Abstract: We extract collinear transversity distributions and dihadron fragmentation functions in a joint neural-network analysis of dihadron production in electron-positron annihilation, semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering, and polarized proton-proton collisions. We incorporate lattice QCD calculation of isovector transversity, which constrains the up-down flavor differences for quarks and antiquarks over broad momentum fraction range complementary to data. Matched fits with and without the lattice input describe the experimental data comparably well. Including the lattice input shifts the down-valence distribution toward more negative values and enhances up-valence transversity at large $x$. The magnitude of the central down-valence result exceeds the reference Soffer inequalities, motivating further test of positivity constraints.

</details>


### [180] [Quality time in extra dimensions: axion quality and radion cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12765)
*Falguni Biswas,Yeshan Juberi,Ahmed Rakin Kamal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how higher-form symmetries and radion stabilization mechanisms in a 5D circle compactification affect the axion solution to the strong CP problem, quantifying residual CP violation from charged matter and magnetic gauging, analyzing radion phenomenology (mass, decay channels, reheating), and showing how radion-dominated cosmology enables viable axion dark matter for fa = 10^14 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether extra-dimensional axions—arising from an Abelian gauge field in 5D with dynamical radius—can robustly solve the strong CP problem despite symmetry-breaking effects from electrically charged matter and magnetic gauging, while ensuring viable radion stabilization and cosmology.

Method: Analyzes Casimir-energy-based radion stabilization in 5D circle compactification with dynamical radius; computes residual theta_CP from charged-matter loops and magnetic gauging contributions; evaluates radion mass, decay channels (including graviphoton), reheating via non-minimal Higgs-curvature coupling; calculates axion abundance evolution in radion-dominated vs. radiation-dominated eras.

Result: Minkowski radion minima yield masses 100–170 TeV; charged-matter breaking gives theta_CP ~ 10^-17; magnetic gauging remains compatible if coupling is small; larger radius suppresses charged but enhances magnetic breaking; radion decay yields T_reh ≈ 85 MeV and ΔN_eff ≈ 0.02; entropy dilution allows θ_i ≈ 0.43 (vs. 0.065 in RD) for fa = 10^14 GeV axion DM.

Conclusion: The 5D axion model with Casimir-stabilized radion can successfully solve the strong CP problem and produce correct axion dark matter abundance, provided magnetic couplings are sufficiently weak and cosmological evolution includes a transient radion-dominated era.

Abstract: Higher-form symmetries can protect extra-dimensional axions, but additional symmetry breaking can still spoil the solution to the strong CP problem. We focus on electrically charged matter and magnetic gauging in a five-dimensional circle compactification where the axion arises from an Abelian gauge field and the radius is dynamical. We discuss two mechanisms for stabilising the radius/radion using Casimir energy from fields neutral under the Abelian gauge field, with and without an additional periodic scalar winding around the compact circle. Both admit Minkowski and de Sitter minima. At the Minkowski minima (with radion masses of $100$--$170\,\mathrm{TeV}$), electrically charged matter induces a residual strong CP angle of order $10^{-17}$ and shifts the radius negligibly. Magnetic gauging remains compatible after including its additional degree of freedom in the Casimir spectrum, provided its coupling is sufficiently small. We observe that increasing the radius suppresses charged-matter breaking relative to QCD but increases magnetic breaking contribution. We also determine the radion decay channels, including the graviphoton, and study reheating from a possible radion-dominated era without scalar winding or magnetic gauging. For a non-minimal Higgs-curvature coupling $\xi=7$, the visible-sector reheating temperature is about $85\,\mathrm{MeV}$ with a dark-radiation contribution of $\Delta N_{\rm eff}\approx0.02$. The entropy released during radion decay dilutes the axion abundance, allowing axions with $f_a=10^{14}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and an initial misalignment angle $\vartheta_i\approx0.43$ to account for the entire observed dark matter abundance. For the same decay constant, reproducing this abundance in radiation domination requires $\vartheta_i\approx0.065$.

</details>


### [181] [Electroweak Parton Distribution Functions for Future Lepton Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12793)
*Tao Han,Wolfgang Kilian,Yang Ma,Krzysztof Mękała,Jürgen Reuter,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper implements Electroweak Parton Distribution Functions (EW PDFs) in the Monte Carlo generator Whizard to model collinear electroweak radiation at future high-energy lepton colliders, enabling resummation of large logarithms and facilitating partonic interpretation of hard scattering involving photons and weak bosons from lepton splittings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: At future multi-TeV lepton colliders (e.g., muon colliders or high-energy e+e− linear colliders), collinear electroweak radiation dominates; standard fixed-order calculations fail due to large logarithms from SM lepton-to-boson splittings, necessitating a resummed, parton-like framework—namely EW PDFs—for accurate phenomenology and BSM studies.

Method: Implementation of the EW PDF formalism—including DGLAP evolution for electroweak gauge bosons and photons—within the Whizard Monte Carlo event generator; validation via comparison with fixed-order calculations and the Effective Weak Boson Approximation (EWBA); analysis of kinematic and dynamical regimes where EW PDFs provide superior accuracy or efficiency.

Result: Successful integration of EW PDFs into Whizard; demonstration of improved description of high-mass diboson, vector-boson fusion, and BSM-sensitive processes over fixed-order and EWBA; identification of key kinematic regions (e.g., high invariant mass, forward production) where EW PDFs are essential; establishment of computational readiness for 'electroweak jet' phenomenology.

Conclusion: The EW PDF implementation in Whizard provides a robust, resummed framework for collider simulations at future lepton machines; it bridges the gap between perturbative QCD-inspired factorization and electroweak dynamics, and is critical for precision SM tests and discovery potential in the electroweak sector.

Abstract: At future high-energy colliders, such as a multi-TeV muon collider or the high-energy stages of an $e^+e^-$ linear collider, collinear radiation of electroweak gauge bosons becomes a dominant phenomenon. In this regime, many key processes are primarily initiated by photons and gauge bosons originating from lepton splittings. Electroweak Parton Distribution Functions (EW PDFs) provide an appropriate framework to describe these interactions by resumming large collinear logarithms arising from all possible Standard Model splittings, thereby enabling a partonic interpretation of the hard scattering. The formalism is particularly suitable for studying new physics beyond the Standard Model in the electroweak sector at higher scales. In this paper, we present the implementation of the EW PDF formalism within the Monte Carlo event generator \texttt{Whizard}. We demonstrate several applications of this framework and analyze its interplay with full fixed-order calculations as well as the purely kinematic Effective Weak Boson Approximation. We highlight scenarios where the EW PDF approach is especially useful for phenomenology and physics-potential studies for future lepton colliders. The EW PDF formalism is a vital step towards the ``electroweak jet regime'' where electroweak particles behave similarly to QCD jets.

</details>
